Path outside every `reference_scopes` entry whose `org_id` is the secret's org id (scopes are keyed by the immutable org id, never the slug; D-107), a path under `<managed_prefix>/`, a missing secret or field, a field whose value is not a string → **422** `reference_not_permitted` (one code, so a tenant cannot probe paths outside its scopes). Vault unreachable → 503 `secret_store_unavailable`. `Secret.reference` echoes `{ store_id, path, field }` (the effective field, never `null` for Vault); never a value.
- C-372 **Stored reference form**: `ext:<store_id>:<pct path>#<pct field>@latest` (D-107; the `08 §2` grammar allows `latest` for reference refs). A reference is always read at the latest KV version; rotation in Vault is picked up within the gateway's 60 s plaintext cache.
- C-373 **Scope check on every open** (SEC-61): `open` of a reference ref re-checks the path against the **current** `reference_scopes` entries whose `org_id` equals the `SealContext` org id (the secret row's org; never matched by slug, so a renamed or re-taken slug moves no scope, D-107) and the managed-prefix exclusion before reading; a refusal is `ReferenceNotPermitted`. The gateway then injects nothing and answers **502** `credential_unavailable` (`details.kind = "secret"`) (D-107: the existing code for a granted secret that cannot be produced); the API maps it to 422 `reference_not_permitted` where it opens values (C-371). Removing a scope from the configuration therefore stops existing references at the next open.
- C-374 **Managed mode** (no wire change): `put` writes `<kv_mount>/data/<managed_prefix>/<org_id>/<purpose>/<owner_id>` with `{ "value": <base64>, "purpose", "owner_id", "org_id" }` and returns `ext:<store_id>:<pct path>@<kv version>`; `get` reads that exact version and fails `SecretRefMismatch` unless purpose, owner and org match the `SealContext`; `delete` destroys that version (`/destroy`). Auth or renewal failure → `StoreUnavailable` → 503 `secret_store_unavailable` (API) / 503 `secret_store_unavailable` with `details.store_id` (gateway).
- C-375 **Migration tooling** (CLI, not HTTP): `aurora-api secrets migrate --to <vault store id> [--purpose …] [--dry-run]` and back `--to inline`, unchanged syntax; reference refs are never migrated (`value_source = reference`).

### Phase 3 — RFE-014 (gateway under a Postgres outage, D-61) and RFE-019 (deployment-neutral defaults, D-63)

Rule: `06 §Ports`, `§Operation during a Postgres outage`, `§Errors`, `§Telemetry`, `01 §Data plane`, `05 §Event bus`, `02 §Limits` (`GATEWAY_DB_PROBE_INTERVAL`, `GATEWAY_LISTEN_GRACE`, `REQUEST_LOG_OUTAGE_BUFFER`), `15 §Observability`, SEC-54; RFE-019: `10 §Default agent`, `11 §Sandbox image`, `15 §Deployment-specific settings example`. Reserved numbers C-380..C-389 (reserved, unused: C-386..C-389). All gateway surfaces below are internal (port 4103) or the proxy port, not `/v1`.

- C-380 **`GET /readyz`** (gateway, port 4103, unauthenticated): `ready` → 200 `{ "state": "ready" }`; `degraded` → **200** `{ "state": "degraded", "reason": "database_unavailable" | "listener_down", "since": Timestamp, "cache_entries": int, "cache_oldest_age_secs": int | null }` (`since` = when the gateway entered `degraded`; `cache_oldest_age_secs` `null` when the caches are empty); `down` → 503 `{ "state": "down", "reason": "snapshot_not_loaded" | "draining" }`. Query `strict=true` answers 503 for `degraded` as well (same body); any other `strict` value is ignored. `reason` = `database_unavailable` when the database is unreachable (whether or not the listener is up), else `listener_down`. Other services' `/readyz` are unchanged.
- C-381 **`GET /internal/v1/status`** (gateway; C-50 auth) gains `state: "ready" | "degraded" | "down"`, `db_unreachable_since: Timestamp | null`, `listener_connected: bool`, `cache: { entries: int, oldest_age_secs: int | null }` (the same two numbers as `/readyz`) and `request_log_buffered: int`.
- C-382 **Refusals while degraded** (proxy port, standard gateway error body): **503** `gateway_degraded` with `details.reason = "database_unavailable"` (credential not in the identity cache — never 407 —, host without a ResolvedHost entry, context credential minted after the outage began) or `"credential_store_unavailable"` (plaintext not cached, OAuth refresh needed), headers `retry-after: 5`, `x-should-retry: true`; a request that needs a new approval hold → 503 `approval_store_unavailable` (unchanged code). In `down`: new `CONNECT`s → 503 `gateway_degraded` with `details.reason = "snapshot_not_loaded"` (D-107) or 503 `gateway_restarting` while draining. A hold whose deadline passes during the outage → 403 `manual_approval_expired` (unchanged). The database lane maps the same conditions to FATAL `57P03` (C-220, unchanged).
- C-383 **Metrics and log** (gateway `/metrics`): `aurora_gateway_state{state="ready"|"degraded"|"down"}` (1 for the current state, 0 otherwise), `aurora_gateway_db_unreachable_seconds`, `aurora_gateway_degraded_requests_total{outcome="served_from_cache"|"refused", reason}`, `aurora_gateway_listener_reconnects_total`, `aurora_gateway_cache_flush_total{reason="listener_reconnect"|"invalidate_all"}`, `aurora_gateway_request_log_buffered`, `aurora_gateway_request_log_dropped_total`. One structured log line per transition: `gateway.state_change` with `from`, `to`, `reason`.
- C-384 **Configuration** (no new key): `gateway.resolve_cache_ttl_secs` (default 60) is also the outage tolerance; above 300 the gateway logs a warning at start (still starts). Constants `GATEWAY_DB_PROBE_INTERVAL` 5 s (timeout 2 s, 2 failures), `GATEWAY_LISTEN_GRACE` 30 s, reconnect backoff 0.5 s → 10 s, `REQUEST_LOG_OUTAGE_BUFFER` 10 000 rows.
- C-385 **RFE-019** (no wire contract): `agents.default_brief` default becomes the neutral brief of `10 §Default agent` (≤ 20 000 chars; empty = the default; applied to new workspaces only); `agents.default_name` stays `Daneel`; the sandbox base image is a build-time argument `SANDBOX_BASE_IMAGE` (default the public AlmaLinux 10 image, digest-pinned), not a runtime key. No route, DTO or wire shape changes.

### Phase 4 — P4-MCON (managed connectors from the dashboard, D-128)

Rule: `13 §3.1`, `01` (connector host), `00` (`auh_`), `03 §Planned changes` (`connectors`, `connector_hosts`, `connector_host_status`), `05` (admin and host routes), `08` (purpose `connector.settings`), `14 §Admin: connectors`, `15` (`connector_host` config, Helm `connectorHost`), `02 §Limits`, SEC-69, SEC-70. Reserved numbers C-450..C-459. Errors use the standard body. Self-hosted connectors (§3 of `13`) keep every phase-0..3 shape unless stated.

- C-450 **`GET /v1/admin/connector-types`** (IA): `{ "host_running": bool, "items": [ConnectorType] }`, sorted by `name`. `ConnectorType` = `{ "type_id": string, "name": string, "version": string, "publisher": string, "icon_png_b64": string | null, "setup_guide_md": string | null, "manifest": object, "manifest_digest": string, "instance_setup": { "fields": [Field] }, "hosts": int, "signed": bool, "added": bool }`. The catalogue is the union of the types announced by hosts seen within `CONNECTOR_HOST_STALE` (90 s); a type whose signature does not verify is omitted (the host's `register` response names it). `signed` is `false` only for a type accepted with an empty signature under `plugins.allow_unsigned` (development only, C-455, D-142); the dashboard badges it "Unsigned (development)". Two live hosts announcing different digests for one type: the newest host version wins and `hosts` counts only hosts with that digest. `host_running` = at least one host seen within `CONNECTOR_HOST_STALE`. `Field` is the `09 §3.4` form-field schema (`name`, `label`, `type`, `required`, `placeholder`, `help_url`, `options`, `show_when`) with, for `instance_setup` only, the extra types `url` (http or https URL), `number` (integer, optional `min`/`max`) and `generated_secret`, and an optional static `default` (JSON value, applied when unset; a PATCH `null` restores it); `file`, `json_file` and `store` are refused in `instance_setup`. Setting values are typed JSON (numbers stay numbers). `public_url` has no static default: the core derives it (C-456).
- C-451 **`POST /v1/admin/connectors`** (IA) gains `managed: bool` (default `false` = phase-0..3 behaviour, unchanged: 201 with the one-time `token`). With `managed: true` the body is `{ "id", "managed": true, "display_name"?: string (1–80; default the manifest name), "settings"?: object, "secrets"?: { name: string }, "approve_manifest_digest": string }`: `id` must be a type in C-450 (422 `unknown_connector_type`); `approve_manifest_digest` is required (422 `invalid_request`) and must equal that type's current digest (409 `stale_manifest`); `settings` keys must be non-secret `instance_setup` fields and validate against them (422 `invalid_request`, `details` per field; unknown key → 422); `secrets` keys must be `secret` fields (≤ 8 KiB each); every required field must be present; `generated_secret` fields are generated by the core and cannot be supplied (422). An existing id → 409 `connector_exists`. A seal failure → 503 `secret_store_unavailable`, nothing created. Response **201 `{ "connector": Connector, "token"?: string }`** (the same envelope as the self-hosted path, C-452) with **`token` absent**; status `awaiting_registration`, `approved_manifest_digest` = the reviewed digest. Audit `connector.created` (`managed`, `type_id`, field names set; never values).
- C-452 **`Connector`** (every admin connector response) gains `managed: bool`, `type_id: string | null` (managed only), `settings: object | null` (managed: the stored non-secret settings with defaults applied; else `null`), `secrets: { [name]: { "set": bool, "generated": bool, "updated_at": Timestamp | null } }` (managed: one entry per `secret` / `generated_secret` field; else `{}`), `host_status: [{ "host_instance": uuid, "state": "running" | "error", "message": string | null, "at": Timestamp }]` (managed; reports from hosts seen within `CONNECTOR_HOST_STALE`; else `[]`). No response ever carries a secret value.
- C-453 **`PATCH /v1/admin/connectors/{id}`** (IA, new): `{ "display_name"?: string, "enabled"?: bool, "settings"?: object, "secrets"?: { name: string | null }, "regenerate"?: [name] }`. `settings` merges per key (a `null` value restores the field default; validation as C-451); `secrets` replaces (string) or clears (`null`; clearing a required secret → 422); `regenerate` names `generated_secret` fields only (422 otherwise). `settings`, `secrets` and `regenerate` on a self-hosted connector → 422 `connector_not_managed`; `display_name` and `enabled` work for both. `enabled: false` → status `disabled`; `enabled: true` on a disabled connector → `awaiting_registration` if it never registered, else `active` when `manifest_digest = approved_manifest_digest`, else `pending_approval`. Any change bumps the assignments revision (C-456). A seal failure → 503 `secret_store_unavailable`, nothing changed. 200 `Connector`. Audit `connector.updated` (changed field names, `enabled`; never values).
- C-454 **Existing admin routes**: `POST /v1/admin/connectors/{id}/token` on a managed connector → 409 `connector_managed`; (FIX-113) a bootstrap token (`AURORA_BOOTSTRAP_CONNECTOR_TOKENS`) never attaches to, or re-keys, a managed connector id. `DELETE` unchanged (409 `connector_in_use`), and for a managed connector also discards its sealed settings (best effort after commit; a failed discard is logged, never blocks). `POST …/approve` unchanged (used for a widened manifest).
- C-455 **`POST /v1/connector-host/register`** (`auh_`): `{ "instance_id": uuid, "version": string, "types": [{ "type_id": string, "manifest": ConnectorManifest, "manifest_signature": string }] }` (≤ 32 types). Upserts `connector_hosts` (`last_seen_at = now()`), verifies each manifest signature as connector `register` does (`13 §3`) and `package.id = type_id`. 200 `{ "poll_interval_secs": int, "rejected": [{ "type_id": string, "reason": "signature" | "id_mismatch" | "invalid_manifest" }] }`. Called at start and every `poll_interval_secs` (default 30) as the host heartbeat. With `plugins.allow_unsigned = true` (development only, warned in the log) a type with an **empty** `manifest_signature` is accepted and marked `signed: false` (C-450); a present but invalid signature, an id mismatch or an invalid manifest is still rejected, and with the flag off (the default) an empty signature is rejected with `reason: "signature"` (D-142).
- C-456 **`GET /v1/connector-host/assignments?revision=<int>&wait=<secs>&instance_id=<uuid>`** (`auh_`; `wait` 0–30, default 25, above 30 → 400; `instance_id` = the calling host's own id from `register`: the token is shared by every host and carries no host identity, so the host names itself; without it the union of the types of all live hosts is served. This is a filter, not an authorization boundary: any `auh_` holder may read every managed connector's assignment): returns at once when the current revision differs from `revision` (or `revision` is absent), else when it changes or `wait` elapses. 200 `{ "revision": int, "connectors": [{ "id": string, "type_id": string, "display_name": string, "settings": object, "secrets": { name: string }, "status": string }] }`, `Cache-Control: no-store`. Lists managed connectors whose status is not `disabled` and whose type the calling host announced. `settings` has defaults applied, including `public_url` = `<server.external_url>/connectors/<id>` when unset. `secrets` holds the **decrypted** secret and generated values; a store failure answers 503 `secret_store_unavailable` (the host keeps its running set). Never logged by either side.
- C-456a (FIX-113) Assignments are served for managed connectors whose status is `active` or `awaiting_registration` only: one in `pending_approval` (a widened manifest nobody approved) or `disabled` gets none, so its decrypted settings are not handed out.
- C-457 **`POST /v1/connector-host/status`** (`auh_`): `{ "instance_id": uuid, "connectors": [{ "id": string, "state": "running" | "error", "message"?: string }] }`; `message` ≤ 300 chars, control characters stripped. Upserts `connector_host_status`. 204. Unknown or non-managed ids are ignored.
- C-458 **Delegated connector authentication**: on `/v1/connector/*`, `Authorization: Bearer auh_…` plus header `Aurora-Connector: <id>` authenticates as connector `<id>` when that connector is `managed` and its status is not `disabled`; otherwise 401 `unauthorized` (same body as a bad `aun_`). `auh_` without the header → 401. With an `aun_` token the header is ignored. Every connector-contract shape is unchanged.
- C-459 **Overview and bootstrap**: `GET /v1/admin/overview` (P4-MCON-1 found no such route in code and added it with only this field; the other overview fields of `05` are not implemented yet) carries `connector_hosts: [{ "instance_id": uuid, "version": string, "last_seen_at": Timestamp, "types": [string] }]` (seen within 24 h). The API upserts the host token from `AURORA_BOOTSTRAP_CONNECTOR_HOST_TOKEN` at start (hash only; like the runner token). (FIX-120: per-host tokens are minted by C-691; the bootstrap token is the shared, unbound one.) Constants: `CONNECTOR_HOST_STALE` 90 s, assignments `wait` ≤ 30 s, settings object ≤ 16 KiB, secret value ≤ 8 KiB, status message ≤ 300 chars.
- C-690 **`GET /v1/admin/connector-host-tokens`** (IA, FIX-120, D-189): `{ "items": [HostToken] }`, newest first. `HostToken` = `{ "id": uuid, "name": string | null, "prefix": string | null, "type_ids": [string] | null, "state": "active" | "revoked" | "refused", "created_at", "last_used_at": Timestamp | null, "revoked_at": Timestamp | null }`. `type_ids`/`name` are `null` for the shared bootstrap token, whose `state` is `refused` once a live bound token exists. The token value is never listed.
- C-691 **`POST /v1/admin/connector-host-tokens`** (IA): `{ "name": string (1–80, unique among live tokens → 409 `conflict`), "type_ids": [string] (1–64 connector type ids, `[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{1,47}`) }` → 201 `HostToken` plus `"token": "auh_…"` **shown once**. Audited (`admin.connector_host_token_issue`, security-critical). From the first live bound token on the shared bootstrap token is refused (401), `15 §Upgrades`.
- C-692 **`DELETE /v1/admin/connector-host-tokens/{id}`** (IA): 204; the token is 401 on its next call; 404 unknown id. Audited (`admin.connector_host_token_revoke`). Revoking every bound token makes the shared token work again.
- C-693 **Binding on the host plane** (amends C-455/C-456/C-457/C-458): a bound token (`type_ids` not null) (a) `register`: any announced type outside `type_ids` → 403 `type_not_bound` naming the type, nothing stored; (b) owns the `instance_id`s it registered — another token's `register`, `assignments?instance_id=` or `status` for such an id → 403 `host_instance_mismatch`; (c) `assignments` serve only connectors whose type is in `type_ids` (and was announced by the calling instance, or, without `instance_id`, by an instance the token registered); (d) `status` reports about connectors of other types are ignored; (e) C-458 delegation acts only for connectors of bound types (other ids: the same 401 as an unknown `aun_`). The shared bootstrap token (unbound) behaves as before until a live bound token exists.
- C-694 **`GET /v1/admin/runners` capacity** (IA, FIX-121, D-211): each item carries `backend`, `max_sandboxes` (the declared `capabilities.max_sandboxes`, `null` = none declared), `sandboxes`, `running_sandboxes`, `live_replicas` and `replicas[]` (`version`, `advertised_host`, `started_at`, `last_seen_at`, `retired_at`, `live`); the dashboard shows them in the Admin › Runners capacity table. Non-admin → 403. No wire break: `previous_token_expires_at` (C-695) is the only new field.
- C-695 **Runner-token rotation with a grace** (`POST /v1/admin/runners/{id}/token?grace_secs=`, IA, FIX-121, D-211): `grace_secs` is 0..86400 (default 600; above → 400 `invalid_request` `/grace_secs`). The new `aur_` token is returned once (`{ runner, token }`); the replaced token keeps authenticating the runner plane until `now + grace_secs` (`runners.previous_token_hash` / `previous_token_expires_at`, migration `20261201000101`), then is refused with 401; `0` refuses it at once. One previous token at most: a second rotation inside the grace drops the first. `RunnerSummary.previous_token_expires_at` is non-null only while a grace lasts. Audited (`admin.runner_rotate`, `metadata.grace_secs`). Not affected: provisioning stays Helm/CLI (`14`).

### Phase 4 — FIX-019..FIX-021 (model readiness, credential-side grants, D-136, D-137)

Rule: `08 §Model selection for hosted agents` and `§Model readiness`, `08 §11.3` (`litellm_team`), `10 §Failure semantics`, `05 §Grants and effective views`, `14` (Credentials, agent Access, chat errors), `02` `GRANT_BULK_MAX`, SEC-77.

- C-500 **`GET /v1/agents/{agent}/model-readiness`** (S/K, `can_own` on the agent's workspace; 404 for a non-hosted agent or one the caller cannot see): 200 `{ "harness": string, "keys": [KeyReadiness], "default": { "endpoint_id": string, "provider": string, "model_id": string, "source": "override" | "endpoint_default" | "provider_default" | "allow_list" | "static_list" } | null, "override_dropped": bool }`. `KeyReadiness` = `{ "secret_id": Uuid, "name": string, "scope": "organization" | "workspace", "endpoint_id": string, "provider": string, "usable": bool, "reason": null | "not_active" | "endpoint_unknown" | "provider_disabled" | "scope_not_permitted" | "origin_unverified" | "origin_not_compliant" | "no_shared_dialect" }`, ordered as the dispatcher considers them (`provider_preference`, then oldest first). `default` is exactly what the dispatcher would choose for the next turn (the same function). `override_dropped` = an agent override exists but is not usable (the next turn drops it with a notice).
- C-501 **Turn errors** (`turn.finished.error_code`, `error` event `code`): new code `no_default_model` (`message`: "An LLM key is usable but no model is chosen for this agent and its endpoint has no default model."). `no_model_key`'s `message` lists reason counts, e.g. "No usable LLM key: 1 granted key not usable (origin_unverified)." or "No LLM key is granted to this agent."; never a key name. Both are durable, set before any sandbox start; open vocabulary (`10`).
- C-502 **`StoredOrigin`** gains `detail: string | null` (≤ 200 chars; `key_reports_no_team`, `key_info_http_<status>`, `endpoint_unreachable`, `bad_response`, or `null` when verified). `team_id` may be `null` with `status: "verified"` and `method: "litellm_team"` (teamless key, no required team ids). Save and `POST /v1/secrets/{id}/verify` refusals keep their codes (`llm_key_wrong_organization` / `llm_key_unverifiable`) and add `details.detail` with the same value.
- C-503 **`GET /v1/secrets/{id}/grants`** (S/K, `can_own` on the secret's scope: workspace owner for a workspace secret, org owner/admin or owner of at least one workspace of the org for an org secret): 200 `{ "items": [SecretGrantView] }`, `SecretGrantView` = `SecretGrant` + `{ "agent_name": string, "workspace_id": Uuid, "workspace_name": string }`, only agents in workspaces the caller can own. **`POST /v1/secrets/{id}/grants`** (S/K): `{ "agent_ids": [Uuid] }` (1–`GRANT_BULK_MAX`; empty, oversized or duplicate lists → 400 `invalid_request`, the repo's validation code); 200 `{ "items": [{ "agent_id": Uuid, "grant": SecretGrant, "status": "created" | "existing" }] }`. Any agent failing the agent-side checks → that route's error (404 `not_found` for an agent the caller cannot see, 403 `forbidden`, 422 as the agent-side route) with `details.agent_id`, and nothing written.
- C-504 **`POST /v1/connections/{id}/grants`** (S/K): `{ "agent_ids": [Uuid], "mode": "full" | "custom", "allow_tools"?: [string], "ask_tools"?: [string], "ask_timeout_secs"?: int }` (validation as `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}`); 200 `{ "items": [{ "agent_id": Uuid, "grant": ConnectionGrant, "status": "created" | "existing" }], "app_restrictions": { "custom_only": bool, "block_untooled": bool } }` (`app_restrictions` is added by P4-GRANTS; FIX-020 ships the response without it, since no app restriction exists yet). An existing grant is returned unchanged (`existing`), never modified. Errors and atomicity as C-503 (incl. 422 `full_grant_forbidden`). `GET /v1/connections/{id}/grants` gains `agent_name`, `workspace_id`, `workspace_name` per item and is filtered like C-503.
- C-505 **Audit**: one row per **created** grant with the same action name the agent-side route emits today (`grant.create`, D-83; the lane confirms it in code), with `details.via = "credential"`; none for `existing`. Atomic with the writes (SEC-32).
- C-506 **Dashboard routes** (no wire change): `…/credentials?tab=secrets|llm|proposals|apps|connections|oauth` (org and workspace credentials pages); agent page tabs keep their existing addressing, and the chat error links use it (`?tab=access`, `?tab=instructions` or the existing equivalent).

### Phase 4 — P4-IDP-1 (roles from IdP claims, D-139, D-143, SEC-78)

Rule: `04 §OIDC role claims` and `§Sign-up semantics`, `15` (`auth.oidc_providers[]`), `03 §org_members, §workspace_access, §users, §user_idp_roles`, `05` (`/v1/admin/identity`, `/v1/admin/users`), `14` (Account, Admin › Identity), `17` SEC-78.

- C-510 **Provider keys** (Helm `config.auth.oidc_providers[]`, no wire change): `role_claim` (dot path), `role_claim_source` (`id_token` default | `userinfo`), `require_role` (default `false`), `role_mappings: [{ value, instance_admin?: bool, org?: { slug, role: owner|admin|member }, workspaces?: [{ slug, role: owner|approver|member }] }]`. Startup refuses (naming the key) unknown keys, duplicate `value`, an invalid role or slug, a mapping that grants nothing, `workspaces` without `org`, `role_mappings` without `role_claim`, and `require_role: true` without mappings.
- C-511 **`GET /v1/admin/identity`** — **removed in SET-4 (D-309(f)); the route answers 404. Use `GET /v1/admin/settings/sign_in` (C-772, C-780).** Former shape: (IA, session): `{ "providers": [{ "id", "display_name": string|null, "issuer", "role_claim": string|null, "role_claim_source": "id_token"|"userinfo", "require_role": bool, "role_mappings": [{ "value", "instance_admin": bool, "org": {slug, role}|null, "workspaces": [{slug, role}] }] }] }`. Read-only: no route writes it; no secrets. Non-admins 403.
- C-512 **`AdminUser`** (`GET /v1/admin/users`, `PATCH /v1/admin/users/{id}`) gains `instance_admin_idp_value: string|null` (the claim value that gave the flag; `null` = manual) and `idp_roles: IdpRoles|null`, `IdpRoles = { "provider", "values": [string] (every value of the last sign-in, mapped or not), "synced_at", "granted": [{ "value", "instance_admin": bool, "org": {slug, role}|null, "workspaces": [{slug, role}] }] }`. `PATCH` with `is_instance_admin` on a claim-derived flag → 409 `managed_by_idp`.
- C-513 **`Me`** (`GET/PATCH /v1/me`) gains `idp_roles: IdpRoles|null` ("Roles from your sign-in").
- C-514 **Claim-sourced roles are read-only**: `Member` (org members list) and `AccessBinding` (workspace access list) gain `idp_value: string|null` (the claim value behind the role; `null` = manual). `PATCH` (role) and `DELETE` of an org member, and `PUT`/`DELETE` of a workspace binding, whose row has `idp_value` → 409 `managed_by_idp`. Suspending a member stays allowed.
- C-515 **Sign-in errors**: `/auth/login?error=no_mapped_role` (`require_role: true` and the validated claim maps to no grant; no user row; audited `user.login` denied with `reason: no_mapped_role`). `signup_closed` is unchanged for an unmapped value in `invite_only` / `sso_jit`.
- C-516 **Admission** (D-143): in `invite_only` and `sso_jit`, a new user whose validated claim (same provider, after every other check) maps to at least one grant is admitted; audited `user.admit_by_claim` in the user-creating transaction (`actor_kind = service`, `actor_label = idp:<provider>`, `metadata { provider, claim_values }`, never the token). `open` is unchanged.
- C-517 **Audit** (sync, in the sign-in transaction; `actor_kind = service`, `actor_label = idp:<provider>`, `metadata.claim_value`): `instance_admin.grant`, `instance_admin.revoke` (new, critical), `member.add|update|remove` (`via: idp_sync`), `workspace_access.set|remove`, and `org_role.idp_sync_blocked` (outcome `denied`, `reason: last_owner`).
- C-518 **Dev IdP** (`aurora-dev-idp`, `aurora_testkit::oidc`): fixture users take `extra` (claims merged into the ID token); `PUT /dev/claims/{email}` with a JSON object sets a user's extra claims for later sign-ins (dev only); the discovery document lists `/userinfo`, which serves the standard claims plus the same extras for a Bearer token the IdP issued (`Claims.userinfo_extra` for userinfo-only claims in tests).
- C-519 **Data**: migration `20261123000001_idp_role_claims.sql` (`org_members` and `workspace_access`: `idp_role`, `idp_source`, `base_role`; `users.idp_admin_source`; table `user_idp_roles`), `03`.

### Phase 4 — P4-CNT (shared rate-limit counters, D-119, SEC-62)

Rule: `15 §Shared rate-limit counters`, `05 §Conventions` (*Shared counters*), `06 §Rate limiting`, `03 §rate_limit_windows, §rate_limit_buckets`, `08 §4` (`ratelimit_key`), `02 §Limits`, `17` (SEC-62), `18 §Shared rate-limit counters`.

- C-400 **Configuration** (`ratelimit.counters.*`, Helm `counters.*`; no wire change): `backend` (`memory` default | `postgres` | `redis`), `api` / `gateway` (`shared` default | `memory`), `on_unavailable.security|management|gateway` (`local` default | `closed`; **`open` or any other value refuses startup naming the key**), `timeout_ms` (50, 10–2000), `postgres.pool_size` (4, 1–32), `redis.address` (`rediss://host[:port]`, no userinfo/path/query; `redis://` only for loopback or with `allow_plaintext`; a password in it refuses startup), `redis.allow_plaintext`, `redis.username_env` / `redis.password_env` (variable **names**; a named unset variable refuses startup), `redis.ca_file`, `redis.db`, `redis.key_prefix` (≤ 64 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9:_-]`). Unknown backend and a missing address for `redis` refuse startup. The default (`memory`) is today's behaviour; an existing `aurora.toml` and the unchanged Helm values render and start unchanged.
- C-401 **503 `counter_unavailable`** (`message` generic, header `retry-after: 5`, `details.limiter` ∈ `api_security`, `api_management`, `api_webhook`, `gateway_rule`): returned **only** by a class set to `closed` when the shared backend is unreachable (timeout, error, open breaker) and the request needs a count. Never a 429, never for exempt routes or requests that match no rate-limited rule. The 429 `rate_limited` shape, `ratelimit-*` headers and `details` are unchanged.
- C-402 **`GET /v1/admin/overview`** gains `counters: { "backend": "memory"|"postgres"|"redis", "state": "local"|"shared"|"fallback" }` (`local`: the `memory` backend or the API limiter kept per replica; `fallback`: a class of this replica is counting per replica because the backend is unreachable).
- C-403 **Store keys** (ids and keyed hashes only): `api:general:<principal>`, `api:class:<class>:<principal>`, `api:org:<org id>`, `api:ip-get:<hash>`, `api:ip-rejected:<hash>`, `api:oidc_start_ip:<hash>`, `api:oidc_callback_ip:<hash>`, `api:hook:<webhook id>`, `gw:rl:<rule logical_id>:<agent_id>:<period>`; `<principal>` is `key:<id>`, `user:<id>`, `agent:<id>` or `channel:<id>`; `<hash>` = `HMAC-SHA256(hkdf("ratelimit_key"), ip)` truncated to 128 bits, hex.
- C-404 **HKDF purpose `ratelimit_key`** (`aurora_crypto::Purpose::RatelimitKey`, derived from the active signing key; `DerivedKeys::ratelimit_hash`).
- C-405 **Data**: migration `20261125000001_rate_limit_counters.sql` (`UNLOGGED` tables `rate_limit_windows`, `rate_limit_buckets`, both with a 1–200 char key check and an `expires_at` index); the gateway role gains `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `rate_limit_windows` only; the API's `counters_prune` arm (60 s, 5 000 rows, `SKIP LOCKED`, `expires_at < now()`).
- C-406 **Trait** (`aurora-counters::CounterStore`): `fixed_window_peek`, all-or-nothing `fixed_window_consume`, all-or-nothing `bucket_take` (GCRA), `health`; errors `Unavailable` / `Invalid`, neither ever read as "allowed"; `Guarded` (timeout, breaker 3 failures / 5 s cooldown with a `health` probe) and `ClassCounters` (class policy) implemented once for every backend.
- C-407 **Gateway request log**: a request whose rule quota was counted on the `local` fallback carries `error_code = counter_fallback` when it has no other code (proxy and database lanes; a CONNECT tunnel's close row does not carry it). A request refused by a lost consume race is `rate_limited` with the same body as an evaluator refusal.
- C-408 **Metrics**: `aurora_counters_calls_total{backend,op,result}`, `aurora_counters_call_seconds{backend,op}`, `aurora_counters_state{backend,class,state}`, `aurora_counters_fallback_total{class,policy}`, `aurora_counters_pruned_total`.
- C-409 **Not on the trait** (unchanged, per replica): the stream concurrency cap, CLI device-code polling and `cli_start` / `cli_approve` limits, BYO admission, the approval e-mail digest. The per-webhook limit is taken from the store before the body is read; the row-based check in the delivery transaction stays authoritative.

### Phase 4 — P4-GRANTS ("custom grants only" and parameter-level tool matching, D-123, D-124)

Rule: `09 §3.4` (`grants`, tool `params`, linter), `09 §9` (snapshot), `09 §10` (conversion on activation), `07` (app-level restrictions), `06` (pipeline steps 4, 5, 7; snapshot refusal), `05 §Grants and effective views`, `14` (Access, plugin approval), SEC-67, SEC-68. Reserved numbers C-410..C-419. Errors use the standard body.

- C-410 **`app_restrictions`**: `AppRestrictions = { "custom_only": bool, "block_untooled": bool }`, both `false` for an app whose manifest has no `grants`. It is carried by `ConnectionGrant` (the `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}` response, every item of `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/grants` `connections` and of `GET /v1/connections/{id}/grants`, every `grant` of the bulk response), by the bulk response of `POST /v1/connections/{id}/grants` at the top (`{ "items": […], "app_restrictions": AppRestrictions }`, C-504), and by `AppSummary` (`GET /v1/apps`) and `AppDetail` (`GET /v1/apps/{app}`, C-414). Read from the active registry snapshot; never user-settable.
- C-411 **`422 full_grant_forbidden`**: `mode = "full"` for a connection of an app with `grants.custom_only` is refused with `{ "error": { "code": "full_grant_forbidden", "message": …, "details": { "app_id": string } } }` (the bulk route adds `details.agent_id`, C-504, and rolls everything back). It is raised by the shared service function `grant_connection_in_tx` (`domains/grants/service.rs`), the only writer of connection grants, so the agent-side `PUT`, the credential-side bulk route and every flow that calls them (first-run, templates, agent proposals, CLI) stop there; it is checked before the other grant validation (`grant_invalid`), also when the existing grant is custom (it is left unchanged). Custom grants of a `custom_only` app are unchanged (rules 1–3, new tools arrive blocked).
- C-412 **Plugin impact: grants to convert**. `PluginImpact` (`GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}`, pending versions only) gains `grants_to_convert: int` (absent when `0`): the `full` agent grants, in every organization, on connections of an app the version marks `grants.custom_only`. `PluginImpactApp` gains `grants_to_convert: int` and `grant_workspaces: int` (distinct workspaces; both absent when `0`). Counts only: no organization, workspace, agent or connection id or name. Advisory: approval recomputes it. `plugin_packages.status_reason` gains `converts_grants`: ingest (upload and built-in) holds a version with a non-zero count in `pending_approval` like `breaks_connections` (which wins when both apply); it is never auto-activated. `POST /v1/admin/plugins/{id}/approve` (and `enable`) converts in the activation transaction: every such grant becomes `mode = custom`, `allow_tools` = every tool id of the new version's catalogue for that app (items and group wildcards), `ask_tools = []`, `ask_timeout_secs = null`; its derived rules are recompiled (allow, block rest) and an `agent` invalidation is sent per grant. A version that introduces only `block_untooled` converts nothing and activates as before; a later version that drops either flag converts nothing back.
- C-413 **Conversion audit and notice**. One security-critical audit row `grant.converted_by_app` per converted grant, committed with the activation and the plugin decision (`target = agent`, `org_id`, `workspace_id`; metadata `{ grant_id, connection_id, app_id, package_id, version, grant: { from: "full", mode: "custom", tools: int } }`); the `plugin.approve` row's metadata gains `grants_converted` and `grants_converted_by_app`. A failed activation (for example an unresolved host claim) leaves the grants and the audit untouched. `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/grants` gains `converted_by_app: [{ "connection_id": Uuid, "app_id": string, "converted_at": Timestamp }]`: the conversions still standing, that is, the grant has not been written since (the Access tab notice "Access for <app> was converted from full to custom by an app update").
- C-414 **`GET /v1/apps` / `GET /v1/apps/{app}`**: `AppSummary` and `AppDetail` gain `app_restrictions: AppRestrictions` (C-410) so the dashboard shows the "Custom access only" badge and the `block_untooled` note, and drops "Full access" from the grant form.
- C-415 **Manifest `grants`** (contract 1, additive; `09 §3.4`): `apps[].grants { custom_only?: bool, block_untooled?: bool }`, absent = both `false`; unknown keys or non-booleans → `unknown_field` / `schema`; `custom_only` on an app with no tool → `custom_only_without_tools`. Copied into the snapshot as `apps[].grants` (always present in a compiled snapshot, `{ "block_untooled": false, "custom_only": false }` by default; absent in an older snapshot = both `false`). Evaluator, defence in depth (`07`): a toolless `connection` / `app_connections` **allow** rule (derived or hand-written) neither matches nor selects a connection of a `custom_only` app (block rules still apply); `block_untooled` blocks a request whose winning credential is that app's connection and whose `tool_id` is `None` before any rule (403 `blocked_by_policy`, `details.reason = "app_untooled_blocked"`, request-log decision `app_untooled_blocked`), never at `CONNECT` time. The package linter's codes: `custom_only_without_tools`.
- C-416 **Tool `params`** (contract 1, additive; `09 §3.4`): `tools.*.items[].params: [ { "source": "query" | "form" | "header", "name": string, "match": { "equals": string } | { "in": [string] } | { "prefix": string } } ]`, 1–4 conditions ANDed; `header` only `x-amz-target`; values `[A-Za-z0-9_.:/-]`, ≤ 128 characters, `in` ≤ 32 values, `prefix` ≥ 4 characters; no regular expressions. Snapshot tool entries carry `params` only when declared (a tool without it serializes exactly as before). Linter codes: `params_invalid` (shape, header name, `form` on a GET/HEAD-only tool), `params_tool_shadowed` (an earlier tool without `params`, `graphql` or `git` matches everything the tool matches), `params_fixture_missing` (for every `params` tool the package's `tests/*.yaml` must hold the positive case and a request for each of: other value, missing, repeated, query/body conflict (`form` conditions), wrong content type or compressed, truncated body); a group wildcard with `params` is an `unknown_field`. Fail closed (SEC-68): the parameter must occur exactly once in its source, percent-decoded once (`+` is a space in a form body), a `form` parameter must not also occur in the query and a `query` parameter must not occur in the body (a request with a body that cannot be checked never matches a `query` tool), the body must be an uncompressed, untruncated, valid UTF-8 `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body (charset `utf-8` allowed); anything else means the tool does not match and classification falls through to the next tool or to `tool = None`. Credentials, cookies and injected headers are never sources.
- C-417 **Gateway**: the body prefix (`CONDITION_BODY_BUFFER`) is buffered when a `form`- or `query`-`params` tool may match the request's host, path and method, besides the existing reasons; the snapshot is refused (previous kept, `gateway_snapshot_rejected_total`) when a tool names a classifier or key the gateway does not know (unknown `source`, unknown key) or an empty or over-long `params` list. `Credential::Connection` in the policy facts carries `custom_only` / `block_untooled` (copied from the snapshot by the resolver).
- C-418 **Fixture format** (`09 §12`): `request.body_truncated: bool` (the body is longer than the gateway's condition buffer; the classifier sees a truncated prefix) and `expect.no_tool: true` (the app matches, no catalogue tool does). `scripts/plugins/check_builtin.py` accepts a read tool with a `params` classifier on a body method.
- C-419 **Built-in packages**: `indico` 1.1.0 sets `grants: { custom_only: true, block_untooled: true }` (activation converts existing full grants, held `converts_grants`); `aws-keys` 1.1.0 and `aws-role` 1.1.0 add the read tools `ec2_describe` (POST, form `Action` prefix `Describe`) and `sts_get_caller_identity` (POST, form `Action` equals `GetCallerIdentity`), claim `sts.*.amazonaws.com` only for it, set `grants.block_untooled` (every other STS action, and any untooled request, is blocked even under a full grant), and keep `ec2_call` as the held write tool for everything else. The new tool ids arrive blocked in existing custom grants (their setup guides say so).

### Phase 4 — P4-ONB-1 (new-user placement, D-140, D-151, SEC-79)

Rule: `04 §New-user placement`, `03 §workspaces, §instance_placement`, `05` (`/v1/admin/placement`), `14 §Admin: Sign-up & placement`, `17` SEC-79. Reserved numbers C-520..C-529.

- C-520 **`GET /v1/admin/placement`** (IA, session): `PlacementView = { saved: bool, signup_mode: "open"|"invite_only"|"sso_jit" (config, read-only), mode: "shared_org"|"personal_org"|"none", default_org: {id, slug, name}|null, default_org_role: "member"|"admin", shared_workspaces: [{workspace_id, slug, name, role: "member"|"approver"}], personal_workspace: { enabled: bool, role: "owner"|"approver"|"member", name_template: string }, starter_grants: [{kind: "secret"|"connection", id, name, detail}], personal_orgs: { count: int, items: [{org_id, slug, name, owner_email, created_at}] (at most 100) }, warnings: [string], updated_at: string|null, updated_by: uuid|null }`. Unsaved (`saved: false`): `mode` is what the instance does today (`personal_org` under `open`, else `none`), the template is `{sub}|{email_local}`, and `warnings` holds `placement_unsaved`. `warnings` also holds `per_user_orgs_active` (signup `open` and mode `personal_org`) and `default_org_missing` (`shared_org` whose default organization was deleted; it then behaves as `none`). `personal_orgs` = organizations named `<name>'s org` with one member, their owner, whose default organization it is. Non-admins 403.
- C-521 **`PUT /v1/admin/placement`** (IA, session; whole document, response = C-520): `{ mode, default_org_id?: uuid, create_default_org?: string (1..80), default_org_role?: "member"|"admin" (default member), shared_workspaces?: [{workspace_id, role: "member"|"approver"}], personal_workspace?: { enabled, role? (default owner), name_template? (default `{sub}|{email_local}`) }, starter_grants?: [{kind, id}] }`; unknown keys 400. `shared_org` needs exactly one of `default_org_id` and `create_default_org` (the latter creates the organization with its default workspace and agent in the same transaction, the caller its owner); other modes accept none of the organization, workspace, personal and starter fields. 400 `invalid_request` with a field path for: unknown organization; a shared workspace outside the default org, listed twice, or **a personal workspace**; a template that does not parse (C-523); a starter grant that is not an active **organization-level** credential of the default org, or any starter grant while personal workspaces are off; more than 50 shared workspaces or starter grants. Saving is audited `placement.update` (critical, `actor_kind = instance_admin`; metadata `{from_mode, mode, default_org_id, default_org_role, shared_workspaces (count), personal_enabled, personal_role, name_template, starter_grants (count)}`), plus `org.create` when it created the organization.
- C-522 **`GET /v1/admin/placement/preview?template=&sample=`** (IA, session): `{ name, slug, used: int|null, unresolved: [string] }`. `template` defaults to the saved one (400 when it does not parse). With `sample` (≤200 chars, no control characters) the typed text stands for every simple variable (`{preferred_username}`, `{name}`, `{email_local}` = the part before `@`, `{given_name}` = first word, `{family_name}` = last word); `{sub}` and `{claim:…}` have no value — the sample never invents an identity (D-162, D-151). Without it the caller's profile is used (`{name}` = display name, `{email_local}`, `{sub}` = the caller's stored identity subject, lowest `provider_id` first; none: `unresolved`), and claim-only variables are `unresolved` (D-151). `slug` is derived like any workspace slug **before** a numbered suffix; `used` is the index of the fallback alternative; `null` = the `user-<short id>` last resort.
- C-523 **Name template**: literal text with `{sub}`, `{preferred_username}`, `{name}`, `{email_local}`, `{given_name}`, `{family_name}`, `{claim:<dot.path>}` (path `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, at most 64, string claims of the validated ID token only; nested objects by dots). `{sub}` is the provider's unique, never-reassigned subject (OIDC Core §2); `{preferred_username}` is a display name, not guaranteed unique or stable — collisions still go through SEC-79's numbered slug. `|` separates a fallback chain, tried left to right (a missing value is the only reason to fall through): the first alternative whose variables all have a non-empty value and whose result has a letter or digit wins; none: `user-<first 8 hex of the user id>`. Values lose control characters and runs of whitespace; the name is cut at 80 characters. At most 200 characters, no control characters, no empty alternative, no unknown variable.
- C-524 **Sign-in order** (`04 §OIDC role claims` step 6 stays): invitation → role claims → provider `jit` → **placement**, all in the sign-in transaction, after every existing check (D-118, `require_role`, domains). Placement acts only in `shared_org` with an existing default organization: a user **created by this sign-in** joins it with `default_org_role` (unless an earlier step already made them a member), joins each shared workspace they have no role in with its role, and gets `users.default_org_id` if unset; **every** sign-in of an active member of the default org gives them a personal workspace when enabled and none is recorded for them (`workspaces.personal_owner_user_id = user`, found only by that id; per-organization advisory lock; slug via the ordinary numbered-suffix derivation, so a name is never matched to an existing workspace). Existing users outside the default org are never moved. The personal workspace is made with `create_workspace_in_tx` (workspace, user bound as `owner`, policy generations, default agent); a non-owner `personal_role` then replaces the binding.
- C-525 **Starter grants** go through the shared grant write path (`domains/grants/service.rs`, FIX-020, SEC-77): `grant_secret_as_system` / `grant_connection_as_system` run the same credential checks (active, same org, org or workspace scope) and write, compile and invalidate exactly like the agent and credential routes, only to the **default agent of the workspace just created**, as `Origin::Credential`, `grants.created_by = NULL`. Connections are granted with mode `full`. Audited `grant.create` (`actor_kind = system`, `actor_label = placement`, `metadata.starter = true`, `via = credential`). A credential that has gone away is skipped and audited `grant.create` outcome `denied` (`reason: credential_unavailable`); it never blocks the sign-in.
- C-526 **Other audit** in the sign-in transaction (`actor_kind = system`, `actor_label = placement`, `metadata.via = placement`): `member.add`, `workspace_access.set` (shared workspaces), `workspace.create` (`metadata.personal_owner_user_id`).
- C-527 **`auth.signup_mode = open` with a saved placement**: `personal_org` (and unsaved on an instance that already has an organization) keeps `<Name>'s org`; `shared_org` admits and places; `none` admits only with a pending invitation, a mapped role claim or a `jit` org, otherwise `signup_closed` with no user row. Every case but the own-organization one accepts the new user's pending invitations. **Fresh instance** (nothing saved, no organization, the signer neither in `auth.instance_admins` nor mapped to instance admin): `/auth/login?error=instance_setup_pending`, no user row, audited `user.login` denied `reason: instance_setup_pending`; the page says "This instance is being set up — try again shortly".
- C-528 **`GET /v1/admin/placement/choices?org_id=`** (IA, session): what the form offers for a candidate default organization without requiring the admin to be a member of it: `{ workspaces: [{id, slug, name}] (ordinary only, never personal; ≤500), secrets: [{id, name, kind}] (active org-level; LLM keys have kind llm), connections: [{id, label, app_id}] (org-level, not orphaned) }`; unknown org 404. `Workspace` DTOs are unchanged; `personal_owner_user_id` is not exposed (SEC-79: nothing may address a workspace by it).
- C-529 **Data**: migration `20261124000001_new_user_placement.sql` (`instance_placement`, `workspaces.personal_owner_user_id` with the partial unique index per (org, user)), `03`.

### Phase 4 — P4-HAR (harness adapters and selection, D-122, D-153, SEC-66)

Rule: `12 §Harness selection and adapter contract` and `§Adapter codex`, `11 §Sandbox image` and `§The boot document`, `10` (agent `harness`, switching), `05` (`/v1/harnesses`, agent PATCH), `14` (Instructions), `15` (`hosted.enabled_harnesses`), `16` (`session.purge`, `sandbox.status`), `03 §Planned changes`, `17` (SEC-66).

- C-420 **`GET /v1/harnesses`** (S/K, any signed-in caller): 200 `{ "items": [HarnessView], "default": string }`; `HarnessView { id: "claude-code" | "codex", display_name: string, dialects: [string], available: bool }`. `items` are `hosted.enabled_harnesses` in configured order (`fake` is never listed); `dialects` are the harness's declared dialects in preference order (`claude-code`: `anthropic_messages`; `codex`: `openai_responses`: the pinned Codex 0.167.0 refuses `wire_api = "chat"`, so the adapter declares only the Responses dialect, D-153); `available` = at least one **live** runner replica (registered and polling within `REPLICA_ONLINE_THRESHOLD`) lists the id in its registered `harnesses`; `default` = `hosted.default_harness`. `401` without a session or key.
- C-421 **Agent `harness` validation** (`POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agents`, `PATCH /v1/agents/{agent}`): a harness outside `hosted.enabled_harnesses` (plus `fake` when `hosted.allow_fake_harness`) is `422 harness_not_enabled` ("This harness is not enabled on this instance."); a hosted agent created without one gets `hosted.default_harness`. The harness is chosen **only** by these routes and stamped in the boot document (`harness.id`); no request from a sandbox can change it.
- C-422 **Switching** (`PATCH` with a `harness` different from the agent's current one, hosted agents only), in one transaction under the agent row lock: any active turn of the agent (`queued`, `dispatched`, `running`) → `409 turn_active` ("The agent's harness cannot change while a turn is running.") and nothing changes; otherwise the harness is written, **every** conversation of the agent gets `harness_session_epoch + 1` and `harness_session_ref = NULL` (the transcript is untouched), a `session_purges` row is queued per conversation that had a reference (stamped with the **old** harness, C-424), a restart of the agent's running computer is requested (so it boots with the new adapter) and work is notified. Patching the same harness changes nothing. A model override whose endpoint no longer serves the new harness is dropped with the existing platform notice at the next turn (`override_dropped` in `GET …/model-readiness`); with no compatible granted key the next turn fails `no_model_key`.
- C-423 **Turn errors** (`turn.finished.error_code`, queued turns of the agent): `harness_unavailable` — the dispatcher fails a queued hosted turn **before any sandbox start** when at least one live runner replica exists and none lists the agent's harness (message: "No runner offers the `<id>` harness. Enable the sandbox image or runner that contains it, or choose another harness for this agent."; with no live replica at all the turn waits as before); `harness_unsupported` — the supervisor refused the boot (C-425): the agent's queued turns fail with the start reason and `GET …/sandbox` reports `failure_code = harness_unsupported`.
- C-424 **`session.purge` gains `harness`** (`16 §W5`, runner ↔ supervisor): `{ conversation_id, session_ref, harness?: string }`; absent = the sandbox's own harness. Migration `20261126000001_harness_purges.sql` adds the nullable `session_purges.harness`. The `render_inputs` home-sync part's `purges[]` entries carry the same optional `harness`. The supervisor deletes the session with **that** harness's adapter (compile-time registry), so the old adapter's `purge_session` runs after a switch; a purge whose runtime is no longer in the image fails and stays pending. A purge of a harness other than the agent's current one is delivered even while a new turn runs (it cannot be in use); a purge of the sandbox's own harness still waits for the conversation's active turn (RFE-010).
- C-425 **Boot refusal**: a supervisor whose boot document names an id it does not know, or whose runtime is not in the image, sends **instead of `ready`** the frame `sandbox.status` `{ "state": "failed", "reason_code": "harness_unsupported", "message": string }` (new supervisor → runner frame kind; the runner forwards it 1:1 as the report of the same kind) and exits. The API maps it like any start failure (`fail_start`: `harness_unsupported` joins `image_unavailable`, `at_capacity`, `agent_start_failed`). There is no fallback to another harness.
- C-426 **Configuration**: `hosted.enabled_harnesses` (list of `claude-code`, `codex`, `fake`; default `["claude-code"]`; empty, unknown, or `fake` without `hosted.allow_fake_harness` refuses startup); `hosted.default_harness` must be in it (or `fake` when allowed). `runner.image_harnesses` (list, default empty): the runtimes of the sandbox image for backends that cannot read the image label (Kubernetes); an unknown id refuses startup; ignored by podman, which reads the label. Helm: `config.hosted.enabled_harnesses`, `config.runner.image_harnesses`.
- C-427 **Image and registration**: `images/sandbox/Containerfile` build argument `SANDBOX_HARNESSES` (default `claude-code,codex`), label `org.aurora.harnesses` = `<SANDBOX_HARNESSES>,fake`; the pinned Codex CLI is release `rust-v0.167.0` (`codex-<arch>-unknown-linux-musl.tar.gz`, SHA-256 `9d64836106d5b374e90ee4454c7ebcde7ca7106558bd7e41a34b9f26ebda251a` for x86_64, `843489acb4d4b1079745c36b03c5963b887b83155af208fd53584e33ee8eb281` for aarch64, verified before unpacking) under `/opt/aurora/harness/codex/codex`. The runner registers `capabilities.harnesses` from the label (absent label = `claude-code,fake`; an uninspectable image or a label-blind backend keeps the backend list or `runner.image_harnesses`). `HarnessId` gains `codex` (open vocabulary).
- C-428 **Codex adapter contract**: one `codex exec --json [resume <thread id>] --skip-git-repo-check -C /workspace -` child per turn, prompt on stdin; the thread id is the session reference; `CODEX_HOME` = `<home>/.codex`; `config.toml` rendered at every session start (model, provider block with `wire_api = "responses"`, `env_key = "OPENAI_API_KEY"`, `approval_policy = "never"`, `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`, update checks/analytics/feedback/web search and the vendor-service features disabled, one MCP server `aurora`), `auth.json` removed; environment = `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` (per-session proxy), the CA bundle, `CODEX_HOME`, `OPENAI_API_KEY=aurora-placeholder`, never `OPENAI_BASE_URL` (the base URL is in the provider block: the provider's own `OPENAI_BASE_URL` placeholder-only client variable when it supplies one, e.g. LiteLLM's `<base>/v1`, else the endpoint base URL); capabilities `resume`, `tool_events`, `platform_tools`, `steer = false`, `background_tool = false`; `instruction_files = ["AGENTS.md"]`, `skills_dir = ".codex/skills/aurora"` (a subdirectory, so the read-only projection never touches Codex's own `.system` skills). Provider errors map to `provider_auth | provider_rate_limited | provider_quota | provider_overloaded`, a gateway envelope to `gateway_refused`, anything else to `harness_failed`.
- C-429 **Dashboard routes** (no wire change): the Instructions tab shows a harness picker (a `select`) only for a hosted agent when `GET /v1/harnesses` lists more than one; an option no runner offers is disabled ("(no runner offers this)") unless it is the agent's current one; saving a changed harness opens the confirmation "Starts fresh sessions for every conversation of this agent; the transcript is kept." and then sends `PATCH { harness }` (a `409 turn_active` or `422 harness_not_enabled` is shown as the form error); an unchanged harness is never sent.

### Phase 4 — P4-GSL (git-backed skill library, D-148, D-149, D-154, SEC-83, SEC-84)

Rule: `10 §Git skill library`, `05 §Skill libraries`, `06 §Library fetch`, `09 §3.4 library`, `03` (`skill_libraries`, `skills` library columns, `skill_library_updates`), `04` (matrix, audit), `12` (skill tools), `14`, `15 §Git skill library`, `02 §Limits`, `17` (SEC-83, SEC-84), `18 §Git skill library`. Errors use the standard body. Every route answers 404 `skill_libraries_disabled` while `skills.library.enabled` is false.

- C-570 **Libraries** (`/v1/orgs/{org}/skill-libraries`, `/v1/skill-libraries/{id}`, `…/refresh`). `SkillLibrary = { id, label, status: "active"|"paused"|"error"|"over_budget", app_id, connection: { id, label, status }|null, repo, ref|null, path, last_head_sha|null, last_refresh_at|null, last_error|null (sanitized, ≤ 200), skill_counts: { agent, workspace, org }, pending_updates, created_at }`; org owners/admins get every field, other people who can use a workspace of the org get only `id`, `label` and `status` of the **active** libraries (every other field `null`; the browse picker). `POST` `{ label, connection_id, repo, ref?, path? }` → 201 (one `resolve_ref` through the gateway; `ref` omitted or `""` = default branch); `PATCH` `{ label?, status?: "active"|"paused", connection_id?, ref? ("" clears), path? }` (a change of connection, ref or path re-resolves, never touches stored skills, and the connection must be of the same app); `DELETE` → 204, detaches every skill of the library; `POST …/refresh` → 202 ("Check now": 409 `library_paused`, 429 `library_check_too_soon` within 60 s). Errors: 422 `library_unreachable` (reason in the message, sanitized, no URL or token), `connection_not_org_scoped`, `app_without_library`; 400 `invalid_request` (label 1–80, repo against the package's `repo_pattern` and the hard grammar, ref, path ≤ 4 plain segments, a connection of another org); 409 `library_exists` (label per org), `library_limit` (10). Audit (security-critical, one row in the change's transaction): `skill_library.create|update|delete`.
- C-571 **Browse** (`GET /v1/skill-libraries/{id}/skills?q=&cursor=`, `GET …/skills/{path}?commit_sha=`; audience: org owner/admin or anyone who can use a workspace of the org; `Cache-Control: no-store`; nothing is stored). `LibraryCatalogue = { commit_sha, items: [{ path, name, description|null, valid, reason|null, size, files, added: [{ tier, id }] }], truncated, next_cursor }` (the head is resolved live, the listing cached 5 minutes per library in memory; pages of 50; `q` filters name and description; at most 200 skills, `truncated` beyond); `LibrarySkillContent = { commit_sha, path, name, valid, reason|null, description|null, body|null, files: [{ path, size, executable, binary, content|null, content_b64|null }], size, content_sha256|null }` (exactly what an add stores; an invalid skill is a 200 with `valid = false` and the reason). 502 `library_unavailable` (sanitized reason), 422 `library_tree_too_large` (truncated tree or more than 20 000 entries). `commit_sha` must be the ref's **current head** or a head the library recorded (refresh, browse, create and patch record; the newest 20 are kept): anything else → 422 `commit_not_in_library` (D-178 Q-R5-3, D-210); a cold listing is single-flight per library under a 90 s deadline. A SKILL.md frontmatter with a YAML anchor or alias is an invalid item (never an error for the listing). Text from the repository is sanitized on load (control, bidi and zero-width characters removed) and is data.
- C-572 **Add** (`POST /v1/agents/{agent}/skills`, `/v1/workspaces/{ws}/skills`, `/v1/orgs/{org}/skills`): `CreateSkillRequest` takes `from_library: { library_id, path, commit_sha }` **instead of** `name`, `description`, `body` (those three are now optional in the schema and required unless `from_library` is given: 400 otherwise, and 400 when both are given). The server reads **exactly** `commit_sha` (a full lowercase hex id; absent → 422 `commit_required`; not the ref's current head nor a recorded one → 422 `commit_not_in_library`, C-571) and stores the skill pinned and read-only. Authority is the tier's (`can_use` / `can_own` / org owner·admin), API keys allowed, never an agent token (401). 422 `library_skill_invalid` (reason: symlink, submodule, non-regular entry, a file over 64 KiB, more than 50 files, a path or name the skill rules refuse, frontmatter without a single-line `description` or whose `name` differs from the directory, a wrong-case `skill.md`, body/total limits), 422 `library_budget_exceeded` (`details`: `tier`, `bytes_used`, `bytes_adding`, `bytes_limit`, `skills_used`, `skills_limit`), 409 `skill_exists`. Audit `skill.library.add` (critical; metadata: library, repo, path, commit, `content_sha256`). Every `Skill` gains `source: null | SkillSource = { kind: "library", library_id|null, label, repo, path, commit_sha, pinned_at, detached, update_pending }` (kept after a detach or a library deletion with `detached = true`).
- C-573 **Read-only**: `PATCH /v1/skills/{id}` with `description` or `body`, `PUT`/`DELETE` of a file of a library skill → 409 `library_skill_readonly` (`enabled`, `DELETE /v1/skills/{id}` stay allowed). Agent tools: `skill_update`/`skill_delete` on a library skill fail with a message starting `library_skill_readonly:`; `skill_list`/`skill_get` return `source` (`{ kind, repo, path, commit_sha, detached }`, `null` for an ordinary skill); `skill_create` has no library argument.
- C-574 **Update review and decisions**: `GET /v1/skills/{id}/library` → `SkillLibraryInfo = { library: { id, label, repo, path }|null, commit_sha, pinned_at, pinned_by, detached, update: null | LibraryUpdateView }`, `LibraryUpdateView = { id, kind: "changed"|"removed"|"invalid", commit_sha, created_at, content_sha256 (hex), over_budget, reason|null, diff: null | LibraryDiff }`, `LibraryDiff = { description: { old, new, changed }, body: { old, new, changed }, files: [{ path, change: "added"|"removed"|"modified"|"unchanged", old|null, new|null, old_executable|null, new_executable|null, binary }] }` (the whole stored-versus-proposed text, never a summary; `diff = null` for `invalid`). `POST /v1/skills/{id}/library/updates/{uid}/approve` `{ content_sha256 }` (**session only**, tier authority) → 200 `Skill`; the proposal's hash must match (else 409 `update_superseded`, also for a decided or superseded one), `invalid` → 422 `library_skill_invalid`, re-validation (the refresh-time rules on the stored proposal: file paths, 50 files, file sizes, description and body) and budget → 422 `library_skill_invalid` / `library_budget_exceeded`; a pending proposal of a **paused** library → 409 `library_paused` (rejecting stays possible; D-178 Q-R5-6); applying replaces content and pin in one transaction and bumps the home-sync generation of exactly the affected agents (agent tier: that agent; workspace tier: the workspace; org tier: every agent of the org); a `removed` proposal deletes the skill; audit `skill.library.update_apply`. `…/reject` (session only) → 204, audit `skill.library.update_reject`; `POST /v1/skills/{id}/library/detach` (session only) → 200 `Skill`, 409 `not_a_library_skill`, audit `skill.library.detach` (all critical, in the change's transaction). `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/skill-library-updates` (org owner/admin), `/v1/workspaces/{ws}/skill-library-updates` (`can_own`), `/v1/agents/{agent}/skill-library-updates` (`can_use`) → `{ items: [{ id, kind, created_at, skill: { id, name, tier, agent_id, workspace_id }, library: { id, label }|null }] }`. The best-effort system row `skill.library.update_proposed` is written by the refresh job.
- C-575 **Gateway `POST /internal/v1/library-fetch`** — **REMOVED by P5-SKLIB-1 (D-448, C-1125, C-1127); kept below as the record of what existed** (internal port, internal secret ring; the route exists only on the internal router). Request `{ org_id, connection_id, step: "resolve_ref"|"list_tree"|"get_blob", params: { repo, ref?, sha?, blob?, page? }, max_bytes }` (unknown keys → 400). `page` is an integer 2–200 appended to the template's query for a paginated tree (the API reads it from the `Link` header's `rel="next"` `page` value; the URL itself is never followed). Response 200 `{ status, headers: { link?, retry-after? }, encoding: "base64", body }` where `status` is the git host's status (4xx/5xx are passed through as data, 3xx is **not followed** and its `Location` is not returned) and `body` the response bytes in base64, at most `min(max_bytes, 4 MiB)` (413 `library_response_too_large` beyond). Gateway errors: 400 `library_param`, 401 (no or wrong internal secret), 404 `connection_not_found` (another org's or unknown id), 409 `library_unavailable` (workspace-scoped, not active, app without or no longer with a `library` block, instance unavailable, host claimed by another app, `rewrite_host`/`finalize`/`intercept` on the host rule), 409 `reauth_required` (also sets the usual flag after a refused refresh), 429 `rate_limited` (20/s per gateway, 5/s per org), 502 `library_upstream`, 504 `library_timeout` (20 s total), 503 when degraded. A `401` from the host triggers one forced token refresh and one resend. Metric `aurora_gateway_library_fetch_total{step,status}`. Without a `ref` the API first asks for `HEAD` (accepted by both built-in hosts) and falls back to the `default_path` template.
- C-576 **Manifest `library` block** (contract 1, additive; `09 §3.4 library`): `apps[].library { repo_pattern, request_host, resolve_ref { path, default_path, sha, default_branch, method? }, list_tree { path, entries, fields { path, type, mode, blob, size? }, file_type, truncated?, paginate: none|link_header|page_param, max_pages?, method? }, get_blob { path, content?, encoding: raw|base64|<json path>, method? }, required_access: read }`; placeholders `{repo}`, `{repo|urlencode}`, `{ref}`, `{ref|urlencode}`, `{sha}`, `{blob}` (every value checked before substitution: repo against the pattern **and** `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` with no empty, `.` or `..` segment; ref `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,200}` with no `..`, leading `-`, `//`, leading or trailing `/`; sha and blob lowercase hex 40–64); JSON paths `$` then `.name` or `[*]` (≤ 3 wildcards, ≤ 8 segments); linter codes `library_host_not_exact`, `library_no_credential`, `library_path_placeholder`, `library_repo_pattern` (must be anchored and refuse a probe set of hostile strings), `library_json_path`, `library_method`, unknown keys → `unknown_field`. Copied into the snapshot as `apps[].library`; a version that **adds or changes** it widens (`Widening::Library`, shown as "<app> can serve a skill library: reads repository files from <host>", re-approval like a new host claim); `PluginAppRef` gains `library_host`, `AppSummary` and `AppDetail` gain `serves_library: bool`.
- C-577 **Refresh job** (every 60 s on every replica; `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, per-library lease `LIBRARY_LEASE` 5 min, at most 4 per replica): libraries with at least one added skill, `status ∈ (active, error, over_budget)` and `next_refresh_at <= now()`; an unchanged head costs one call (a library left `over_budget` re-checks, and an add of a commit other than the last compared head clears `last_head_sha` and bumps `head_epoch`, and a pass writes its head only if the epoch is the one it read; a git-host 429/503 is not a failure: `refresh_failures` and status stay, the next attempt is after the host's `Retry-After` (seconds, default 300, capped 1 h); the open-proposal sum and insert run under the organization's lock — D-210); otherwise one recursive tree and, per added skill, a comparison of blob ids and executable bits. A proposal's `content_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the canonical content (`changed`: description, body, files by path with the executable bit, length-prefixed), of the marker `removed`, or of the marker `invalid` + reason + blob set, so the same repository state hashes the same (this is also how a rejection is remembered). One pending proposal per skill (a newer one supersedes; a repository back at the pinned state withdraws it), open proposals ≤ 4 MiB per org (`status = over_budget` beyond), `next_refresh_at` = interval (floor 5 min) → 1 h → 6 h by consecutive failures, ±10 % jitter. The job never writes `skills`, `skill_files` or a home-sync generation. Retention (daily, singleton): decided proposals older than 90 days are pruned, stale leases expire, libraries whose connection vanished are marked `error` (`last_error = connection_deleted`). Metrics `aurora_skill_library_refresh_total{result}`, `aurora_skill_library_pending_updates`, `aurora_skill_library_refresh_duration_seconds`.
- C-578 **Data**: migration `20261127000001_skill_libraries.sql` (`skill_libraries`, nine `skills.library_*` columns with CHECK `skills_library_cols`, `skill_library_updates`; `03`). The gateway database role gets nothing on these tables.
- C-579 **Configuration, audit, limits, packages**: `skills.library.enabled` (default `true`), `skills.library.refresh_interval_secs` (900, ≥ 300), `skills.library.bytes_per_agent` (1 MiB, lower only), `api.gateway_internal_url` (default `http://aurora-gateway:4103`; refuses a non-http(s) URL naming the key) (D-154); security-critical audit actions `skill_library.create|update|delete`, `skill.library.add|update_apply|update_reject|detach`; limits in `aurora_core::limits` (`LIBRARIES_PER_ORG` 10, `LIBRARY_BYTES_PER_AGENT|WORKSPACE|ORG` 1/2/4 MiB, `LIBRARY_SKILLS_PER_AGENT|WORKSPACE|ORG` 25/50/100, `LIBRARY_FILE_MAX` 64 KiB, `LIBRARY_TREE_MAX` 20 000, `LIBRARY_BROWSE_MAX` 200, `LIBRARY_PENDING_BYTES_PER_ORG` 4 MiB, plus 50 files per skill); built-in packages `github` 1.1.0 (both apps) and `gitlab` 1.2.0 add the `library` block (GitLab: project path URL-encoded, tree paginated by `link_header`, `raw` blobs).

### Phase 4 — FIX-025 (sandbox start readiness deadline and start diagnostics, D-158)

Rule: `10 §Failure semantics`, `11 §Lifecycle, §Start readiness deadline`, `14` (agent header, Computer tab, Admin › Runners), `15` (`hosted.sandbox_ready_timeout_secs`, operator troubleshooting), `16` (`sandbox.status`), `03 §Planned changes`. Reserved numbers C-580..C-589. Errors use the standard body.

- C-580 **End-to-end readiness deadline** (`hosted.sandbox_ready_timeout_secs`, 60–1800, default 300; `15`): from "a turn needs a sandbox" (the start is claimed) to "the supervisor is ready" (the runner's capacity wait and image pull count inside it). The API's readiness sweep (10 s interval, `domains/hosted/readiness.rs`) fails the attempt at the deadline with the last observed phase and the code naming where it stopped (C-581), exactly as if the runner had reported that failure. Config validation: 60 ≤ value ≤ 1800, else startup fails. (FIX-093) Every start attempt is bounded from its claim, whether or not a turn is waiting: a `starting` sandbox whose lease has expired with its runner no longer live, or whose attempt is older than the readiness deadline, is failed by the sweep with `runner_unavailable` (its runner is not live; `runner_id` is cleared so another runner can claim the next start) or `sandbox_start_failed`. C-584 refers to it.
- C-581 **Start-failure codes** (`10 §Failure semantics`; recorded via the existing `fail_start` path): `runner_unavailable` (no runner claimed the start), `pod_refused` (the cluster refused pod creation), `pod_failed` (the pod ended while starting), `home_volume_unbound` (the home PVC never bound), `image_unavailable` (the image pull failed), `supervisor_unreachable` (the pod is running but no supervisor connected to the runner's sandbox channel :4105 within `RUNNER_SUPERVISOR_HELLO` = 60 s; the message names the runner host:port and adds the Cilium/NetworkPolicy hint), `supervisor_boot_failed` (the supervisor connected but the boot never finished), `at_capacity` (the cluster had no room for the pod; chat copy "The cluster has no room to start the agent's computer right now. Try again in a few minutes.") and `agent_start_failed` (the catch-all the API records for any other start failure; copy "The agent's computer failed to start."), both with the Computer tab link and the server message beside the copy (FIX-099). Each failure message is a human sentence, sanitised (`aurora_core::sanitize::reason`), never containing tokens or secret material. Queued turns of the agent fail with the code and message; `sandbox.state = failed` with `failure_code`/`failure_message`. (FIX-093) Member-facing failure text never contains an address or a service name; the runner endpoint appears in the admin start log (`sandbox_start_failures.message`, the start log) only. A runner's late `failed` for a boot the API already gave up (sandbox `stopping`, code recorded) lands the sandbox in `failed` but adds no failure and keeps the code.
- C-582 **Start-attempt bookkeeping** (migration `20261128000001_sandbox_start_diagnostics.sql`, `03`): `sandboxes` gains `start_attempt_at` (when the attempt was claimed), `start_phase`/`start_detail` (the runner's last reported phase and its detail), `start_events` (jsonb array of the pod's Warning events, bounded, newest first), `start_progress_at` (the runner's last progress report), `running_at`. Written by the runner's phase reports and the readiness sweep through the existing fenced lifecycle reports; reset by every new boot.
- C-583 **Retry bound**: automatic start retries stop after 3 failures per sandbox (`start_failures`); the next message retries once the backoff has passed (the failed turn's message already says until when). `start_failures` is reset by a successful start.
- C-584 **`GET /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox`** gains `start_attempt: null | { started_at, phase: string|null, detail: string|null, events: [string], progress_at: string|null }` (the last attempt, `C-582`); `null` when the sandbox has none. The Computer tab's **Last start attempt** section renders it (`14`); the agent header pill shows "Sandbox failed" with the reason as tooltip on `failed`, and "Sandbox starting" only while a start is genuinely in flight.
- C-585 **Chat error copy** (`14`, `10`): every code of C-581 maps to a human message in the chat error block with an "Open Computer tab" link and the server's `message` shown beside it (the block's generic copy names the class; the server message names the endpoint or resource). Unknown start codes render generically.
- C-586 **`GET /v1/admin/runners/{id}/start-failures`** (IA, session; operation `adminRunnerStartFailures`): 200 `{ "items": [RunnerStartFailure] }`, newest first, at most 50; `RunnerStartFailure = { id, sandbox_id, agent_id, code, message, phase: string|null, events: [string], elapsed_secs: int|null, created_at }`. Unknown runner 404; non-admin 403. Backed by `sandbox_start_failures` (C-587), written on every start failure (runner report or readiness deadline) with the runner's id, the phase and the elapsed seconds of the attempt.
- C-587 **`sandbox_start_failures` table** (migration `20261128000001_sandbox_start_diagnostics.sql`, `03`): `id uuid PK, runner_id uuid → runners ON DELETE CASCADE, sandbox_id uuid NOT NULL → sandboxes ON DELETE CASCADE, agent_id uuid NOT NULL, boot_id uuid, code text NOT NULL, message text NOT NULL, phase text, events jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]', elapsed_secs int, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`. Rows older than 30 days are pruned on insert; messages are sanitised at write time (C-581).
- C-588 **Admin › Runners page** (`/admin/runners`, `14`): runners with backend and live/offline badges; per runner an expandable **Start failures** section fed by C-586 (time, code, reason, phase, elapsed, events). `AdminGuard` as the other admin pages; the sidebar's admin nav gains "Runners".
- C-589 **Cilium compatibility** (`15`, operator docs): the chart's sandboxes-namespace NetworkPolicies select pods by identity (namespace + pod labels), which Cilium enforces natively; the egress rule sandbox → runner :4105 needs **no** CiliumNetworkPolicy beyond the existing runner → kube-api CNP (FIX-017b). With `networkPolicy.cilium.enabled=true` the chart renders `cilium.io/v2` CNPs for those two paths only; the e2e evidence (blocked :4105 → `supervisor_unreachable` within the deadline; allowed → the turn proceeds) is recorded in the operator docs.

### Phase 4 — FIX-029 (sandbox start retry and start log, D-160)

Rule: `11 §Start readiness deadline`, `10 §Failure semantics`, `05 §Agents and sandboxes`, `14` (chat error block, Computer tab, Admin › Runners), `03 §Planned changes`, `02 §Limits`. Reserved numbers C-610..C-615. Errors use the standard body.

- C-610 **Retry** (`POST /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox/retry`, S/K `can_use`, operation `retryAgentSandbox`; `05`; **amended by FIX-049/D-164**: an explicit retry overrides the failure-backoff lease): valid only from sandbox state `failed` — 409 `sandbox_not_failed` from any other state (`starting`, `running`, `stopping`, `stopped`, `unprovisioned`); a live failure-backoff lease on a `failed` row **never** refuses (the C-583 bound stops automatic failure storms, not the human recovery this route performs, and a live lease on a `failed` row fences no in-flight start — an in-flight start leaves the row `starting`); 423 `frozen` on an effectively frozen agent; 404 unknown agent (hosted-only agents, as `/restart`). In one transaction: clear `failure_code`/`failure_message`, reset `start_failures = 0`, clear `lifecycle_lease_until` (the C-583 automatic-retry bound and its backoff are untouched for starts nobody forces), append a retry note to the start log (C-612) and write a best-effort audit row `agent.sandbox_retry` (metadata: the previous `failure_code` and `via` = `"retry"`, or `"restart"` from C-626). The retry never starts anything itself: it notifies due work so the **ordinary due-work start arm** claims the sandbox with a fresh `boot_id`; the retried start is bounded by the same readiness deadline (C-580) and its failures write failure rows and end turns exactly as any start (C-581). 202 → `SandboxView` (state `starting` once claimed or `stopped`/`unprovisioned` before the arm runs).
- C-611 **`SandboxView` gains `retryable: bool` and `start_log: [StartLogEntry]`** (`GET /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox`, the M4 DTO): `retryable = state == "failed"`; `StartLogEntry = { ts: Timestamp, kind: "phase" | "note", phase: string | null, detail: string | null }` — the start log of the current or last boot, newest first (C-612).
- C-612 **Start log** (`11`, `03`): `sandboxes.start_log` jsonb, a ring of at most 30 entries and 8 KiB (oldest dropped; each `detail` ≤ 300 chars, sanitised at write like every reason, C-581), reset by every new boot together with the other per-boot fields. Appended (fenced, through the existing lifecycle-report path): every runner phase report (`kind: "phase"`, the phase and its detail), every start failure from a runner report or the readiness sweep's verdict (`kind: "phase"`, `phase = "failed"`, detail = the human reason), and the retry note of C-610 (`kind: "note"`, detail "Start retried by <label> after <code>."). The log is diagnostics, never a turn event: nothing enters the transcript.
- C-613 **Per-agent start-failure history** (`GET /v1/agents/{agent}/start-failures`, S/K `can_use`, operation `agentStartFailures`): 200 `{ "items": [RunnerStartFailure] }` — the C-586 shape, newest first, at most 20, filtered to the agent; 404 unknown agent. (D-178, Q-R5-5, FIX-093) A person with `can_use` sees each failure's `code`, `phase`, `elapsed_secs` and a **hint** in `message` (a class-level sentence per code); `events` (pod/volume warnings) are empty for them. Only instance admins see the C-586 record in full (D-424, FIX-196, extends SEC-141: amended — before, the agent's workspace owners saw it too; the runner's wording and events carry registry, image tags and cluster addresses). Workspace owners get what members get.
