It is derived from the installed manifest (one shared function used by the API and the gateway), not stored.
- C-896 **`PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/apps/{app}`** (FIX-154; S/K, same caller rule as `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}`).
  - **Body.** As that route: `{ mode, allow_tools?, ask_tools?, ask_timeout_secs? }`, same validation (incl. 422 `full_grant_forbidden` for `custom_only`).
  - **Checks, before any write.** 404 `app_not_found` when not installed. 403 `app_unavailable` when the posture does not make it available to the agent's workspace. 422 `app_not_credential_free` (`details: { app_id }`) when C-895 is false.
  - **Effect, in one transaction serialized per (workspace, app)** (e.g. `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the pair), so parallel calls never create two connections:
    1. Take the oldest `active` workspace-scope connection of the app in the agent's workspace. If there is none, create one through the same service path as `POST /v1/apps/{app}/connect` with the first credential-free method. Its label is `connect_note.label_hint`, else the app name. It is audited `connection.created` with `details.via = "agent_grant"` and `details.agent_id`.
    2. Upsert the agent's grant on that connection exactly as `PUT …/grants/connections/{conn}` does, with the same audit, snapshot invalidation and `app_restrictions`.
  - **Answer.** `200 { connection: Connection, grant: ConnectionGrant, created: boolean }`.
  - **Removing access.** Uses the existing `DELETE /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}`; the connection is kept.
- C-897 **Credential-free apps in the dashboard and the gateway** (FIX-154).
  - **Access tab.** The agent's Access tab (Connections section) also lists each available credential-free app that has no connection in the agent's scope: app icon and name, a "No sign-in needed" badge and **Give access**. It opens the same grant editor (`GrantForm`), which saves through C-896. Once granted, the row becomes the ordinary connection row.
  - **Connect dialog.** For a credential-free app the Connect dialog (C-842) shows the app's `connect_note` and the label, with no form step. It goes straight to the agent picker (`GiveAccessPanel`) with one primary action that connects and then grants (`POST /v1/connections/{id}/grants`).
  - **Gateway.** For a credential-free app, `ldap_no_connection` `not_connected` and `app_not_connected` name `access_url` first ("A workspace owner can give this agent access on its Access tab: <access_url>"); `connect_url` stays in details.
  - **Agent notice.** The agent's `request_app_connection` notice (I-29) for a credential-free app links to the Access tab of the requesting agent instead of the connect deep link.

### Phase 5 — P5-FILES (the agent's files in the dashboard, D-316, SEC-125, SEC-126)

Rule: `11 §Files`, `12 §SSH channels` (the `sftp` exec mode), `16 §W21` (on `16 §W20`'s leg 2), `14` (Files page), `04 §Permission matrix` and `§Audit`, `03 §Planned changes`, `02 §Limits`, `00` (`auf_`), SEC-125, SEC-126. Reserved numbers C-898..C-907. Errors use the standard body. **No gate of its own** (owner, D-316(b)): the routes exist exactly where the web terminal does — instance `terminal.enabled`, hosted agent, `can_use` — and every route answers 404 `terminal_disabled` while the terminal is off. P5-FILES-1 implements C-898..C-903 and C-906; P5-FILES-2 C-904; P5-FILES-3 C-905; P5-FILES-4 the journey of C-907.

- C-898 **Routes and schemas** (all **S** — session cookie only, like the terminal; API keys, service keys and agent credentials 401; mutating routes carry the dashboard's CSRF protection; rate class `files`, C-900). `path` is always an absolute path under `/workspace` (C-899); absent on a listing = `/workspace`.
  - `FileEntry = { name: string, path: string, kind: "file"|"dir"|"symlink"|"other", size: int, modified_at: string|null, mode: string ("0644", octal permission bits only), writable: boolean (owner write bit and not read-only per C-899), target?: { path: string, kind: "file"|"dir"|"other"|"missing", outside_workspace: boolean } }` (`target` only for a symlink; the API resolves it with SFTP `realpath`+`stat`; a dangling link is `missing`).
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/files?path=` → 200 `{ path, entries: FileEntry[], truncated: boolean }` — directories first, then by name (byte order); at most `FILES_LIST_MAX` (5,000) entries, `truncated` when more exist; `.` and `..` never listed. A path that is a file → 422 `not_a_directory`.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/files/content?path=` → 200 the bytes, streamed (C-903 headers); a directory → 422 `is_directory`. Best-effort audit `agent.files.download` (`via: "download"`).
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/files/text?path=` → 200 `{ path, content: string, etag: string, size: int, modified_at: string|null, line_ending: "lf"|"crlf" }` for a regular file of ≤ `FILES_EDIT_MAX` (1 MiB) that is valid UTF-8 without a NUL byte; otherwise 422 `file_not_editable` with `details.reason: "too_large"|"binary"|"not_utf8"`. `etag` = `"sha256:" + hex(SHA-256 of the bytes)`; `line_ending` = `crlf` when the first line break is CRLF. Audit `agent.files.download` (`via: "editor"`).
  - `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/files/content?path=` — body = the raw bytes (`Content-Type: application/octet-stream`), `Content-Length` required (411 `length_required`), ≤ `FILES_UPLOAD_MAX` (512 MiB, 413 `file_too_large` before any byte is written). Preconditions (at most one): `If-None-Match: *` = create only (an existing path → 409 `file_exists`); `If-Match: "<etag>"` = replace only if unchanged — the API reads the current file (≤ `FILES_EDIT_MAX`, else mismatch) and compares SHA-256 (mismatch or missing file → 409 `file_changed` with `details.current_etag` when readable); neither = create or replace. The parent directory must exist (404 `path_not_found`, `details.missing: "parent"`). Write path: the bytes go to a temp file `.<name>.aurora-upload-<16 hex>` in the same directory (created `0600`, then set to the replaced file's mode, or `0644` for a new file), then **`posix-rename@openssh.com`** onto the target (atomic replace); any failure removes the temp file. Replacing a file whose owner write bit is clear → 403 `permission_denied` (the rename would otherwise bypass it). → 201 (created) or 200 (replaced) `{ entry: FileEntry, etag: string|null }` (`etag` when the size is ≤ `FILES_EDIT_MAX`). Audit `agent.files.write` (path, bytes, `created`).
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/files/ops` — one of:
    - `{ op: "mkdir", path, parents?: boolean }` → 201 `{ entry }`; existing path → 409 `file_exists` (with `parents: true` an existing **directory** is 200, like `mkdir -p`). Mode `0755`. Audit `agent.files.mkdir`.
    - `{ op: "rename", path, to }` → 200 `{ entry }` (the renamed entry at `to`); `to` existing → 409 `file_exists` (no silent replace; the dashboard asks first and deletes); `to` inside `path` → 422 `invalid_path`. A symlink is renamed, never its target. Audit `agent.files.rename` (from, to).
    - `{ op: "delete", path, recursive?: boolean }` → 200 `{ deleted: int }` (entries removed); a non-empty directory without `recursive` → 409 `directory_not_empty`; with `recursive` the API walks the tree with `lstat` (never follows a symlink; a link is removed as a link), deleting depth-first, refusing up front with 422 `too_many_entries` when the walk passes `FILES_DELETE_MAX` (10,000) entries (nothing deleted); a failure part-way answers 422 `files_operation_failed` with `details.deleted` (the count so far). Audit `agent.files.delete` (path, `deleted`).
- C-899 **Paths and the `/workspace` scope** (owner, D-316(c)). Request paths are validated before any SFTP call: valid UTF-8, absolute, equal to `/workspace` or starting with `/workspace/`, no empty, `.` or `..` component, no NUL, a trailing `/` stripped, each component ≤ 255 bytes, the whole ≤ `FILES_PATH_MAX` (4,096 bytes) — else 400 `invalid_path`. Then the **resolved** path decides: for an existing path the API calls SFTP `realpath`, for a path to be created (upload of a new file, `mkdir`, rename's `to`) it resolves the parent; a result outside `/workspace` → 403 `path_outside_workspace`, and the operation then uses the resolved path. **Read-only**: a resolved path equal to or under `/workspace/.aurora` refuses every write, mkdir, rename (from or to) and delete with 403 `path_read_only` (platform-managed, `12 §Home projection`); `/workspace` itself cannot be renamed or deleted (403 `path_read_only`). Listing names that are not valid UTF-8 are returned with U+FFFD replacements and `writable: false`; any operation on such a path is 422 `invalid_path`. The scope is a **presentation boundary, not a security boundary** (SEC-125): every operation already runs as the agent uid, so a symlink swapped between `realpath` and the operation gives a person nothing the agent's own code could not read or write. SFTP status mapping: `NO_SUCH_FILE` → 404 `path_not_found`; `PERMISSION_DENIED` → 403 `permission_denied`; `FAILURE` and anything else → 422 `files_operation_failed` (OpenSSH's `sftp-server` does not distinguish a full disk from other failures; the message says "The sandbox refused the operation (for example, the disk is full)").
- C-900 **Availability, wake, keep-awake, limits.** Authority per request: `can_use` of the agent's workspace (404 when invisible), hosted only (BYO 422 `files_not_supported` with `details.reason: "byo"`), frozen 423 `frozen` (owner, D-316(d): no access, like the terminal), `terminal.enabled` off 404 `terminal_disabled` (the setting in force, which defaults from `terminal.available`; read live, SET-5, never from the boot config: FIX-168). **Wake**: a request on a parked sandbox is a wake demand on the terminal's path (`terminal_wake_at`, `TERMINAL_WAKE_WINDOW`, the shared `TERMINAL_WAKES_PER_AGENT_HOUR` budget → 429 `wake_limited`) and answers 503 `sandbox_waking` with `retry-after: 2` at once (no long-poll); a sandbox that cannot wake (stopped, failed, unprovisioned) → 409 `sandbox_unavailable` with `details.state`. **Keep-awake**: each files request on a running sandbox stamps `sandboxes.files_active_until = now() + FILES_AWAKE_GRACE` (120 s; at most one stamp per sandbox per 30 s per API replica) and sets `files_awake_since` on the boot's first stamp; the keep-awake reason **`files`** holds while `files_active_until > now()` and fewer than `FILES_AWAKE_CAP` (4 h) have passed since `files_awake_since`; `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox`'s `keep_awake_reasons` gains `"files"`. Never a turn, a transcript event or a `last_engaged_at` stamp. **Limits**: rate class `files` (300 / min, burst 60; replaces `write`/`upload` for these routes; `general` and `org` still apply); at most `FILE_TRANSFERS_PER_USER` (4) concurrent content GETs/PUTs per person per API replica (429 `too_many_transfers`); an upload or download with no progress for `FILES_TRANSFER_IDLE` (60 s) is aborted (the PUT's temp file removed); these two routes are exempt from the API's global body-size and request timeouts and from response buffering.
- C-901 **`auf_` grants, the SFTP session pool and leg 1 (API → runner)** (`16 §W21`). The API is the SFTP client: it keeps at most one SFTP session per (person, agent) per replica, opened on demand, closed after `FILES_SESSION_IDLE` (60 s) without a request or at `FILES_SESSION_LIFETIME` (1 h, reopened transparently on the next request), and on any leg close. To open one it mints a **files stream grant** `auf_…` (single use, ≤ `FILES_GRANT_TTL` 30 s, hashed in `file_stream_grants`, bound to `(agent, sandbox, boot, person)`, never sent to a browser) and dials the runner's stream listener :4108 (`GET /v1/preview-stream`, `Authorization: Bearer auf_…`); the runner redeems it with `POST /v1/runner/preview-streams/open { kind: "files", grant }` → `{ sandbox_id, boot_id, window, idle_secs, max_lifetime_secs }` (403 `grant_invalid`, 409 `not_current`: stale boot, frozen agent, `terminal.enabled` off), checks it holds that boot's channel (404 `not_here`) and completes the handshake. The API's first message is the W20 first frame with `mode: "sftp"` and no `command`, `tty` or `env` (the runner refuses anything else on an `auf_` leg with 4502); `exec.opened` → the SFTP version-3 stream rides Binary `[0x02]` frames both ways, `[0x03]` (the server's stderr) is discarded by the API (byte count in metrics only). Runner cap `FILE_SESSIONS_PER_SANDBOX` (4) live `auf_` legs → 4409 (the API answers the request 429 `too_many_transfers`). An older runner refuses the unknown `kind` (4502 before 101) and a supervisor or image without the `sftp` exec mode answers `exec.refused` (4502): the request answers 422 `files_not_supported` with `details.reason: "runner"` or `"image"`. A W6 reconnect, park or restart closes the leg 4503: the in-flight request answers 503 `files_unavailable` (`retry-after: 2`) and the pool drops the session. `file_stream_grants`: `id, agent_id, sandbox_id, boot_id, granted_for (user), grant_hash, expires_at, consumed_at, created_at`, consumed by compare-and-set at redemption, pruned after 24 h; a table of its own so the `auf_` prefix can never open a PTY, an SSH exec or a port (D-239(f) pattern). Every request re-checks authority (C-900) before it touches the pool, so revocation is immediate for new requests; an in-flight transfer of a person who lost access ends at the next 10 s re-check (`ACCESS_RECHECK`).
- C-902 **Leg 2 and the supervisor's `sftp` exec** (`12 §SSH channels`, `16 §W20` leg 2): the `exec.*` frames of C-796 (`exec.open`, `exec.opened`/`exec.refused`, `exec.eof`, `exec.exit`, `pty.credit`, `pty.close`, data tags `0x02`/`0x03`) are introduced by P5-FILES-1 with the pipes-only engine and the `sftp` mode (the image's fixed `/usr/libexec/openssh/sftp-server`, no `tty`, agent uid/gid, cwd `/workspace`, the container environment minus every `AURORA_*` variable and the boot document, stdout `0x02`, stderr `0x03`, CLOEXEC ends, process-group kill on close); P4-SSH-2 adds the `shell` and `exec` modes and `tty` on the same engine. The supervisor caps concurrent execs at `EXECS_PER_SANDBOX` (12 = `SSH_CHANNELS_PER_SANDBOX` 8 + `FILE_SESSIONS_PER_SANDBOX` 4; per-kind caps are the runner's) → `exec.refused { limit }`. The sandbox images (`sandbox` and the e2e `sandbox-min`) carry the package that provides the fixed `sftp-server` path; `sshd` is never started.
- C-903 **Content safety headers** (SEC-126). `GET …/files/content` always answers `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<ASCII fallback>"; filename*=UTF-8''<RFC 5987-encoded name>` (the fallback replaces non-ASCII, `"`, `\`, `/` and control characters with `_`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`, and `Content-Length` from the `stat` (a file that grows while streaming is cut at that length; one that shrinks ends the stream with an error, never padded). No route serves agent bytes inline or with a type derived from the name or the content.
- C-904 **Files page** (`14 §Files`, P5-FILES-2): `?tab=files` in the agent page's **Computer** section (Computer · Terminal · **Files** · Previews · SSH), shown exactly where the Terminal page is; the status rail's Computer part gains "Files". Breadcrumb path bar and Go to path; toolbar Upload (Files / Folder), New folder, New file, Refresh, Show hidden (off by default; hides dot-names); `DataTable` of entries (rows collapse to cards at 390 px) with per-row Open, Download, Rename, Delete, Copy path; `.aurora` and other non-writable entries show a read-only `Badge` and no write actions; drag and drop of files and folders onto the list; an upload tray with progress, cancel and failure chips (the chat attachment chip states); name clashes → Replace / Keep both (`name (1).ext`, the next free) / Skip, with "Do this for all"; recursive delete via `ConfirmDialog` (`danger`) naming the item count from the listing ("and everything inside it" when not known); "Waking…" state on 503 `sandbox_waking` (retries with `retry-after`, up to the C-621 twelve attempts); freeze banner and no listing when frozen; errors of C-898..C-901 in words. Tokens and shared primitives only, both themes, no horizontal scroll at 390 px.
- C-905 **Viewer and editor** (`14 §Files`, P5-FILES-3): Open on a file whose `GET …/text` succeeds shows the **editor** (CodeMirror 6, lazy-loaded chunk, styles through `EditorView.cspNonce` with the page nonce — no CSP change; a small fixed set of language modes by extension; the document's line separator kept from `line_ending`); Save (button and Ctrl/Cmd+S) sends `PUT` with `If-Match`; a 409 `file_changed` opens a dialog **Reload** (discard mine) / **Overwrite** (`PUT` without `If-Match`) / **Copy my text** (to the clipboard, then stay); unsaved changes are marked in the title and leaving the file or page asks first. Keyboard: Tab indents; Esc, then Tab (within 1.5 s) leaves the editor (the terminal's WCAG 2.1.2 rule). A PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP file of ≤ 10 MiB (by the magic bytes of the fetched content, never the name) is previewed as a `data:` URL with that fixed MIME (`img-src` already allows `data:`); SVG and everything else get a "No preview" card with Download. New file: a name prompt, then an empty editor; the first Save uses `If-None-Match: *`.
- C-906 **Audit and logging**: best-effort audit rows `agent.files.download` (agent, workspace, path, bytes, `via`), `agent.files.write` (path, bytes, `created`), `agent.files.mkdir` (path), `agent.files.rename` (from, to), `agent.files.delete` (path, `deleted`); paths are truncated to 1,024 bytes in metadata; contents are never logged, stored, audited or request-log-rowed (byte totals in metrics only); listings are not audited. `file_stream_grants` is the session record.
- C-907 **Tests** (`18 §Files`): per SEC-125/SEC-126 "Proven by"; the kind e2e journey `scripts/e2e/files.sh` and a Playwright journey (upload a folder, edit and save, conflict, download with matching sha256, rename, recursive delete, `.aurora` read-only, symlink out of `/workspace` refused, frozen 423, parked wake).
- C-908 **Chat receipt lifetime and the working indicator** (phase 5, FIX-158, D-319; `13 §4.5`, `§5.1` step 11, `§5.2`, `20`). No HTTP route changes. (1) The seen-receipt (`reaction.add` `eyes`, or the placeholder) belongs to the message: it is removed only when the message's thread link gets the answer or failure line, when the turn is `aborted`, or when the turn ended with nothing posted and no follow-on work is pending (armed watch, pending approval or gateway hold of that turn, `joining` follow-up, queued/dispatched/running turn); otherwise it stays open and the next post to the same thread link clears every open receipt of that link. The channel-op sweep clears an open receipt after `RECEIPT_ORPHAN_SECS` (600 s) with no active turn and nothing pending. A `reaction.remove` is never claimed while its `reaction.add` is `pending`/`claimed`; an add still `pending` at clear time is dropped with no remove. (2) When an integration declares `native_status`, a turn going `running` on a chat thread enqueues `status.set` (`working:<turn_id>`) and the finish enqueues `status.clear` (`working-clear:<turn_id>`) before the answer post; none without the capability, none for web/API turns. (3) Mattermost: `status.set` runs one typing refresher per (integration, sender, channel, thread) calling `POST /users/{user_id}/typing` every `TYPING_REFRESH_SECS` (4 s) until `status.clear`, `TURN_CEILING_WARNING` or a 403/404; typing calls do not count toward `writes_per_space_per_minute`; a connector restart drops refreshers. Verified on Mattermost 11.7.15: 200 `{"status":"OK"}` for the bot in a channel and in a thread (`parent_id`), a `typing` websocket event reaches members, 403 for a channel the sender is not in, a bogus channel or a token that is not the sender's.

### Phase 5 — P5-WEBHOOK (outgoing webhooks, D-320, SEC-127)

- C-909 **Send-mode secret link: manifest, package, storage, connect and edit** (P5-WEBHOOK-1/2; extends C-843).
  - **Manifest.** `secret_link: { credential, mode?: "fetch" | "send" (default "fetch"), expect_prefix?, presets?: [file] }`.
    - In `send` mode the app has exactly one tool. It is `access: write`, method `POST` or `PUT`, host `*.link.aurora.internal`, path `/`.
    - `expect_prefix` is refused in `send` mode, and `presets` is refused in `fetch` mode.
    - The only declared credential is the URL. The core adds `secret_values` and `signing_secret` to the sealed credential JSON, and `credential_keys` lists them by name.
    - Linter codes: `secret_link_mode`, `secret_link_send_tool`, `secret_link_send_expect_prefix`, `secret_link_presets_mode`, `webhook_preset_invalid` (a preset file that fails `WebhookConfig` validation without secret values).
  - **Package `webhook`** (built-in): app **Outgoing webhook**. It has one `fields` method (`name`: text, required, ≤ 60, `store: metadata`; `url`: secret, required), one tool `send`, a setup guide and presets:
    - `slack` (`{"text": {{text}}}`), `mattermost`, `google-chat`;
    - `discord` (`{"content": {{content}}}`);
    - `teams-workflow` (an Adaptive Card with `title` and `text` slots);
    - `ntfy-json` (`topic` as a secret value; `title`, `message`, `priority`, `tags`);
    - `home-assistant` and `generic-json` (`{{payload}}`, a `json` slot);
    - `ifttt` (`value1..3`).

    A preset file is `{ id, name, suggest_for_hosts?: [host], secret_value_names: [string], config: WebhookConfig }`. It holds no secret values; the names say which ones the person must enter.
  - **Storage.** Migration `20261201000243` adds `app_connections.config jsonb null`, `CHECK (config IS NULL OR jsonb_typeof(config) = 'object')`. It is a core-owned configuration, non-null only for a send-mode connection. The gateway role may `SELECT` it. The API enforces ≤ `WEBHOOK_CONFIG_MAX` (32 KiB).
  - **`WebhookConfig`** = `{ version: 1, method: "POST"|"PUT", content_type: "application/json"|"application/x-www-form-urlencoded"|"text/plain", template: string, slots: [Slot], headers: [{ name, value }], description: string, signing: bool, rate: { per_minute: int|null, per_day: int|null }, preset: string|null }`. Then the parts:
    - **`Slot`** = `{ name: ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,39}$, type: "string"|"integer"|"number"|"boolean"|"string_list"|"enum"|"json", required: bool, description: string (≤ 300), max_length?: 1..65,536 (`string`, `string_list` items), max_items?: 1..100 (`string_list`), enum?: [string] (1..50, `enum` only), schema?: object (`json` only; the subset `type`, `properties`, `required`, `items`, `enum`, `maxLength`, `minimum`, `maximum`, `additionalProperties`), example?: JSON value }`. There are ≤ `WEBHOOK_SLOTS_MAX` (20) slots and ≤ 4 secret values.
    - **Template grammar.** `{{name}}` is a slot and `{{secret:name}}` is a secret value. It is parsed once when saved.
      - **JSON:** the template must be valid JSON when every placeholder outside a string literal is replaced by a JSON value.
        - A placeholder outside a string is the slot's JSON value.
        - Inside a string literal, it inserts a scalar slot (or a secret) escaped as string content.
        - An optional slot that is left out removes its object member or array element when it is the whole value, and is empty inside a string.
        - A slot at the root must be required.
      - **Form:** `key=value` pairs separated by `&`. Keys are constant and values may contain placeholders, which are percent-encoded. A `string_list` repeats the key, and a left-out optional pair is dropped.
      - **Text:** values are inserted as UTF-8, with left-out optionals empty.
      - `json` slots are refused for form and text.
    - **Headers.** ≤ 4 headers. A value is a constant or a constant with `{{secret:name}}`; slot placeholders are refused. Names are tokens. The denylist is `Host`, `Content-Length`, `Content-Type`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `Connection`, `Cookie`, `Forwarded`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `X-Aurora-*` and the hop-by-hop headers.
    - **Every declared slot and secret value must be used.** Validation codes go in `webhook_config_invalid.fields[{ path, code }]`: `template_json`, `template_syntax`, `placeholder_unknown`, `slot_unused`, `secret_unused`, `slot_name`, `slot_duplicate`, `slot_type_content_type`, `slot_limits`, `optional_slot_at_root`, `schema_unsupported`, `header_name_denied`, `header_placeholder`, `rate_above_ceiling`, `too_large`.
    - **Size limits:** template ≤ `WEBHOOK_TEMPLATE_MAX` (16 KiB), description ≤ 1,000, secret value ≤ 4 KiB.
  - **Connect.** `POST /v1/apps/webhook/connect` takes `{ …C-843 fields (method_id, fields: { name, url }), webhook: WebhookConfig + { secret_values: { name: value }, signing_secret?: { mode: "generate" | "provided", value? } } }`.
    - The URL is validated as in C-843 (`https`, DNS host, ≤ 2,048).
    - The host is resolved and checked by the destination guard and the org egress policy. **Nothing is sent.**
    - It answers `{ kind: "connected", connection, signing_secret?: string }`. The signing secret is present only when it was generated, and only in this response.
    - Refusals are 422: `link_not_https`, `link_invalid`, `link_destination_refused`, `webhook_config_invalid`. With the org switch off it is 403 `outgoing_webhooks_disabled`.
    - A reconnect (`reconnect_connection_id`) replaces the URL and keeps the configuration unless `webhook` is given.
  - **Edit.** These need the right to manage the connection:
    - `GET /v1/connections/{id}/webhook` → `WebhookConfig + { host, secret_value_names: [string], signing: bool }`. It never returns a secret.
    - `PUT /v1/connections/{id}/webhook` replaces the configuration.
      - `secret_values: { name: value | null }`: a value sets, `null` deletes and an absent name is kept.
      - `signing_secret: { mode: "keep" | "generate" | "provided" | "off", value? }`.
      - The response is as for GET, plus `signing_secret` when one was generated.
      - A change of secrets bumps `credentials_version`, and any change sends the gateway's connection-change signal.
  - **Preview** (no network). `POST /v1/apps/webhook/webhook-preview` `{ config: WebhookConfig (+ secret_value_names), values }` → `{ method, content_type, headers: [name], body }`, with secrets as `«secret:name»`. Or 422 `webhook_config_invalid` / `invalid_slot`. It needs a session.
  - **Presets.** `GET /v1/apps/{app}/webhook-presets` → `[preset]` from the installed package version.
  - **Send test.** `POST /v1/connections/{id}/webhook/test` `{ values, dry_run?: bool }`.
    - It answers `{ status, content_type, body (≤ 4 KiB, echo-checked), truncated: bool, delivery_id }`, or with `dry_run` the preview of the stored configuration.
    - The request is sent by the API through the same `aurora-webhook` renderer, destination guard, egress policy, redirect rule and per-connection rate limit as the gateway (C-910). The person, not an agent, is the actor.
    - Best-effort audit `webhook.test_sent` `{ connection_id, status, delivery_id }`.
- C-910 **Gateway send path** (P5-WEBHOOK-2; SEC-127).
  - **Admission:** grant, policy verdict, Read only and the request log as C-843 (an `ask` verdict's approval hold comes only after the checks below and the slot validation), then:
    - only the configured method (405 otherwise);
    - the agent's `Content-Type` must be `application/json` with an object body ≤ `WEBHOOK_VALUES_MAX` (64 KiB): 415 `webhook_content_type`, 413 `values_too_large`;
    - the org switch must be on: 403 `outgoing_webhooks_disabled`.

    Validation failures are 422 `invalid_slot { slot, rule }`, with `rule` ∈ `missing`, `unknown`, `type`, `max_length`, `max_items`, `enum`, `schema`. Validation runs **before** any approval is created, so a person is never asked about an invalid send.
  - **Dry run:** the header `X-Aurora-Dry-Run: 1` validates, renders and answers 200 `{ dry_run: true, method, content_type, headers: [name], body }`, with secrets masked. A `deny` verdict refuses it. An `ask` verdict does not hold it, because nothing leaves. It is not rate-counted, and its request-log row is `dry_run: true`.
  - **Rate limit:** one all-or-nothing take of both fixed windows (minute, day), keyed by the connection on the shared counters (D-119). It is taken after admission and approval, just before the upstream request. A refusal is 429 `webhook_rate_limited` with `retry_after_secs`. The effective limit is C-912's.
  - **Upstream request:**
    - to the stored URL exactly, with the configured method and the rendered body ≤ `WEBHOOK_BODY_MAX` (256 KiB, 413 `rendered_too_large`);
    - headers: `Content-Type` (configured), `User-Agent: Aurora-Webhook/1`, `X-Aurora-Delivery: <uuid v7>`, the configured headers, and `X-Aurora-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(signing_secret, "<t>.<raw body>")>` when signing is on. Nothing from the agent's request headers.
    - The time-out is `WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT` (15 s). There is no retry and no cache.
  - **Redirects:** ≤ 3, `https`, destination guard and egress policy per hop. `307`/`308` resend the method and body (and the signature, recomputed with a new `t`). `301`/`302`/`303` continue as a body-less `GET`. `Location` is never returned.
  - **Response to the agent:**
    - the receiver's status, except that 404/410 become 404 `link_not_found` and `3xx` is consumed;
    - `Content-Type` and `Retry-After` only;
    - the body capped at `WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_MAX` (64 KiB) with `X-Aurora-Truncated: 1`;
    - `X-Aurora-Delivery`;
    - the echo check with the needles of SEC-127, giving 502 `upstream_echoed_credential`.
  - **Gateway refusals** have the `06 §Errors` body and `X-Aurora-Error: <code>`:
    - 403 `link_destination_refused`;
    - 502 `link_unreachable { may_have_been_delivered: false }`, when nothing was sent;
    - 504 `webhook_timeout { may_have_been_delivered: true }`.
  - **Logs and spans:** connection id, host, method, status, slot **names**, rendered bytes, `delivery_id`, `dry_run`. Never the path, query, slot values, body, headers' values or secrets.
  - **Approval decoder `slots`** (`07`, `09 §Decoders`): the summary is `{ title: "Send to <name>", fields: [host, method, <slot>: <value>…] }`. Each value is clamped to 500 characters with the full length shown, and `json` values are pretty-printed and clamped the same way. It shows neither fixed template parts, headers nor secrets. The package's `send` tool defaults to **Ask** (D-320(d)).
- C-911 **`aurora-agent` exposure** (P5-WEBHOOK-3; amends C-652, C-653, `24 §5.4`, `§10a`).
  - **Render inputs:** `accounts[]` of a send-mode app gain `webhook: { name, description, method, slots }`, never the template, headers, URL or secrets.
  - **`api.json` of app `webhook`.** `accounts[].base_url` = `https://<connection_id>.link.aurora.internal`. One operation per account:
    - `id` = `send_` + the slug of the name (`[a-z0-9_]`, ≤ 40), with `_2`, `_3`… for duplicates in account-letter order;
    - `method`, `path: "/"`, `host: "<connection_id>.link.aurora.internal"`;
    - `summary` = the name plus the first line of the description (≤ 200), `tags: ["webhook"]`;
    - `params: [{ name: "X-Aurora-Dry-Run", in: "header", type: "string", enum: ["1"], description: "Check and show the body without sending" }]`;
    - `body: { content_type: "application/json", fields }`, with one field per slot: `string`/`enum` → `string` (+`enum`), `integer`, `number`, `boolean`, `string_list` → `array`, `json` → **`json`** (new type, rendered operations only; passed with `name:=<json>`). The limits go into the field's `description`.
    - `tool: "send"`, `access: { <its letter>: … }`;
    - two new keys: **`account: <letter>`** and **`notes`** (the person's description, ≤ 1,000);
    - `source: "connection"`.

    Every other operation has `account: null`, `notes: null`.
  - **`tools.md`:** each webhook is listed with its slots and a `curl` example to its `base_url` (the fallback without `agent_cli`).
  - **`aurora-agent`:**
    - `call` on an operation with `account` uses that account. `--account` naming another is refused (exit 2), and several accounts are not an error.
    - `describe` prints the notes, the slots and both example lines (send, and dry run).
    - `search` covers webhook names, descriptions and slot names.
    - A gateway refusal (`X-Aurora-Error`) exits 3, and a receiver's 4xx/5xx exits 1.
  - **No `webhooks.json`.** The home sync's `links` (C-843) keeps fetch-mode connections only.
- C-912 **Limits and switches** (P5-WEBHOOK-2).
  - **Instance settings section `outgoing_webhooks`** (movable keys, `15 §Instance settings`; rendered by SET-5's generic section form):
    - `outgoing_webhooks.per_minute_max`: default 30, 1–600;
    - `outgoing_webhooks.per_day_max`: default 500, 1–100,000.

    Helm `config.outgoing_webhooks.*` locks them as for any movable key.
  - **Org settings**, stored and edited like `calendar_invitations.enabled` (C-844):
    - `outgoing_webhooks.enabled` (default `true`);
    - `outgoing_webhooks.per_minute_max` and `.per_day_max` (null = the instance's; above it → 422 `above_instance_ceiling`).
  - **Connection:** `config.rate.per_minute` / `.per_day` (null = the ceiling; above the current ceiling → `rate_above_ceiling`).
  - **Effective limit at each send** = the lowest set value of the instance, the org and the connection, so a lowered ceiling applies at once to existing connections. `GET /v1/connections/{id}/webhook` returns `effective_rate: { per_minute, per_day, source: "instance"|"org"|"connection" }`.
  - **Constants** in `crates/aurora-core/src/limits.rs`: `WEBHOOK_CONFIG_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_TEMPLATE_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_SLOTS_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_VALUES_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_BODY_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_RESPONSE_MAX`, `WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT`.
- C-913 **Dashboard** (P5-WEBHOOK-4; `14`).
  - **Connect dialog for app `webhook`, three steps:**
    1. Name and URL.
    2. "What the receiver expects":
       - a preset picker, which proposes a preset whose `suggest_for_hosts` contains the URL's host;
       - **Start from an example**: paste JSON, then click a scalar, a string array or an object to make it a slot (name from the key, type inferred, editable description, required, limits);
       - a raw template and slot editor for form, text, or anything the picker cannot express;
       - **Preview**, through the preview route, with sample values from the slots' `example`.
    3. Extras (folded): description for the agent, secret values, headers, signing (generate shows the secret once with copy; or paste), method, rate.
  - **Connection card:** name, host, Read only, Edit (the same steps without the URL), **Send test** (values form → result: status, body, delivery id; or a dry-run preview), and the effective limit with its source.
  - **Admin › Settings** `outgoing_webhooks` section, and the org settings switch and limits.
  - Shared primitives and page patterns only, both themes, 390 px. The code editor is the shared one used for JSON elsewhere, added to the shared set if missing. `webhook_config_invalid.fields[].path` maps to the field in the editor.
### Phase 5 — P5-UX and P5-IA (agents, accounts and access; one structure for every level; D-321..D-353, SEC-128..SEC-132)

Rule: `14 §Information architecture` › "One structure for every level" and `14 §Agents, accounts and access` (normative for every screen, name and place), with `04`, `05`, `07`, `09`, `03 §Planned changes`, `15 §Instance settings` and `17`. Errors use the standard body.

Numbers: C-914..C-949, C-955..C-959, C-960..C-975, C-980..C-990. Spare: C-928, C-929, C-950..C-954, C-976..C-979, C-991..C-999. **Not built** (numbers kept, no content): C-918, C-921, C-935. This section replaces the three sections drafted earlier on the branch (first run, app setup, P5-IA) after the design review (D-343).

Briefs, in four fixed waves (D-352, order inside the waves per D-353; "→" means "then"):
- Wave 0: FIX-159 C-930 · P5-UX-2 C-931, C-946 (API), C-955..C-958, C-981 (server), C-988 · P5-IA-1 C-926 (all but the Internet access labels), C-960..C-962, C-971, C-975, C-987.
- Wave 1: P5-UX-1 C-915, C-916, C-981 (component), then P5-IA-2 C-926 (Internet access labels), C-963..C-965, C-986 (Internet access sentences), C-989 and P5-UX-4 C-917, C-925, C-938, C-948, C-980 · P5-UX-13 C-939..C-942 (packages and API), C-949.
- Wave 2: P5-UX-3 C-914, C-942 (the Copy blocks) → P5-UX-9 C-932, C-933, C-946 (forms) → P5-IA-3 C-966, C-967 (Administration, organization) · P5-UX-3 → P5-UX-14 C-943..C-945, C-985 → P5-UX-16 C-982, C-983 · P5-IA-4 C-967 (workspace), C-968..C-970 · P5-IA-5 C-972..C-974.
- Wave 3: P5-UX-7 C-927, C-986 (agent sentences) → P5-UX-5 C-919, C-920 · P5-UX-6 C-922..C-924 · P5-UX-10 C-934 · P5-UX-17 C-959, C-984 → P5-UX-11 C-936, C-937, C-947, C-990.

#### Structure

- C-960 **Routes, tabs and redirects.** The route of each topic and tab is the table of `14 §Information architecture`. Every old route redirects to its new place, query and anchor preserved (Next.js `redirects` for static paths, a client redirect for `?tab=` mappings):
  - **Administration:**
    - `/admin/setup` → `/admin`
    - `/admin/users` → `/admin/people?tab=users`
    - `/admin/orgs` → `/admin/people?tab=organizations`; `/admin/orgs/<id>` → `/admin/people/organizations/<id>`
    - `/admin/identity` → `/admin/people?tab=sign-in`
    - `/admin/placement` → `/admin/people?tab=new-users`
    - `/admin/plugins` → `/admin/apps?tab=apps`; `/admin/plugins/<id>` → `/admin/apps/packages/<id>`
    - `/admin/addon-sources`, `/admin/trusted-keys`, `/admin/host-claims` → `/admin/apps?tab=advanced` (anchors `#package-sources`, `#publishers`, `#host-conflicts`)
    - `/admin/instances` → `/admin/apps?tab=self-hosted`
    - `/admin/oauth-clients` → `/admin/apps?tab=oauth-clients`
    - `/admin/connectors` → `/admin/chat-platforms`
    - `/admin/secret-stores` → `/admin/platform?tab=secret-stores`; `/admin/runners` → `/admin/platform?tab=runners`
    - `/admin/storage` → `/admin/data?tab=storage`; `/admin/audit` → `/admin/data?tab=audit`
    - `/admin/settings/<section>` → its topic page per C-971; `/admin/settings` stays (All settings).
  - **Organization:**
    - `/o/<org>/members` → `/o/<org>/people?tab=members`; `/o/<org>/groups` → `…?tab=groups`; `/o/<org>/groups/<id>` → `/o/<org>/people/groups/<id>`; `/o/<org>/settings/directory` → `…?tab=directory-sync`
    - `/o/<org>/credentials` (no tab, `secrets`, `connections`) → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=accounts`; `?tab=llm` → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=accounts&kind=ai-key`; `?tab=oauth` → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=oauth-clients`
    - `/o/<org>/apps` → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=apps`
    - `/o/<org>/governance?tab=model|compliance` → `/o/<org>/ai-models`; `?tab=egress` → `/o/<org>/internet-access`; `?tab=apps` → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=apps`; `?tab=byo` → `/o/<org>/agents?tab=byo`
    - `/o/<org>/policy` → `/o/<org>/internet-access`
    - `/o/<org>/skills` → `/o/<org>/agents?tab=skills`; `/o/<org>/skills/libraries` → `/o/<org>/agents/skills/libraries`; `/o/<org>/settings/previews` → `/o/<org>/agents?tab=previews`
    - `/o/<org>/channels` → `/o/<org>/chat-platforms`; `/o/<org>/instances` → `/o/<org>/apps?tab=self-hosted`
    - `/o/<org>/api-keys` → `/o/<org>/settings?tab=api-keys`; `/o/<org>/audit` → `/o/<org>/data?tab=audit`; `/o/<org>/storage` → `/o/<org>/data?tab=storage`
  - **Workspace:**
    - `…/credentials` (no tab, `secrets`, `connections`) → `…/apps?tab=accounts`; `?tab=llm` → `…/apps?tab=accounts&kind=ai-key`; `?tab=apps` and `?tab=oauth` → `…/apps?tab=apps`; `?tab=proposals` → `…/apps?tab=accounts#requests`
    - `…/policy` → `…/internet-access`
    - `…/settings?tab=access` → `…/people`
  - Query parameters the new pages accept: `connect=`, `reconnect=` (C-842), `kind=` on Accounts, `add=1` on `…/people`, `package=` and `client_key=` on the OAuth clients tabs, `package=` on the Self-hosted apps tabs, `focus=` on Approvals (C-983).
  - Every `href` in the web app comes from one helper module (`web/src/lib/routes.ts`), and a test fails on a hard-coded old path. The API and the gateway share one table of the same paths, in `crates/aurora-core`, for the links they emit (C-959, C-981).
- C-961 **Visibility per role** (`14` "Who sees what"). One function `visibleTopics(level, roles)` drives the navigation, the tour (C-975) and the Welcome links. On a page a role sees read-only, the page's main action is replaced by the `WhoCan` sentence (C-981) in the same place, other controls the viewer cannot use are not rendered, and values read as text (`14` "What a person sees of something they cannot do"). Pages a role cannot open answer the C-915 refusal.
- C-962 **Administration entry, level badge, your account.**
  - The sidebar footer and the account menu carry **Administration** for instance admins. The org switcher lists organizations only.
  - `PageHeader` takes `level: { kind: "instance"|"org"|"workspace", label }` and renders the badge; every page passes it.
  - The account menu item and the `/account` page title read **Your account**.
- C-987 **"Pages have new names" notice.**
  - `user_onboarding.notices_seen text[]` (at most `ONBOARDING_NOTICES_MAX` = 50 keys). `GET /v1/me/onboarding` returns it; `PATCH /v1/me/onboarding` accepts `{ notice_seen: "<key>" }` and appends it.
  - Notice key `renames-2026-11`. People whose account was created after the release never see it (the migration marks it seen for accounts created later).
  - The notice lists old name → new name for the pages the viewer can see (from one table next to `routes.ts`). It is dismissible, and replayable from the account menu (**What moved**).
  - (D-407, P5-REFINE-1) **The web no longer shows the renames notice, and "What moved" is gone; the field and `PATCH { notice_seen }` stay** for other notices. A new row is no longer seeded with `renames-2026-11` (a new person starts with `notices_seen = []`); rows that already carry the key keep it.
- C-926 **Labels** (strings only).
  - On the Internet access pages, applied by P5-IA-2 when it rebuilds them (P5-IA-1 hosts the old editor with its old labels, inside the release gate):
    - "No default (defer)" → **Let the organization decide** (workspace) / **None: only rules decide** (organization; there is no instance rule scope).
    - "Uncredentialed requests" → **Requests without an account**.
    - "Browser tunnels (open lane)" → **Browser connections (not inspected)**.
    - Simulate → **Check what would happen**, with Speaker and Lane under **More**.
    - Empty rules: "Until you add one, requests follow the limits and defaults below."
  - On every page P5-IA-1 moves:
    - Approve / Deny → **Allow once** / **Block** (C-983 rebuilds the card later).
    - Disable / Enable → **Turn off** / **Turn on**.
    - Page and tab names per the vocabulary table.
- C-975 **Tour.** One stop per topic the viewer sees at workspace level (`visibleTopics`), with the new names. The Agents stop says that an agent gets apps on its Access tab.
- C-963 **Scope ladder** (`ScopeLadder` in the shared primitives): `above: { label, value_display, source: "instance"|"org", who_can: WhoCan }[]`, `here: ReactNode` with an optional `minimumNote`, a `collapsed` mode for workspaces (**Tighten…**), and `below?: { columns, rows: { label, values, link }[] }`.
- C-964 **What the levels below tightened** (Internet access only).
  - Administration → organizations: the existing `GET /v1/admin/settings/governance/orgs` (SET-2).
  - Organization → workspaces: `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/workspace-overrides` (**S/K**, org owner/admin) → `Page<{ workspace: { id, name, personal_of? }, uncredentialed: "inherit"|"block", open_lane: "inherit"|"allowlist_only"|"disabled", default_rule: "none"|"allow"|"block", rule_count }>`; only workspaces that differ from the organization unless `?all=true`.
- C-1020 **A member reads the workspace's published Internet access** (FIX-164, D-365; amends I-27 for this one read). `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/internet-access` (**S/K**, `can_use`: any member of the workspace; a non-member and a member of another workspace get the 404 of every workspace route) → `{ number, default_action, uncredentialed_default, uncredentialed_allowlist, open_lane_posture, rules: PolicyRule[] }`: the **published** generation's defaults and rules (same shapes as `GET …/policy`'s `published`), and nothing else: no draft, `draft_revision`, `published_by`, generation history, warnings, other workspaces' overrides. GET only. `GET …/policy`, `…/generations`, `…/warnings`, `…/simulate` and every write stay `can_own`. The workspace Internet access page (C-965) renders a member's read-only view from it, and the agent's summary (C-986) uses it for every viewer.
- C-1021 **A connector update that waits for approval** (FIX-167, D-369, SEC-134; amends C-31 and C-455). `ConnectorAdmin` (`GET /v1/admin/connectors`, `…/{id}`, the approve response) gains `update_pending: bool` (the announced manifest differs from the approved baseline and waits), `pending_version: string | null` (the announced version while `update_pending`, else `null`), and `widening` is filled whenever an update is pending (it was filled for `pending_approval` only). A registration whose manifest widens an approved baseline answers `active` (wire `register` and the stored status); a first registration still answers `pending_approval`. While an update waits, the core grants, routes and assigns only what the approved manifest declares (`13 §3`). `POST /v1/admin/connectors/{id}/approve` approves the pending update as it approves a first manifest (409 `stale_manifest`). Audit `connector.update_pending` once per pending digest.
- C-1022 **`GET /v1/admin/attention`** (FIX-167 Part B, D-370; **S**, instance admins; 403 otherwise). `{ items: [{ kind, subject_id, title, detail, since, href, decision }] }`, unpaginated, computed on read from existing tables, no cache. `kind` ∈ `connector_approval`, `package_approval`, `addon_key_changed`, `host_claim_conflict` (`decision: true`) and `connector_down`, `ownerless_org` (`decision: false`). `subject_id` is the connector id, the package row or addon source id, or the organization slug. `title` and `detail` are fixed sentences filled with names and versions only (`14 §Needs your attention`); `since` is when the item first appeared (the row's last change for an item that predates `admin_attention_notices`); `href` is a dashboard path (`/admin/chat-platforms`, `/admin/apps/packages/{id}`, `/admin/apps?tab=advanced#<source id>` or `#host-conflicts`, `/admin/people?tab=organizations`). Order: decisions first, oldest first, then the rest. The `GET /v1/admin/overview` `ownerless_orgs` field and the FIX-050 banner are unchanged. **Amended by C-1080** (P5-REFINE-8): `?state=open|done`, the item's added fields, `count`, and the request kinds of the Administration level; the six kinds and their fields are unchanged.
- C-1023 **Admin attention notices and push** (FIX-167 Part B, D-370). Migration `20261201000253_admin_attention.sql`: table `admin_attention_notices` and `users.admin_attention_email_enabled` (`03`). A decision item **first appearing** writes one row per active instance admin in the transaction of the state change that creates it (connector `pending_approval` or a new widened digest; a package version reaching `pending_approval`, `host_claim_conflict` when its reason is `claim_conflict`; an addon source reaching `signing_key_changed`); a replayed registration or ingest finds the row and writes nothing. A sweeper (every 10 s) claims `mailed_at` and sends one e-mail ("Aurora needs your decision: <title>", the detail, a link to `/admin`); the Web Push loop claims `pushed_at` and sends one push per opted-in device: payload `{ "v": 1, "type": "admin_attention", "url": "/admin" }`, plus `title` (the item's title, `NAME`-capped) only on a device with `show_details`, `Urgency: normal`, `TTL` = `PUSH_TTL_MAX`, `Topic` = `topic(subscription, kind\nsubject\nversion)`. Notices older than 1 h are never pushed; rows older than 30 days are pruned. Items that were already pending when this shipped and the non-decision kinds are never e-mailed or pushed. The service worker (C-835) shows "Aurora — something in Administration needs your decision" / "Open Administration to review it." (with `title`: "Aurora — <title>"), `tag` `aurora-admin`, and may open exactly `/admin` besides the `^/o/…` paths.
- C-1024 **Admin attention e-mail switch** (FIX-167 Part B, D-370; amends C-195's `/v1/me`). `Me.admin_attention_email: bool` (default `true`) and `PatchMeRequest.admin_attention_email?: bool`. It is stored for any user and used only for an instance admin; the dashboard shows it to instance admins only, under Account › Notifications. Push follows each device's own opt-in (C-832) and ignores this switch.
- C-965 **Internet access pages.**
  - **Workspace:** the summary (C-986); **Common changes** (C-989); the rules people wrote, with **Add rule**; the line "What agents may do in apps is set for each agent, on its Access tab." linking to Agents; the limits from above as ladder rows with **Tighten…** holding the workspace's four controls (default rule, requests without an account, browser connections, allow-list); **Check what would happen** (C-930).
  - **Organization:** the same order, with the organization's limits open (posture, allow-list, browser connections), the Administration limits above, and the workspaces table below (C-964).
  - **Administration:** the `governance` section's egress and browser-connection keys (C-971) and the organizations table.
  - Members see the pages read-only (C-961).
- C-989 **One way to save Internet access, and Common changes.**
  - (P5-REFINE-2, web only, no API change) `ApplyBar` is retired for the shared `SaveBar`: "n changes not applied yet" is "n unsaved changes", **Apply changes** is **Save**, and "Saved" shows for two seconds. Everything below about what the button does is unchanged.
  - The page has one pending-changes bar: **Apply changes** and **Discard**. There is no "Save draft" button; edits to rules and workspace defaults are saved to the policy draft as they are made.
  - Workspace: Apply publishes the draft (`07 §Authoring`, routes unchanged).
  - Organization: Apply sends the governance document (`PUT /v1/orgs/{org}/governance`) when it changed, then publishes the policy draft. If the second step fails, the bar says which part was applied and keeps the rest pending.
  - **Common changes** are two buttons that add an ordinary rule to the draft and show it for confirmation: **Ask before changes on a website** (methods `POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE`, a host, ask; the words of the access choice, for a site where the agent has no account) and **Block a website**.
- C-966 **AI models pages.**
  - **Administration:** Endpoints (the `llm` section) and Rules (the `governance` AI-key rules), with each organization's effective values.
  - **Organization:** one page with the model rules (was Governance › Model access) under the Administration limits, and a notice "<n> AI keys no longer follow these rules" linking to Accounts filtered to them (was Compliance).
  - **Workspace:** none. AI keys are accounts (C-917).
- C-967 **Agents pages.**
  - **Administration:** Defaults (`agents`, `approvals`), BYO (the `governance` BYO and agent-token keys), Computers (`terminal`, `previews`), Skills (`skills`).
  - **Organization:** BYO (was Governance › BYO agents) under the Administration limits, Skills (and libraries), Previews.
  - **Workspace:** the agents list; the workspace BYO setting is a field of Settings › General, shown to owners when there is a choice (D-442; it was a ladder row in the Agents list header after P5-IA-4).
- C-968 **People pages.**
  - **Administration:** Users, Organizations (and detail), Sign-in (`sign_in`, `scim`, and the role-mapping view that was Identity), New users (placement).
  - **Organization:** Members, Groups, Directory sync.
  - **Workspace:** People (was Settings › Access), with Add people (C-919).
- C-969 **Data pages.**
  - **Administration:** Retention (`retention`), Storage, Audit log.
  - **Organization:** Retention as one form (conversation days, attachment days and quota) under the Administration maximums; Storage; Audit log.
- C-970 **Platform, Chat platforms, Settings.**
  - **Administration › Platform:** Runners, Secret stores (one page), Email (`email`), Rate limits (`limits`).
  - **Chat platforms:** Administration (was Connectors) and organization (was Channels); content unchanged.
  - **Organization › Settings:** General, API keys, Danger zone. **Workspace › Settings:** General, API keys, Audit, Danger zone.
- C-971 **Settings sections in topic pages.** `SettingsSectionFields { section, keys? }` renders a section, or a subset of its keys, inside any page with the section's draft, preview, Helm locks and save. Saving a subset sends the section's whole document with untouched keys unchanged. `/admin/settings` (All settings) lists every section under its topic, with search over field labels and help (C-972), Export and Import.
- C-972 **Field metadata.** `GET /v1/admin/settings/{section}` items gain `fields: { key, label, help, kind: "bool"|"int"|"float"|"duration"|"enum"|"list"|"text"|"secret"|"keyed_list", choices?: { value, label, description }[], default_display, unit?, doc?: { title, path } }[]` (one per setting, in the setting order; `unit` is the unit the control edits in, and for a `duration` the value stays in seconds and `unit` is `seconds`, `minutes`, `hours` or `days`), from one registry next to the section registry in `crates/aurora-config`. `GET /v1/admin/settings` (C-771) carries the same `fields` on each section summary, for All settings' search. A test fails if a movable key (`deploy/helm/aurora/movable-keys.txt`) has no metadata. The dashboard renders from it; `labelOf(key)` is removed.
- C-973 **Read-only current values.** For a section whose keys are not editable yet, `GET /v1/admin/settings/{section}` answers `editable: false` and `current: { key, value_display, source: "helm"|"default" }[]` from the running configuration; secret keys read only "set" or "not set". The page shows them read-only with the operator doc, never an empty form.
- C-974 **UI text rules.** A web test scans the rendered strings of `web/src/components` and `web/src/app` and fails on: `§` followed by a digit; the substring `docs/`; a raw enum id shown as a label (a list of known ids); `[]` shown as a value; a key-derived label; and the retired words of the vocabulary table used as page names or labels ("Governance", "Policy", "Credentials", "Simulate", "grant", "secret", "connection" as a noun for an account). The button labels "Disable" and "Enable" fail too. For the vocabulary check, the test keeps a list of the files that still fail when P5-IA-5 merges and that a brief of wave 2 or 3 rewrites; P5-IA-5 fixes every other file. The list can only shrink: the test also fails when a listed file no longer contains a retired word, so the brief that cleans a file removes its entry. The list is empty at the end of wave 3.

#### Who can fix this

- C-981 **`WhoCan`.**
  - Shape: `WhoCan = { action: string, role: "workspace_owner"|"workspace_approver"|"org_admin"|"org_owner"|"instance_admin", people: { name }[] (at most 3 display names; always empty for `instance_admin`), path?: string (a C-960 path, present only when `you_can`), you_can: bool }`.
  - The server puts it in: `403 forbidden` (`details.who_can`, from the shared role helpers, so every refused route carries it); blocked connect methods (C-931, C-955); `above_account_limit` (C-980); the organization's accounts (`managed_by`, C-917); guide link fallbacks (C-940).
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}` and `GET /v1/orgs/{org}` gain `contacts: { owners: { name }[], admins?: { name }[] }` (at most 3 each, readable by every member of that level), for places where nothing was refused (empty states, ladder rows).
  - The dashboard renders all of them with one component, `WhoCan`: the sentence ("Ask Ada Lovelace or Alan Turing (organization owners and admins).", "An Aurora administrator can set this up."; the role phrases are the fixed ones of `14 §Who can fix this`) or, when `you_can`, the action or link.
- C-915 **Refusals in words.** Every page gate returns an `ApiError`-shaped value with `who_can`, never a bare object. `errorMessage` maps any value with a string `code` through `ERROR_MESSAGES`. "Could not reach the server" is only for a missing response, an abort or a non-JSON 5xx. A refused page reads "Only <role phrase> can open this page." with `WhoCan`.
- C-916 **Empty states and first-run fixes.** `EmptyState` takes `ask` only together with `canAct: boolean` and renders it (through `WhoCan`) only when `!canAct`; a viewer who can act sees the action. The first-run fixes are the list in `14 §Agents, accounts and access` §9.

#### Accounts and access

- C-917 **Accounts API.** `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/accounts?agent_id=&kind=&app_id=` (**S/K**, `can_use`) and `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/accounts?kind=&app_id=` (**S/K**, org owner/admin) → `Page<Account>`:
  - (P5-REFINE-20 batch 6) **A list never calls an endpoint.** Both lists read stored state only: a key's origin and health are the stored ones (a check that is due goes to the background re-check, C-1065), and `default_model` / the agent's `model` come from the cached key listings (`readiness_cached`); a cache miss answers without it and warms the cache in the background (one read per key). A workspace with several keys on an endpoint that never answers lists in well under a second (IT `refine20_listing_accounts_never_waits…`).
  - `Account = { ref: { type: "connection"|"secret", id }, kind: "app"|"website"|"ai_key", name, app?: { app_id, name, family?, custom_only }, scope: "workspace"|"organization", limit?: "read"|"ask"|"act", levels: ("read"|"ask"|"act")[], health: Health (C-944), connected_by?: { name }, agents: AgentAccess[], managed_by?: WhoCan, can: { give_access, change_access, manage } }`.
  - `AgentAccess = { agent: { id, name, handle }, workspace: { id, name }, level: "read"|"ask"|"act"|"custom"|"use", always_allowed?: { tool_id, name }[], custom?: { base: "read"|"ask"|"act", differs: int }, model?: string }`. `use` is the level of accounts with no choice (websites, AI keys, read-only apps). A saved choice that is `ask` plus write tools set to allow reads as `ask` with those tools in `always_allowed`; `custom` is any other saved choice that is not a level. `model` is set on AI-key rows: the model the agent's next turn uses through that key (built, P5-UX-4: only on a list read for one agent with `agent_id`, only for the agent's own entry, and only for a caller who can own the workspace; one readiness call per list, not per row). `app.custom_only` (C-410, added in P5-UX-4) tells the dashboard to open the actions table directly.
  - `limit` is present only for an organization's account of an app that can change things (C-990; built, P5-UX-11: it is the stored `app_connections.limit_level`). `levels` lists the choices that exist for the account, already cut to the limit.
  - (FIX-163) An app account that is turned off for a reason other than a person's choice carries `status_reason` (`instance_deleted`: its self-hosted app instance was removed; the API refuses to turn it on, `409 instance_deleted`). The dashboard offers no **Turn on** for it and says who can add the instance again (organization owners and admins).
  - A workspace's list includes the organization's accounts its agents can be given. With `agent_id`, each item adds `given: bool` and the given ones come first.
  - A level is reported as it takes effect: a saved choice above the limit (possible only for data saved before C-990) reads as the limit.
  - One query per list, with joins; no per-row lookups. It replaces the separate secrets, LLM-key and connection lists in the dashboard (their routes stay for the API).
- C-980 **Access API.**
  - `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/grants/connections/{conn}` accepts `{ level: "read"|"ask"|"act" }` in place of `mode` and the tool lists. The server expands it from the app's tools in the snapshot: `read` allows every read tool; `ask` allows reads and asks for writes; `act` is mode `full`. `mode: "custom"` with tool lists stays for the Custom editor.
  - Choosing a level replaces what was saved, always-allowed changes included.
  - **A workspace's account**: in the same transaction the connection's `access` is set to `read` when every agent's access to it is `read`, and to `write` otherwise (for custom: `write` when any write tool is allowed or asked). Removing an agent's access recomputes it. The connection has no access setting of its own to manage.
  - **An organization's account**: a level above the account's limit, or a custom choice that allows what the limit does not (a write under `read`; a write without asking under `ask`), is refused `422 above_account_limit` with `who_can`. The limit is changed by whoever manages the account (C-990).
  - `act` on a `custom_only` app is refused `422 full_grant_forbidden`, as today.
  - No data migration: saved grants are read through the level mapping of C-917.
  - `PUT …/grants/secrets/{secret}` is unchanged (level `use`).
- C-925 **Accounts list (dashboard)**, `14 §Agents, accounts and access` §1: one component `AccountsList` over C-917, used by the workspace Accounts tab, the agent's Access tab (`agent_id`) and the organization's Accounts tab. Rows, kind chips, who can use it, health line, "Shared by <org>", each agent's entry opening the access choice and **Remove access**, the row menu (Give access, Rename, Reconnect or Replace, Test, Turn off or Turn on, Remove), **Add** (Connect an app or website; Add an AI key; on an agent's Access tab **Give <Agent> an app**), the empty states, a slot above the list for the requests banner (filled by P5-UX-17, C-984) and the "Not given to <Agent>" group. The picker preselects the contextual agent, else the only agent the person may choose, else none; its button names the result.
- C-938 **Access control and the actions table (dashboard)**, §2: one component `AccessControl` (three `ChoiceCards` from `levels`; "Limited by <org>" with `WhoCan` when choices are cut; the "Always allowed: …" list with a remove button per entry; "Custom: <n> actions differ from <level>") used in the row, the picker and the journey. One actions table lists each tool's name and description with its saved choice (Allowed, Asks you, Blocked), editable by people who may change the access, with method and host pattern under **Technical details**; at least one tool must be allowed or asked. It opens from **Custom…** under the choices and, on a row, from **What exactly?** (C-934 adds what actually happens and why).
- C-948 **Agent rows.** Wherever agents of more than one workspace are listed, a row reads "<workspace> · <agent>". (A separate agent name on New workspace is not built.)
- C-934 **What exactly** `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/apps/{app}/access` (**S/K**, `can_use`; rate class `simulate`) → `{ accounts: [{ ref, name, tools: [{ tool_id, name, description, access: "read"|"write", decision: "allow"|"ask"|"block", own: "allow"|"ask"|"block", reason?: { layer: "limit"|"workspace_rule"|"org_rule"|"organization"|"package", label, link? } }] }] }`. (Built, P5-UX-10: `own` is the agent's saved choice, so a reader who cannot read the grants still shows "Daneel's access: Allowed" beside a reason; `link` is the topic key `internet_access` for a rule people wrote, the dashboard builds the address; the accounts are the app's accounts the agent has been given; a tool restricted to other connect methods reads `block` with layer `package`, in `/effective` too; the reason is the first layer in this order: the account's limit, the app's own tool list, a rule people wrote, a limit set by the organization or Administration.) `reason` is present only when something other than the agent's access decided; for `limit` it reads "Limited by <org>". The dashboard shows it in the actions table of C-938 (the row's **What exactly?**), next to the saved choice; there is no second table. It is computed with the C-930 shared code over one synthetic request per tool; `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/effective` is rebuilt on the same path and a test asserts they agree.
- C-986 **Summary sentences.** Two pure functions in `web/src/lib/summary/`, each a table of condition → sentence that the unit tests walk; every sentence must be true under `07 §Evaluation`.
  - `internet.ts` (P5-IA-2): from a level's effective Internet access (limits, defaults, rules) to a reach sentence ("<Subject> can reach any website." / "… only the websites on the allow-list.") and an ask sentence ("Nothing asks you first." / "A rule asks you first on <n> websites."), with the subject as a parameter ("Agents here", "It"). Rendered at the top of the Internet access pages, followed by the line "What agents may do in apps is set for each agent, on its Access tab."
  - `agent.ts` (P5-UX-7): from one agent's accounts (C-917) plus the `internet.ts` sentences to the agent's summary, exactly the table of `14` §8 (the ask sentence is used only when a rule asks). Rendered on Welcome and at the top of the agent's Access tab.
- C-930 **Check what would happen decides like the gateway** (amends C-83 and `07 §Authoring`; FIX-159, D-329).
  - For `lane: request`, the simulator identifies the app (host, port and path against **Active** host claims, instance-aware), adds that app's connections in scope as candidates, selects them by the rules, picks the winner with the gateway's rule (connections before secrets), identifies the tool (`params` and `connect_methods` included), sets `tool_tags`, builds `Credential::Connection` with `read_only`, and evaluates with the same facts the gateway builds.
  - App and tool identification is one shared function in `crates/aurora-registry`, used by the gateway, the simulator and C-934.
  - The response's `candidates` may be `kind: connection`; a new `app: { app_id, tool_id|null }` is added; the trace includes the checks that run before any rule.
  - A table test runs the same cases through the gateway's decision function and the simulator and asserts the same verdict, winner and reason.

#### Connecting and setting up apps

- C-931 **Setup status** `GET /v1/apps/{app}/setup?org_id=&workspace_id=` (**S/K**). `workspace_id` needs `can_own` and gives the workspace view; `org_id` alone needs org owner/admin and gives the org view.
  - Answer: `{ app_id, name, family?, available, methods: SetupMethod[], suggested_method: string|null, clients: { workspace: ClientRef[]|null, organization: ClientRef[]|null, instance: { set_up: bool } }, instances: { id, label, base_url, scope }[], values: { callback_url, external_id?: string }, can: { register_workspace_client, register_org_client, register_instance, set_availability, connect_workspace, connect_org } }`.
  - `SetupMethod = { id, label, description, kind, audience: "person"|"admin", ready: bool, needs: ("oauth_client"|"instance"|"admin_material")[], client_key?, who_can?: WhoCan }`.
  - `ClientRef = { id, client_key, enabled, used_by_methods: { id, label }[] }`. Client secrets and client ids are never included.
  - `suggested_method` is the first ready method with `audience = person`, in the order: `recommended`, `oauth2`, `fields`, `hook`.
  - `external_id` is present only when the app's `permissions.context` includes `org_external_id`.
- C-955 **Connect plan** `GET /v1/apps/{app}/connect-plan?workspace_id=|org_id=`: a projection of C-931 for the journey (`methods`, `suggested_method`, `scope_presets` per method, `available` with `who_can`). One function computes both. It lists no accounts: the journey reads the existing accounts the person may give from C-917 with `app_id`.
- C-956 **Manifest keys** (`09 §3.4`; contract 1, additive):
  - `connect[].audience: person|admin` (default `person`); an `admin` method is never suggested.
  - `connect[].scope_presets: { read: [scope], write: [scope] }`; each a method scope, `read` only `access: read` scopes. Without it, `read` is the method's read scopes and `write` is all of them.
  - The builtin `github` package marks the GitHub App installation method `admin`; `linear` sets `scope_presets.write: [read, write]`.
  - (A `limit` field key for repository or project limits is not built in this phase.)
- C-957 **Connect with access** (amends `POST /v1/apps/{app}/connect`). The body gains `grant?: { agent_ids: uuid[] (1..`GRANT_BULK_MAX`), level: "read"|"ask"|"act" }`.
  - Before any write or redirect, each agent is checked like C-504, and `act` on a `custom_only` app is refused.
  - The connection's `access` follows C-980: with one agent it is derived from the level; with several it is the highest any of them needs. For `scope: organization` (no `grant`), `access` is given as today (`read`|`write`); P5-UX-11 adds `limit: "read"|"ask"|"act"` to this body (C-990), the limit the journey asks for.
  - **`kind: connected`**: the connection and the access are written in one transaction; the answer adds `grants`.
  - **`kind: redirect`**: the intent (agent ids, level) is kept only inside the sealed OAuth flow object, re-authorized at the callback, and applied in the connection's transaction (SEC-130). A failure keeps the connection and reports `grant_failed: { code }`; the callback's `BroadcastChannel` message adds `granted_agent_ids` and `grant_failed?`.
  - A reconnect with `grant` → 422 `grant_not_allowed_on_reconnect`.
- C-988 **A website or an AI key with access, in one request.** `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/secrets` (generic and LLM) accepts `grant?: { agent_ids }`, checked like C-504 and written in the same transaction as the secret. The Welcome AI-key form and the journey's "Another website or API" use it.
- C-958 **Refusals the journey reads.**
  - `422 connect_probe_failed`: `details.cause: "rejected"|"unreachable"|"unexpected"`; `rejected` is a 401 or 403 from the probe; `unreachable` is DNS, connect, TLS or timeout, with `details.host`; `unexpected` keeps `upstream_status`.
  - `422 oauth_client_required` and `instance_oauth_client_required`: `details.who_can`.
- C-959 **Links.** The journey's deep link is `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/agents/<agent id>?tab=access&connect=<app>` (the agent page accepts an id, FIX-153). The gateway's link builder (C-891) emits it as `connect_url` for refusals that know the agent, keeps the workspace link (`…/apps?tab=apps&connect=<app>`) as `connect_url_workspace`, and takes every path it emits (`reconnect_url`, `create_secret_url`, `access_url` too) from the shared table of C-960. A credential-free app keeps FIX-154's `access_url`.
- C-914 **Connect journey (dashboard)**, `14` §3: the button labels of its table, steps App → Account → Access → Sign in → Done (an existing account skips only Sign in), "Another website or API", the permissions to tick for a token, **Advanced** holding the C-842 controls, the Done sentences, failure sentences per C-958, `grant_failed` retry. Reconnect's behaviour is unchanged. Test id `ws-connect-journey`.
- C-932 **App setup page**, `14` §4: routes `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/apps/<app>` and `/o/<org>/apps/<app>`; status sentences per method; what is set up and where; the workspace's OAuth clients for the app managed on the page; values to copy; actions per `can` with `WhoCan`; the app's tools; the guide (C-940). The Apps tab's **Details** opens it. Test ids `ws-app-setup`, `org-app-setup`.
- C-933 **Lists link to the setup page.** Every app name in the Apps, OAuth clients, Self-hosted apps and Accounts tabs links to the app's setup page at the viewer's level. The organization's Apps tab renders the availability switch (C-20) above the list, with one Save; the Governance › Apps tab is removed.
- C-946 **OAuth clients named by method.** `AppDetail.oauth_clients[]` and `ClientRef` carry `used_by_methods: { id, label }[]`. The OAuth client forms and lists show "<app> — for <method label>" and never a bare `client_key`.
- C-939 **Tool descriptions.** Every builtin tool has a one-sentence `description` (at most 200 characters); `check-builtin.sh` fails without one; third-party packages get the linter warning `tool_description_missing`. In `aws-keys`, `ec2_call` is named "Any other EC2 action (changes, and reads not covered above)".
- C-949 **Method labels.** Every builtin connect method has a `label` and a `description` (`check-builtin.sh`); the builtin `gitlab` package gains them ("Personal access token", "Sign in with GitLab").
- C-940 **Guide placeholders.** `GET /v1/apps/{app}/guide?workspace_id=|org_id=` fills `{{aurora.callback_url}}`, `{{aurora.external_id}}` (only for a member of the organization), `{{aurora.app_setup}}` and `{{aurora.page:<name>}}` (`oauth_clients`, `self_hosted`, `packages`) for the caller. A page placeholder becomes a Markdown link to the page at the highest level the caller can open (a workspace has no such tabs: `oauth_clients` resolves to the app's setup page there), else the `WhoCan` sentence. Substitution is literal and non-recursive; values are Markdown-escaped. (P5-REFINE-8, D-407: the sentence names the role only, "OAuth clients (ask an organization admin)", never people.)
- C-941 **Guide checks** (`09 §6`): `guide_unknown_placeholder` (error) for a `{{aurora.…}}` token outside the C-940 set; `check-builtin.sh` refuses, in builtin guides and `connect_note`s, the literal strings `<your Aurora URL>`, `<external_url>`, `Admin, Apps,` and "Instances" used as a page name.
- C-942 **`connect[].show_context`** (manifest, optional; `org_external_id` only, and it must be in `permissions.context`): the journey's sign-in step and the Bedrock assume-role AI-key form (both P5-UX-3) and the setup page (P5-UX-9) show the value with **Copy** above the method's fields, using the `CopyValue` primitive P5-UX-13 adds. `aws-keys` `assume_role` declares it.

#### Approvals and requests

- C-982 **Answering an approval.**
  - The gateway fills `app_id`, `tool_id` and `connection_id` when it creates an approval.
  - `Approval` gains:
    - `sentence: { agent: { id, name }, action: string, app?: { app_id, name }, account?: { ref, name } }`. `action` is the app's approval title when the package provides one, else the tool's name, else "send a <METHOD> request to <host>".
    - `asked_by: { kind: "access", agent_id, connection_id, tool_id } | { kind: "rule", rule_id, rule_name, scope: "workspace"|"organization" }`, resolved from the derived-rule record of the rule that asked.
    - `can_always_allow: bool`.
  - `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decision` accepts `{ decision: "approve"|"deny", always?: true }`. `always` is valid only with `approve`, when `asked_by.kind = "access"`, the caller is `can_own` of the agent's workspace, and the limit of an organization's account is `act`; otherwise `422 always_not_available`.
  - With `always`, one transaction approves the request, moves the tool from asked to allowed in the agent's access for that account, and approves the other pending approvals of the same agent, account and tool. It is audited as an access change with `via: "approval"` and the approval id (SEC-131). The answer adds `always_applied: { tool: { id, name }, also_approved: int }`. The agent's access then reads `ask` with the tool in `always_allowed` (C-917).
  - The batch route and chat-platform decisions do not take `always`.
- C-983 **Where approvals appear.**
  - The API writes the transcript events `approval.pending`, `approval.decided` and `approval.expired` (`10 §Events`) for an approval that belongs to a conversation, so the conversation shows the card where the agent paused.
  - The card (conversation and Approvals page) shows the sentence, the app's details, the account, the expiry and the answers: **Allow once**, **Always allow** (when `can_always_allow`), **Block**; "Asked by the rule <name>" for `asked_by.kind = "rule"`. People who cannot answer see "Waiting for <names>" (`WhoCan`).
  - The Approvals page has **Waiting** and **History**, with the empty states of `14` §5; `?focus=<id>` puts that approval first and highlights it; the browser notification links there.
  - Chat-platform cards and e-mail use the same sentence and the labels Allow once and Block.
- C-984 **An agent asks for access.**
  - Table `agent_access_requests` (`03 §Planned changes`): one open request per (agent, app) or (agent, proposed website); `request_app_connection` and `propose_custom_connection` create or refresh it next to the notice they already write, and the notice payload gains `request_id`.
  - The conversation renders those notices as a card: "<Agent> needs <App> for this", the reason marked as the agent's words, **Give <Agent> <App>** (the journey, C-959) or `WhoCan`, and **Not now** (`POST /v1/access-requests/{id}/dismiss`, `can_own`). A proposed website reads "<Agent> needs a token for <host>" with **Add the token**.
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access-requests?status=open` (**S/K**, `can_use`) feeds the banner on Accounts ("<Agent> asked for <App>", each with **Give <Agent> <App>**).
  - When access to an account of that app is written for that agent (any path), the same transaction marks the request `given` and appends the notice `app_access_given` (payload `{ app_id, app_name, level }`; the conversation shows the Done sentence of `14` §3 for that level) to the conversation. If the requesting turn has ended, the API queues one system turn (door `system`, speaker label "Access") with the fixed text "Access to <App> was given (<level>). Continue with what you were doing." (SEC-132).
  - Open requests expire after `ACCESS_REQUEST_TTL` (14 days).
  - For a `direct` conversation mirrored to a chat platform, the mirror posts one line: "<Agent> needs <App> for this. Give access: <link>". Nothing is posted in shared spaces.

#### Health

- C-943 **Test** `POST /v1/accounts/{type}/{id}/test` (**S/K**; `can_own` of the account's workspace, or org owner/admin for an organization's account; rate class `check`; SEC-129). It runs, in order of what the account has: the connect method's `probe`; for a `database` app a gateway login up to `ReadyForQuery` with no statement; for a hook-minted token one mint; for an AI key the existing verify (C-502). Answer `{ result: "ok"|"rejected"|"unreachable"|"unexpected"|"not_testable", detail?: { host?, status?, code? }, checked_at }`. It never returns an upstream body and is audited `account.test`.
- C-944 **Health fields.** `app_connections` and `secrets` gain `last_check_at timestamptz null` and `last_check_result text null` (CHECK in `ok`, `rejected`, `unreachable`, `unexpected`). `Health = { state: "ok"|"rejected"|"sign_in_expired"|"unreachable"|"disabled"|"unknown", detail?: string, checked_at?, last_used_at? }`: `sign_in_expired` is connection status `reauth_required`; `disabled` is status `disabled` (shown as "Turned off"); otherwise the last check result; `unknown` when never used or tested. `detail` carries the host for `unreachable` and the word for what the app refuses (`token`, `password` or `key`, from the account's field) for `rejected`.
- C-985 **Health from real use.** When an upstream answers 401 to a request in which the gateway injected an account's credential, the gateway records `rejected`; when a later such request gets a 2xx or 3xx, it records `ok`. It writes only on a change of state, at most once a minute per account, in the batch that already writes `last_used_at`. A 403 is not recorded (it is usually a permission, not a broken credential). Recording never disables an account.
