- C-945 **Health in the dashboard**, `14` §7: the health line (only the wordings of the table in §7) and **Test** on every account row; the fix beside a failure (**Reconnect**, **Replace token**, **Replace password**, **Replace key**, **Turn on**); "1 account needs attention" or "<n> accounts need attention" on the agent header; the texts of the C-842 reconnect dialog in the vocabulary; a banner above the message box of the agent's conversations; the workspace Overview. The journey's Done step runs C-943 once for a method without a probe and shows a failure as a warning.

#### First run, people, organization

- C-927 **Welcome**, `14` §9: three cards from the five sentences, the third being the agent summary (C-986); the guide with two steps (AI key with "<Agent> can use this key" ticked, C-988; Say hello) and a give-access step only when a key exists that the agent cannot use; **Next** links (**Give <Agent> an app**, the journey for the default agent; instructions; schedule; Add people when the viewer may). Welcome is the landing page of a workspace until the guide is finished or skipped; its title is "Welcome to <workspace>", or "Welcome, <name>" in the viewer's own personal workspace.
- C-922 **Starter grants by default and backfill.**
  - Adding a default-organization AI key in the setup checklist also adds it to `placement.starter_grants` whenever placement allows starter grants (C-521); the switch shows on, and turning it off removes the key.
  - `POST /v1/admin/placement/starter-grants/backfill` (**S**, instance admin) with `{ credential_ids?: uuid[] }` gives the starter grants to the default agent of every existing personal workspace of the default organization that lacks them. Idempotent, audited per grant (`actor_label = placement`, `backfill = true`), answer `{ granted, skipped }`.
  - Setup status (C-533) gains `personal_workspaces_without_starter`.
- C-923 **Personal workspace names.** A new personal workspace's **name** is the person's given name, else their full name, else the local part of their e-mail. There is no setting. The existing template keeps setting its **slug** (D-162), and its field on People › New users is labelled "Workspace address". Nothing existing is renamed.
- C-924 **Apps people can't sign in to yet.** Setup status (C-534) gains the reasons `app_needs_oauth_client` (`app_id`, `package`, `client_key`) and `app_token_only`, computed with the C-931 function for the default organization. One component renders them on Administration › Overview and on the organization's Apps tab, each with **Register an OAuth client** (prefilled). Setup status also gains `email_not_configured`, shown on the Overview as a health line linking to Platform › Email.
- C-919 **Add people by e-mail** `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access/by-email` (**S/K**, `can_own`; rate class `invite`, and 30 per hour per workspace). Body `{ email, role: "member"|"approver"|"owner" }`; `422 owner_requires_member` when the role is owner and the e-mail is not an active org member's. Answer `{ result: "added"|"pending", org_entry: "member"|"open_signup"|"invitation_needed" }` (`member`: the person was already in the organization and has the access now; `open_signup`: anyone who signs in is placed in this organization (open sign-up with a shared-organization placement whose default organization is this one); `invitation_needed`: otherwise; built, P5-UX-5; the answer carries no name and no other person). One exact address per call, no search (SEC-128). The dashboard offers Member and Owner, and Approver only when the workspace already has an approver. No e-mail is sent; the dialog gives the workspace's address to copy. At `PENDING_ACCESS_MAX`, `422 pending_access_limit`.
- C-920 **Pending access.** `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access/pending` and `DELETE …/pending/{id}` (`can_own`). Table `workspace_pending_access`. A row is applied at a sign-in once the person is an active member of the organization and holds that verified e-mail, then deleted. Rows expire after `PENDING_ACCESS_TTL` (30 days); at most `PENDING_ACCESS_MAX` (50) per workspace.
- C-936 **Connect for the whole organization.** The journey gains an org mode: `scope: "organization"`, no agent step; the access step sets the account's **limit** with the three choices (`limit`, C-957, C-990); the Done step offers **Give access** (the picker of C-925 over agents of the workspaces the caller owns, saved with C-504) or **Done**. Org owners and admins only. The organization's Accounts tab also offers **Add › Add an AI key** (the existing organization key form).
- C-937 **The organization's accounts.** The organization's Accounts tab lists them (C-917) with the limit control (C-990) and Reconnect, Turn off and Remove for org owners and admins. Workspace owners see them in their workspace's list, marked "Shared by <org>" with `managed_by`, and can give them to their agents within the limit.
- C-947 **Where to connect.** `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/workspaces` items gain `personal_of?: { id, name }`. The chooser lists **For the whole organization**, then the caller's own and shared workspaces, then other people's personal workspaces under a collapsed group.
- C-990 **The limit of an organization's account.**
  - `app_connections.limit_level text null` (CHECK in `read`, `ask`, `act`; set only for organization scope; the migration fills it from `access`: `read` → `read`, `write` → `act`). `access` stays in step: `read` for `read`, `write` otherwise.
  - `PATCH /v1/connections/{id}` accepts `{ limit: "read"|"ask"|"act" }` for an organization's connection (org owner/admin); `access` on such a connection is no longer accepted from the dashboard and maps to a limit for API callers (`read` → `read`, `write` → `act`).
  - **Lowering** the limit lowers, in the same transaction, every agent's access above it: a level becomes the limit; under `ask`, tools allowed without asking become asked; under `read`, write tools are removed. Each change is audited with `via: "limit"`. `GET /v1/connections/{id}/limit-preview?limit=` answers `{ lowered: { agent, workspace, from, to }[] }` for the confirmation. Raising the limit changes no agent.
  - The policy evaluator is unchanged: `read` is still enforced on the connection (D-240), and `ask` holds because no agent's access can allow a write without asking (C-980, C-982, SEC-131).
  - **Built (P5-UX-11, migration `20261201000251`):** `Connection.limit` (`read`|`ask`|`act`, organization scope only); `PATCH` takes `limit` (an organization's account: `422 full_grant_forbidden` for `act` on a `custom_only` app, `400` on a workspace's account or with both `limit` and `access`); the connect body takes `limit` for a new organization account (`access` follows it; `400` with a reconnect, which keeps the limit it has). The preview is `GET /v1/connections/{id}/limit-preview?limit=` → `{ lowered: [{ agent: { id, name, handle }, workspace: { id, name }, from, to }] }` with `from`/`to` one of `read`, `ask`, `act`, `custom`, and `to: "none"` when nothing of the agent's grant is left (a grant that only had changes is removed). The lowering is one transaction with the limit (the connection row is locked `FOR UPDATE`; every grant write takes it `FOR SHARE`), written through the one grant writer with `via: "limit"` (`grant.create`, or `grant.delete` for a removed grant), and tells no agent that access was given. One `connection.update` row carries `from_limit`, `to_limit` and `agents_lowered`. C-947: `personal_of` is built on `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/workspaces`.

### Phase 5 — P5-USAGE (workspace usage: tokens and time, D-359)

Reserved C-1000..C-1009 (C-1007..C-1009 spare).

- C-1000 **`GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/usage?from=&to=&agent_id=`** (**S/K**; whoever can read `…/activity`, i.e. the caller passes the workspace check; aggregates only, so invisible direct conversations are counted, `05`). Query: `from`, `to` UTC dates `YYYY-MM-DD`, both inclusive; default `to` = today (UTC), `from` = `to` − 29 days; `agent_id` optional (any id, also a deleted agent's; no 404). 400 `validation_error` for a malformed date; 422 `usage_range_invalid` with `details.reason` `from_after_to` | `too_long` (more than `USAGE_RANGE_MAX_DAYS` = 366 days). Response, unpaginated:
  ```
  UsageCounters = { turns, turn_seconds, awake_seconds, input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_tokens, cache_write_tokens }   // integers ≥ 0
  {
    from: date, to: date,
    totals: UsageCounters,
    by_day:   [{ day: date, ...UsageCounters }],                       // every day of the range, ascending, zero days included
    by_agent: [{ agent_id: uuid, agent_name: string|null, ...UsageCounters }],   // agents with any non-zero counter; name null once the agent is deleted; ordered by input_tokens + output_tokens desc, then agent_id
    by_model: [{ provider: string|null, endpoint_id: string|null, model: string|null,
                 turns, turn_seconds, input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_tokens, cache_write_tokens }],   // no awake_seconds; ordered as by_agent, then provider, model
    runtime_as_of: timestamp|null                                      // the last runtime rollup (C-1002); awake time after it is not counted yet
  }
  ```
  Tokens and turns come from `usage_daily` (C-1001), awake time from `runtime_daily` (C-1002). `turns`/`turn_seconds` in `by_model` count under the turn's default model, so a model used only by subagents shows tokens and zero turns. `turn_seconds` and `awake_seconds` differ on purpose: a sandbox stays awake between turns. In the OpenAPI document with an operation id `getWorkspaceUsage`; `USAGE_RANGE_MAX_DAYS` is in `02 §Limits`.
- C-1001 **Ledger `usage_daily`** (no FKs; migration `20261201000247`). Columns `org_id`, `workspace_id`, `agent_id` uuid not null; `day` date not null (UTC day of `turns.finished_at`); `provider` text null, `endpoint_id` text null (an endpoint id is a configured slug), `model` text null; `turns`, `turn_seconds`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens` bigint not null default 0; `created_at`, `updated_at`. `UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (workspace_id, agent_id, day, provider, endpoint_id, model)`; indexes `(workspace_id, day)`, `(org_id, day)` and `(day)` (C-1003).
  - **Written** in the transaction of the fenced terminal transition (`turns::finish` affecting one row; a replayed report writes nothing), with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET x = usage_daily.x + EXCLUDED.x`. Never decremented, never deleted with the conversation, agent, workspace or organization.
  - A turn that never started (`started_at` null) writes nothing. Otherwise `turns += 1` and `turn_seconds += floor(finished_at − started_at)` go to the key of its **default model** (`usage.provider/endpoint_id/model`, else `turns.model_basis`, else nulls).
  - Tokens: each `usage.by_model` entry adds its four counters under `(usage.provider, usage.endpoint_id, entry.model)`. Without `by_model` the totals go under the default model's key. No usage: turn count and time only.
  - Every counted `usage` has passed the D-304 trust rule (D-359(d)): when the terminal transition carries no usage and the stored value came from a streaming `usage` report, the finish path reduces it with the same function first (`model::usage_for_turn`), stores the reduced value and counts that.
  - **Backfill**: the migration fills the ledger once from every terminal turn still present (`started_at` not null), with the same rules; turns already deleted are not recoverable.
- C-1002 **Runtime rollup.** `runtime_daily` gains `workspace_id uuid not null` (the table is empty everywhere: nothing ever wrote it; the migration truncates it and the first run rebuilds it from `runtime_intervals`) and indexes `(workspace_id, day)` and `(day)`; the primary key stays `(org_id, agent_id, day)`. The hourly singleton job "Runtime daily rollup" (`05 §Background jobs`) keeps a watermark (the start time of its last successful run; none = rebuild all) in a one-row table `usage_rollup_state (singleton bool primary key default true CHECK (singleton), runtime_watermark timestamptz null)` from the same migration. Each run at time T takes every interval with `ended_at IS NULL` or `updated_at ≥ watermark − 5 min`, and recomputes, for each (agent, UTC day) from the day of its `started_at` to the day of `coalesce(ended_at, T)`, `awake_seconds` = the overlap with that day of all the agent's intervals (open ones end at T), upserted; then the watermark becomes T, in the same transaction. Idempotent; a run after downtime catches up because the watermark did not move. `runtime_as_of` (C-1000) is the watermark.
- C-1003 **`GET /v1/admin/usage?from=&to=&org_id=`** (**IA**, instance admins; D-360). Range rules, errors and `UsageCounters` as C-1000; `org_id` optional (any id, also a deleted organization's). Response, unpaginated:
  ```
  {
    from, to, totals: UsageCounters, by_day: [{ day, ...UsageCounters }],
    by_org:       [{ org_id, org_name: string|null, ...UsageCounters }],                    // every org with a non-zero counter (one row with org_id)
    by_workspace: [{ workspace_id, workspace_name: string|null, org_id, org_name: string|null, ...UsageCounters }],   // the top USAGE_TOP_MAX (50) by input_tokens + output_tokens
    other_workspaces: UsageCounters | null,                                                   // the sum of the rest; null when none
    by_model: [...as C-1000], runtime_as_of
  }
  ```
  Names are null once the organization or workspace is deleted (the ledgers keep their ids). Ordering as C-1000. Operation id `getInstanceUsage`. Built in P5-USAGE-1 with C-1000.
- C-1004 **Where the usage view is** (P5-USAGE-2; `14 §Usage`). Workspace: Activity gains tabs `?tab=requests|usage` (`requests` the default, today's request log). Agent: the Computer page's Usage card (C-1000 with `agent_id`, last 30 days) and **See usage** → `…/activity?tab=usage&agent=<id>`. Administration: Data's fourth tab `?tab=usage` (C-1003). Organization: Data's fourth tab `/o/<org>/data?tab=usage` (C-1006). URL state: `range=7d|30d|90d` or `from=`/`to=` (UTC dates), `agent=`, `org=`, `workspace=`. `routes.ts` gains the helpers; old links keep working (Activity without `tab` opens Requests).
- C-1005 **Two primitives** in `web/src/components/ui` (`14 §Visual design`: tokens only, both themes):
  - `StatTile { label, value, detail?, title? }`: a label in `muted`, the value in `ink` with tabular figures, an optional detail line in `ink-2`; `title` carries the exact value.
  - `BarChart { data: { key, label, value }[], format(value) → string, unitLabel, onShowTable? }`: inline SVG, one series in `accent`; bars anchored to the baseline with 4 px rounded tops and a 2 px gap; gridlines in `line`, axis labels in `muted`, at most 5 y ticks with compact numbers; no legend (the heading names the measure); a tooltip per bar on hover and on keyboard focus (the bar group is one tab stop, arrow keys move between bars, each bar has an accessible name "<label>: <value>"); `role="img"` with a summary label; text never in the series colour; fills its container's width, so no horizontal scroll at 390 px. No chart library is added.
- C-1006 **`GET /v1/orgs/{org}/usage?from=&to=&workspace_id=`** (**S/K**, org `owner`/`admin`, the guard of `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/storage`; D-360, owner follow-up 2026-11-17). Range rules, errors and `UsageCounters` as C-1000; `workspace_id` optional (any id, also a deleted workspace's; rows outside the org are never read). Response, unpaginated: `{ from, to, totals, by_day, by_workspace (top USAGE_TOP_MAX, items as C-1003 without the org fields), other_workspaces, by_model, runtime_as_of }`, ordering and null names as C-1003. Aggregates only, like C-1000: an org admin sees no conversation, agent or person through it. Operation id `getOrgUsage`. Built in P5-USAGE-1. (Phase 6 P6-ORGTREE will decide whether it sums a sub-org tree.)

### Phase 5 — P5-HARN (more harnesses: Pi, goose, OpenCode; D-361, D-362)

Reserved C-1010..C-1019 (C-1017..C-1019 spare). No migration: `agents.harness` and `session_purges.harness` are text, validated against `KNOWN_HARNESSES`.

- C-1010 **`GET /v1/harnesses`** (amends C-420): `HarnessView.id` adds `"pi" | "goose" | "opencode"`, with `display_name` "Pi", "goose" and "OpenCode". `dialects` are each adapter's declared list in preference order (`12` table), and `model_roles` is the declared roles (C-878). The other fields and rules are unchanged; `default` is still `hosted.default_harness`.
- C-1011 **Configuration** (amends C-426): `KNOWN_HARNESSES` = `claude-code`, `codex`, `pi`, `goose`, `opencode`, `fake`. `hosted.enabled_harnesses` stays `["claude-code"]` by default (owner, D-363(a): the new harnesses are opt-in) and `hosted.default_harness` stays `claude-code`. The SET-5 instance setting lists the new ids, with its `harness_not_available` rule unchanged.
- C-1012 **Image and registration** (amends C-427): `SANDBOX_HARNESSES` defaults to `claude-code,codex,pi,goose,opencode`, and the label `org.aurora.harnesses` = `<SANDBOX_HARNESSES>,fake`. Build arguments `PI_VERSION` (npm tree pinned by `images/sandbox/harness/pi/package-lock.json`, `npm ci --ignore-scripts`), `GOOSE_VERSION` + `GOOSE_SHA256_{X86_64,AARCH64}`, `OPENCODE_VERSION` (a v1.19.x release, D-363(b)) + `OPENCODE_SHA256_{X86_64,AARCH64}`. Each install checks `--version` against its adapter's `PINNED_VERSION`. Runtimes live in `/opt/aurora/harness/<id>/`. The chart's `runner.image_harnesses` default lists the new ids (FIX-188) only for the standard `aurora-sandbox` image; an explicit value wins.
- C-1013 **Adapter contracts** (like C-428): command lines, rendered files, environment, state directories and event mapping as in `12 §Adapters pi, goose and opencode`, with the exact names pinned beside each `PINNED_VERSION`. The golden fixtures sit under `services/aurora-supervisor/src/adapters/<id>/fixtures/`.
- C-1014 **Purge** (amends C-424): `session.purge.harness` may name any of the new ids. A new adapter's `purge_session` removes `<home>/.aurora/harness/<id>/sessions/<conversation_id>/`; it is idempotent and needs no live session.
- C-1015 **Terminal** (amends C-750 and C-881): the wrappers `/opt/aurora/bin/{pi,goose,opencode}` refuse with exit 127 when the harness is absent or not enabled. Their state lives in `/workspace/.aurora/terminal/<id>`. `harness.env` gains one section per new harness, with the variables a turn sets (placeholder, proxy, CA, vendor switches off, the model and base URL when known; every value single-quoted). At grant redemption the API runs `model::select` for each enabled terminal harness, as today.
- C-1016 **Dashboard** (amends C-429, C-811 and P5-MODELS-3's note): the Instructions harness picker lists the new harnesses by display name. The roles group's note reads "Not used by <display_name>" for any harness whose `model_roles` is empty, and only the declared roles are shown for the others. The endpoint badges and the "no enabled harness" notice use the declared dialects, so an `openai_chat` endpoint lists `pi`, `goose` and `opencode` when they are enabled. `docs/operator/harnesses.md` covers all five harnesses.
- C-1025 **Terminal approvals** (FIX-169, D-371; amends C-750 (3) and C-1015). By default, the wrappers start their CLI with a turn's approval and sandbox settings. `claude` uses bypass-permissions mode through the root flag `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` placed before the person's arguments (not an inline `--settings`), never by writing the person's `settings.json`. It adds nothing when the person passed `--permission-mode` (either spelling), `--dangerously-skip-permissions` or `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` (or `--` first), and subcommands keep working. `codex` gets `-c approval_policy="never"` and `-c sandbox_mode="danger-full-access"` ahead of the person's arguments in both exec branches; a later `-c` of theirs wins, and a dedicated `-a/--ask-for-approval`, `-s/--sandbox`, `--full-auto` or `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` suppresses the default it covers. `goose` gets `GOOSE_MODE=auto` unless `GOOSE_MODE` was in the environment the wrapper received (read before `harness.env`) or a mode is saved in its `config/config.yaml`. `opencode` and `pi` are unchanged. The mechanism and what was checked are recorded in `12 §Terminal`. No wire, API route or database change.
- C-1026 **Default model from the key's listing** (FIX-170, D-372; amends C-500 and C-878). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/model-readiness`: `default.source` gains `"key_listing"` (a `litellm` endpoint, the first allowed chat model of the key's `GET /v1/models` in LiteLLM's order). The response gains `warnings: string[]` (empty when none; values `"default_model_not_served"`, `"override_not_served"`; additive, unknown values are ignored by clients). `RoleView.warning` gains `"role_not_served"`. The `no_default_model` turn error keeps its code; its `message` names the cause (the key's model list could not be read, or the key serves no allowed chat model). The key whose listing is read is, per endpoint, the one the gateway injects for this agent (`06` step 6). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/models` lists from that same key. The dispatcher's start config carries the model readiness resolved (same function). No database change.
- C-1027 **Audit row details and URL filters** (FIX-171, D-373). No API change: dashboard and e-mail only. `AuditEntry.metadata` (unchanged) is rendered by the shared audit panel's **Details**. A `metadata.change` array of `{ key, entry_id?, section?, before, after }` shows as a before/after table. Objects with exactly `{from,to}`, `{before,after}` or `{old,new}` show as before → after, and anything else shows as labelled values or wrapped JSON. Everything renders as text. Dashboard search parameters: `/admin/data?tab=audit` reads and writes `action` (prefix), `scope` (`instance`\|`all`), `org`, `critical=1`, `since` and `until` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, UTC days, `until` inclusive). The organization and workspace audit tabs read and write `action` (exact) and `critical=1`. Unknown or invalid values are ignored. The instance-settings e-mail's link becomes `{external_url}/admin/data?tab=audit&action=instance_settings.&since=<UTC date of the change>`. The body is still key names only (SEC-90).
- C-1028 **Harness settings by name** (FIX-172, D-374; amends C-782 and C-972). `agents.extra` gains `offered_harnesses: string[] | null`: the sorted union of the harness ids that registered runners report, the same set `harness_not_available` judges by, or `null` when no runner is registered (nothing is then judged). Field metadata: `hosted.enabled_harnesses` (`kind: "list"`) and `hosted.default_harness` (`kind: "enum"`; the stored type stays a string validated against the known ids) carry `choices` for the five harnesses `claude-code`, `codex`, `pi`, `goose` and `opencode`, each with its display name as `label` and one sentence as `description`. `fake` is not a choice. A stored value that is not a choice is kept and shown raw. No route, request body or validation change.

### Phase 5 — P5-POLISH (UX review 4 fixes; D-380..D-393, SEC-135, SEC-136)

Rule: the briefs `docs/tasks/P5-POLISH-1..11.md`, with the reference artifact of D-380 (<https://claude.ai/artifact/WXbcpcrkbCLuxMSurpkuQW>; local copy `/home/alex/orca/workspaces/aurora/design-ux-fixes/`). Errors use the standard body. Every change is additive unless it says "amends"; clients ignore unknown values.

Numbers: C-1029..C-1036 (C-1037..C-1039 spare). Migrations `20261201000254` (P5-POLISH-3, account use and tests recorded apart) and `20261201000255` (P5-POLISH-4, app setup requests). Briefs: P5-POLISH-3 C-1029, C-1031..C-1034 · P5-POLISH-4 C-1030 · P5-POLISH-5 C-1035 (web) · P5-POLISH-6 C-1036 (web).

- C-1029 **App readiness in the catalogue** (P5-POLISH-3, D-388; amends C-895). `GET /v1/apps?workspace_id=` → each `AppSummary` gains:
  - `readiness: { state: "ready" | "needs_admin", needs?: ("oauth_client" | "instance" | "admin_material")[], who_can?: WhoCan, requested?: { by_me: boolean, count: number } }`. `ready` means the method the connect plan would suggest to this caller (C-955) has `ready: true`. `needs_admin` means none of the app's methods is ready for this caller; `needs` is the union of the blocking methods' `needs` and `who_can` the people who can fix it (C-981, the same projection the connect plan's `available_who_can` uses). `requested` is filled by C-1030 (absent before P5-POLISH-4 merges). Apps that are not `available` (switched off for the organization or workspace, D-344) are **not returned** to callers who cannot change availability; organization owners and admins and instance admins still get them with `available: false`.
  - `accounts: { id: string, name: string, agent_ids: string[] }[]`: the workspace's accounts of this app the caller may give (the same set as `GET …/accounts?app_id=`, C-917), so the App step can say "connected, Daneel can't use it yet" without one request per app. `connection_count` stays (deprecated; the web stops showing it).
  - `summary: string | null`: the manifest's `about.summary` (C-1031), else `null` (clients then show `description` clamped to two lines, never cut mid-word).
  - Readiness for all apps is computed in **one** pass per request (one query for the usable OAuth clients of workspace, organization and instance, one for instances), not one plan per app. p95 of the route stays within its current budget with 20 apps.
- C-1030 **App setup requests** (P5-POLISH-4, D-386, SEC-135). A person asks the organization's owners and admins to make an app ready. **Amended by C-1081** (P5-REFINE-8): an optional `reason` on the ask, an optional `message` on the decline, the decline notice names the role, history, `level`.
  - `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/apps/{app}/setup-request` (any active member of `ws` who could connect apps there if the app were ready, i.e. workspace owner or member with `can_own`; same check as the connect plan). Body: none. Idempotent per person: 200 with `AppSetupRequest` whether new or existing. 409 `app_ready` when C-1029 would say `ready`; 404 when the app is unknown or not visible to the caller. At most 20 new asks per person per hour (429 `rate_limited`).
  - `DELETE /v1/workspaces/{ws}/apps/{app}/setup-request` (the asker): withdraws this person's ask; the request closes when no asker is left. 204.
  - `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/app-setup-requests?status=open` (organization owners and admins): `AppSetupRequest[]`, newest ask first.
  - `POST /v1/orgs/{org}/app-setup-requests/{id}/decline` (organization owners and admins): closes it as `declined`; every asker gets one notice ("<Name> isn't setting up <App> now."). 200.
  - `AppSetupRequest { id, org_id, app_id, app_name, status: "open" | "resolved" | "declined" | "withdrawn", askers: { user_id, display_name, workspace_id, asked_at }[], needs: (as C-1029), created_at, closed_at? }`. There is no free text.
  - **Resolution:** whenever an app's readiness for the organization can change (an OAuth client is registered or deleted, an instance is added, availability changes, a package is approved), the API re-evaluates open requests of that organization; a request whose app is now `ready` for its askers closes as `resolved`, and each asker gets one notice ("<App> is ready. Give it to <Agent> from its Access tab.") with a link to the journey (`…/agents/<agent>?tab=access&connect=<app>` for the asker's default agent, else the workspace Apps tab).
  - **Notices:** to the organization's active owners and admins: one e-mail (through the RFE-011a mailer, `approvals.email_notifications`, C-265 rules: at most one mail per request, then folded into one digest per hour) and one Web Push of type `app_setup_request`; to askers, push type `app_setup_resolved` / `app_setup_declined` and the same mail rules. Mail and push carry the app name, the askers' display names and a link; never anything else.
  - **Where it shows:** `WorkspaceOverview`'s Needs you (C-927's card) gains rows of kind `app_setup_request` for viewers who are owners or admins of the workspace's organization (from the GET above, no new route); the organization Apps list (C-1029 with `available` data) shows "Bob asked" on the app's row.
  - Audit: `app_setup_request.ask`, `.withdraw`, `.decline`, `.resolve` (target: app; actor: person or `system`).
- C-1047 **Setup requests have a subject** (P5-APIGAP-2, D-399, D-386, SEC-135; amends C-1030; **amended by C-1080/C-1081**, P5-REFINE-8: `reason`, `?status=open|history` on the caller's own list, the request's resolving `level`). A person can ask for a chat platform or an AI key, not only an app. A request has a `subject`: `app` (C-1030, unchanged, its routes stay), `chat_platform` (optional `connector_type`, e.g. `mattermost`: the person's organization has no chat platform connected, or none of that type) or `ai_key` (the organization has no AI key the person's agents can use). One open request per `(organization, subject, app_id | connector_type | none)`; a second person's ask joins it exactly as C-1030's.
  - `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/requests` body `{ subject: "app" | "chat_platform" | "ai_key", app_id?: string (subject `app`, required), connector_type?: string (subject `chat_platform`, `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,31}`) }`: any active member of `ws` (not only `can_own`: the places are the organization's AI models and chat platforms pages and an agent's Channels tab, which a member sees). Idempotent per person; 200 `AppSetupRequest`; 409 `already_available` when the thing exists already for the person (`chat_platform`: an `active` channel integration of the organization, of that type when named; `ai_key`: an `active` LLM key of the organization, organization-wide or of `ws`, with a provider and endpoint); `subject: "app"` behaves as `POST …/apps/{app}/setup-request` (409 `app_ready`, needs `can_own`). 400 `invalid_request` for an unknown subject or a malformed `connector_type`. Same limit as C-1030's ask: 20 new asks per person per hour in all subjects together (429 `rate_limited`, class `setup_request`).
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/requests` (any active member of `ws`): the caller's **own** open requests in the organization, all subjects, each with only their own ask (so the page can say "Asked <name> · Undo"); `{ items: AppSetupRequest[] }`.
  - `DELETE /v1/workspaces/{ws}/requests/{id}` (the asker): withdraws the caller's ask (204; unknown or not theirs is also 204, nothing revealed); the request closes `withdrawn` when no asker is left.
  - `AppSetupRequest` gains `subject` (`app` | `chat_platform` | `ai_key`, default `app`) and `connector_type?`; for the new subjects `app_id` is `""`, `app_name` is the label ("A chat platform", "Mattermost", "An AI key") and `needs` is `[]`, `unavailable` `false`. The org list `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/app-setup-requests` and `…/decline` return and close every subject; the app routes and their ITs are unchanged.
  - **Who receives it:** `app` and `ai_key` → the organization's active owners and admins. `chat_platform` → the organization's active owners and admins (they connect an integration), **and** the instance's active admins when no connector of the asked type (any type when none is named) is `active` on the instance, since only an instance admin can add one (their notice links to Administration › Chat platforms; they act there, the list route stays the organization's). Notices as C-1030: fixed sentences from the subject label and display names, one mail and push per request, a digest per hour when others join.
  - **Resolution:** the same re-evaluation as C-1030 (after the commit of anything that can change it and in the periodic sweep): a `chat_platform` request resolves for an asker when the organization has an `active` channel integration (of the type when named); an `ai_key` request when an `active` LLM key exists that is the organization's or the asker's workspace's. Each asker gets one notice ("Your organization connected a chat platform" / "An AI key was added"); a request without askers closes `resolved`. An owner or admin can decline (`declined`, askers told once).
  - **Where it shows:** Needs you's rows gain kinds `chat_platform_request` and `ai_key_request` beside `app_setup_request` (the web renders the card from the org list; the action is **Connect a chat platform** / **Add an AI key**); the web places **Ask <Name>** on an agent's Channels tab with no chat platform, on Organization › AI models for a person with no key or model, and on the organization's chat platforms page for a member.
  - Audit: `app_setup_request.ask|withdraw|decline|resolve`, target type the subject (`app`, `chat_platform` or `ai_key`), metadata `subject`, `connector_type?`, `request_id`. Storage: `app_setup_requests.subject` (03).
- C-1031 **Package text for people** (P5-POLISH-3, D-387; amends the manifest of `09 §3`). Optional manifest fields: `about.summary` (string, 1..100 characters, one sentence, no trailing period required; rejected at ingest when longer); `connect[].access_levels[].permission_hints: string[]` (each 1..60 characters, at most 12; the token permissions to tick for that level in the app's own words, e.g. `"Contents · Read"`); `about.first_prompt` (1..120 characters; a question a person could send to try the app). Exposed: `summary` on `AppSummary` (C-1029); `permission_hints` per level on `GET /v1/apps/{app}` methods and on the connect plan's methods (C-955), next to `scope_presets`; `first_prompt` on `GET /v1/apps/{app}`. Every builtin package (`plugins/builtin/*`) fills `summary` and `first_prompt`, and `permission_hints` for each access level of each token method. A widened manifest because of these fields alone is **not** a permission widening (no re-approval).
- C-1032 **Builtin packages approved with the release** (P5-POLISH-3, D-381, SEC-136; amends C-340 and `09 §10`). Ingest of a package from `plugins.builtin_dir` signed by the project key activates it at first install (unchanged) **and at every later version or digest change**, including `permissions_widened` (today pending), with audit `plugin.auto_approved { version, previous_version?, reason: "builtin_release" }`. With `plugins.allow_unsigned = true` (dev values only), an unsigned package in `plugins.builtin_dir` is treated the same way, audited with `reason: "builtin_dev_unsigned"`; with it false such a package fails closed (unchanged, D-75). Packages from `plugins.dir`, upload and addon sources keep their review. The dev thin image and `deploy/dev`/`deploy/kind` install the packages into `plugins.builtin_dir` instead of `plugins.dir`. `GET /v1/admin/plugins` items gain `origin: "aurora" | "uploaded" | "directory" | "addon"` (from `Source`), `summary` (C-1031) and `reaches: string[]` (the manifest's egress hosts, deduplicated, at most 5 plus a count), so the list can say "From Aurora · reaches api.github.com".
- C-1033 **Real use and tests recorded apart** (P5-POLISH-3, D-383; amends C-943..C-945, C-985; migration `000254`). `app_connections` and `secrets` gain `last_ok_at timestamptz`, `last_error_at timestamptz`, `last_error text` (`rejected | unreachable | unexpected | expired`), `last_test_at timestamptz`, `last_test_result text` (`ok | rejected | unreachable | unexpected`). The gateway writes `last_ok_at` on a 2xx/3xx answer and `last_error_at`/`last_error` on a 401/403 (`rejected`), a connection failure (`unreachable`) or another failure it attributes to the account, for **agent calls only** (at most one write per account per minute, as today). The Test route (C-943) writes only `last_test_*`. The existing `last_check_at`/`last_check_result` are backfilled into the new columns by the migration (`ok` → `last_ok_at`, otherwise `last_error_*`) and kept, unwritten, until a later cleanup. `AccountHealth` (C-944) gains `last_ok_at`, `last_error_at`, `last_error`, `test: { at, result } | null`; `state` becomes: `sign_in_expired` (status `reauth_required`) · `disabled` · `failing` (`last_error_at` newer than `last_ok_at`) · `working` (`last_ok_at` set and newer) · `not_used` (neither) — `unknown` is retired; old clients map `failing` to their old failure wording.
- C-1034 **Endpoint role warnings move to the endpoint** (P5-POLISH-3, D-385; amends C-1026 for the dashboard only). New route `GET /v1/admin/llm-endpoint-warnings` (instance admin; the endpoints themselves stay in the effective settings, `llm.endpoints`) → `{ endpoint_id: string, roles: { role: "large" | "medium" | "small", warning: "role_unset" | "role_not_allowed" }[] }[]` (`role_not_served` needs a key's model listing, which an endpoint alone does not have: it stays on `model-readiness`), computed with the same function as `resolve_roles` (`hosted/model.rs`) for the endpoint's defaults (no agent overrides), listing only endpoints with at least one warning. Administration › AI models shows them once on each endpoint's row, with the fix (set the roles there), so an admin sees that agents on it may ask for a model it does not serve. `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/model-readiness` is unchanged (its `roles[].warning` stay for the API and the chat state); the agent's pages stop showing them (P5-POLISH-6).
- C-1035 **A chat draft from a link** (P5-POLISH-5, D-388; web only). The agent page accepts `?draft=<text>` (URL-encoded, at most 500 characters): it opens a **new** conversation with the composer filled and focused, never sent; the parameter is removed from the URL once read. The connect journey's Done step builds it from `first_prompt` (C-1031). (P5-REFINE-13, owner's bug report: the link did nothing from the agent's Access tab.) The link also carries **`tab=chat`** (`?tab=chat&draft=…`; `?tab=chat` alone for "Open chat"); the agent page **follows the router**: a navigation that arrives with no `tab` after one that had it lands on the default page (Chat for hosted agents); and the composer takes the draft **from the router** (`useSearchParams`, handed down as `initialDraft`), not from `window.location`, which still shows the previous URL during a client-side navigation. The page keeps the draft until the person edits the box or sends, so a remount cannot lose it; a stored draft never wins over a link's. Until "New chat" (P5-REFINE-11, D-409) the draft opens the person's direct chat (the link drops `?c=`). On a phone the Chat page opens the person's conversation (P5-REFINE-11, chat-11), so the link needs nothing more there.
- C-1036 **Agent configuration in the … menu** (P5-POLISH-6, D-382; amends C-960's agent rows; web only). The agent page's section tabs are Chat, Triggers and Computer. Behaviour (`?tab=instructions|memory|skills|evals`) and Access (`?tab=access`) are reached from the … menu and keep their addresses; old links keep working. `routes.ts` keeps one helper per page.
- C-1041 **Hardening batch** (FIX-181, D-396; amends every route that names a frozen agent, a name field or a list). (a) A frozen agent is **423 `frozen` on every route**, including runner-plane redemption of an SSH (`kind: "ssh"`) or Files (`kind: "files"`) stream grant (were 409 `not_current`). (b) Every user-set name or label (groups, agents, secrets, app instances, connections, schedules, webhooks, workspaces, organisations, API keys, SSH keys, skills) rejects the invisible formatting characters of `sanitize::is_stripped` with the route's usual 422 (`aurora_core::names::validate_name`); existing rows are not rewritten and an edit of one is refused until it is renamed. (c) List routes authorise (404/403) **before** parsing `limit`/`cursor`, so a bad cursor is 400 only for someone who may list. No request or response schema changes.
- C-1040 **`408 request_body_timeout`** (FIX-180, D-395; gateway proxy listener, no API route). A proxied request whose body delivers no byte for `GATEWAY_BODY_IDLE_TIMEOUT` (60 s; idle, not total) is answered `408` with the standard error envelope (`error.code = "request_body_timeout"`, `x-should-retry: false`), the HTTP/1 connection closes after the answer (`Connection: close`), and one request-log row records status 408 and that code. It covers absolute-form `http://` requests and the requests inside a MITM tunnel (both go through the same request path); an open-lane tunnel is raw bytes and has no request body to time (it keeps `TUNNEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`). What the decision path held is dropped as a client abort is today (a parked approval stays parked).
- C-1042 **Manifest: a write tool's default of Ask** (P5-TOOLASK, D-397; `09 §3.4`). `tools.write.items[].approval: ask` (absent = none; the only value; additive in contract 1, omitted from serialization so other packages' hashes hold). On a `tools.read` item: lint `tool_approval_read`. `GET /v1/apps/{app}` `Tool` gains `approval: "ask" | null` (always present). Policy `ToolTags` gain `ask_default` (a gateway snapshot field only).
- C-1043 **Decision and card for a package default of Ask** (P5-TOOLASK, D-397; `07 §A package's default of Ask`). After both scopes are combined, `allow` becomes `needs_approval` when the matched tool has `approval: ask`, the request is not a `CONNECT` and no scope's matched allow rule names the tool in a target's `tools`; blocks, rate limits and asks are unchanged. The hold's `approvals` row has `rule_logical_id: null` and `rule_name: "This app asks before this action"`; `Approval.asked_by` gains `{ kind: "package", app_id, tool_id }` (and no `can_always_allow`); the approval e-mail says the sentence without "Rule:". Policy simulate adds to the workspace trace `{ rule_logical_id: nil, rule_name: "App default: asks first", source: "default", matched: true, reason: "package_default_ask" }`; the effective view (C-934) gives the tool `reason: { layer: "package", label: "The app asks before this action." }`.
- C-1050 **When someone joined a workspace** (P5-APIGAP-4, D-399; amends `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access`). `AccessBinding` gains `joined_at: string (date-time) | null`: the creation time of the person's direct `workspace_access` row (kept across role changes and suspension; a removal and a later re-add start a new date). An implicit row (organization owner or admin) without a direct row has `null`: the access comes from the org role, and when that role was given is not recorded. `derived_members[]` rows gain the same field: the earliest moment one of their current group sources started (the later of the group membership's and the group binding's creation), or the direct row's time when it is older; `null` when the only source is an org role. No backfill: every existing direct row already had a creation time.
- C-1051 **Suspend one person in one workspace** (P5-APIGAP-4, D-399, SEC-138; migration `000258`). `AccessBinding` and `derived_members[]` rows gain `status: "active" | "suspended"` (always present; derived rows are always `active`, a suspended person derives nothing; an `implicit: true` row reports its direct row's stored status, but the org role's access is unaffected by it: a person suspended here who later becomes an org owner or admin has access as such, and the suspension applies again if they stop being one). New route `PATCH /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access/{user}` with `PatchAccessRequest { status: "active" | "suspended" }` (`can_own`) → 200 `AccessBinding`.
  - Refusals, in this order after `can_own`: 404 `not_found` when the person has no direct binding in the workspace (group-only access is ended at the group binding); 409 `cannot_suspend_self` (suspend only); 409 `implicit_access` when the person is an effective org owner or admin (suspend only); 409 `managed_by_idp` when the binding's role comes from an IdP claim (suspend only; restoring is always allowed); 409 `last_owner` when the person is the workspace's only active owner (suspend only; with org owners always implicit owners this cannot happen through the API, the check is defensive). The same status again → 200 unchanged, no audit row, no invalidation.
  - Effects of a suspension, in one transaction: `workspace_access.status = suspended`; the person's queued and live speaker turns in this workspace end `aborted` with `error_code = user_suspended` and a notice (as SCIM deprovisioning does org-wide, `04 §SCIM`); `aurora_auth_revoked { kind: "user" }` (gateway identities of the person: their speaker binding ends at once) and `{ kind: "access", id, org_id }` (open conversation and approval streams re-authorize and end). Then every authorization path refuses the person in this workspace: routes 404/403 as for a non-member, workspace API keys of the person 404, terminal / SSH / Files / preview access ends at the next re-check, approval decisions refused and approval mail no longer sent to them, schedules and webhooks with `anchor = creator` stop firing as for a lost `can_use`. Kept: the binding and its role, the person's sessions (other workspaces and orgs are unaffected), API keys (refused, not revoked: a restore brings them back), conversations, memory, schedules and every other row they own.
  - Restore (`status: active`) brings everything back except what ended (aborted turns stay aborted). Audit (security-critical, in the transaction): `workspace_access.suspend` / `workspace_access.restore`, target the user, `metadata.change.status = "active → suspended"` / `"suspended → active"`.

- C-1048 **An AI key's default model** (P5-APIGAP-3, D-399; extends C-917). `Account` (`GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/accounts`, `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/accounts`) gains `default_model: string | null` (always present; null on every other kind): on `kind: "ai_key"` rows the model an agent gets on that key with no override, by D-372's rule (the endpoint's configured default when the key serves it, else the first allowed chat model the key serves; the listing is read from the cache or the endpoint). `null` when unknown (endpoint gone, key not active, the listing unreadable and no default, nothing allowed). A member reads it from the workspace list, which they already may call (`can_use`), so they learn only the model name of a key they can already see; no endpoint URL, no other key's data.
- C-1049 **The starter key** (P5-APIGAP-3, D-399; extends C-917). `Account` gains `starter: boolean`, present only on an organization-scoped `ai_key` row of `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/accounts` (owners and admins; omitted on every other row and in the workspace list): `true` when the instance's saved placement gives that key to every new agent of this organization, that is, placement mode `shared_org`, personal workspaces on, this organization is the default organization and the key is one of the placement's starter grants. Read-only; starter grants change only through `PUT /v1/admin/placement` (instance admins).
- C-1044 **Connector `has_icon`** (FIX-183, D-398). `ConnectorAdmin` (`GET /v1/admin/connectors`, the create/approve/patch responses) gains `has_icon: bool`: true exactly when `GET /v1/connectors/{id}/icon` would answer 200 (status `active` and the stored manifest carries a decodable `about.icon_png_b64`); both read one predicate. Additive; the icon route is unchanged (404 `not_found` without an icon). The dashboard requests the icon only when `has_icon`, else shows the monogram tile.
- C-1045 **Wake a sleeping computer** (P5-APIGAP-1, D-399; `05`). `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox/wake` (S/K, `can_use` of the agent's workspace, operation `wakeAgentSandbox`), no body. Sets the same wake demand a terminal open sets (`sandboxes.terminal_wake_at`, C-621; the due-work start arm does the start), so there is one way to start a sandbox. Answers: sandbox `starting`/`running` → **200** `SandboxView` (no second start); `stopped`/`unprovisioned`/`stopping` → **202** `SandboxView`; `failed` → 409 `sandbox_failed` (`/sandbox/retry` is the way out); frozen agent → 423 `frozen`; BYO agent → 409 `not_hosted`; agent not visible → 404; past the shared per-agent hourly wake budget (`TERMINAL_WAKES_PER_AGENT_HOUR`) → 429 `rate_limited`. Audit `agent.sandbox_wake` (metadata `{ "previous_state" }`), best-effort. Cheap and side-effect free beyond the demand (P6-WARMPOOL reuses it).
- C-1046 **`SandboxView.idle_stop_after_secs`** (P5-APIGAP-1, D-399). `int | null`: the effective idle timeout after which this computer is stopped, read from `hosted.idle_stop_secs`, the value the runner plane applies; `null` when it is 0 (idle stop off). Additive.

### Phase 5 — P5-PERSON (the agent knows who it is working for; D-402, SEC-139)

- C-1052 **The turn's person** (P5-PERSON, D-402, SEC-139; generalizes C-844's SEC-108 rule). One function (`domains/people/turn_person.rs`) used by the turn context block (C-1053), `send_message` `to: "me"` (C-1055) and calendar invitations (C-844). The person is: the turn's `speaker_user_id` when `speaker_kind = person` and the door is `web`, `api` or `thread`; on a `schedule` turn with no speaker, the `creator_user_id` of the schedule named by `door.ref`, only when that schedule belongs to the turn's agent. `webhook`, `watch`, `peer`, `eval` and `system` turns, and creator-less (`anchor = workspace` with no creator) schedules, have **no person**. A user counts only while **not disabled**, an **active member** of the agent's organization **and** holding **`can_use`** on the agent's workspace, read from the effective access view the speaker checks use (a workspace suspension, C-1051, counts). Result `{ user_id, display_name, email, email_verified: bool, timezone: string | null }`, all read from `users` at the moment of the call, never from the turn text. Calendar keeps its codes on top (`no_person` when there is none, `no_verified_email` when `email_verified` is false); the added `can_use` check makes calendar stricter (intended).
- C-1053 **Turn context block** (P5-PERSON, D-402, SEC-139; `10 §Turn context`). At dispatch (due-work arm 6) the API puts one plain-text block, then a blank line, in front of `TurnDeliver.input.text`. It is **never stored**: the transcript, search, export and `turns.text` keep the person's original text (as C-725/C-726's quote block). Eval turns (`door.kind = eval`) get no block (C-860). Follow-ups (`TurnMessage`) get none. No supervisor, protocol or adapter change. Format (lines in this order, each present only when stated):
  ```
  <turn_context>
  Now: <Weekday> <YYYY-MM-DD> <HH:MM> (<IANA zone>)
  Working for: <display name>[, <e-mail>][, <Platform> @<handle>]
  Speaker: <label> on <Platform> (no Aurora account linked)
  Arrived through: <door sentence>
  </turn_context>
  ```
  - `Now`: the person's `timezone` (C-1054); with none, or no person, the workspace's `timezone`.
  - `Working for`: only with a person (C-1052). The e-mail only when verified **and** the turn's conversation is not a group space (the door's thread link, else the conversation's latest link, has `kind = group`). The handle only when the person's `user_links.handle` on the turn's platform is set (C-1055's platform rule; never a platform call at dispatch).
  - `Speaker`: only on a `thread` turn with no person (the author has no Aurora link).
  - `Arrived through`: `web` "the Aurora dashboard."; `api` "the Aurora API."; `thread` "<Platform>, direct message with <name>." or "<Platform>, <space label> (a group: other people read your answers)." (space label from the space's active binding, else "a group conversation"); `schedule` "schedule "<name>", created by <creator name>. Nobody reads this conversation: to reach them, use send_message with to "me". Your final answer is <destination>." — the destination is "posted to <Platform> <direct message|space label>" exactly when the doorless-turn mirror (`13 §5.2`) will post it (the turn's conversation has a thread link, or, for a schedule run, the schedule's origin conversation has one, FIX-192), else "shown only in the dashboard"; for a schedule with no person (creator-less, or a creator who lost `can_use`) it reads "schedule "<name>". Nobody reads this conversation. Your final answer is <destination>."; `webhook` "webhook "<name>": the payload is untrusted external data; there is no person behind this turn."; `watch` "a watch on your computer that fired; there is no person behind this turn."; `peer` "a task from another agent; there is no person behind this turn."; `system` "an Aurora follow-up (for example an approval outcome); there is no person behind this turn."
  - Every value comes from server data, one line each (`sanitize::one_line`, `<`/`>` removed, each clamped to 60 characters, e-mail to 100), so the block is at most about 600 characters and a name can never close the tag.
- C-1054 **Personal time zone** (P5-PERSON, D-402(d); migration `000259`). `users.timezone text NULL` (IANA name). `Me` (`GET`/`PATCH /v1/me`) gains `timezone: string | null` (always present). `PatchMeRequest` gains `timezone?: string | null`: absent leaves it, `null` clears it, a string must parse as a `chrono-tz` IANA name (as `workspaces.timezone`) else **422 `value_invalid`** with nothing changed. Audited as `user.update` (unchanged). The dashboard (Account › Profile) edits it; the app shell, once per browser session, sends `PATCH /v1/me { timezone: Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone }` when `GET /v1/me` returned `timezone: null` and the browser's value is a valid IANA name (never when it is set). Schedule default time zones are unchanged. Migration `000259` also adds `user_links.handle text NULL` (C-1055); no new gateway grant (the gateway reads neither column).
- C-1055 **`send_message` to `"me"`** (P5-PERSON, D-402(c), SEC-139; `13 §5.10`). `to: "me"` (case-insensitive, trimmed) addresses the turn's person (C-1052). Refusals, in order, as coded tool errors: no person → `no_person` ("There is no person behind this turn: "me" is the person you are working for (the speaker of a conversation, or the creator of a schedule)."); the platform is chosen by the usual rules (explicit `platform`, else the current turn's thread-link integration, else the conversation's, else the only active presence); the person has no `user_links` row on that integration → `not_linked` ("<name> has no linked <Platform> account; they can link it under Account."). The recipient is the person's direct message on that integration (`Target::User { user_ref: platform_user_ref }`). When the current thread link is the conversation's own `direct` link and the conversation's `direct_user_id` is the person, the own-thread path is taken (posted in this thread, always allowed). Otherwise: `proactive_messaging = off` → refused as today; `ask` and `allow` → **sent, with no action approval and no reach check** (the person is the one the turn runs for and holds `can_use`). The result is the usual `{ status: "sent", message_id, recipient: { kind: "user", label: <display name> } }`. Every other `to` form is unchanged; `me` reaches only this person. **Handle cache**: whenever the API reads a `user.lookup` result (`find_recipient` and the exact-handle lookup of `send_message`/`read_conversation`), it writes each non-bot `PlatformUser.handle` (one line, at most 80 characters) to the `user_links` row of `(integration, ref)` when one exists; nothing else writes it and there is no backfill.

### Phase 5 — P5-REFINE (UX review 5; D-405..D-419, SEC-140..SEC-143)

Contracts of the P5-REFINE briefs (`docs/tasks/P5-REFINE-*.md`). Each backend brief lands its entries' code; web briefs consume them after the backend has merged. Where an entry amends a shipped contract it says so.

#### P5-REFINE-3 — altitudes (D-406; migration `20261201000260`)

- C-1056 **"Show more controls": the per-person altitude switch** (P5-REFINE-3, D-406; extends C-530/C-531; migration `20261201000260`). `user_onboarding.more_controls boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (`03` `user_onboarding`). `MeOnboarding` (`GET`/`PATCH /v1/me/onboarding`) gains `more_controls: bool`, always present; a person with no row reads `false`. `PatchOnboarding` gains `more_controls?: bool`: absent leaves it, `true`/`false` sets it; the body is still `deny_unknown_fields` and every other field behaves as in C-531. The upsert creates the row when none exists (as for `welcome_seen`) and changes only the fields sent. Not audited (a personal preference with no security effect, like `welcome_seen`). Any signed-in caller, session or API key, may read and write **their own** value only; there is no admin route to set it for someone else. The server does **not** use the value to authorize anything: it only stores what the dashboard shows. **Role stays the floor in the dashboard** (`lib/api/roles.ts`): the switch widens what a person sees only up to what their role may use (`14` §Altitudes), never what the API allows; every route keeps its own authorization (SEC-140..SEC-142 do not change). Existing rows get `false` (migration default). `welcome_seen` rows created by `20261129000001` are not touched. OpenAPI (`web/openapi.json`) and `web/src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` are regenerated (`UPDATE_OPENAPI=1 cargo test -p aurora-api --test it openapi_matches_checked_in`, then `pnpm gen:api`).

Data (add to `docs/03-data-model.md` `user_onboarding`): row `| more_controls | boolean not null default false | (phase 5, P5-REFINE-3, C-1056, D-406) the person's "Show more controls" switch: whether the dashboard shows Expert-level controls; per person, follows them across browsers |`.

`docs/05-control-plane-api.md` row for `GET/PATCH /v1/me/onboarding`: append "`more_controls` (C-1056): the person's Show more controls switch".


#### P5-REFINE-4 — runner and computer (D-408, D-412, D-416; no migration)

- C-1057 **The runner re-decides its stream listener at every poll** (P5-REFINE-4, D-416; the bug of UX review 5; amends C-623, C-796 and `16 §W18`). `POST /v1/runner/work` (the runner's long poll) → `WorkResponse` gains `streams?: { previews: bool, terminal: bool, ssh_enabled: bool }`, computed on **every** poll from the live configuration with the same rules `register` uses for `RunnerConfig` (`previews` = `previews.enabled`; `terminal` = (`terminal.enabled` or `terminal.available`) and the runner advertised `terminal`; `ssh_enabled` = `ssh.enabled` and the runner advertised `ssh`). The field is omitted by an older API (the runner then keeps what its registration said) and ignored by an older runner (`#[serde(default)]`). The runner applies it after every poll: `set_previews_enabled(previews || terminal || ssh_enabled)` and `set_ssh_enabled(ssh_enabled)` (SSH can turn off again, as before); the :4108 listener opens the first time it is on and stays open for the life of the process (unchanged); every grant is still re-checked live by the API (`409 not_current`). Registration keeps `RunnerConfig`'s three flags (they stay the first answer). Effect: a runner that registered with an API pod that said "off" opens the port within one poll (at most `poll_wait_secs`) after the setting turns on, with no runner restart; a `helm upgrade` that turns the terminal on no longer leaves Terminal, Files and SSH dead. No new route, no schema change; `16 §W18` and `15` get one sentence.
- C-1058 **An open terminal or SSH session keeps the computer awake** (P5-REFINE-4, D-416; amends C-620, C-796, `11 §Web terminal`). The runner stamps keep-awake **while a terminal or SSH leg is open**, whether or not bytes flow: one `POST /v1/runner/terminal/keep-awake` per `TERMINAL_AWAKE_GRACE / 2` (60 s) per sandbox and kind, started when the leg is registered and stopped when it ends (any close reason), in addition to the byte-triggered stamp (which stays, one per grace). The API route and the columns (`terminal_active_until`, `ssh_active_until`) are unchanged; the cap is unchanged (`terminal.awake_cap_secs`, default 4 h per boot, and `ssh.awake_cap_secs`; past it the reason lapses and the computer may sleep). `keep_awake_reasons` of `SandboxView` shows `terminal` / `ssh` for as long as a session is open. **Restart notice data:** no new field. `SandboxView.boot_id` (already visible to everyone who can use the agent, never reduced) changes when the computer restarts; the dashboard compares the `boot_id` it opened the terminal on with the current one and prints "The computer restarted; your running commands were stopped" (UX review 5, computer-1).
  - (P5-REFINE-20 batch 5; no shape change) The runner's path is exactly `POST /v1/runner/terminal/keep-awake` (its client's base is `/v1/runner`; it had posted `/v1/runner/runner/terminal/keep-awake`, a 404 it swallowed, so no stamp ever arrived; a refused stamp is now logged by the runner). The idle stop's clock is `now − max(last_engaged_at, last_active_at)` (`last_active_at` is stamped at every boot's `running`): a computer woken for a session gets the whole `hosted.idle_stop_secs` to be used before the first stamp, instead of being parked at the first stop arm after `running` (`11 §Stop`). `computer.sleeps_in_secs` (C-1059) counts from the same clock.
- C-1059 **What a person sees of the computer** (P5-REFINE-4, D-408, D-412, SEC-141; amends C-583, C-611, C-1046 and D-178 Q-R5-5). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox` (any caller who can use the agent) → `SandboxView` gains:
  - `computer: { word, ready, working, asleep, phase, sleeps_in_secs, started_at }`:
    - `word` is `off` | `starting` | `on` | `stopping` | `failed` (D-412): state `unprovisioned` or `stopped` → `off`; `starting` → `starting`; `running` → `on` when `ready`, else `starting`; `stopping` → `stopping`; `failed` → `failed`.
    - `ready` is true only when the computer **answers**: state `running`, the supervisor connected and the harness reported ready (the `start_phase = 'ready'` that `record_harness` writes). D-408: the header says Ready only then.
    - `working` is true while `keep_awake_reasons` contains `turn` or `process`.
    - `asleep` is true for state `stopped` or `unprovisioned` with no failure (the header's "Asleep").
    - `phase` is the raw start phase (`claimed`, `provisioning`, `waiting_capacity`, `creating`, `booting`, `ready`, …) or null: a fixed vocabulary, shown at Expert as the secondary line.
    - `sleeps_in_secs` is the seconds until the idle stop (`idle_stop_after_secs` minus the quiet time since `last_engaged_at`, never below 0) while the computer is `on` with **no** keep-awake reason and idle stop is on; otherwise null.
    - `started_at` repeats `SandboxView.started_at` ("Started 23:55 · on").
  - **Who sees what (D-412, amends D-178 Q-R5-5):** `image`, `runner_id`, `start_attempt.detail`, `start_attempt.events` and every `start_log[].detail` are returned **only to instance admins**. Workspace owners and members get `null` / `[]` / null there, the failure's member hint as `failure_message`, `start_attempt.phase`, `start_attempt.started_at`, `start_attempt.progress_at` and the `start_log` entries' `ts`, `kind`, `phase`. (Before: workspace owners also got the detail.) `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/start-failures` (C-613) follows the same split since FIX-196 (D-424): workspace owners get the member view; `GET /v1/admin/agents/{agent}/start-failures` (C-614) is unchanged.
  - **Stale phase fixed:** the runner's `running` report must not move `start_phase` backwards: `waiting_supervisor` is written only while the phase is not already `booting` or `ready` (the supervisor can connect before the runner's `running` report is applied). The Computer page's start log no longer sticks on `waiting_supervisor` after the computer woke.
- C-1060 **Sleep now, and two Files additions** (P5-REFINE-4, D-408; extends C-898, C-900).
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox/sleep` (S/K, same caller rule as `…/sandbox/wake`: any caller who can use the agent; hosted agents only; operation `sleepAgentSandbox`). Body: none. Asks the computer to sleep now: sets `stop_requested_at = now()` with `stop_reason = 'idle'` on the running sandbox (the due-work stop arm stops it; the reason vocabulary is unchanged). **202** `SandboxView` when requested; **200** `SandboxView` when it is already `stopping`, `stopped` or `unprovisioned` (idempotent, nothing written); **409 `sandbox_busy`** with `details { reasons: string[] }` when `keep_awake_reasons` contains `turn`, `process` or `watch` (work is running: "Stop the work first"); 409 `not_hosted` for a BYO agent; 409 `sandbox_failed` from `failed` (retry is the way out); 404 as the other sandbox routes. Open terminal, SSH, Files and preview sessions do **not** block it (they end: a person chose Sleep now). Audited `agent.sandbox_sleep` (`previous_state`, `reasons`). Rate: shares the wake budget bucket (`TERMINAL_WAKES_PER_AGENT_HOUR`).
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/files/ops` is **unchanged**: `rename` already takes a full destination path and moves a file or folder across folders (`FileOp::Rename { path, to }`, `files/service.rs` `rename`); the "name cannot contain a slash" refusal was the dashboard's. Move to… is `rename` with `to = <folder>/<name>`.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/files/text?path=&preview=1` (S, `can_use`): with `preview=1` a text file larger than `FILES_EDIT_MAX` (1 MiB) is no longer `422 file_not_editable (too_large)`; the answer is `FileText` of its **first 256 KiB** cut at a line end, with `truncated: true`, `size` the whole file's size, **no `etag`** (null) so it can never be saved over the file, and audited like any read. Without `preview=1` nothing changes. Files that are binary or not UTF-8 stay `422 file_not_editable`.
  - SSH needs no API change: `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/ssh-certificate` already returns `valid_before` and `username`; `aurora ssh <handle>` already resolves a handle (`services/aurora-cli/src/ssh.rs`). The terminator still requires the agent id as the SSH user name (`aurora-terminator/src/cert.rs`); mapping handles is **not** done here (the designer's note keeps the id behind "Copy command"). **Superseded by C-1121** (FIX-208, D-445): the handle is the SSH user name.

#### P5-REFINE-5 — agents, keys, identity and setup (D-414; migration `20261201000261`)

- C-1061 **A starter key reaches every hosted agent, old and new** (P5-REFINE-5, D-414; amends C-922, C-1049, `04 §New-user placement`).
  - **Every new hosted agent.** `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agents` with `kind: "hosted"` (and the default agent a new workspace or organization is created with) in the placement's **default organization** (saved placement mode `shared_org`, personal workspaces on) is given the saved placement's starter grants in the creation transaction, with the same function the sign-in placement uses (`grants::grant_secret_as_system` / `grant_connection_as_system`, one savepoint per grant, a refused or unavailable credential skipped and audited as `grant.create` `Denied`). Audit `grant.create` with `actor_label = placement`, `metadata { starter: true, via: "new_agent" }`. Agents of other organizations and BYO agents get nothing (unchanged).
  - **Agents that already exist when a key is added.** `PUT /v1/admin/placement` gives every starter grant the save **adds** (ids that were not in the previously saved set) to every existing hosted agent of the default organization (every workspace, not only personal ones), skipping agents that already have it. The answer `PlacementView` gains `starter_applied: { kind: "secret" | "connection", id, name, agents_granted: number, agent_names: string[] (at most 3 agent display names), already_had: number }[]` (empty when the save added no grant) and `starter_pending: number` (agents not reached because one save is bounded to 2,000 agents; the backfill route finishes them). Audited per agent and grant (`starter: true, via: "placement_save", backfill: true`). A save that only removes or reorders grants applies nothing.
  - `POST /v1/admin/placement/starter-grants/backfill` (C-922) now covers **every hosted agent of the default organization**, not only the default agent of each personal workspace; the answer `{ granted, skipped }` is unchanged. Setup status (C-533) gains `agents_without_starter: number` next to `personal_workspaces_without_starter` (kept for older clients).
  - No starter key saved: nothing is granted, and nothing changes for agents (D-414's "no starter key" path is C-1062).
  - Granting widens what those agents can do; the dashboard says so ("Given to Daneel and 1 other agent", from `agent_names` and `agents_granted`).
- C-1062 **Can this agent answer, and what fixes it** (P5-REFINE-5, D-414, D-408). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/answer-readiness` (S/K; any caller who can use the agent, so members see the same reason as owners; operation `getAgentAnswerReadiness`) → `{ can_answer: bool, reason: "no_ai_key" | "key_not_usable" | "no_model" | null, usable_keys: number, fix: Fix | null }`.
  - Hosted agents: `can_answer` is the dispatcher's own decision (the same function as `GET …/model-readiness`, C-500: an active granted key that is usable and a default model resolves). `reason`: `no_ai_key` (no key is granted to the agent), `key_not_usable` (granted keys exist but none is usable now: origin, provider, dialect), `no_model` (a usable key serves no allowed model, D-372). BYO agents: `{ can_answer: true, reason: null, usable_keys: 0, fix: null }` (they bring their own model).
  - `Fix` is chosen **for the caller**: `{ action: "give_key", key: { secret_id, name, scope: "organization" | "workspace" } }` when a usable key the caller may give exists that is not granted to this agent and the caller can own the agent's workspace (the starter key first); `{ action: "add_key" }` when no key exists in the agent's organization and the caller is an organization owner or admin or an instance admin; `{ action: "ask_admin", who_can: WhoCan }` otherwise (the roles that can fix it, C-981, never names). `key.name` is included only when the caller can own the workspace (as `model-readiness` does).
  - With **no starter key** and no key at all the answer is `reason: "no_ai_key"`, `fix` per the caller: the dashboard shows "<Agent> has no AI model yet" with Add a key (admins) or Ask an admin (everyone else), never a Welcome asking for a key that does not exist. The setup step 3 keeps saying "Add an AI key so agents can answer" (C-1063 `try_it.available` is false until a key exists).
- C-1063 **Try it completes by itself; Health reports real calls** (P5-REFINE-5, D-383, D-414; amends C-533 and the note "Try it is the client's").
  - `SetupStatus` (`GET /v1/admin/setup`) gains `try_it: { available: bool, done: bool, done_at: timestamp | null, agent_id: uuid | null, workspace_id: uuid | null }`. `done_at` (P5-REFINE-20 send-back: "Daneel answered Alice Admin 2 min ago") is when the newest such finished turn ended (`turns.finished_at`), null while `done` is false. `done` is true when the calling admin has **one finished turn** (`turns.status = 'done'`, `role = 'turn'`, `speaker_user_id` = the caller) in a conversation of any hosted agent in a workspace the caller owns; `agent_id` and `workspace_id` name the default agent of the caller's personal workspace (else the default workspace's default agent), or null; (P5-REFINE-20 send-back b1#2, 2026-11-21) once `done`, they name **the agent that answered** (the agent of the newest such finished turn) and its workspace, and `agent_name: string | null` (additive) carries that agent's name, so the row reads "Daneel answered Alice Admin 1 h ago" without a second lookup (the dashboard read "Your agent" when the agent list did not have it). `available` is false while `GET …/answer-readiness` (C-1062) of that agent says it cannot answer (no starter key: Try it is not offered).
  - Each `endpoint:<id>` check of the `llm` step gains `evidence: "call" | "probe"`, `last_ok_at`, `last_error_at` (RFC 3339 or null) and, since the P5-REFINE-20 send-back, `last_error` (the code of the newest failed real call, `secrets.last_error`: `rejected` | `unreachable` | `unexpected` | `expired`; absent when there is none, so the dashboard can say "Did not answer at 14:02: the endpoint could not be reached"). `evidence: "call"` when an agent's **real** call through a key of that endpoint succeeded or failed (`secrets.last_ok_at` / `last_error_at`, C-1033, the newest across the endpoint's keys); the check is `ok` when the newest real call succeeded within 24 h, `warning` when the newest real call failed after the last success, else the existing reachability probe decides (`evidence: "probe"`). `message` names the endpoint by its **display name** (`llm.endpoints[].display_name`, else the id; never a provider id or a suffix) and says "answers" / "did not answer", never "from the API"; the dashboard renders "Answered 2 min ago" from `last_ok_at`.
- C-1064 **People are named by the sign-in's name** (P5-REFINE-5, D-407; migration `20261201000261`; amends C-923, `04 §Identity`). `users.display_name_edited_at timestamptz NULL` (`03 users`).
  - On **every** OIDC sign-in (a new person, an existing identity, and the e-mail bind of a bootstrap, invited or SCIM-provisioned account): when `display_name_edited_at IS NULL` and the validated token carries a non-empty `name` (cleaned exactly as `oidc::display_name` does: trimmed, no control or bidi characters, at most 80 characters) that differs from `users.display_name`, `display_name` is set to it. A missing `name` never overwrites a name. The e-mail's local part is used only when a **new** person is created with no `name` (unchanged).
  - `PATCH /v1/me { display_name }` that **changes** the name sets `display_name_edited_at = now()`; SCIM writes of `displayName` set it too; a person's own name is never overwritten again.
  - **Migration backfill:** `display_name_edited_at = created_at` for every user whose `display_name` is not the local part of the e-mail (a name that looks chosen is kept); `NULL` for those whose `display_name` equals the e-mail's local part (the bootstrap admin "alice"), so their next sign-in corrects it.
  - `Me` gains `display_name_source: "sign_in" | "you"` (`you` when `display_name_edited_at` is set).
  - **Personal workspace name:** a new personal workspace is named `<First name>'s workspace` (C-923 amended: before, the bare name): first name = the token's `given_name`, else the first word of `name`, else the e-mail's local part (cleaned like a template value); the slug still comes from the template (D-162). Nothing existing is renamed; the person can rename it as any workspace. The organization created for an `open` sign-up keeps `<name>'s org`.
- C-1065 **An AI key says whether it works** (P5-REFINE-5, D-383; extends C-917, C-943, C-502). `Account` (`GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/accounts`, `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/accounts`) of kind `ai_key` gains `verification: { state: "checking" | "works" | "rejected" | "unreachable" | "not_checked", checked_at: string | null }` (omitted on other kinds). `works` when the key's stored origin is `verified`, or a real call succeeded (`health.state = working`), or the last Test passed; `rejected` when the last Test or real call says the provider refused the key; `unreachable` when the last Test found nothing answering; `checking` while the origin is `pending` **and** a background re-check is queued; `not_checked` when it is `pending` and none is queued. **Background re-check:** after a key is created or rotated with origin `pending` (the endpoint did not answer at save, no origin rule), the API retries `secrets::verify` (the one function Test uses) at +5 s, +30 s and +2 min on the replica that saved it; a pass stores the origin `verified` and notifies the gateway as `verify` does; three failures leave `not_checked`. (Amended P5-REFINE-20 send-back b1#1, 2026-11-21: an endpoint that never answers the API, e.g. one only the gateway can reach.) **The save is bounded:** when no origin rule applies, the save's own check waits at most **3 s** (`INLINE_CHECK_BUDGET`); past that the key is saved `pending` and the background re-check takes over (with a rule the proof is required, so the save waits for it as before). A `pending` origin caused by an endpoint that did not answer carries `detail: "endpoint_unreachable"` and the `checked_at` of the last attempt (each re-check rewrites it). The stored origin **stays `pending`** after the re-checks (a governance fact: the account is unproven, and without a rule the key is usable); the **final** word is `verification`: once no re-check is queued, a pending key is `not_checked` with `checked_at` = the last attempt (null only when nothing was ever tried) — never `checking` — and the dashboard says "Not checked: the AI service didn't answer Aurora's check. It shows Works after its first answer." with Check again (Test). A real call that succeeds (`health.last_ok_at`, written by the gateway) makes it `works` (newest evidence wins), as for any key. The dashboard never shows a stored `pending` origin as "Checking…": only `verification.state = checking` is. The Test button (C-943) always works and resolves the state. A rejected key at save keeps answering `422 llm_key_invalid` and saves **nothing** (unchanged; a test proves it). Welcome's "AI key" step counts only keys whose `verification.state` is `works` (firstday-3).
- C-1066 **New agents default to hosted; a refused key-origin save says why and keeps nothing** (P5-REFINE-5, D-414; amends the `05` row of `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agents`).
  - `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agents`: `kind` becomes **optional**; absent means `hosted` when `hosted.enabled`, else `byo`. An explicit `kind` behaves as before (`byo` is still governed by the BYO posture). The dashboard's New agent form defaults to "Runs on Aurora".
  - Saving the `governance` section (`PUT/PATCH /v1/admin/settings/governance`, import) with `governance.llm.require_key_origin = true` while any endpoint has no `key_origin` is refused `422 key_origin_required` with `details { endpoints: [{ id, display_name, source: "helm" | "dashboard" }] }` and the message "These AI endpoints need a rule for where their keys come from: <display names>. They are set in Helm, so the rule is added there." (when every missing endpoint is `source: "helm"`; "…add one on the AI models page" otherwise), instead of the validator's raw `llm.endpoints/<id>/key_origin: required because …` strings. **Nothing is written**: the section's generation does not change, no `instance_settings` row is written and no audit event is recorded (the whole save is one transaction; a test asserts `GET` returns the old value and the generation is unchanged). `source` is `helm` when the endpoint comes from deployment values (the settings `Source` of its key, `aurora_config::Source`), else `dashboard`.

#### P5-REFINE-6 — apps, chat platforms, invitations, organizations (D-418, D-419, SEC-140; migration `20261201000265`, number to be confirmed)

- C-1067 **Send-only apps have their own levels** (P5-REFINE-6, D-419; amends C-980, C-957, C-917; fixes the 422 `empty_custom` of UX review 5, access-1). An app is **send-only** when none of its tools is in group `read` (the Outgoing webhook; derived from the snapshot's tools, nothing stored). `AppSummary` and `AccountApp` gain `send_only: bool`. For a send-only app `levels_for` returns `["ask", "act"]` (never `read`; `act` is dropped for a `custom_only` app as before) and `Account.levels` follows; `expand(Ask)` is "ask for every send tool" (`allow: [], ask: [send]`, a valid custom grant), `expand(Act)` is `full`. The connect journey's `grant.level: "read"` (C-957) and a `PUT` of level `read` on a send-only app are refused `422 level_not_offered` with `details { app_id, offered: ["ask","act"] }` (never the `empty_custom` of the hand-built list, which stays for custom lists). Reading a saved grant back (`classify`) gives `ask` / `act` for send-only apps (a test pins it). The notice text of an access grant (`access_requests/service.rs`) reads "<Agent> can now send to <Account>, and asks you first." for `ask` and "<Agent> can now send to <Account> without asking." for `act` on a send-only app (other apps unchanged). The dashboard words the two levels "Ask before each send" / "Send without asking".
