(Built: `AppDetail` carries `send_only` too, for the journey's level step; a new organization account's `limit: "read"` on a send-only app is the same `422 level_not_offered`; the restart turn says "(Ask before each send)" / "(Send without asking)". A saved grant that blocks every send, which only a lowered limit can leave, reads as `custom` with base `ask`, never `read`.)
- C-1068 **One readiness for a chat platform** (P5-REFINE-6, D-407; amends C-712; fixes UX review 5 org-1 and admin-5). `GET /v1/connector-availability` items and the Administration connector list (`GET /v1/admin/connectors`, `ConnectorAdmin`) are produced by **one function** (`connector_readiness(row, live catalogue, host status)`), so the two pages cannot disagree. Each availability item gains `host_running: bool` (a live connector host announces the type, or reports the connector's own host status as running), `can_connect: bool` (true when `setup_state = "active"`), and `next_step: "connect" | "owner_connects" | "start_host" | "approve" | "enable" | "add"` (the next thing a person does: `connect` for an organization owner or admin of an `active` type, `owner_connects` for an instance admin who added it, "Next: an organization owner connects Mattermost", `start_host` only when **no** live host announces the type). `ConnectorSetupState` gains `registering`: a connector whose type a live host announces and whose first registration is still pending is `registering` (`can_connect: false`) and **becomes `active` within one host poll**, so a running host never shows "waiting for the connector host"; `waiting_for_host` is returned only when no live host announces the type. `ConnectorAdmin` gains the same `host_running` and `next_step`. The brief's investigation task: reproduce why a row added while its host runs stays `awaiting_registration` (`managed::live_catalogue`, `connectors::list_managed_enabled`, the registration path) and fix the cause, not only the label. (Built: `ConnectorAdmin` also gains `setup_state`, so the equality is visible. A `registering` connector's `next_step` is already the one of `active` (it resolves within a poll). `connect` is answered to a caller who is an active owner or admin of some organization; the wording is the only thing that depends on the caller. **The cause found:** a development build of the connector host embeds an empty manifest signature; the host's own `register` accepts it under `plugins.allow_unsigned` (D-142) but the managed connector's `POST /v1/connector/register` always verified it, so it failed (422), the runtime stopped, the host restarted it on its next heartbeat and reported it `running`. The connector `register` now applies D-142 the same way: only with `plugins.allow_unsigned`, only an **empty** signature, only a **managed** connector; a present signature is always verified.)
- C-1069 **Invitation links for organization owners and admins** (P5-REFINE-6, D-418, SEC-140; extends the invitation routes of `05` and `04 §Invitations`).
  - `POST /v1/orgs/{org}/invitations` (unchanged request): the `201` `Invitation` gains `link: string` (`<external_url>/join?token=…`), present **only on this response** (a create, or the re-issue of a pending e-mail); never in a list, a `GET`, an audit row or a log.
  - `POST /v1/invitations/{id}/link` (organization owner or admin of the invitation's organization; session or API key; `management` rate class and at most 20 per organization per hour; operation `createInvitationLink`): re-issues the link. A **new token replaces the old one** (the old link stops working at once; the expiry is reset to 7 days); answer `200 { link, expires_at }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`. 404 for an unknown invitation or another organization's; 409 `invitation_not_pending` for an accepted, cancelled or expired one. Audited `invitation.link` (invitation id and e-mail, never the token).
  - `POST /v1/invitations/{id}/resend` (same callers and limits; operation `resendInvitation`): re-issues the token and e-mails it; `202` with the `Invitation` (no link). **409 `mail_disabled`** when the instance's mail is log-only (`email_delivery = "log"` on `GET /v1/instance`), so Resend is offered only when mail works. Audited `invitation.resend`.
  - The development-only log line of an invitation link (`email.log_links`) is unchanged.
  - (Built) The 20-per-hour limit counts `link` and `resend` together per organization (the `api_limiter` per-key hourly charge `invitation-link:org:<org>`, as `by-email` does), on top of the `write` (management) bucket; a refused re-issue (409) still counts. `resend` checks `mail_disabled` before it counts. The audit rows are security-critical (written with the token change). `mail_disabled` reads the same flag as `GET /v1/instance` (`email_delivery`).
- C-1070 **Test a sign-in app** (P5-REFINE-6, D-407; extends C-244; migration `20261201000265`: `oauth_clients.last_test_at timestamptz NULL`, `last_test_result text NULL`). `POST /v1/oauth-clients/{id}/test` (the callers who may `PATCH` that client: the workspace's owners, the organization's owners and admins, instance admins; rate class `check`; session or API key; operation `testOauthClient`) → `200 { result: "ok" | "rejected" | "unreachable" | "unexpected" | "not_testable", checked_at, detail?: { host?: string, status?: number } }`.
  - How: one `POST` to the app's token endpoint (from the active manifest's OAuth method, through the destination guard; a bound self-hosted instance's host included) with `grant_type=authorization_code`, a random `code`, the instance's redirect URI and the client's id and secret in the way the method declares (body or HTTP Basic). **`ok`** when the provider answers `invalid_grant` (it accepted the client and refused the made-up code); **`rejected`** for `invalid_client` or `unauthorized_client` (HTTP 400/401); **`unreachable`** for a network error, timeout or a guard refusal; **`unexpected`** for anything else; **`not_testable`** when the method has no token endpoint (or the client is disabled). It never returns an upstream body and stores no response text; audited `oauth_client.test` (`result`, `package_id`, `client_key`).
  - `OAuthClient` gains `test: { result, at } | null` and `state: "ready_not_tested" | "ready" | "test_failed" | "disabled"`: `disabled` when `enabled = false`; `ready` when the last Test was `ok` **or** a connection has been made with it (`connection_count > 0`, "verified by use"); `test_failed` when the last Test was not `ok` and nothing connected since; else `ready_not_tested`. The name in every sentence is "sign-in app" (the dashboard's words, D-407); routes and ids keep `oauth`.
  - (Built) The table is `app_oauth_clients`. The request is built by the code exchange's own `token_request` (`connect/service.rs`), sent by the connect environment's guarded client (the instance client for a client bound to a self-hosted instance), with a 10 s timeout; a PKCE method also gets a random `code_verifier`. GitHub answers the same two cases with its own words, so `bad_verification_code` counts as `ok` and `incorrect_client_credentials` as `rejected` (HTTP 200, 400 or 401). A templated (tenant) token URL is `not_testable`. "Nothing connected since" compares the newest connection's `created_at` with `last_test_at`. The Test is a security-critical audit row (`oauth_client.test`, written with the result).
- C-1071 **A bad package says so in words** (P5-REFINE-6; amends C-340, C-600). `POST /v1/admin/plugins` and the add-on source routes answer `422 package_invalid` with: `message` in a sentence (one violation: its sentence; several: "That app package has 3 problems. The first: <sentence>"), and `details.violations[]` gaining `sentence` next to `code`, `path`, `message` (the existing technical `message` stays, for the dashboard's Details). The sentences come from one table keyed by the violation code (`archive_corrupt` and `archive_entry_type`: "That isn't an app package. Use a `.tar.gz` made with `aurora plugin pack`." (P5-REFINE-20: the brief's words; the dashboard sets both backticked words as code); `file_missing`: "The package is missing <path>."; `icon_not_png` and `icon_not_square`: "The icon must be a square PNG."; `path_*`: "The package has a file with an unsafe name: <path>."; `guide_not_utf8`: "The setup guide must be plain UTF-8 text."; manifest codes: the validator's message without the code word; unknown codes: "The package has a problem: <path>."); `413 package_too_large` says "That package is too large (limit <N> MB)." The raw `code`s are unchanged and stay the machine contract. (Built: the 413's error code stays `payload_too_large` as before; only its message changes, also when the multipart body itself is over the limit. Further sentences: `archive_link` as `archive_corrupt`; `icon_too_small` as `icon_not_square`; `icon_too_large`, `guide_too_large`, `manifest_not_utf8`, `manifest_too_large`; the signature codes (`signature_*`, `key_revoked`, `digest_mismatch`, `signer_not_source_key`, `builtin_not_project_signed`): "The package isn't signed by a key this server trusts." Stored violations of an ingested package carry `sentence` too.)
- C-1072 **Storage by what people store, and who may create an organization** (P5-REFINE-6; extends C-172, C-1054).
  - `GET /v1/admin/storage` and `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/storage` gain `categories: { id: "conversations" | "files" | "activity", bytes: number }[]` (always the three, in that order): **conversations** = `transcript_events` (+ `transcript_search`), **files** = `blobs` + `attachments`, **activity** = `request_log` + `audit_log`; the sum of the tables of each group from the same snapshot as `tables` (which stays, for the technical view). The cached snapshot JSON (`attachments::snapshot_*`) deserializes with `#[serde(default)]` so snapshots written before this change still read (and show zero categories until refreshed). (Built: `bytes` is `number | null`. `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/storage` counts **files only** (the organization's live attachment bytes, `used_bytes`); its `conversations` and `activity` are `null`, since no per-organization size can be computed from the table sizes. The instance `tables` now also lists `transcript_search`, and each table carries its `category`.)
  - `GET /v1/me` gains `can_create_org: bool`: true for an instance admin or when `auth.org_creation = anyone` (the same rule `POST /v1/orgs` enforces, `403 org_creation_forbidden` otherwise), so the switcher offers "New organization" exactly when it would work. `POST /v1/orgs` for a person who already belongs to an organization is allowed (a test proves it; nothing about it changes).

#### P5-REFINE-7 — conversations and automation runs (D-409; migration `20261201000262`)

- C-1073 **Several direct conversations per person and agent** (P5-REFINE-7, D-409; migration `20261201000262`; amends D-230 and the one-direct-conversation rule of `05` and `10`).
  - **Schema:** `DROP INDEX conversations_direct_user_uq` (one direct conversation per `(agent, person)` ends); `CREATE INDEX conversations_direct_person_idx ON conversations (agent_id, direct_user_id, last_activity_at DESC) WHERE kind = 'direct'`; `conversations.title_source text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'auto' CHECK (title_source IN ('auto','person','automation'))`. A direct conversation's `external_ref` is `user:<uid>` (the first, as before) or `user:<uid>:<uuid>` (every later one). `Conversation` gains `title_source: "auto" | "person" | "automation"`.
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/conversations/direct` (session; operation `getOrCreateDirectConversation`) stays get-or-create and now returns the person's **most recently active non-archived** direct conversation with the agent, creating the first one (`user:<uid>`) when there is none (it never returns an archived one: UX review 5 chat-1).
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/conversations/direct/new` (session; `can_use`; operation `createDirectConversation`): a fresh direct conversation with empty history. **200** with the person's latest conversation when it is still empty (no events: no pile of empty chats), **201** with the new one otherwise; 422 `conversation_limit` past 500 direct conversations (active and archived) per person and agent (`DIRECT_CONVERSATIONS_PER_PERSON_AGENT`).
  - `PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}` body `{ title: string | null }` (operation `updateConversation`): renames; 1–200 characters trimmed, control characters refused (422 `invalid_title`), `null` clears the person's title and re-derives the auto title. Callers: the person a `direct` conversation belongs to (workspace owners cannot rename it), a workspace owner for `group`/`api`; `409 title_managed` for `schedule` and `webhook` threads (their title follows the automation, C-1074). A rename sets `title_source = 'person'`; audited `conversation.rename` (ids only, never the title).
  - **Auto-title:** when the first **person** turn of a conversation with `title IS NULL` and `title_source = 'auto'` is created, the title becomes the first line of the text with whitespace collapsed, markup removed, cut at 60 characters with "…" (no model call); a title the person set is never replaced.
  - **Privacy is unchanged:** a direct conversation is visible to its person and to workspace owners (`05 §Conversation visibility`); `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/conversations` still lists others' direct conversations to owners only (`direct_user` set). **D-230's search scope** (the speaker's own direct conversation) now reads "the speaker's own direct **conversations**" (all of them), for `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/messages/search` (C-727) and for the agent tools `conversation_search` / `conversation_read` (C-728); other members' direct conversations are still never searchable by the agent. Existing tests of both are extended.
- C-1074 **One thread per automation run, folded under its automation** (P5-REFINE-7, D-409; migration `20261201000262`; amends `10 §Schedules` and `10 §Webhooks`; keeps FIX-192 and C-1053).
  - **Schema:** `conversations.automation_kind text NULL CHECK (automation_kind IN ('schedule','webhook'))`, `automation_id uuid NULL` (no foreign key; both null or both set), `automation_deleted_at timestamptz NULL`; `CREATE INDEX conversations_automation_idx ON conversations (automation_kind, automation_id, last_activity_at DESC) WHERE automation_id IS NOT NULL`. **Backfill:** each existing `kind = 'schedule'` / `'webhook'` conversation whose `external_ref` is `schedule:<uuid>` / `webhook:<uuid>` gets `automation_kind`/`automation_id`; when that schedule or webhook no longer exists it gets `automation_deleted_at = now()`; `title_source = 'automation'`.
  - **Runs:** each schedule run (a fire or Run now) creates its **own** conversation `external_ref = schedule:<schedule_id>:<run_id>` (`kind = 'schedule'`, `automation_kind = 'schedule'`, `automation_id`, `title` = the schedule's name, `origin_conversation_id` = the schedule's origin, so the FIX-192 mirror still finds the origin's thread link); each **accepted** webhook delivery creates its own `webhook:<webhook_id>:<delivery_id>`. A run or delivery is never appended to an older conversation. `schedules.run_conversation_id` / `webhooks.run_conversation_id` keep pointing at the latest thread. Earlier threads (`schedule:<id>`) stay as the oldest of their automation.
  - **Busy rule (replaces "the run conversation is busy"):** a schedule fire is skipped `skipped_busy`, and Run now answers `409 schedule_busy`, while the schedule's **latest run's turn is still active** (`queued`, `dispatched`, `running`); webhook deliveries are never skipped for being busy (each has its own thread) but stay under the per-webhook rate limit.
  - `Conversation` gains `automation: { kind: "schedule" | "webhook", id, name, deleted: bool } | null` (`name` read from the schedule or webhook now, so a rename shows at once; the stored `title` follows, see below) and `run_at: string | null` (the thread's `created_at` for automation threads).
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/conversations` gains `automation=all|none|only` (default `all`: today's behaviour for API clients) and `automation_id=<uuid>` (with `only`); the dashboard asks `automation=none` for the main list. New `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/conversation-folders` (any caller who can use the agent; paged by `cursor`, newest activity first; operation `listConversationFolders`) → `{ items: { kind, id, name, deleted: bool, thread_count: number, last_activity_at, last_result: RunResult | null }[] }`, one item per automation that has threads (the folders the list shows under "Automations"); a folder's threads page through `GET …/conversations?automation=only&automation_id=`.
  - **Title sync and the deleted marker:** renaming a schedule or webhook updates `title` of all its threads (one `UPDATE … WHERE automation_kind AND automation_id`, `title_source = 'automation'`); deleting one sets `automation_deleted_at = now()` on its threads (their transcripts stay) and the dashboard marks them "Schedule deleted" / "Webhook deleted" (`automation.deleted`). Retention and the agent's `conversation_search` scope treat these threads as today's schedule and webhook conversations (non-direct, visible to anyone who can use the agent).
  - **Volume guard:** automation threads older than the organization's retention window are removed by the existing retention job like any conversation; an automation's threads are additionally capped at `AUTOMATION_THREADS_KEPT = 200` newest (older ones are deleted by the same job; their run rows then answer `conversation_id: null`).
- C-1075 **Stop leaves a mark and keeps what was written** (P5-REFINE-7, D-409; amends `10 §The answer rule`; UX review 5 chat-4). When a turn ends `aborted` (a queued turn aborted at once, or a running turn the supervisor ended after `POST /v1/turns/{id}/abort`), the API appends, in the same transaction as `turn.finished`, a durable `notice` event `{ code: "turn_stopped", text: "You stopped this answer.", turn_id }` **after** the answer buffer was drained into durable `text` events (the answer rule already writes the partial text on every ending, `lifecycle::drain_answer`; the text event of an aborted turn carries `partial: true`). `Turn` already exposes `abort_requested`; the dashboard treats `abort_requested = true` as "stopped" at once (header Ready, composer free) and shows the marker when the durable event arrives. The brief measures abort latency (the 10–25 s the review saw between Stop and `turn.finished`) and reports where the time goes; a fix that is a few lines (an abort frame that is queued late) is in scope, a redesign is not.
- C-1076 **An error shows once** (P5-REFINE-7; amends `10 §The answer rule`; UX review 5 chat-7). A failed turn's durable events no longer repeat the harness's error line as the agent's answer: when a turn ends `failed` and the buffered answer text is the harness's own error text (it equals, contains or starts with the failure `message`, or starts with `API Error`), `drain_answer` does not write it as a `text` event; the one `error` event carries it as `detail`. The `error` event payload is `{ turn_id, code, message, detail? }`: `message` keeps the existing text (clients that read it are unchanged), `detail` is the technical line (≤ 2,000 characters, sanitised) the dashboard shows under Details; the person-facing sentence for `code` ("Something went wrong. Try again", and the specific ones) is the dashboard's `lib/labels.ts`. A text the agent wrote on its own before failing is kept.
- C-1077 **Run now, Runs and schedule times in words** (P5-REFINE-7, D-409, D-407; amends the `05` schedule rows; UX review 5 schedules-3, schedules-4).
  - `ScheduleRun` gains `conversation_id: uuid | null` (the run's thread, from its turn), `result: RunResult` and `reason: string | null`, where `RunResult = "running" | "answered" | "skipped_by_gate" | "failed" | "skipped"`: stored `outcome` `fired` + turn `queued|dispatched|running` → `running`; `fired` + `done` → `answered`; `fired` + `failed|aborted` → `failed` (`reason` = the turn's `error_code`); `gated_quiet` → `skipped_by_gate`; `gate_failed` → `failed` (`reason: "gate_failed"`); `skipped_unauthorized` → `skipped` (`reason: "unauthorized"`); `skipped_frozen` → `skipped` (`reason: "frozen"`); `coalesced` → `skipped` (`reason: "missed"`). `outcome` stays unchanged for compatibility. No new column: the result is derived by joining the run's turn.
  - `POST /v1/schedules/{id}/run-now` → `201 ScheduleRun` with `conversation_id` and `result: "running"` (so "Started · Open the conversation" needs no second request); 409 `schedule_busy` per C-1074.
  - `Schedule` gains `next_runs: string[]` (the next **three** instants after now, RFC 3339 UTC; empty when paused; the dashboard formats them in `timezone`) and `last_result: RunResult | null` (the newest run's result). `Schedule.timezone` and `cron` are unchanged.
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/schedules/preview` (S/K, `can_use`; rate class `check`; operation `previewSchedule`) body `{ cron: string, timezone?: string }` → `200 { next: string[] (three instants), interval_secs: number | null, too_frequent: bool, min_interval_secs: 300 }` or `422 invalid_cron` / `invalid_timezone` with the field's message; it saves nothing. `too_frequent` is true when the smallest gap among the next 12 instants is below `MIN_INTERVAL` (5 minutes): create and update still refuse it exactly as today, the preview lets the dashboard say it as you type.
- C-1078 **A webhook is signed by default, with a secret Aurora makes** (P5-REFINE-7, D-407; amends the `05` webhook rows, SEC-31 unchanged; UX review 5 webhooks-1, webhooks-2).
  - `CreateWebhookRequest.verification` absent means `{ kind: "hmac_sha256", generate_secret: true }` (before: none). `VerificationInput` gains `generate_secret?: bool` (kind `hmac_sha256` or `bearer`; mutually exclusive with `secret`, 422 otherwise): the API makes 32 random bytes as 64 hex characters, seals it as any secret and returns it **once**. An explicit `{ kind: "none" }` stays allowed (the dashboard calls it "Anyone with the address").
  - `WebhookWithUrl` (create and rotate) gains `secret?: string`, present only when one was generated on this call.
  - `POST /v1/webhooks/{id}/rotate-secret` (same callers as `rotate`; operation `rotateWebhookSecret`) → `200 { webhook: Webhook, secret: string }`: new secret, the old one stops working at once; only for kinds `hmac_sha256` and `bearer` (422 `verification_not_signed` for `none`); audited `webhook.rotate_secret` (no value). `PATCH` with `generate_secret: true` is refused 422 (use this route).
  - `Webhook.verification` always carries the **effective** `header` (`X-Signature` for `hmac_sha256`, `Authorization` for `bearer`, null for `none`) and gains `format: "sha256=<hex of the HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body, keyed with the secret>" | "Bearer <secret>" | null`, so the dashboard's "How to call it" panel (curl, header, signature recipe) needs no guess. The URL itself is still shown only on create and rotate.
- C-1079 **Deliveries in words, and the message people read** (P5-REFINE-7, D-409; migration `20261201000262` for `duplicate`; amends the `05` webhook rows; UX review 5 webhooks-3, webhooks-4).
  - `webhook_deliveries.status` also allows `duplicate` (`DROP`/`ADD` the CHECK): a repeated delivery (same `Idempotency-Key`, delivery id or body hash inside the window) is now **recorded** (before: dropped silently) with `turn_id` of the original's turn, answered as today (`202` with the original `delivery_id`), and counted in the per-webhook rate limit.
  - `WebhookDelivery` gains `result: "answered" | "running" | "failed" | "refused_signature" | "refused_too_large" | "refused_paused" | "refused_frozen" | "refused_unauthorized" | "duplicate"`, `conversation_id: uuid | null` (the delivery's own thread, from its turn) and `duplicate_of: uuid | null` (a duplicate row stores the original delivery's id in `reason`); `status` (`accepted | rejected_signature | rejected_size | disabled | unauthorized`, plus `duplicate`) stays. `result` for an accepted delivery is derived from its turn exactly as `RunResult` (running / answered / failed); the others from `status` and `reason`.
  - **The message people read:** the `turn.queued` event of a schedule or webhook turn gains `display` in its payload: `{ kind: "webhook", name, delivery_id, content_type, size, truncated, body }` for a webhook (`body` is the sanitised, clamped payload text; **no** "UNTRUSTED EXTERNAL DATA" wrapper, no instructions) and `{ kind: "schedule", name, schedule_id, run_id }` for a schedule. The turn's own `text` (the wrapper, the owner's instructions, the fenced payload; SEC-31, FIX-110) is **unchanged and still what the agent receives**; only the transcript's event gains the display object. Old events have none (the dashboard falls back to `text`). `NewMessage` gains an optional `display` field written into that event.

---


#### P5-REFINE-8 — requests and attention (D-417, D-407; migration `20261201000263`)

- C-1080 **One attention feed per level** (P5-REFINE-8, D-417, D-407; extends C-1022). The same item shape and the same word *attention* at three levels. Each route returns only what the caller can act on at that level (plus, for the asker, their own waiting requests, never counted).
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/attention?state=open|done` (any active member of `ws`; 404 for a non-member). Kinds: `approval` (a pending gateway or action approval the caller may decide, `can_approve`), `access_request` (an agent asked for an app or website, open, `can_use`/`can_own` as C-981's access-request list), `broken_account` (an account that agents of this workspace use and that is `failing` or `sign_in_expired`, D-383, that the caller can fix; else, for everyone else with `can_use`, the same item with `action: null` and the sentence "Ask <role>"), and **`my_request`** (the caller's own open setup request, `own: true`, never counted: "Waiting for an admin since Tuesday"). A viewer who cannot decide approvals sees none.
  - `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/attention?state=open|done` (organization owners and admins; others 404/403 as the org routes). Kinds: `app_request`, `chat_platform_request`, `ai_key_request` whose resolving level is `organization` (below), plus `ownerless_org` is **not** here (it stays in Administration).
  - `GET /v1/admin/attention?state=open|done` (instance admins; 403 otherwise; amends C-1022). The six kinds of C-1022 are unchanged, plus `app_request` and `chat_platform_request` whose resolving level is `administration`.
  - **Item** `AttentionItem` (the C-1022 fields are kept, so today's Administration page and badge read it unchanged): `{ id: string, kind, level: "workspace" | "organization" | "administration", topic: string, subject_id: string, title: string, detail: string, since: string (date-time), href: string, decision: boolean, actors: { user_id: string, display_name: string }[] (at most 5 askers, oldest first), actors_total: integer, reason: string | null, own: boolean, action: { label: string, href: string } | null, can_decline: boolean, state: "open" | "done" | "declined", closed_at: string | null, message: string | null, closed_by_role: "org_admin" | "instance_admin" | "system" | null, closed_by: { display_name: string } | null }`.
    - `id` is stable: the request id for request kinds, `<kind>:<subject_id>` for the rest. `topic` names the page of the level's rail that resolves the item (`chat-platforms`, `apps`, `ai-models`, `people`, `approvals`, `accounts`), so a rail can badge a topic.
    - `title` and `detail` are the fixed sentences of `14 §9` filled with display names and subject names only (SEC-135). Text that points to an administrator names the **role** ("Ask an admin", D-407). `title` for requests: "Bob Builder wants Jira", "Bob Builder and Carol Member want a chat platform", "Bob Builder, Carol Member and 2 others want an AI key".
    - `reason` is the **first asker's** reason (C-1081) or null; the other askers' reasons are on the request (C-1081). `own` is true only on `my_request`.
    - `action` is the one primary action: `{ label: "Set up", href }`, `"Connect a chat platform"`, `"Add an AI key"`, `"Review"` (approvals: opens the conversation), `"Sign in again"` / `"Fix"` (accounts). `href` is the existing field and equals `action.href` when `action` is set. `can_decline` is true on request kinds the caller may decline (C-1081).
    - `state` is `open` unless `state=done` was asked. `done` returns items that closed in the last **30 days** (`REQUEST_HISTORY_DAYS = 30`): `state: "done"` (resolved or decided) or `"declined"`, with `closed_at`, `message` (the decline message, C-1081), `closed_by_role` and, only for viewers who may act at that level, `closed_by`. A viewer who is the asker sees the role, never the admin's name.
    - Done sources: request kinds from `app_setup_requests` (`resolved`, `declined`; `withdrawn` is not listed); `approval` from `approvals`/`action_approvals` decided in the window (`state: "done"`, `closed_by_role: null`); Administration's six C-1022 kinds from `admin_attention_notices` (kept 30 days) joined with the subject's current status when it is no longer pending (approved, rejected, removed); a kind with no clean current-status source simply has no done rows. `broken_account` and `access_request` open only (access requests given or dismissed are `done`).
  - **Response** `AttentionList { items: AttentionItem[], count: integer }`. `count` is the number the rail shows: the open items **with `own: false`**, after the rule below. Order: decisions first, then oldest first (as C-1022); `done` newest first. No pagination (at most 200 items; the rest are summarized by `count`).
  - **One request, one level.** A request appears at exactly **one** level, the level that can resolve it, so no request is counted twice:
    - `app`: `administration` when the app's readiness `who_can.role` (C-1029/C-981) is `instance_admin` (a server, a package not approved, switched off for the instance); else `organization`;
    - `chat_platform`: `administration` when no connector of the asked type (any when none is named) is `active` on the instance (only an instance admin can add one); else `organization`;
    - `ai_key`: `organization`.
    The level is **computed on read**, not stored. An organization owner who is also an instance admin sees the item at its level only.
  - **Overview versus topic.** The level's Overview carries the total (`count`); a topic entry shows a badge only for its own items (C-1082 `topics`). Both numbers count the same request once per place by design: the Overview is the total, the topic a part of it.
  - Audit: none (a read). The sentences are the ones C-1022 and C-1030 already fixed, plus the request kinds above.
  - **Corrections at build (P5-REFINE-8; the code differs from the draft):**
    - *The app rule.* C-1029's readiness never names `instance_admin` today: every need it reports (`oauth_client`: an organization-level sign-in app; `instance`: an organization-level server; `admin_material`; an app switched off for the organization) is fixed by an organization owner or admin, and an app whose package is not approved is not in the catalogue, so it cannot be asked for. The level of an app request is computed from the request's stored `needs`/`unavailable` (C-1030; re-evaluated on every readiness change and every 10 minutes) through one table *need → role that fixes it* (`app_setup_requests::level`), which is the same table `setup.rs` uses for `who_can`; today every row of it is `org_admin`, so **every app request is `organization`**. A need that only an instance admin can fix, when one is added, is one row of that table and moves its requests to `administration` with no other change. The chat-platform and AI-key rules are as drafted (the connector check is the same `EXISTS` as C-1047's notice routing, computed in the query).
    - *`broken_account`* is every account of the workspace's accounts list (its own and the organization's, `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/accounts`) whose health is `failing` or `sign_in_expired` (the set the Overview's Needs you card reads today, `14 §7`), not only accounts given to an agent. `action` is "Sign in again" (`sign_in_expired`) or "Fix" (`failing`) for whoever may manage the account (workspace accounts: `can_own`; organization accounts: organization owners and admins); everyone else gets `action: null`, the detail ending "Ask a workspace owner." or "Ask an organization admin.", and the item **is counted** (C-1080's rule: open items with `own: false`).
    - **`agent_cannot_answer`** (P5-REFINE-20 batch 4 item 3, D-384, D-408, C-1062; a workspace kind added to the draft). A hosted agent of the workspace that is not paused and whose answer check (`hosted::answer::reason_of`, the dispatcher's own readiness, the same facts as `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/answer-readiness`) gives a reason. Shown, and **counted**, only to viewers who can own the workspace (the people who can give the key: D-384, amber only where the viewer has an action). **A poll never calls a provider**: the check is `reason_of_cached` (key listings from the cache only; a `litellm` key whose listing is not cached is "listing unknown" and never yields a reason by itself, so no false item), and at most 50 running hosted agents of the workspace are checked per call. A plain member sees none and counts none (their own agent page still says "Can't answer"). `subject_id` is the agent id, `id` `agent_cannot_answer:<agent id>`, `topic` `agents`, `decision: false` (not e-mailed or pushed), open only (no `done` rows). `title` "<Agent> can't answer: <reason>" with the reason in words: `no AI key` (`no_ai_key`), `the key stopped working` (`key_not_usable`), `choose a model` (`no_model`); `detail` says what fixes it ("Give it an AI key so it can reply.", "Its AI key does not work any more. Give it another or fix the key.", "No allowed AI model is chosen for it."); `action` `{ label: "Fix", href: <the agent's Access tab> }`. The workspace `count` and `topics.agents` include it, so the Overview badge, the Needs you feed and the rail agree.
    - *`access_request`* is shown to viewers who can own the workspace (the people who can give the access or dismiss it); a plain member sees none. The agent's own words (`AccessRequest.reason`) are **not** copied into the item (`reason: null`): the title names the agent and the app or website only.
    - *`approval`*: gateway approvals the caller may decide (`approvals.may_decide`, the direct-conversation visibility rule included) and action approvals of the workspace when the caller `can_approve`; `done` lists both decided in the last 30 days.
    - *Done request actors.* A request resolves asker by asker (C-1030: an asker for whom the thing is ready leaves the request with a `resolved` notice). A done item's `actors` are its remaining askers plus the people who received its `resolved` notice; a resolved asker's `reason` is gone with their ask, so `reason` is null on resolved items. The asker's own history lists a request when they received its `resolved` notice (`closed_at` = that notice's time) or when it closed `declined` with their ask on it.
    - *Administration `done`* lists the request kinds only: the six C-1022 kinds have no clean "closed" record yet (their notices are written once when an item appears, not when it is decided), so they have no done rows (allowed above).
    - *`action` of the six C-1022 kinds:* "Review" for the four decisions, "Open" for `connector_down` and `ownerless_org`, `href` as C-1022. `topic`: `chat-platforms` (connector kinds), `apps` (package, key, host-claim kinds), `people` (`ownerless_org`); requests: `apps`, `chat-platforms`, `ai-models`; `approval` → `approvals`; `access_request`, `broken_account` → `accounts`; `my_request` → its subject's topic.
    - *Sentences* (one place, `domains/attention/text.rs`): requests "Bob Builder wants Jira" / "… want a chat platform" / "…, Carol Member and 2 others want an AI key"; app detail by need, naming the role: "Needs a sign-in app for Jira that only an organization admin can register.", "Needs a Jira server that only an organization admin can register.", "Needs set-up that only an organization admin can do.", "Jira is switched off for the organization; only an organization admin can switch it on."; chat platform "Your organization has no chat platform connected." (with a named type: "Your organization has no Slack connected."); AI key "Your organization has no AI key its agents can use."; `my_request` "You asked for Jira" / "Waiting for an organization admin." (or "an instance admin"), done "An admin isn't setting up Jira now" / "Jira is ready"; `approval` "<Agent> is waiting for your approval" / the app's name or the host; `access_request` "<Agent> asks for Jira" (or "for a token for <host>") / "Give it access from the agent's Apps & access tab."; `broken_account` "<Account> needs you" / the health clause ("the sign-in expired", "it stopped working").
- C-1081 **Requests carry a reason and a decline message; history; the asker sees their own** (P5-REFINE-8, D-417, D-407; amends C-1030, C-1047 and SEC-135; migration `20261201000263`).
  - **Reason on an ask.** `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/requests` (C-1047) and `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/apps/{app}/setup-request` (C-1030) accept an **optional** body field `reason?: string` (plain text, 1..300 characters after trimming; control characters and the invisible formatting characters of `sanitize::is_stripped` are removed; line breaks kept; 400 `invalid_request` when longer). It is stored **per asker** (`app_setup_request_askers.reason`). A repeat ask by the same person with a new `reason` replaces their reason (200, no new notice, no new audit row). `POST …/setup-request` had no body: a missing body is still valid.
  - **Decline with a message.** `POST /v1/orgs/{org}/app-setup-requests/{id}/decline` takes an optional body `{ message?: string }` (same rules, at most 300). `closed_message` and `closed_by_role` are stored on the request. Each asker gets one notice (push and mail as C-1030) whose title is "An admin isn't setting up <Thing> now" (role, not a name, D-407) and whose detail carries the message when there is one ("They wrote: <message>"). The mail and the push carry the first 140 characters of the message.
  - **Instance admins resolve Administration-level requests.** `GET /v1/admin/requests?status=open|history` (instance admins) lists the requests whose level (C-1080) is `administration`; `POST /v1/admin/requests/{id}/decline` (instance admins, same optional body, `closed_by_role: "instance_admin"`) closes one. A request of the organization level is declined only through the organization route; each route answers 404 `not_found` for a request of the other level (nothing is revealed).
  - **History.** `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/app-setup-requests?status=history` (owners and admins) returns the requests closed (`resolved` or `declined`) in the last 30 days, newest first, **every level** of the organization (so an organization admin can read "Waiting for an instance admin" on a request that is theirs to watch but not to resolve). `status=open|resolved|declined|withdrawn` are unchanged. Closed rows are still purged by the retention sweep after 90 days (`03`); the **display** window is 30 days.
  - **The asker's own list.** `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/requests?status=open|history` (any active member; default `open`, as C-1047). With `status=history` it returns the caller's own requests closed in the last 30 days, each with only their own ask, `status`, `message` and `closed_by_role` (never the admin's name).
  - **`AppSetupRequest`** (C-1030/C-1047) gains: `level: "organization" | "administration"` (always present), `message: string | null` (the decline message), `closed_by_role: "org_admin" | "instance_admin" | "system" | null`, `closed_by: { display_name: string } | null` (only for owners, admins and instance admins reading the list), and `Asker` gains `reason: string | null` (the asker's own reason; owners, admins and instance admins see every asker's; a member sees only their own ask). **Names only:** an e-mail is never returned, as before.
  - **SEC-135 amendment:** the request is no longer "free text free". The two free-text fields are `reason` (by the asker, shown to the people who may resolve the request) and `message` (by the decliner, shown to the askers). Both are plain text, bounded to 300 characters, sanitized on write, rendered by the web as text (never as markup or a link), and never given to an agent or put in a prompt. Everything else in SEC-135 stands.
  - Storage (`03`): `app_setup_request_askers.reason text NULL CHECK (reason IS NULL OR char_length(reason) BETWEEN 1 AND 300)`; `app_setup_requests.closed_message text NULL` (same check) and `closed_by_role text NULL CHECK (closed_by_role IN ('org_admin','instance_admin','system'))`. Migration `20261201000263` backfills `closed_by_role`: `declined` with a `closed_by` → `org_admin`; `resolved` → `system`; others null. The gateway role keeps no grant on these tables.
  - Audit: `app_setup_request.ask` metadata gains `has_reason: bool`; `app_setup_request.decline` gains `has_message: bool` and `closed_by_role`. The text itself is not audited.
  - **Corrections at build (P5-REFINE-8):** `?status=history` on the organization list is a new value next to `open|resolved|declined|withdrawn` (400 for anything else, as before). The asker's history is described under C-1080's corrections (a request resolved for them while it stays open for others is in their history from the `resolved` notice). A re-ask **without** a `reason` keeps the stored one (only a new reason replaces it). The 140-character cut of the message in the mail and the push is on a character boundary and ends with "…". `closed_by` is the decliner of the reader's own level only: the organization list carries it for a request an organization owner or admin declined, never for one an instance admin declined (C-981: an instance admin is never named outside Administration; the list says `closed_by_role: "instance_admin"`); the Administration list and feed carry it for both. The admin routes answer 404 `not_found` both for an unknown id and for a request of the organization level; the organization's decline answers 404 for an Administration-level request.
- C-1082 **Rail counts** (P5-REFINE-8, D-417). `GET /v1/attention/counts?org=<id>&workspace=<id>` (any signed-in person; both parameters optional). It returns only the scopes the caller can act on and asked for: `{ administration: { count: integer, topics: { [topic: string]: integer } } | null, organization: { org_id, count, topics } | null, workspace: { workspace_id, count, topics } | null }`. `null` for a scope the caller cannot act on or did not ask for (never a 403 or a 404, so a rail can ask for all and show what comes back). `count` is C-1080's `count` for that level; `topics` is the number of open items per `topic`. It is cheap by design: one query per level over the same tables as C-1080, no sentence building, no names. The dashboard calls it from the shell for the rails (Administration entry, the organization's rail, the workspace's rail) on window focus and every 5 minutes, and invalidates it when the person decides, declines or resolves an item; `GET /v1/admin/attention` is still the page's data.
  - **Notifications are unchanged** (C-1022 e-mail and push, C-1030 mail and push): they say when a thing first appears. The requests of the Administration level (chat platform without a connector, an app that needs the instance) already notify instance admins (C-1047); this contract only adds the feed and the counts.

#### P5-REFINE-9 — agent timeline (D-415)

- C-1083 **The agent timeline** (P5-REFINE-9, D-415). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/timeline` (`can_use` of the agent's workspace; 404 as every agent route; BYO agents have no transcript events but still list their request log and approvals rows) → `TimelinePage { items: TimelineItem[], next_cursor: string | null }` (operation `getAgentTimeline`). Query: `limit` (1..100, default 50; 400 `invalid_request` outside), `cursor` (opaque; older rows), `kind` (comma-separated `TimelineKind`s; an unknown one is 400), `outcome` (comma-separated outcome words). Newest first; the keyset is `(at, id)` over the union. A page may hold fewer than `limit` rows while `next_cursor` is not null (see Pagination below).
  - **Corrections at build (P5-REFINE-9):** no `newest_cursor` (no route reads it; newer rows come from C-1084); `kind`/`outcome` are comma-separated (the query extractor takes no repeated keys); `id`s of `called` rows are `call:<turn_id>:<app_id|host>:<outcome>` (one row per outcome, so a blocked call never hides in an allowed count) and `call:<request_id>` for a request without a turn; `link.type` adds `action_approval`; outcomes add `abandoned` (`asked_before`: the request went away before anyone answered) and `not_approved` (`called`: `approval_denied`/`approval_expired`); `model` is the model **id** (`request_log.llm_model`, checked with `MODEL_ID_PATTERN`): the endpoint configuration has no per-model display names, and the provider's model listing is a network call the feed does not make; turns of **automation threads** (`conversations.automation_kind` set) have no `answered` row: their `ran_schedule` / `received_webhook` row carries the turn's `model`, `count` (tool calls) and `duration_ms`, and its `link` is the run's conversation when the viewer may open it, else `run`/`delivery`; `eval` conversations are not on the timeline. Exact outcome words per kind: `answered` `answered | failed | stopped`; `computer` `ok | failed`; `asked_before` `allowed_once | always_allowed | blocked | expired | abandoned` (Always allow is read from the approval's `approval.decided` event); `called` `allowed | blocked_by_rule | blocked_default | not_approved | rate_limited | failed`; `ran_schedule` C-1077's `RunResult` without `running` (`answered | skipped_by_gate | failed | skipped`); `received_webhook` C-1079's `result` without `running` (`answered | failed | refused_signature | refused_too_large | refused_paused | refused_frozen | refused_unauthorized | duplicate`); `handed_task`/`got_task` `completed | cancelled | expired`; `asked_for_access` `given | dismissed | expired`. `outcome` is null while `state` is `running` or `waiting`.
  - **Computer rows at build:** a `computer` row is a command and the files written after it in the same turn (writes before any command are a row of their own), `id` `computer:<turn_id>:<seq of its first step>`; beyond three rows per turn the rest fold into the third (its `files` and `count` grow, its `command` stays the third command). `count` = the distinct files written; `files` the first five, workspace-relative (`/workspace/notes.txt` → `notes.txt`); `link` → `file` (`id` = the agent, `path` = the absolute `/workspace/…` path) when the row wrote exactly one file inside `/workspace`, else the conversation at the first step's `seq`. Commands and paths pass a credential filter (long tokens, known key prefixes, `Bearer`/`-u` arguments, `token=`/`password=` assignments → `[redacted]`); a redacted path never links. Redacted transcript events are never read; `tool.finished`'s summary (the tool's output) is never read.
  - **`TimelineItem`**: `{ id: string, at: string (date-time), ended_at: string | null, state: "running" | "waiting" | "done" | "failed" | "blocked" | "skipped", kind: TimelineKind, outcome: string | null, agent: { id: string, name: string }, person: { display_name: string } | null, app: { id: string, name: string } | null, tool: string | null, host: string | null, rule: { logical_id: string, name: string } | null, model: string | null, schedule: { id: string, name: string } | null, webhook: { id: string, name: string } | null, peer: { id: string, name: string } | null, count: integer | null, command: string | null, files: string[] | null, duration_ms: integer | null, link: { type: "conversation" | "approval" | "run" | "delivery" | "file" | "rule" | "app" | "peer_task", id: string, seq?: integer, path?: string } | null }`. The API returns **structured kinds and names, never sentences**; the web writes the sentence from `lib/labels.ts` (D-415). `id` is stable across updates of one row: `turn:<turn_id>`, `approval:<id>`, `action_approval:<id>`, `call:<turn_id|request key>:<app_id|host>`, `run:<id>`, `delivery:<id>`, `computer:<turn_id>:<seq>`, `peer:<task_id>`, `access:<id>`.
  - **Kinds and outcomes** (`TimelineKind`, `outcome`):
    - `answered` (a turn finished or running; `outcome` `answered | failed | stopped`; `model` is the display name of the AI model the turn's LLM request used (the most frequent `request_log.llm_model` of the turn, else null); `count` = the turn's tool calls; `link` → the conversation and the turn's last `seq`);
    - `computer` (a command the agent ran or files it wrote on its computer: `command` is the first line, at most 120 characters; `files` at most 5 paths (the agent's workspace-relative paths), `count` the number of files; `outcome` `ok | failed`; `link` → `file` with `path` for a single written file, else the conversation);
    - `asked_before` (an approval: `state` `waiting` while pending, then `done`; `outcome` `allowed_once | always_allowed | blocked | expired`; `person` the decider; `app`, `tool`, `host`, `rule` of the held request; `link` → `approval`);
    - `called` (non-model requests from the request log folded by turn and app or host: `count` requests; `outcome` `allowed | blocked_by_rule | blocked_default | rate_limited | failed`; `rule` when a rule decided; `link` → `rule` or `app`);
    - `ran_schedule` (a schedule run: `schedule`; `outcome` as C-1076/C-1077 of P5-REFINE-7 define the run's outcome words (`answered | skipped_by_gate | failed`; `state` `running` while it runs); `link` → `run` and the run's conversation);
    - `received_webhook` (a delivery: `webhook`; `outcome` as C-1078/C-1079 (`accepted | refused_signature | duplicate | failed`); `link` → `delivery` and the conversation);
    - `handed_task` and `got_task` (a peer task, `peer` the other agent; `outcome` `completed | cancelled | expired`; `link` → `peer_task`);
    - `asked_for_access` (an agent access request; `app` or `host`; `outcome` `given | dismissed | expired`, `state` `waiting` while open; `link` → `app`).
  - **Folding.** Gateway calls with `llm_model` not null are **never** their own rows (the model call is part of `answered`); consecutive rows of one turn and one app or host become one `called` row; at most 3 `computer` rows per turn are separate, the rest fold into `count`. The Technical view (below) shows every request unfolded.
  - **Computer steps** come from the durable `tool.started` / `tool.finished` events (`10 §Transcript events`) whose `name` is a harness shell or file-writing tool; the API owns one table of those names per harness adapter (`claude-code`, `codex`, `pi`, `goose`, `opencode`: shell and write/edit tools; a read-only tool is not a `computer` row). `command` and `files` come from the event's already-sanitized `summary` (≤ 200 characters, no raw arguments); a summary that does not parse gives a row with `command: null` and `files: null`.
  - **Who sees what (privacy, RFE-009, D-409).** A row that comes from a conversation (`answered`, `computer`, `asked_before` and `called` of a turn, `asked_for_access`) is returned only when `can_see_conversation` holds for the caller: another person's `direct` conversation is omitted entirely, as `GET /workspaces/{ws}/activity` does today (workspace owners see them, as everywhere). Rows with no conversation (schedule runs, webhook deliveries, peer tasks) are workspace-visible to `can_use`. `command`, `files` and `host` of a row are returned only when the row's conversation is visible too; a `called` row from a conversation the caller cannot see is omitted, never reduced to a count. Nothing in a row is an e-mail, a secret, a header, a path with a credential or the body of a request.
  - **Live state.** A row that is `running` or `waiting` is updated in place by C-1084 (same `id`).
  - **Technical view** (Expert) is the existing `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/activity?agent_id=` (unchanged); only its live updates are new (C-1084).
  - Pagination and cost: each source is read newest first with the same keyset and `limit + 1` rows, merged in memory; a page is served from indexes that exist (`request_log (workspace_id, ts desc)`, `turns`, `transcript_events (conversation_id, seq)`, `schedule_runs (schedule_id, fired_at desc)`, `webhook_deliveries (webhook_id, created_at desc)`). A source query that cannot be bounded by agent and time is a defect. **At build:** migration `20261201000267` adds the indexes the sources need (`turns (agent_id, created_at desc, id desc) where role = 'turn'`, `transcript_events (turn_id, seq) where turn_id is not null`, `approvals`/`action_approvals`/`agent_access_requests (agent_id, created_at desc, id desc)`, `peer_tasks (from_agent_id, …)` and `(to_agent_id, …)`, `request_log (turn_id) where turn_id is not null`, `request_log (agent_id, ts desc) where turn_id is null`). A source that returned `limit + 1` rows has a horizon (its oldest row's time); rows are emitted down to the highest horizon only and `next_cursor` is then the horizon, so a row is never skipped or repeated even when a page ends early (turns that started below the horizon but were still running above it are read too, bounded by `TURN_CEILING`).
- C-1084 **The timeline stream** (P5-REFINE-9, D-415). `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/timeline/stream?technical=false` (session only, as `GET /v1/approvals/stream`; `can_use` of the agent's workspace; ends when access is revoked, `revoked()`), Server-sent events on the same SSE machinery and keep-alive as the conversation stream (`domains/conversations/routes.rs` `stream`): the chat page opens this one stream **per agent** beside the conversation stream; there is no polling.
  - Events (no SSE ids; the client refetches on `resync`): `event: row` with data `{ op: "upsert", item: TimelineItem }` (a new row, or the **same `id`** with a new `state`/`outcome`: "Running “Morning summary”…" turns into "Ran “Morning summary” · answered" in place); `event: request` with data `TimelineRequestEvent` (**SEC-143 send-back:** `{ id, ts, agent_id, method, host: string | null, decision, status, app_id, tool_id }`, no path, context label, secret or connection id; the host only when the request has a visible conversation; not sent for a request whose conversation the viewer cannot see or that belongs to an evaluation; sent only when `technical=true`, which the web sets when the Technical view is open; on it the Technical view reads the full row from `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/activity`, which has its own authorization). The OpenAPI body of the stream is `TimelineStreamEvents { row: TimelineRowEvent, request: TimelineRequestEvent }`, and 403 is documented on both routes; `event: resync` `{}` (notifications may have been missed: the listener reconnected, the receiver lagged, or the 30-second heartbeat check found drift). The privacy rules of C-1083 apply to every event.
  - **How it is fed.** One new database channel `aurora_timeline` (`Channel::Timeline` in `crates/aurora-db/src/bus.rs`, added to `Channel::ALL` and to the listeners that enumerate channels) carries small hints `{ a: <agent_id>, s: "turn" | "request" | "approval" | "run" | "delivery" | "peer" | "access", i: <id>, q?: <seq> }` (≤ 200 bytes). Writers send the hint after the change commits: the API for runs, deliveries, peer tasks, approvals and access requests (in the transaction, as `append_durable_event` does); the gateway after each request-log flush (one hint per agent and flush, `status.rs`/`reqlog.rs`); turn events reuse the conversation channel's `{c, s}` hint (the stream maps the conversation to its agent). The stream turns a hint into the item(s) by loading the row by id, applying C-1083's rules, and emits `row`. A hint never carries text.
  - **Limits.** At most `TIMELINE_STREAMS_PER_USER` (5) open timeline streams per person **per API replica** (429 `rate_limited` beyond; the count is in the replica's memory); the stream **fails closed**: when the reader's access cannot be re-read (any error, not only a refusal) the stream ends; events for one stream are coalesced to at most one per row per 500 ms; the per-stream queue holds 200 events, then it sends `resync` and drops the queue.
  - **Corrections at build (P5-REFINE-9):** operation `streamAgentTimeline`; `technical` is `?technical=true`. Hint sources add `action_approval` (`s`), written by `repo::action_approvals` on create, decide, execute, fail and expire; gateway approvals need no second hint: the stream also listens to the existing `aurora_approval` channel (`{id, workspace_id}`) and loads the approval. A `request` hint is `{ a, s: "request", i: <the agent's newest row in the batch>, q: <the oldest ts of its rows, µs> }`; the stream reads the agent's rows from `q` (bounded, de-duplicated by id). The gateway only sends `aurora_timeline`, so its own listener list is unchanged. Schedule runs and webhook deliveries hint from `repo::schedules::insert_run_with_id` / `repo::webhooks::insert_delivery`; a run's later states follow its turn's transcript events. The stream re-reads the viewer's workspace access before every batch and every `STREAM_RECHECK` (losing `can_use` ends it; a role change applies at once). The 30-second check recomputes the newest 20 rows and sends `resync` only when one changed or appeared without an event. A row is sent again only when it changed.
  - Audit: none. Metrics: `aurora_timeline_streams` gauge, `aurora_timeline_events_total{kind}` counter.

#### P5-REFINE-10 — agent links created by people (D-411, SEC-142)

- C-1085 **People create agent links** (P5-REFINE-10, D-411, SEC-142; amends C-29 and `07 §Agent links`; migration `20261201000264`). Until now a link was created by the agent's first `message_agent` (`peer_tasks::send_message`). The agent cannot know which agents exist; a person creates each link.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/linkable` (`can_use` of the agent's workspace) → `{ items: { agent_id, name, handle, workspace_id, workspace_name, linked: boolean, hosted: boolean, frozen: boolean }[] }`: the agents of the **same organization** that the caller may link to, which are the agents of every workspace where the caller has `can_use` (an organization owner or admin: every workspace of the organization; a workspace API key: its own workspace only), except the agent itself. Frozen agents are listed with `frozen: true` and BYO agents with `hosted: false` (neither is linkable; they are listed so the web can say why). `linked: true` when a link exists already. Name, handle and workspace name only: nothing else about the other agent. Sorted by workspace name, then agent name; at most 200.
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/links` (`can_use` of `{agent}`'s workspace **and** `can_use` of the other agent's workspace) body `{ peer_agent_id: uuid, direction: "both" | "to_peer" | "from_peer" }` (`deny_unknown_fields`) → 201 `AgentLink` (200 with the existing link when the pair is already linked and `direction` equals it; 409 `link_exists` with the existing link's id when it differs; the change goes through `PUT …/direction`).
    - `direction` is read from `{agent}`'s side: `both` (either agent may hand the other a task), `to_peer` (`{agent}` hands tasks to the peer; the peer only works on what `{agent}` sends), `from_peer` (the reverse). It is stored on the pair as `agent_links.direction text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'both' CHECK (direction IN ('both','a_to_b','b_to_a'))` (a = the lower id, as the table orders them); existing links are `both`.
    - **Starting policies** (SEC-142): each side starts `allow` when the creator **`can_approve`** that side's workspace (the creator's act is the consent, FIX-110: `allow` is an approver's decision), else `ask`. The creator never sets `blocked`. A person who is only a member (`can_use`) on one side therefore leaves that side at `ask`, and the first message needs that side's approval as today.
    - Checks, in this order: `{agent}` visible (`can_use`, else 404); `peer_agent_id = {agent}` → 409 `link_self`; the peer exists, is in the same organization and the caller has `can_use` of its workspace (else 404; a workspace API key confined elsewhere also gets 404, not `workspace_mismatch`); either agent BYO → 409 `not_hosted`; either agent frozen → 423 `frozen` (details `level`).
    - Errors: 400 `invalid_request` (unknown `direction`, unknown field); 404 `not_found` for an agent the caller cannot see (the same answer for "no such agent" and "not yours", nothing revealed); 409 `link_self`; 409 `not_hosted` (a BYO agent); 409 `link_exists` (details `{ link_id }`); 423 `frozen` (either agent frozen); 409 `other_organization` is **not** a code: an agent of another organization is a 404.
    - Audit (critical, in the transaction, `agent_link.create`): target `agent_link`, workspace `{agent}`'s, metadata `{ agent_a_id, agent_b_id, direction, policy_a, policy_b, via: "person" }` (`direction` stored form). The idempotent 200 writes nothing.
  - `PUT /v1/agent-links/{id}/direction` body `{ agent_id, direction: "both" | "to_peer" | "from_peer", revision }` (`deny_unknown_fields`) → 200 `AgentLink` seen from `agent_id`. `agent_id` names the side the `direction` is read from (as in the create body, where it is `{agent}`); the caller needs `can_use` of `agent_id`'s workspace (404 otherwise, and 404 when `agent_id` is not one of the link's agents). **Widening** (the new direction lets an agent hand tasks it could not before: `to_peer`/`from_peer` → `both`, or `to_peer` ↔ `from_peer`) needs `can_approve` on **both** sides' workspaces (403 `approver_required` naming the side that lacks it), because it gives the other agent more reach; **narrowing** (`both` → one way) needs only that `can_use`. The same direction is a no-op (200, nothing written, revision unchanged). 409 `stale_revision` when `revision` differs. Audit `agent_link.direction` (critical, in the transaction): `{ agent_a_id, agent_b_id, from, to }` (stored forms). Tasks already open or queued are not affected by a change.
  - `DELETE /v1/agent-links/{id}` (`can_use` on **either** side's workspace: removing only restricts) → 204; unknown or not visible → 404. In one transaction, under the link's row lock: every **queued**, then every **open** peer task of the pair is closed with the platform's cancellation line to its origin conversation ("Task to <Peer> was cancelled: the link between these agents was removed."; the helper `peer_tasks::close_with_promotion` with `Close::Cancelled`, queued first so nothing is promoted); pending `message_agent` action approvals of the link end `expired` (`action_approvals` has no `abandoned` status; rows a concurrent decision holds are skipped and that decision then fails with "The link no longer exists.") with the line "The request "…" ended without a decision: the link between these agents was removed." in their origin conversation; then the link row goes (its `peer_tasks` follow by `ON DELETE CASCADE`). Audit `agent_link.delete` (critical): `{ agent_a_id, agent_b_id, cancelled_tasks: n, expired_approvals: m }`. A later `message_agent` from an agent to the same peer **creates the link again** (unchanged: agent-created links stay as they are; whether to retire them is an owner question, out of scope).
  - `AgentLink` (C-29) gains `direction: "both" | "outgoing" | "incoming"` read **from the asked agent's side** (`outgoing` = this agent hands tasks to the peer), `created_by: { kind: "person" | "agent", display_name: string | null }` (the person's display name, null once that user is deleted; `agent` with a null name for links an agent opened, which is every link created before this contract), and `LinkPeer` gains `workspace_name: string`. All additive; existing fields and `PUT /v1/agent-links/{id}/policy` are unchanged (a side's policy is still set by `can_use`, `allow` by `can_approve`, FIX-110).
  - **Enforcement.** `peer_tasks::send_message` refuses to **open** a task against the direction, before any approval is created, with the refusal text "This link is one-way: <Sender> only works on tasks that <Target> sends it, so you cannot hand <Target> a task." (labels `Name (@handle)`); the `message_agent` approval handler re-checks it when an approval is decided (a direction narrowed while the approval waited fails it with the same text). A message inside an open task, its report and its cancellation are not directional. `message_agent` on an agent that has no link behaves as today.
  - Storage (`03`): `agent_links.direction`, `agent_links.created_by_kind text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'agent' CHECK (created_by_kind IN ('person','agent'))` (every row before this migration was created by an agent; the agent path keeps the default) and `agent_links.created_by_user_id uuid NULL REFERENCES users (id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (set for `person`; `NULL` for `agent` or once the person is deleted).
  - Dashboard: **Settings › Other agents** (P5-REFINE-12) is the place; the list `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/links` keeps working.

---


#### P5-REFINE-6 additions — small fields the area briefs need (coordinator, 2026-11-20)

- C-1086 **Has this person signed in yet** (P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-16 workspace-4; amends C-1050). Each `AccessBinding` of `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/access` gains `signed_in: boolean` (`users.last_login_at IS NOT NULL`). Same callers, same authorization; no new route.
- C-1087 **What a rule change changed** (P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-17 org-14). Each `PolicyGenerationSummary` of the rule history gains `changes: { kind: "added" | "removed" | "edited", rule_name: string }[]` (at most 20, then `more: integer`) and `actor: { display_name: string } | null`, computed from the generation's diff with its predecessor. No change to publish or roll back. (Built: `more` is always present (`0` when nothing was left out); rules match by `logical_id`, an edit is any change of the rule's stored fields; `actor` is the publisher, `null` for a draft.)
- C-1088 **Hide system events in the audit** (P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-18 admin-10). `GET /v1/admin/audit` (and the organization's audit list) take `include_system: boolean` (default `false` in the dashboard's request; the route's default stays `true` for API callers); system events are rows whose actor is the `system`/`plugin-builtin` service actor. The response carries `hidden_system: integer` (the count left out) when `include_system=false`. (Built: system events are the rows with `actor_kind = 'system'`, which covers `plugin-builtin`, `plugin-directory`, sweeps and connectors; the workspace audit list takes the same parameter; the count is over the other filters, not the page.) **Amended (P5-REFINE-20, workspace-3):** every `AuditEntry` of the three audit lists gains `actor_name: string | null`, the acting person's current display name (`users.display_name`) when `actor_kind` is `user`, `instance_admin` or `service` (a key acts as its owner) and the user still exists, else `null`; `actor_label` is unchanged. The dashboard says "Alice changed …", never the e-mail; a machine actor (`system`, `service`, `agent`) has no `actor_name` and is named by the dashboard from its kind and label.
- C-1089 **Which chat platforms a person can link** (P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-16 account-3). `GET /v1/me/chat-platforms` (any signed-in person) → `{ platforms: { kind: string, display_name: string, bot_handle: string | null, linked: boolean }[] }` — the connected integrations of the organizations the person belongs to, by kind, with the bot's handle where the connector reports one; no workspace, channel or token data. (Built: one entry per kind (the first reported bot handle; `linked` when linked on any of them); an API key sees its own organization only.)
- C-1090 **The organization's agents** (P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-17 org-6). `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/agents` (organization owners and admins) → `{ agents: { id, name, handle, workspace: { slug, name }, kind: "hosted" | "byo", state: string, last_active_at: string | null }[] }`, paged like other org lists (`limit`, `cursor`); personal workspaces' agents are included with the workspace named from the owner's side (C-923). (Built: the page is the house shape `{ items, next_cursor }`, not `{ agents }`; `workspace` also carries `id` and `personal_of: { id, name } | null` (as `Workspace`), so the list groups by workspace. **D-425 (owner, 2026-11-20):** `state` is the agent header's state, real and in the header's precedence (P5-REFINE-11 `agentStateOf` over C-1059's computer words): `paused` (the agent, its workspace or the organization is paused) → `needs_key` (a hosted agent cannot answer: `decide_reason` of the dispatcher's readiness, as C-1062's answer-readiness) → `failed` (its computer's word is `failed`) → `working` (a turn queued/dispatched/running or a busy process) → `starting` → `asleep` (off with no failure, or stopping; a hosted agent without a computer yet) → `ready`. A BYO agent is `paused`, `working` or `ready`. The dashboard words them Paused, Needs a key, Can't start, Working, Starting its computer…, Asleep, Ready. `last_active_at` is its newest turn's `created_at`.)
- C-1091 **A form field names the connection** (FIX-194, D-420; `09 §3.4`).
  - **Manifest:** `connect[].fields[].store` gains `label`, allowed only in a `kind: fields` method, at most one per method, `type: text` only. Linter codes `label_field_method`, `label_field_duplicate`, `label_field_type`. Additive in contract 1; never a widening.
  - **Connect** (`POST` of a `fields` method, new or reconnect): the value is checked like `PATCH /v1/connections/{id}` `label` (`names::clean`, 1–120 characters, no invisible formatting characters); a failure is the usual connect field error (`invalid_request`, **400**, as every other bad field of a connect; the brief said 422) at `/fields/<name>`, and nothing is stored. A blank (whitespace-only) value counts as empty. The value is stored only as the connection's `label`, never in `metadata` or credentials.
  - **Label precedence:** the field when non-empty, else the probe's `label`, else the hook's, else the app's name. On a reconnect an empty field keeps the current label.
  - `FormField.store` (`GET /v1/apps/{app}`, the connect plan) may be `"label"`; the dashboard prefills that field with the connection's current label on a reconnect.
  - The builtin `ics-calendar` 1.2.0, `webhook` 1.1.0 and `caldav` 1.3.0 declare their `name` field this way, replacing the API's hard-coded rule; CalDAV's `<username> at <host>` fallback stays.
  - Additive: no response shape changes, and an existing package behaves as before.

### Phase 5 — P5-SCHED-ONCE (one-time schedules and reminders; D-421, D-422, SEC-144)

- C-1092 **One-time schedules** (P5-SCHED-ONCE Part A, D-421; migration `20261201000266_schedule_run_at.sql`; amends the `05` schedule rows and C-1077).
  - **Data (`03`).**
    - `schedules.cron` becomes nullable.
    - New columns `schedules.run_at timestamptz NULL` (the instant) and `schedules.completed_at timestamptz NULL`, with `CHECK ((cron IS NULL) <> (run_at IS NULL))` and `CHECK (completed_at IS NULL OR run_at IS NOT NULL)`.
    - `last_outcome` also allows `waiting_paused`.
    - New column `schedule_runs.answer_pushed_at timestamptz NULL` (C-1095).
  - **Requests.** `CreateScheduleRequest` and `UpdateScheduleRequest` gain `run_at?: string`: a local date-time `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM[:SS]` without an offset, read in `timezone` (default: the workspace's).
    - Exactly one of `cron` / `run_at` on create; at most one on a patch. Otherwise 400 `invalid_request` (`details.fields`) at `/run_at` "set either cron or run_at, not both", or at `/cron` "set either cron or run_at" when neither is set. (As built: 400 like every other schedule field error, `cron` and `timezone` included; the brief said 422. The preview keeps its 422 codes.)
    - Each refusal is a 400 `invalid_request` at `/run_at`:
      - an offset or `Z`;
      - a daylight-saving gap ("does not exist in that time zone (a daylight-saving gap)");
      - before the start of the current minute ("is in the past");
      - more than `SCHEDULE_ONCE_MAX_AHEAD` (5 years) ahead.
    - An ambiguous local time is the earlier instant. No minimum interval applies.
  - **Switching kinds.** A patch with `run_at` makes the schedule one-time (`cron` null, `completed_at` null, re-armed if enabled); a patch with `cron` makes it recurring (`run_at` null, `completed_at` null). A `timezone`-only patch on a one-time schedule keeps the local wall time and recomputes the instant.
  - **Done.**
    - `enabled` on a schedule with `completed_at` set → 409 `schedule_completed` ("This schedule has already run. Set a new time to run it again.").
    - Resuming a pending one-time schedule whose time has passed arms it for now. The reassignment `re_enable` path follows the same rule.
    - The 50-per-agent limit (`schedule_limit_reached`) counts only rows with `completed_at` null.
  - **`Schedule`.**
    - `cron: string | null`;
    - `run_at: string | null` (the local wall time in `timezone`, the request's format);
    - `completed_at: string | null`;
    - `next_runs` is `[instant]` for a pending enabled one-time schedule and `[]` when Done or paused.
  - **`ScheduleRun.outcome`** may be `waiting_paused` (C-1077 `result: "skipped"`, `reason: "paused"`).
  - **Preview.** `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/schedules/preview` also takes `{ run_at, timezone? }` (exactly one of `cron` / `run_at`). It returns `{ next: [instant], interval_secs: null, too_frequent: false, min_interval_secs: 300 }`, or 422 `invalid_run_at` with the messages above.
  - **Firing.**
    - A due one-time schedule fires once and in the same transaction sets `next_fire_at = null`, `completed_at = now`, `last_fire_at`, `last_outcome = ok`. It is never `skipped_busy`, never coalesced and never disabled for failures; a quiet or failed gate still completes it.
    - When the agent is effectively paused it writes one `waiting_paused` run (not again while `last_outcome = waiting_paused`) and sets `next_fire_at = now + SCHEDULE_ONCE_RECHECK` (60 s).
    - Fire-time authority failure is as for recurring schedules.
    - Run now is a manual run that changes neither `run_at` nor `completed_at`.
  - **Retention.** The daily pass deletes Done schedules with `completed_at` older than `SCHEDULE_DONE_RETENTION` (30 days) through the ordinary delete path (C-1074's deleted-automation marking).
- C-1093 **Where a one-time run happens, and its turn context** (P5-SCHED-ONCE Part B, D-422, SEC-144; amends `10 §Schedules`, `10 §Turn context`, C-1074).
  - **Where it runs.**
    - A one-time run is created in `origin_conversation_id` when, at fire time, that conversation exists, belongs to the schedule's agent, is not archived and has `direct_user_id = creator_user_id`. Otherwise it gets its own run thread (C-1074), and FIX-192 is unchanged.
    - In the origin, the turn has the schedule door and `display` (C-1074), queues behind an active turn as a follow-up, and `ScheduleRun.conversation_id` is the origin. No `schedule_run` "finished" notice is appended for it; failure notices stay.
  - **Turn context.** The `schedule` sentence for a one-time run states:
    - that it is a one-time schedule due at `<local time> (<zone>)` and will not run again;
    - "running late: <agent> was paused at the time" when it fires more than 5 minutes after `run_at`;
    - for an origin run, "<creator> set this one-time schedule in this conversation and reads it; your answer is shown here" (instead of "nobody reads the run").
- C-1094 **Schedule tools take `run_at`** (P5-SCHED-ONCE Part A, D-421; amends the `12` schedule tools row).
  - `schedule_create {name, prompt, cron?, run_at?, timezone?, gate_command?, gate_description?}` and `schedule_update {id, …, run_at?}` follow C-1092 (exactly one of `cron` / `run_at`, the same messages). The tool result and `schedule_list` items gain `run_at` (local) and `completed_at`; `cron` may be null.
  - The default `timezone` for both kinds is the speaker's `users.timezone` (C-1054), else the workspace's. Creating still needs a person speaking.
  - The description tells the agent to use `run_at` for reminders and states that a one-time schedule made in a person's own chat runs in that chat.
  - The agent home's schedules summary lists a pending one-time schedule as ``once at `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` (<zone>)`` and leaves Done ones out.
- C-1095 **Reminder push** (P5-SCHED-ONCE Part B, D-422; extends C-832, C-835 and the `05` push delivery row).
  - When a one-time run that happened in its origin conversation (C-1093) ends `done`, the push loop claims `schedule_runs.answer_pushed_at` and sends one push to each opted-in device of the schedule's creator.
  - Payload `{ "v": 1, "type": "schedule_answer", "url": "<the conversation's dashboard path>" }`, plus `title` (the schedule name, `NAME`-capped) only on a device with `show_details`; never answer text. `Urgency: normal`, `TTL` = `PUSH_TTL_MAX`, `Topic` as C-1023.
  - Runs older than 1 h are never pushed.
  - The service worker shows "Aurora — a scheduled task has an answer for you" / "Open the conversation to read it." (with `title`: "Aurora — <title>"), tag `aurora-schedule`, and opens exactly the given conversation path.
  - Recurring runs and run-thread runs are never pushed.

- C-1096 **Who paused a schedule or webhook** (P5-REFINE-14 send-back, D-407; migration `20261201000268_automation_pause_actor.sql`; UX review 5 schedules-4; amends the `05` schedule and webhook rows).
  - New columns `schedules.disabled_by uuid NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` and `webhooks.disabled_by uuid NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`. Written only by a person's own pause (`PATCH { enabled: false }` by a caller: `disabled_reason = 'user'`, `disabled_by` = the caller); cleared whenever the row is re-enabled (any path, including a reassignment that re-enables). A pause by an agent tool, by the authorization pass (`authorization`), by the failure counter (`failures`) or by anything else leaves it null.
  - `Schedule` and `Webhook` gain `paused_by: { id: uuid, display_name: string } | null` (the shape of `created_by`): non-null only while `enabled = false` and `disabled_reason = 'user'` and the actor still exists. Whoever can read the row reads the field; nothing else is added (no e-mail, no role). The dashboard writes "Paused by <display_name>"; with null it keeps the notices for `authorization` and `failures`, and "Paused" alone for a pause without a name.
- C-1097 **How a delivery was verified** (P5-REFINE-14 send-back, D-409; UX review 5 webhooks-4; amends C-1079). No column.
  - The `display` object of a webhook turn's `turn.queued` event (C-1079) gains `verification: "signed" | "token" | "none"`, set by the receiver at delivery time from the webhook's configuration then: `signed` (kind `hmac_sha256`, and the signature matched), `token` (kind `bearer`, and the token matched), `none` (kind `none`: nothing was checked). Only an accepted delivery has a thread, so a refused one never carries it. Old events have none; the dashboard then writes only "accepted".
  - The dashboard's first line reads "<time> · signed and accepted" (`signed`), "<time> · accepted with its token" (`token`), "<time> · accepted, not signed" (`none`).
- C-1098 **A renamed automation's threads remember the old name** (P5-REFINE-14 send-back, D-409; migration `20261201000268`; UX review 5 schedules-7; amends C-1074).
  - New column `conversations.previous_title text NULL`.
