The title-sync `UPDATE` of C-1074 (renaming a schedule or webhook) sets `previous_title` to the thread's title as it was, only for the threads whose title actually changes; a thread never renamed has null. A second rename replaces it with the name before that rename. A person's own rename of a direct chat does not touch it.
  - `Conversation.automation` gains `previous_name: string | null` (the stored `previous_title`; null unless the automation was renamed). Whoever may read the conversation reads it (it is the automation's former name, already visible on the schedule or webhook row at that time). The dashboard shows "was "<previous_name>", renamed with it" as the first rename note of the thread.
- C-1099 **Read one skill file** (P5-REFINE-19 send-back, D-406; UX review 5 export; amends C-332 and the `05` skill row). No column, no migration.
  - `GET /v1/skills/{id}/files/{path}` (operation `getSkillFile`) returns the stored bytes of one file of that skill as `application/octet-stream`, with the header `X-Skill-File-Executable: true|false`. Because it serves bytes people wrote, it answers like attachment downloads, whatever the stored file is: `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<last path segment>"` (the path is validated to `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, so the name is safe) and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. It is the read side of `PUT /v1/skills/{id}/files/{path}` and takes the same `{*path}` wildcard.
  - Authority is exactly that of `GET /v1/skills/{id}`: agent tier `can_use` on the agent, organization tier a member of the organization (as the item route), workspace tier `can_use` on the workspace; the tier of the skill decides, never the caller's reach into another skill. Session or API key.
  - The path is validated as for `PUT` (relative, `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, depth at most 4, no `.` or `..` segment, no `SKILL.md` segment): a bad path is 400 `validation_failed` with `/path`. A path the skill does not have is 404 `not_found` ("Skill file not found."); a skill id that is not visible is 404. A file is looked up by the pair (skill id, path), so a path of another skill is never reachable through this skill's id.
  - The list of a skill's file paths is the `files` array of `GET /v1/skills/{id}` (`path`, `size`, `executable`); no change to it. No audit row (a read).
  - The dashboard's agent export (`docs/14` Expert speed) reads each file of a skill with it and writes it to `skills/<name>/<path>` beside `SKILL.md`.
- C-1100 **Which key answered** (P5-REFINE-20 item 14 follow-up; amends `10 §Transcript events` and `05 §Turn and transcript persistence rules`). No column, no migration.
  - `turns.model_basis` gains `key_id` (uuid string): the secret the dispatcher's selection chose (`readiness.keys[default.key]`), written with the rest of the basis. Never returned by any route.
  - The durable `turn.finished` event payload gains optional `ai_key: "starter" | "organization" | "workspace"`, computed in `finish_turn` from `key_id`: a key with a `workspace_id` is `workspace`; else `starter` when the saved placement (`mode = shared_org`, `personal_enabled`, default organization = the agent's) lists it among its starter secrets (C-1049's rule); else `organization`. Absent when there is no `key_id`, no such secret, or the turn is not a hosted `turn`. It is a class only (no name, no id), so it is readable by exactly those who may read the transcript (SEC-143's rule); the dashboard says "the Company key" only for `starter`.
  - Not in `usage`, not on `Turn`; the openapi schema is unchanged (event payloads are free-form objects).
- C-1101 **The grader's last test is kept; a short test budget** (P5-REFINE-20 batch 5 item 4, behaviour-4; amends C-863, `05` eval-settings row). Migration `20261201000269`: `agents.eval_judge_test_result` (`ok`|`failed`, null), `eval_judge_test_reason`, `eval_judge_test_ms`, `eval_judge_tested_at`.
  - `EvalSettings.test` is the **last test of the current judge**: a `PUT` that changed the judge replaces it in the same transaction with the new test (a cleared judge nulls it); `GET` and a `PUT` that did not change the judge return it unchanged and make no call. Null with no judge or never tested. `EvalJudgeTest` gains `tested_at` (RFC 3339).
  - The test call's timeout is `EVAL_JUDGE_TEST_TIMEOUT` (8 s, `02 §Limits`), not `EVAL_JUDGE_TIMEOUT`: the API's path to the endpoint is the one the key check budgets (C-1065). `reason` is `rejected` | `unreachable` | `rate_limited`: a transport failure, a non-429 non-2xx answer or no answer within the budget is `unreachable` (the web says "Aurora couldn't reach the model provider"; `timeout` is no longer produced), an HTTP 429 is `rate_limited`, a refusal before any request (not granted, not permitted, not usable) is `rejected`. Never the provider's text.
  - `PlatformLlm` (C-869) gains the failure `rate_limited` (HTTP 429); a judge call during a run that gets one errors that question with `judge_rate_limited` (the web says so; before, `judge_unavailable`).
  - Web: the Grader section shows a kept failed test (and no "Ready") after a reload; a passing test is said only right after saving. A run that is `queued` says "Waiting to start" in its result rows, never "Running…".
- C-1102 **A person's name beside the pause and settings labels** (P5-REFINE-20 batch 10 item 4; amends C-200, C-771, C-772 and the `05` freeze and admin-settings rows). No column, no migration.
  - `Frozen` (every route that returns one: the agent, workspace and organization reads, the freeze and unfreeze results, the instance-admin organization list) gains `by: { id: uuid, display_name: string } | null` (the shape of `paused_by`, C-1096): the freezer by name, read from `users` at request time; null when the user row is gone. `by_label` stays (the e-mail, `(deleted user)`), unchanged, for compatibility.
  - `SectionSummary` (C-771) and `SettingsSection` (C-772, also the `PUT` result) gain `updated_by: { id: uuid, display_name: string } | null`, from `instance_settings.updated_by` joined to `users` at read time; null when the section was never written, the writer was `system` or `cli` (no user) or the user is gone. `updated_by_label` stays.
  - The dashboard shows the person with `personName` ("Paused by Erin Example", "Last change: Alice Admin, <date>"); with `by` or `updated_by` null it writes "Paused on <date>" and "Last change: <date>", never the label (an address is not a name, D-402). The web guard that bans a raw `display_name` also bans reading `by_label`, `updated_by_label` and `decided_by_label` in a component.
- C-1120 **The admin package list filters by package id** (FIX-201, D-438; amends the `GET /v1/admin/plugins` row here and in `05`). No column, no migration.
  - `GET /v1/admin/plugins` gains `?package_id=<string>`: an exact match on the package id, combinable with `?status`; same order (`created_at` desc, `id` desc), cursor and page size as without it. An id that matches nothing is an empty page (200), never 404. Instance admins only, unchanged.
  - The dashboard's package page `/admin/apps/packages/<package id>` uses it to find the active version (else the newest) and replaces the address with that version's UUID.
- C-1121 **The SSH user name is the agent's handle** (FIX-208, D-445, SEC-147; amends C-791, C-798, C-1060's SSH note and `16 §W20` Authentication). No column, no migration; the response shape is unchanged.
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/ssh-certificate` signs **two** principals, `[<agent id>, <handle>]`, the agent id first (the handle from the same agents row the mint reads; handles are `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}`, valid OpenSSH user names, and immutable, C-12). `username` in the 201 answer is the **handle**; `principal` stays the agent id (the first principal). Key id, serial, validity, extensions and the comment `aurora:<agent id>` are unchanged.
  - The certificate law (terminator, offline): principals `[uuid]` (certificates minted before this change) or `[uuid, handle]`; the first a canonical lowercase hyphenated UUID equal to the key id's `a=`; the second, when present, a valid handle shape; the SSH user name typed by the client must be one of the principals. The session is opened for the key id's agent. Nothing in the terminator or the API looks an agent up by name.
  - `POST /v1/terminator/sessions` (`SessionOpenRequest.username`): the terminator sends the **agent id** the law returned (the first principal), never the typed name. The API re-verifies the same principal shape and requires `username` to equal the first principal and the key id's agent; a handle there is refused (403 `denied`, hint-free).
  - Agents never rename their handle (C-12). A certificate keeps the handle it was minted with until it expires (≤ `ssh.cert_ttl_secs`, 15 min by default); it lands on its own agent whatever another agent's handle or id is. A handle that happens to spell another agent's UUID connects to the certificate's agent, never to that other agent.
  - Rolling upgrade: a terminator older than this change refuses two-principal certificates (its law wanted exactly one); the mint and the terminators ship in one release.
  - `aurora ssh <agent>` (C-798): connects as the certificate's user name (the mint answer's `username`; for a reused certificate on disk, its second principal, else its first), so `aurora ssh daneel` runs `ssh … daneel@<host>`; a certificate on disk is reused when its first principal is the agent id.
  - Web: the agent's SSH page shows and copies `ssh -i ~/.ssh/aurora-<handle> -o CertificateFile=~/.ssh/aurora-<handle>-cert.pub [-p <port>] <user>@<host>`, `<user>` the minted certificate's `username`, else the handle.

### Phase 5 — P5-SKLIB (skill libraries from any git URL, and at the instance level; D-448..D-450, SEC-148, SEC-149)

Amends C-570..C-579 (P4-GSL). Every route still answers 404 `skill_libraries_disabled` while `skills.library.enabled` is false. Everything read from a repository stays untrusted text (C-571). Briefs: `docs/tasks/P5-SKLIB-1.md` (C-1122..C-1127), `P5-SKLIB-2.md` (C-1128), `P5-SKLIB-3.md` (C-1129).

- C-1122 **`SkillLibrary`, create and patch by URL** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448; amends C-570, the `05` library rows).
  - `SkillLibrary = { id, scope: "org"|"instance", org_id|null, label, status: "active"|"paused"|"error"|"over_budget", url|null, repo, ref|null, path, token_set, token_updated_at|null, last_head_sha|null, last_refresh_at|null, last_error|null, skill_counts: { agent, workspace, org }, pending_updates, created_at }`. `url` is the stored clone URL (null only for a library migrated without one, C-1127); `repo` is its display form `host/path` without `.git` (kept for compatibility, also what `skills.library_repo` stores at add); `token_set` and `token_updated_at` say whether a token is saved, **never** the token. `app_id` and `connection` are removed. People who are not managers keep getting only `id`, `label`, `status` and `scope` (every other field null), as in C-570.
  - `POST /v1/orgs/{org}/skill-libraries` `{ label, url, ref?, path?, token?, token_username? }` → 201. The URL is normalized by C-1123 (a page URL fills `ref` and `path` when those fields are absent; when present, the fields win). Creation reads the repository once (C-1125: resolve the ref, list the tree at `path`); failure → 422 `library_unreachable` with the sanitized reason, nothing stored. `token` 1..1024 printable ASCII, no whitespace; `token_username` 1..100 `[A-Za-z0-9._@-]`, default `x-access-token`; a `token_username` without `token` → 400 `invalid_request` (field errors answer 400 here as everywhere in this API; the reader's failures are 422). 409 `library_exists` now means the same normalized `url` + `ref` + `path` already exists in that organization (labels stay unique too, `skill_libraries_label_uq`).
  - `PATCH /v1/skill-libraries/{id}` `{ label?, status?: "active"|"paused", ref? ("" clears), path?, token?: string|null, token_username?, url? }`. `token: "<value>"` sets or replaces (re-reads the repository with it first: 422 `library_unreachable` keeps the old token), `token: null` removes it; omitted leaves it. `url` is accepted **only** while the stored `url` is null (409 `library_url_fixed` otherwise: a different repository is a new library). A change of url, ref, path or token re-reads and never touches stored skills.
  - Errors removed: `connection_not_org_scoped`, `app_without_library`. Added: 422 `library_url_invalid` (`details.reason`: `not_https`, `ssh_url`, `credentials_in_url`, `query_or_fragment`, `not_a_repository_path`, `too_long`), 409 `library_url_fixed`.
  - Audit: `skill_library.create` and `skill_library.update` record `url`, `ref`, `path` and `token: "set"|"replaced"|"removed"` (never the value); both stay security-critical.
- C-1123 **Repository address rules** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448(a)). One parser in `aurora-core` used by create, patch and check; the dashboard never parses.
  - Accepted: `https://<host>[:port]/<path>` with 1..20 path segments of `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]` (percent-escapes decoded once, then the same charset), at most 500 characters. The stored form keeps the scheme, lowercased host, port if not 443, the path without a trailing `/`; a trailing `.git` is kept if given (some servers need it), else not added.
  - Page URLs, recognised on the path whatever the host: `…/-/tree/<ref>[/<dir>]` and `…/-/blob/…` (GitLab: the repository is the part before `/-/`), `…/tree/<ref>[/<dir>]` (GitHub-style, the repository is the first two segments), `…/src/branch|tag|commit/<ref>[/<dir>]` (Gitea, Forgejo), `…/src/<ref>[/<dir>]` (Bitbucket, repository = first two segments). The first segment after the marker is the ref (a ref with `/` cannot be taken from a page URL: the person types it in the field). `<dir>` becomes `path` (the existing path rules: at most 4 segments).
  - Refused (422 `library_url_invalid`): another scheme (`http:` included), `git@host:path`, `ssh://`, `git://`, `file:`, user info, a query or a fragment, an IP-literal host that the destination guard refuses (checked again at connect time).
  - Unit-tested with a table of at least 30 inputs (GitHub, GitLab with subgroups, gitlab.cern.ch, Gitea, Codeberg, Bitbucket, a bare self-hosted `https://git.example.org/team/skills.git`, every refused form).
- C-1124 **Check without saving** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448(f); P5-SKLIB-2 adds the admin route).
  - `POST /v1/orgs/{org}/skill-libraries/check` (org owner/admin, session or API key) and `POST /v1/admin/skill-libraries/check` (instance admin, session) take the create body without `label` and answer 200 `LibraryCheck = { url, repo, ref|null, resolved: { kind: "branch"|"tag"|"commit", name, default: bool }, commit_sha, path, skills: { found, valid, names: [string] (the first 20 valid names, sorted) }, truncated, suggested_label }`. `suggested_label` is the last repository segment without `.git`, sanitized, cut to 80.
  - The same read as a create (C-1125) and the same errors (422 `library_url_invalid`, 422 `library_unreachable`, 422 `library_tree_too_large`); nothing is stored, no head is recorded, no audit row. A token in the body is used for this read only. `Cache-Control: no-store`.
  - Rate: 10 checks per minute per person (429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`).
- C-1125 **Reading a repository** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448(b,c), SEC-148; replaces C-575 and the gateway half of C-577).
  - **HTTP**: the API's `connect::Env::http_instance` client (guarded resolver at dial time: public addresses plus `gateway.allowed_private`; `tls.extra_ca_bundle`). User agent `aurora-skill-library/<version> git/2.0`. Per request timeout `LIBRARY_FETCH_TIMEOUT` (20 s).
  - **Discovery**: `GET <url>/info/refs?service=git-upload-pack` with `Git-Protocol: version=2`. Redirects are followed only here, at most 3, each https and through the same guarded client; the final base is used for the rest of that read (never stored). Every other request refuses redirects (a 3xx is the existing "the repository may have moved" error).
  - **Refs**: protocol v2 `ls-refs` (`ref-prefix` for the asked ref, `symrefs` for `HEAD`); a v0/v1 advertisement is parsed from the discovery answer. Ref grammar as C-576 (`[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,200}`, no `..`, no leading `-`). A ref is tried as `refs/heads/<ref>`, then `refs/tags/<ref>` (peeled), then as a full commit id (40 hex) that the server must be able to send; none → "The branch or tag was not found".
  - **Trees**: one `fetch` (`want <commit>`, `deepen 1`, `no-progress`, `filter blob:none` when the server advertises `filter`, `ofs-delta`; side-band demultiplexed). Without `filter`, the depth-1 pack is fetched whole under `LIBRARY_PACK_MAX`. Only the commit and the trees under `path` are kept.
  - **Blobs**: one `fetch` per read with every needed blob id as a `want` (browse: the `SKILL.md` blobs of the listed page; add and content: the skill's files; refresh: the changed skills' files), at most `LIBRARY_WANTS_MAX` (500) ids per request. A server that refuses wanting a blob by id ("not our ref") is read with the whole depth-1 pack instead, under `LIBRARY_PACK_MAX`.
  - **Packs**: parsed in memory (no file is written; the API's root filesystem is read-only): object headers, zlib streams, `ofs-delta` and `ref-delta` resolved against objects of the same pack only; every object's id is recomputed (SHA-1 of `<type> <size>\0<bytes>`) and an object that does not match what was asked or listed fails the read. Caps: response body `LIBRARY_PACK_MAX` (32 MiB), inflated total `LIBRARY_INFLATE_MAX` (64 MiB), one blob `LIBRARY_FILE_MAX` (64 KiB; bigger is the existing per-file refusal, read no further), objects per pack `LIBRARY_TREE_MAX` × 2, delta chain depth 50. A repository using SHA-256 object ids → "SHA-256 repositories are not supported".
  - **Token**: when the library has one, `Authorization: Basic base64(<token_username>:<token>)` on requests to the library URL's origin (scheme, host, port) only, never after a redirect to another origin, never logged; a 401 without a token → "The repository needs an access token"; a 401 or 403 with one → "The access token was refused"; 404 → "The repository was not found, or the token cannot read it".
  - **Throttling**: 429/503 → the existing `Throttled` with `Retry-After` (C-577 unchanged).
  - The reader exposes exactly what `content.rs` uses today (`resolve`, `tree`, `blob`) so assembly, validation, hashing, pinning and proposals are unchanged; blob ids and modes are git's own, so skills pinned through the gateway compare equal after the switch.
- C-1126 **Token storage** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448(c), SEC-148). `aurora_secrets::store::Purpose::SkillLibraryToken` (in `Purpose::ALL`; source table `skill_libraries.token_ref`, so `secrets migrate` and key rotation cover it); owner = the library id, org = the library's org or the nil UUID for an instance library; sealed `{ username, token }`. Opened only by the repository reader. Deleted with the library and on `token: null`. Columns (migration `20261201000270`): `skill_libraries.token_ref text NULL`, `token_updated_at timestamptz NULL`, CHECK both null or both set.
- C-1127 **Data and removals** (P5-SKLIB-1, D-448(d,e)). Migration `20261201000270_skill_library_git_url.sql`: `skill_libraries.url text NULL CHECK (url IS NULL OR char_length(url) BETWEEN 9 AND 500)`; `token_ref`, `token_updated_at` (C-1126); `app_id` and `repo` become nullable (`repo` is rewritten to the display form); backfill `url` for `app_id = 'github'` (`https://github.com/<repo>.git`) and `'gitlab'` (the connection's instance base URL + `/<repo>.git`, else null); every row left with `url` null gets `status = 'error'`, `last_error = 'Enter the repository''s git address'`; a new unique index on `(org_id, url, coalesce(ref,''), path)` where `url` is not null. `connection_id`, `app_id` stay, unused, until a later cleanup. Removed: the gateway route `POST /internal/v1/library-fetch` (C-575) and its module and tests, `domains/skill_libraries/gateway.rs`, `AppState::with_library_gateway`, `repo::plugins::move_skill_libraries` and the supersede report's `skill_libraries` count; the `library` blocks of the built-in `github` and `gitlab` manifests (a version bump of each). The manifest `library` block (C-576) is still parsed, never used, and the linter warns `library_ignored`. New limits (`02 §Limits`): `LIBRARY_PACK_MAX` 32 MiB, `LIBRARY_INFLATE_MAX` 64 MiB, `LIBRARY_WANTS_MAX` 500, `LIBRARIES_PER_INSTANCE` 20 (used by C-1128).
- C-1128 **Instance libraries** (P5-SKLIB-2, D-449, SEC-149; migration `20261201000271_skill_library_instance.sql`).
  - Data: `skill_libraries.org_id` nullable (null = instance); the label unique index becomes `(coalesce(org_id, nil), label)`; table `skill_library_org_state (library_id uuid FK cascade, org_id uuid FK cascade, over_budget_since timestamptz not null, PRIMARY KEY (library_id, org_id))`; rows with `status = 'over_budget'` become `active` with one `skill_library_org_state` row for their org; `status` no longer takes `over_budget` on write (the CHECK keeps the value for one release).
  - `GET/POST /v1/admin/skill-libraries` (instance admin, **session only**): list every instance library with the full `SkillLibrary` plus `orgs_using` (organizations with at least one skill added from it) and totals in `skill_counts` and `pending_updates`; create as C-1122 with `scope = "instance"`, at most `LIBRARIES_PER_INSTANCE` (409 `library_limit`). `POST /v1/admin/skill-libraries/check` (C-1124).
  - `GET/PATCH/DELETE /v1/skill-libraries/{id}` and `POST …/refresh`: authority by the row's scope: organization library → that org's owner/admin (unchanged); instance library → instance admin (session only); anyone else 404 for an instance library they cannot manage, as today for another org's library. `GET` by an org owner/admin of an instance library is allowed with `?org=<org>` and answers that org's view (below).
  - `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/skill-libraries` lists the org's libraries by label **and then** the instance libraries by label (`scope: "instance"`; owners/admins see every status, so a paused instance library shows as Paused; members see only `active` ones, as for the org's own). For an instance library, an org owner/admin gets `url`, `repo`, `ref`, `path`, `last_head_sha`, `last_refresh_at`, `last_error`, `token_set` (not `token_updated_at`), and `skill_counts`, `pending_updates` counted **in this org only**; `status` is `over_budget` when this org has a `skill_library_org_state` row, else the library's. Members get `id`, `label`, `status`, `scope`.
  - Browse and content (`GET /v1/skill-libraries/{id}/skills[...]`) of an instance library require `?org=<org>`: the caller must be able to use a workspace of that org (400 `invalid_request` without it; 404 when the caller has no such right); `added` lists only skills of that org that the caller can see. For an org library `org` is optional and must equal the library's org when given.
  - Add (`from_library`) accepts an instance library's id at any tier of any org, with the tier's authority (C-572 unchanged). `skills.library_label` and `library_repo` are copied at add as before.
  - Refresh: one pass per library as before; proposals are written per skill; the open-proposal sum is per org of the skill (under that org's lock, C-577); an org over budget gets a `skill_library_org_state` row and no proposals; when its sum is back under, the row is deleted. An unchanged head still short-circuits unless the library has a `skill_library_org_state` row.
  - Audit: instance actions write instance rows (`org_id` null) `skill_library.create|update|delete`; deleting an instance library also writes one `skill_library.delete` row in each organization whose skills were detached, with the count. Adds, approvals, rejections and detaches stay org rows.
- C-1129 **Dashboard** (P5-SKLIB-3, D-450).
  - Routes: `orgRoutes(slug).skillLibraries()` unchanged; `adminRoutes.skillLibraries()` = `/admin/skill-libraries` (instance admins; others get the admin guard's answer). What agents may do › Defaults, Skills group: a row "Skill libraries" with "<n> shared with every organization" and **Manage**.
  - One `LibrariesTable` and one `AddLibraryDialog`/`EditLibraryDialog` used by both pages (`scope` prop), on `DataTable`, `FormDialog`, `Field`, `SecretInput`, `Notice`, `StatusDot`, `Fold`, the 390 px card layout of `DataTable`.
  - Add: step 1 fields Repository address (required), Branch or tag, Folder, fold "This repository is private" (Access token, User name) → **Check** (C-1124) → step 2 shows `repo`, "<branch> (default branch)" or the tag or commit, `sha7`, "<valid> skills can be added" (+ "<found − valid> can't be added" when any), up to 20 names as plain text, the truncated notice, Label prefilled from `suggested_label` → **Add library** (C-1122). **Back** returns to step 1 with the fields kept. `library_url_invalid` shows on the address field in words per `details.reason`; `library_unreachable` above the fields.
  - Status words: `active` Active, `paused` Paused, `error` "Can't be read" + reason, and "Needs an access token" when the reason is that one, `over_budget` "Pending updates over budget"; a migrated row without `url` shows "Enter the repository's git address" with **Set address**.
  - Add from library: the picker groups "Your organization" and "Shared with every organization" and passes `?org=` for instance libraries; the badge is "Library · <repo> · <sha7>".
- C-1140 **`SKILLS_DIR` in the sandbox** (P5-SKILLDIR, D-454; no wire change).
  - Value: `<supervisor working dir>/<adapter skills_dir>` with no trailing slash: `/workspace/.claude/skills` (claude-code, fake), `/workspace/.codex/skills/aurora` (codex), `/workspace/.agents/skills` (pi, goose, opencode).
  - Set in the harness process environment (next to `AURORA_SESSION`), in `process_start` processes, in terminal shells and in SSH execs (after the `AURORA_*` sweep). Aurora sets it last; it is not an SSH client-accepted name.
  - Instruction file, Skills section (all harnesses): `Skills are read-only folders in <abs path> (also $SKILLS_DIR in every shell); list and read them with the skill tools. A skill's own files are under <abs path>/<skill name>/; run its scripts as "$SKILLS_DIR/<skill name>/scripts/<file>".`
  - `12 §Harness interface`: `skills_dir` is required for every adapter.

### Phase 5 — P5-APPSKILL (apps ship skills; the agent guide is removed; D-452, D-453, SEC-150)

Amends C-640..C-649 and C-652/C-653 (P5-GUIDE). Everything a package ships stays untrusted third-party content (SEC-117 for the summary, essentials and operations file; SEC-150 for skills). Briefs: `docs/tasks/P5-APPSKILL-1.md` (C-1130..C-1132, C-1138), `P5-APPSKILL-2.md` (C-1133..C-1137), `P5-APPSKILL-3.md` (C-1139), `P5-APPSKILL-4.md` (Indico content, no contract).

- C-1130 **Package archive and linter** (P5-APPSKILL-1, D-453(a), (b)).
  - New archive paths `skills/<app_id>/<name>/SKILL.md` and `skills/<app_id>/<name>/<path>`: regular files only (symlinks and hard links refused as today), `<app_id>` an app of this package (`app_skill_unknown_app`), `<name>` a skill name (`[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*`, ≤ `SKILL_NAME_MAX`, not `aurora` / `aurora-*`) that equals `<app_id>` or starts with `<app_id>-`, and whose longest matching app-id prefix among the package's apps is `<app_id>` itself (in a package with `jira` and `jira-dc`, `jira-dc-x` belongs under `skills/jira-dc/`) (`app_skill_name`), unique in the package. **FIX-221 (D-465(a)(b)):** the owner is taken over the package's apps **and the apps of every other active package** (`ValidationContext.active_app_ids`; ids owned by this same package id, an upgrade of itself, do not count); when it is an app of another package the error is `app_skill_name_claimed` at `skills/<app_id>/<name>`, the message naming the owner app and its package. `aurora plugin validate`/`test` take `--other-app-id <id>` (repeatable) for the apps of other packages and report which they checked against (`skill_names_checked_against`); `check-builtin.sh` passes every other built-in's app ids.
  - `SKILL.md` and files: exactly the rules of `10 §Skills` through the **shared validator** (`aurora-registry::skill_format`, used by the linter, the CLI and the API's library reader): frontmatter (`app_skill_frontmatter`: YAML between `---` lines, `name` absent or equal to the folder, single-line `description` ≤ `SKILL_DESCRIPTION_MAX`, no anchors or aliases), body ≤ `SKILL_BODY_MAX` (`app_skill_body_too_large`), body + files ≤ `SKILL_TOTAL_MAX` (`app_skill_too_large`), paths `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, depth ≤ 4, ≤ 200 characters, no `..`, no second `skill.md` in any case (`app_skill_path`), ≤ `SKILL_FILES_MAX` files (`app_skill_too_many_files`), each ≤ 64 KiB (`app_skill_file_too_large`). **Files are UTF-8 text** (no NUL, no byte-order mark; `app_skill_not_text`): the home projection sends text only.
  - Per app at most `APP_SKILLS_MAX` (16) skills (`app_skills_too_many`) and `APP_SKILLS_APP_MAX` (256 KiB of `SKILL.md` and files, `app_skills_app_too_large`).
  - `agent.summary` is required when the app has skills or `operations` (`agent_summary_required`).
  - **Ignored (warning, never an error):** `agent.guide` (any content) and every file under `agent/<app_id>/` other than the `operations` file → `agent_guide_ignored` once per app; the package stays valid, the entries are parsed so an old manifest still compiles, and nothing of them is stored for agents or put in the snapshot. The `agent_guide_*` and `agent_recipe_not_markdown` error codes are removed; the operations-file codes (`agent_operations_*`) are unchanged.
  - Limits removed: `AGENT_GUIDE_FILES_MAX`, `AGENT_GUIDE_FILE_MAX`, `AGENT_GUIDE_APP_MAX`, `AGENT_GUIDE_SYNC_MAX`. Added: `APP_SKILLS_MAX` 16, `APP_SKILLS_APP_MAX` 256 KiB, `APP_SKILLS_SYNC_MAX` 2 MiB.
- C-1131 **Canonical digest with the executable bit** (P5-APPSKILL-1, D-452(a)). For an entry under `skills/` whose tar mode has the owner-execute bit, the digest line is `<path>\0<sha256 hex>\0x\n`; every other line is unchanged (`<path>\0<sha256 hex>\n`), so the digest of every package without `skills/` is byte for byte what it was. One function computes it for `aurora plugin build`, `sign`, `verify` and API ingest. `build` writes mode `0755` for a source file that is executable on disk (git mode `100755`) and `0644` otherwise; ingest reads the bit from the same entry it hashed.
- C-1132 **Snapshot and storage** (P5-APPSKILL-1).
  - `apps[].agent.skills?: [{ name, description, sha256, size, files: [{ path, sha256, size, executable: bool }] }]`, sorted by `name`, files by `path`; `sha256`/`size` of `SKILL.md` as shipped; omitted when the app has none. `apps[].agent.guide` is no longer emitted (also for stored versions that carry it). `summary`, `operations` unchanged (C-641, C-651). No content in the snapshot.
  - Migration `20261201000272_plugin_blobs_agent_file.sql`: `plugin_blobs.kind` value `agent_guide` becomes **`agent_file`** (check constraint replaced, existing rows updated in the same transaction). Each skill file, each `SKILL.md` and the operations file of a non-`invalid` package is one blob of kind `agent_file`, keyed by the SHA-256 of its bytes, in the ingest transaction; content is read by digest only, whatever the row's kind (as C-642).
- C-1133 **Effective skills with tier `app`** (P5-APPSKILL-2, D-453(c), (d)).
  - Tier order agent > workspace > organization > **app**. The app skills of an agent are those of every app its home lists (C-644's apps: active connections with a grant, app available to the org and workspace), from the active snapshot. A name taken by an enabled skill of any other tier → the app skill is shadowed (`shadowed_by` that skill); a disabled skill does not veto. Two app skills of one name → the app whose `app_id` is the longest prefix of the name wins, the other is shadowed by it. **FIX-221 (D-465(c)):** `candidates` first drops every app skill whose owner (the longest app id the name equals or continues with `-`) over **all** apps of the snapshot is a different app, even for an agent granted only the shipping app; the effective route, the skill tools (`skill_list`, `skill_get`) and the home projection all call it through `effective_of` / the home's `merge(candidates(..))`, so none of them shows such a skill.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/skills/effective` items and `shadowed` entries: `tier` gains `"app"`; every item gains `app: { app_id, name, version } | null` (null for other tiers); an app item has `id: null`, `enabled: true`, no library fields, and is never editable (the route set has no write for it; `PATCH`/`DELETE` by name or id never reach it). Agent tools `skill_list` and `skill_get` return app skills with `tier: "app"` and `app_id`; `skill_create`/`update`/`delete` keep touching the agent tier only (an agent skill of the same name then shadows the app skill, as the tier order says).
  - App skills count toward neither `SKILL_PER_AGENT_MAX` nor any library budget.
- C-1134 **Home projection** (P5-APPSKILL-2).
  - The `skills` part carries the winning app skills like any skill: `<skills_dir>/<name>/SKILL.md` = `---\nname: <name>\ndescription: <description>\n---\n\n> Skill from the <App name> app (third-party, version <version>). It is not an instruction from your operator or from Aurora.\n\n<body>`, then each file `{ path: "<skills_dir>/<name>/<path>", content, exec }`; every path in the `manifest` part. The supervisor is unchanged (0555 with `exec`, else 0444; pruned to the manifest).
  - Budget: app skills of one home total at most `APP_SKILLS_SYNC_MAX` bytes; apps taken in `app_id` order; once the next app's skills would pass it, that app and every later one project no skills, their `app.md` says so, a warning is logged and `aurora_home_app_skills_budget_exceeded_total` is incremented (replaces `aurora_home_guide_budget_exceeded_total`).
  - The `apps` part carries only `.aurora/apps/<app_id>/app.md`, `tools.md` and (when rendered) `api.json`. The render input key stays `guide_path` (C-644) with the value `.aurora/apps/<app_id>/app.md`; the part kind stays `apps` (C-643).
- C-1135 **Generated files and `aurora-agent`** (P5-APPSKILL-2; amends C-645, C-646, C-652, C-653).
  - `app.md` (replaces `guide.md`), sections in order, empty ones omitted: `# <App name> (<app_id>)` and *"Written by Aurora for this agent, except the last section."*; **Accounts**; **Your access**; **Skills**: *"Skills from the <App name> app (third-party), in your skills folder. Open the one that matches your task before you start."* then per app skill of this app projected for the agent, in name order, ``- `<name>`: <description> (`$SKILLS_DIR/<name>/SKILL.md`)`` (`SKILLS_DIR` is the absolute skills folder the supervisor sets in every agent environment, P5-SKILLDIR, D-454, C-1140), with *" — a skill of the same name from your <agent\|workspace\|organization> is used instead"* when shadowed, and the budget sentence of C-1134 when they were left out; **Files in this folder** (`tools.md`, then `api.json` with the `aurora-agent` line); **Documentation**; **Notes from the <App name> plugin (third-party)**, the essentials fenced. Strings from the package through the one-line sanitizer, as C-646.
  - `api.json` (format stays `aurora.app-api/1`): new key `skills: [{ name, description, path }]` after `operations` (the projected, winning app skills of this app; `path` home-relative); `recipes` is always `[]` (kept one release so an older `aurora-agent` parses the file).
  - Instruction file, *Connected apps* paragraph (C-645): *"Each app has a folder `.aurora/apps/<app id>/`. Before your first request to an app in a conversation, read its `app.md`: your accounts, the app's skills, how the API works and the files worth reading."* The `aurora-agent` sentence says *"finds operations and skills across your apps"*. Everything else in C-645 is unchanged.
  - `aurora-agent`: `list` shows a skill count per app; `search` ranks app skill names (weight 5) and descriptions (weight 3) beside operations, result line ``<app> skill <name> — <description>``; `describe <app> <skill name>` prints the description and the path; the fallback message names `app.md`. Recipes are no longer read.
- C-1136 **Admin API** (P5-APPSKILL-2; replaces C-648).
  - `PluginPackage.agent_guides` is replaced by **`agent: [{ app_id, summary: string|null, instructions: string, operations: { file, size, sha256, count } | null, skills: [{ name, description, size, files: [{ path, size, sha256, executable }], claimed_by?: { app_id, app_name } }], changed }]`** (FIX-221, D-465(d): `claimed_by` is present only when the skill's name is owned by an app of another package in the current snapshot, so no agent gets the skill; additive), one entry per app of the package. `changed` is `null` when there is no active version of the package, else `{ summary: bool, instructions: bool, operations: bool, skills: [{ name, change: "added"|"changed"|"removed", files: [{ path, change: "added"|"changed"|"removed"|"mode" }] }] }` (`mode`: the executable bit differs; a file whose content and bit both changed is listed twice, `changed` and `mode`; `SKILL.md` appears as a file of its skill).
  - `GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}/apps/{app_id}/skills/{name}/file?path=<path>` (instance admins; `path` absent → `SKILL.md` as shipped) and `GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}/apps/{app_id}/operations`: the file as `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; 404 for an unknown package, app, skill, path or operations file; 400 `invalid_request` for a `path` that fails the grammar. Operation ids `adminGetPluginAppSkillFile`, `adminGetPluginOperationsFile`. `GET /v1/admin/plugins/{id}/agent-guides/{app_id}/{file}` (`adminGetPluginAgentGuideFile`) is removed.
- C-1137 **Audit** (P5-APPSKILL-2). `plugin.approve`, the system row `plugin.activate` and `plugin.auto_approved` carry **`agent_changed: [app_id, …]`** (replaces `agent_guide_changed`) when, against the previously active version, the summary, the essentials, the operations file digest, or any app skill (added, removed, `SKILL.md` digest, a file added, removed, changed or its executable bit) differs; omitted when empty.
- C-1138 **CLI** (P5-APPSKILL-1; amends C-649, C-650).
  - `aurora plugin preview [--app <id>] [--access full|custom] [--out <dir>]` replaces `aurora plugin guide`: renders for a sample account what the agent gets, `apps/<app_id>/{app.md,tools.md,api.json}` and `skills/<name>/…` exactly as projected (provenance line included, modes from the bit), to the directory or to stdout. `aurora plugin guide` is removed.
  - `aurora plugin new <id>` scaffolds `agent.summary`, a short `agent.instructions` and `skills/<id>/<id>-example/SKILL.md`; `build` packs `skills/**` with the bit (C-1131); `validate` runs C-1130; `docs` lists each app's skills (name and description).
  - `aurora plugin import-openapi` writes `agent/<app_id>/operations.json` and the `tools:` skeleton on stdout only: no `operations.md`, no `ops-<tag>.md`, no `guide:` entries (`aurora-agent search`/`describe` serve the index); its goldens change accordingly.
- C-1139 **Dashboard** (P5-APPSKILL-3).
  - Plugin page and permissions review: the **Agent guide** section becomes **For agents**, per app: the summary, the essentials, the operations file with its operation count, and **Skills** (name, description, size; each file with its size, executables marked "Script"; change marks from `changed`, `mode` shown as "now a script" / "no longer a script"); a file opens in the shared code viewer as plain text, never rendered. The review sentence: *"Agents that use this app get these skills. They are written by the publisher; scripts run in the agent's sandbox with the agent's access."*
  - The approve control of a pending version whose `changed` lists skill changes shows one line above the button: *"Skills for agents changed: <n> added, <n> changed, <n> removed"* (+ *", <n> scripts"* when any changed file is executable), linking to the section.
  - Agent › Skills (the skills view): app items under their own group "From apps", each "From the <App name> app · <version>", read-only (no edit, disable or delete), shadowed app skills in the shadowed list with the winner named. Follows `14 §Visual design`.

### FIX-225 (queued messages: Steer, Send now, Edit, Cancel; D-469, D-470, SEC-152)

Rule: `10 §Turns` (follow-ups), `12 §Turn controls by harness`, `13 §action.invoked`, `14` (queued follow-ups), `03` (`turns`), `17` (SEC-152). Migration `20261201000275_followup_controls.sql`.

- C-1152 **Turn controls by harness**: `aurora_protocol::runner::harness_turn_controls(id) -> { steer: bool, background_tool: bool }` is the one table (from the FIX-225 Part A probe; unknown id → both `false`); a supervisor test pins every adapter's declared `steer`/`background_tool` to it. `HarnessView` (C-420) gains `steer: boolean` and `background_tool: boolean` from it. A BYO agent has neither.
- C-1153 **Follow-ups wait** (amends `10 §Follow-ups / steering`): due-work arm 7 steers a `joining` follow-up only when `steer_requested_at` is set, or when its door is `system` (approval resume, C-824, unchanged). A `SteerResult` of `missed` or `refused` sets `turns.steer_error` (`missed` | `refused`) and appends the durable `followup.steer_failed { followup_id, reason }`; the follow-up stays `joining`, is not re-sent, and is promoted when the turn ends. Promotion skips `cancelled` follow-ups.
- C-1154 **Follow-up shape and list**. `Followup` gains `status: … | "cancelled"`, `speaker_label: string | null`, `edited_at: Timestamp | null`, `steer_requested: boolean`, `steer_error: "missed" | "refused" | null` and `controls: { steer: boolean; send_now: boolean; edit: boolean; cancel: boolean }`, computed **for the caller**: `steer` = harness steers ∧ parent active ∧ not yet requested or delivered ∧ caller has Stop authority; `send_now` = harness declares `background_tool` ∧ ¬ harness steers ∧ parent `running` ∧ Stop authority; `edit` = waiting (`joining`, steer not delivered, or delivered and failed) ∧ caller is the follow-up's speaker; `cancel` = waiting ∧ (speaker ∨ Stop authority). Where a route returns a `Followup` outside the list (C-25, C-323), `controls` is computed the same way. `GET /v1/conversations/{id}/followups` (S/K, `can_see_conversation`) → 200 `{ items: Followup[] }`: the conversation's `joining` follow-ups whose parent is active, oldest first (at most 50).
- C-1155 **Follow-up actions** (S/K; never an agent or sandbox token; 404 when the caller cannot see the conversation, checked first):
  - `POST /v1/followups/{id}/steer` → 202 `Followup` (sets `steer_requested_at`, appends durable `followup.steer_requested { followup_id }`, notifies work; a repeat is a no-op 202). 403 `followup_forbidden` (no Stop authority); 409 `followup_not_waiting`; 409 `steer_unsupported` (`harness_turn_controls` or the running sandbox's reported capabilities lack `steer`); 423 `frozen`.
  - `POST /v1/followups/{id}/cancel` → 202 `Followup` (`status: "cancelled"`, durable `followup.cancelled { followup_id, by: { id, display_name } }`). 403 `followup_forbidden`; 409 `followup_not_waiting`. Not refused for a frozen agent.
  - `PATCH /v1/followups/{id}` `{ text }` → 200 `Followup` (text replaced, `edited_at` set, durable `followup.edited { followup_id, text }`). Same text validation as `POST …/messages` (400, 413 above `TURN_TEXT_MAX`); attachments and reply target unchanged. 403 `followup_forbidden` (not the speaker); 409 `followup_not_waiting`.
  - *Waiting* = `status = 'joining'` ∧ (`steer_delivered_at IS NULL` ∨ `steer_error IS NOT NULL`); for steer additionally `steer_delivered_at IS NULL`. Each action takes the conversation lock, then the follow-up row `FOR UPDATE`; arm 7's claim and promotion re-check the state, so an edit or cancel never races a delivery.
- C-1156 **Send now honesty** (amends the `/v1/turns/{id}/send-now` row): 409 `send_now_unsupported` when the agent's harness lacks `background_tool` or steers. A `SendNowResult` of `nothing_running` or `unsupported` appends the durable `turn.send_now_failed { turn_id, outcome }` (`moved` keeps `message.sent_now`). Chat cards (`13`): the turn status card shows Stop, plus **Steer** (new action kind `followup.steer { turn_id }`: steers every waiting follow-up of the turn the clicker may steer) when the harness steers, or **Send now** when it declares `background_tool` and does not steer; every card action answers the clicker with a visible line, including "No message is waiting to be steered." and "This agent can't take messages mid-turn; they run when the current turn ends." (also for an old card's `followup.send_now`). `followup.steer_failed` and `turn.send_now_failed` in a channel-door conversation are posted to the thread as a platform note.

### Phase 6 — P6-TEMPLATES (agent templates from template repositories; D-435, D-436, D-456, D-457, SEC-146)

Rule: `docs/tasks/P6-TEMPLATES.md` (design), `docs/25-agent-templates.md` (the public format, written by P6-TPL-1), `03 §Planned changes` (until the migrations land), `17` SEC-146. Briefs P6-TPL-1..6. Errors use the standard body. **IA** = instance admin (session), audited, 403 for anyone else. A template **never gives access by itself**: every grant is a person's choice at creation, written through the one grant writer (SEC-146). Numbering: C-1141 is the template format (it was C-1102 on the branch, which collided with master's C-1102); C-1103..C-1119 keep their numbers; C-1142..C-1144 were added with D-456.

- C-1141 **Template format 1** (P6-TPL-1; `25` is normative for publishers; replaces the branch's C-1102).
  - **Two forms of the same files.** A template is a directory named by its `id`. In a repository it is `<folder>/<id>/` (C-1103). **Packed**, it is a zip (stored or deflate) whose only top-level directory is `<id>/`, with every file under it; this is what `aurora template pack` and Save as template write. `read_archive` (zip) and `read_tree` (files with their git modes) produce the same `TemplateContent` from the same files (C-1104). A directory or root whose name is not the `id` in `template.yaml` is `template_dir_name`.
  - **Size and names.** Packed: at most `TEMPLATE_ARCHIVE_MAX` (5 MiB). Unpacked: at most `TEMPLATE_UNPACKED_MAX` (8 MiB) and `TEMPLATE_FILES_MAX` (200) files, in either form. A zip entry whose unpacked size is more than 100× its packed size is refused. Names are relative, `/`-separated, `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, at most 6 segments below the root. Absolute names, `..`, segments starting with `.`, symlinks (zip external mode `120000`, git mode `120000`), submodules (git `160000`) and other non-regular entries are refused. Directory entries are ignored. The executable bit is mode `0o100` of the zip external attributes, or git mode `100755`.
  - **Files under the root** (the agent export's layout, D-413, plus `template.yaml` and `helpers/`):
    - `template.yaml` (required): below.
    - `agent.yaml` (required): the export's `format: 1`; `name` (1..80, the suggested name); `purpose` (accepted, ignored); `model` (must be `default`, else `template_model_named`, because model names differ between installations); `may_message_first` (bool; `true` → `proactive_messaging = allow`, `false` → `ask`).
    - `instructions.md` (required, may be empty): at most `BRIEF_MAX` (20 000) characters.
    - `memory/<key>.md` (at most `TEMPLATE_MEMORIES_MAX`, 20): front matter `key` (equal to the file name, the memory key rules), `updated` (accepted, ignored) and, optionally, `locked` (a boolean, default false; C-1151, D-463(d)); the text after it, at most `MEMORY_CONTENT_MAX`.
    - `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` plus that skill's other files (at most `TEMPLATE_SKILLS_MAX`, 25 skills): front matter `name` (equal to the directory name; the skill name rules, never `aurora-*`) and `use-it-when` (one line, which becomes the skill `description`); then the body. The skill rules of `10 §Skills` and the library's file rules apply: `LIBRARY_FILE_MAX` (64 KiB) per file, at most 50 files per skill, depth ≤ 4, no `SKILL.md` other than the one at the skill's root.
    - `helpers/<path>` (optional; at most `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_MAX`, 50 files): each at most `TEMPLATE_HELPER_FILE_MAX` (256 KiB), all together at most `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_TOTAL_MAX` (2 MiB), depth ≤ 4 below `helpers/`. Every file is UTF-8 text without a NUL byte (`template_helper_invalid`, owner decision 2026-11-24 — see the text rule below). They are copied byte for byte (no placeholders) and projected read-only at `.aurora/helpers/<path>` (C-1143). Problems are `template_helper_invalid`.
    - `automations/<file>.yaml` (at most `TEMPLATE_AUTOMATIONS_MAX`, 10): the export's shapes. A schedule needs `cadence: { cron }` (5-field, `croner`, at least 5 minutes apart). A one-time `cadence: { preset: once, … }` is refused (`template_schedule_once`). `time_zone` and `enabled` are accepted. The schedule runs in the workspace's time zone unless the person picks another when creating; the template's `time_zone` is not used. A webhook carries `name`, `instructions` and `verification` (`signed`|`password`|`none`). Its `enabled` is ignored: webhooks are always created paused. Names are unique per kind.
    - `avatar.png`, `avatar.jpg` or `avatar.webp` (optional, at most one, `AVATAR_MAX`).
    - `README.md` (**required**, D-458): what the agent is designed for, written for the people who decide whether to deploy it. It should say what the agent does and doesn't do, who it suits, what it needs (apps, accounts, libraries), what it runs on its own, and how to adapt it afterwards. It is 1..`TEMPLATE_README_MAX` (20 000) characters of Markdown (CommonMark + GFM). It is part of the content and of `content_sha256`, so the admin approves it with the rest. It gets the text hygiene below and **no placeholders**: it is shown before any input is filled. If it is missing or empty, that is `template_readme_missing`. Relative links and images in it are not resolved, so a README stands alone.
    - `CHANGELOG.md` (optional; not part of the content and not hashed; the reference CI uses it as release notes, C-1144).
    - Anything else: `template_file_unexpected`.
  - **Executable files are text** (D-457(e)). A file with the executable bit, whether in `helpers/` or in a skill, must be valid UTF-8 without a NUL byte (`template_executable_binary`), so the admin can read everything that will run. In `skills/`, files that are not executable may be binary. In `helpers/`, **every file must be UTF-8 text without a NUL byte, executable or not** (owner decision 2026-11-24, option A: the home sync carries UTF-8 text only, so a non-text helper would be stored but silently never installed; `template_helper_invalid` at the file's path, refused by the template validation, the API's helper writer and the Save as template dialog).
  - **`template.yaml`** (strict: unknown keys refused; YAML anchors, aliases, tags and multiple documents refused):
    ```yaml
    format: 1                     # integer; any other value: template_format_unsupported
    id: service-monitor           # [a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*, at most 64; equals the directory name
    version: 1.0.0                # semver, no build metadata
    name: Service monitor         # 1..80
    summary: Watches dashboards …  # 1..280
    min_core: 6.0.0               # optional semver: the oldest Aurora that may use this version
    effort: medium                # optional: low | medium | high | max
    inputs:                       # at most TEMPLATE_INPUTS_MAX (10)
      - id: jira_project          # [a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}, unique
        label: Jira project key   # 1..80
        help: The key in issue numbers, like OPS in OPS-123.   # optional, at most 200
        kind: text                # text (default) | choice
        required: true            # default true
        pattern: "^[A-Z][A-Z0-9]+$"   # text only, optional, at most 200 chars; Rust `regex` syntax, compiled with a 64 KiB size limit; must match the whole value
        max_length: 20            # text only, 1..TEMPLATE_INPUT_VALUE_MAX (200), default 100
        options: [Low, High]      # choice only: 1..20 distinct, each 1..80
        default: OPS              # optional; must satisfy the rules above
    apps:                         # at most TEMPLATE_APPS_MAX (10)
      - app: atlassian            # exactly one of app / any_of
        required: true            # default true
        level: ask                # read (default) | ask | act
        tools:                    # optional, only with `app`: replaces `level` by a custom choice
          allow: [jira_search, jira_issue_get]
          ask: [jira_issue_create, jira_comment_add]
        why: Files and updates tickets in your project.   # 1..200
      - any_of: [microsoft-365, caldav, google-workspace]  # 2..5 distinct app ids
        required: true
        level: ask
        why: Reads and books events in your calendar.
    skills:                       # skill references, C-1142; at most TEMPLATE_SKILL_REFS_MAX (20)
      - library: https://gitlab.cern.ch/agents/skills.git
        skill: incident-writeup
        required: false
        why: Writes the incident summary the way the service desk wants it.
    ```
    An app id is syntax-checked only (`[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*`). Whether this installation has the app is decided when the template is offered and used (C-1110, C-1111). An app id appears in at most one entry. `tools` needs at least one tool in `allow ∪ ask`, and no tool may be in both.
  - **Placeholders.** `{input.<id>}`, `{agent.name}` and `{person.name}` are filled in `instructions.md`, memory texts, a skill's `use-it-when` and body, schedule prompts and webhook instructions, and nowhere else. They are not filled in names, keys, cron, other skill files, helpers or `template.yaml`. `{input.<id>}` naming an undeclared input is `template_placeholder_unknown`. Any other text in braces is kept literally. Substitution is **one pass**: an inserted value is never scanned again, so a value containing `{input.x}` stays as typed. `{person.name}` is the creating person's display name and `{agent.name}` the name they chose. An optional input left empty becomes the empty string.
  - **Text hygiene.** Control characters (except newline and tab in bodies), bidirectional controls and zero-width characters are removed from every text field and body before anything is stored, as for library skills (`10 §Git skill library`). Helpers and other skill files are not changed.
  - **Validation result.** All problems at once, at most 20: `{ code, path, message }`, with `path` the file and, where it applies, the YAML key (`template.yaml#/apps/1/level`). Codes: `template_archive`, `template_dir_name`, `template_readme_missing`, `template_file_unexpected`, `template_format_unsupported`, `template_yaml`, `template_field`, `template_model_named`, `template_schedule_once`, `template_placeholder_unknown`, `template_skill_invalid`, `template_skill_ref_invalid`, `template_helper_invalid`, `template_executable_binary`, `template_memory_invalid`, `template_automation_invalid`, `template_too_large`.
- C-1103 **Template repository, format 1** (P6-TPL-1 defines and parses it, P6-TPL-2 reads it; `25` is normative; D-456(a)–(c), D-457(a), (b)).
  - **Any git repository** that a C-1123 address names and the C-1125 reader can read over https: public, or with a read-only token (C-1107). No GitHub or GitLab app, connection or account is involved. A source is the address, an optional branch or tag (`ref`; default the default branch) and a **folder** (`path`, default the root, at most 4 segments).
  - **Layout in the folder**, at the tracked ref:
    ```
    aurora-templates.yaml             # the repository manifest (required)
    schedule-manager/                 # one directory per template, named by its id (C-1141)
      README.md                       # required: what this agent is for, for the people deciding whether to deploy it
      template.yaml
      agent.yaml
      instructions.md
      skills/what-is-on/SKILL.md
      skills/what-is-on/format.py
      helpers/…
    service-monitor/
      …
    README.md                         # the repository's own README; it and anything else outside template directories (docs/, CI files) is ignored
    .github/workflows/aurora-templates.yml    # the reference CI (C-1144)
    ```
    A template is **every directory directly in the folder that contains `template.yaml`**. Other directories and files are ignored. At most `TEMPLATES_PER_SOURCE_MAX` (100) templates. **No zip is kept in the repository**: packages are CI artifacts.
  - **`aurora-templates.yaml`**: at most `TEMPLATE_MANIFEST_MAX` (64 KiB). **No signing key and no publisher** (D-435(b)). Strict parse:
    ```yaml
    api: 1                                   # integer; any other value: template_manifest_invalid
    repo: { name: CERN agent templates, description: …, homepage: https://…, contact: mailto:… }   # name 1..80, description ≤ 280, homepage/contact optional
    packages:
      url: https://github.com/cern/agent-templates/releases/download/{id}-v{version}/{id}-{version}.zip
    ```
    `packages.url` is an https URL template with exactly the placeholders `{id}` and `{version}` (each at least once, nothing else in braces). Rendered, it must be at most 500 characters, without user info, query or fragment, on a host the destination guard allows (checked when downloading). Examples in `25`: GitHub release assets (above); GitLab generic packages `https://gitlab.example.org/api/v4/projects/<numeric id>/packages/generic/{id}/{version}/{id}-{version}.zip`; any static https host that serves the same bytes.
  - **A version is published** by setting `version` in `<id>/template.yaml` on the tracked ref; CI then packs that directory (`aurora template pack <id>`, C-1105) and publishes the zip at the rendered `packages.url`. A package is **immutable**: a published `<id>-<version>.zip` is never replaced (CI refuses, C-1144; Aurora never downloads an accepted version again). Aurora reads only the head of the tracked ref, so versions skipped between two checks are never offered.
  - **The package must be the source.** Aurora accepts a package only when its `content_sha256` (C-1104) equals that of the template directory at the head commit (C-1108, `source_mismatch` otherwise). So a package cannot carry files that the repository does not have, such as tools built in CI (not in this version).
- C-1104 **Crate `aurora-template`** (P6-TPL-1; pure: no IO, no async, no database; the API, the CLI and the tests use only it; it may depend on `aurora-core` for the limits and the C-1123 address parser).
  - `read_archive(&[u8]) -> Result<TemplateContent, Vec<TemplateProblem>>` and `read_tree(root_name, &[TreeFile { path, bytes, mode }]) -> Result<TemplateContent, Vec<TemplateProblem>>` (C-1141; the same model and checks from either form).
  - `parse_meta(&[u8]) -> Result<TemplateMeta, Vec<TemplateProblem>>`: `template.yaml` alone, with the same strict parse. The ingest uses it to list a repository without reading every file.
  - `parse_manifest(&[u8]) -> Result<TemplateManifest, ManifestProblem>` (C-1103) and `render_package_url(&TemplateManifest, id, version) -> Result<String, ManifestProblem>`.
  - `content_sha256(&TemplateContent) -> String`: SHA-256 of a canonical serialization (the content struct with every map sorted by key, file bytes in base64 and executable bits; the template's `README.md` included, `CHANGELOG.md` excluded). It is stable across releases and pinned by a golden test.
  - `check_inputs(&[InputDecl], &BTreeMap<String, String>) -> Result<CheckedInputs, Vec<InputProblem>>`. Rules: `missing`, `unknown`, `too_long`, `pattern`, `not_an_option`, `control_characters` (a value may not contain newlines or control characters).
  - `render(&TemplateContent, &CheckedInputs, agent_name, person_name) -> RenderedTemplate` (one pass, C-1141).
  - The **part hashes** used by updates (C-1112): `instructions_hash(text)`; `skill_hash(description, body, files: [(path, bytes, executable)] sorted by path)`; `helpers_hash(files sorted by path)`; `skill_ref_key(&SkillRef)` (the normalized reference that identifies a `library_skill` part).
  - `pack(root_name, files) -> Vec<u8>`: a deterministic zip with sorted names, a fixed DOS timestamp (1980-01-01), stored entries and the executable bit, so CI can compare a fresh pack with a published package byte for byte (C-1144).
  - Its own small zip reader (central directory; stored and deflate through `flate2`, already in the workspace); no `zip` crate without asking.
- C-1105 **CLI** (P6-TPL-1). None of these commands needs a server or a login. Each prints problems as `<path>: <code>: <message>`, one per line, and exits 1 when there are any.
  - `aurora template check <path>`: `<path>` is a template directory, a packed zip, or a **repository folder** (it has `aurora-templates.yaml`). For a repository it checks the manifest and every template directory, that each directory name is its id, and that `packages.url` renders for every template. It prints `ok <id> <version> sha256:<content_sha256>` per template, and exits 1 on any problem.
  - `aurora template pack <dir> [-o <file>]` validates the directory, writes the deterministic zip (default `<id>-<version>.zip`) and prints `<file> sha256:<archive sha256> content:<content_sha256> <size> bytes`.
  - `aurora template list <repository folder> [--json]` prints each template's `id`, `version`, `name` and directory (JSON: `[{ id, version, name, dir }]`). CI uses it to decide what to publish.
- C-1106 **Data** (P6-TPL-2, one migration, next free number when placed; `03` gains the real sections and `§Planned changes` shrinks).
  - `agent_template_sources` = `{ id uuid pk, name text not null (from the manifest's repo.name, cleaned), url text not null (C-1123 stored form), ref text null, path text not null default '', token_ref text null, token_updated_at timestamptz null (CHECK both null or both set; C-1126's shape with purpose TemplateSourceToken), homepage text null, contact text null, packages_url text not null (the manifest's URL template), manifest jsonb not null (the last accepted manifest as parsed), last_head_sha text null, update_interval text not null CHECK in (6h,12h,1d,1w,2w,1mo,never) default 1d, status text not null CHECK in (ok, check_failed) default ok, check_failed_at timestamptz null, check_failed_reason text null, last_checked_at timestamptz null, next_check_at timestamptz not null default now(), check_lease_until timestamptz null, lease_owner uuid null, created_by uuid null FK→users set null, created_at, updated_at; unique (url, coalesce(ref, ''), path) }`.
  - `agent_templates` = `{ id uuid pk, template_id text not null unique, source_id uuid not null FK→agent_template_sources on delete cascade, dir text not null, name text not null, summary text not null (both from `template.yaml` at the head, cleaned), head_version text null, head_state text not null CHECK in (ok, needs_newer_aurora, invalid) default ok, head_problems jsonb null (at most 20 C-1141 problems of the head's `template.yaml`), offered bool not null default true, removed_upstream bool not null default false, created_at, updated_at }`.
  - `agent_template_versions` = `{ id uuid pk, agent_template_id uuid not null FK→agent_templates on delete cascade, version text not null, status text not null CHECK in (awaiting_package, pending_approval, approved, rejected, invalid), status_reason text null, commit_sha text not null (the head commit where `template.yaml` had this version), package_url text not null (rendered), archive_sha256 text null, size_bytes integer null, content_sha256 text null, content jsonb null (the parsed TemplateContent; files base64; the avatar re-encoded to a 256×256 PNG), last_attempt_at timestamptz null, decided_by uuid null FK→users set null, decided_at timestamptz null, created_at; unique (agent_template_id, version); CHECK (status IN ('awaiting_package', 'invalid')) = (content IS NULL) }`.
  - `org_disabled_agent_templates` = `{ org_id uuid not null FK→organizations on delete cascade, template_id text not null, disabled_by uuid null FK→users set null, created_at; pk (org_id, template_id) }`. It is keyed by the template id, so a source removed and added again keeps the organization's choice.
  - `aurora_secrets::store::Purpose::TemplateSourceToken` (in `Purpose::ALL`; source table `agent_template_sources.token_ref`; owner = the source id, org = the nil UUID; sealed `{ username, token }`; opened only by the repository reader and the package download; deleted with the source and on `token: null`).
  - The gateway role gets nothing on these tables.
- C-1107 **Template sources (admin)** (P6-TPL-2; all **IA**; the addon-source routes C-591..C-598 with the signing parts removed, and the skill library's address, check and token rules C-1122..C-1126).
  - `AgentTemplateSource` = `{ id, name, url, repo (display form, as C-1122), ref, path, token_set, token_updated_at, homepage, contact, packages_url, update_interval, status, check_failed_at, check_failed_reason, last_checked_at, last_head_sha, templates: [{ template_id, dir, name, summary, head_version, head_state, state: "new"|"ingested"|"id_taken"|"invalid" }], created_at, updated_at }`. It is built from stored rows, never a live read; the token is never returned.
  - `GET /v1/admin/agent-template-sources` (`adminListTemplateSources`) → `{ items }`; `GET …/{id}` (`adminGetTemplateSource`).
  - `POST …/preview` `{ url, ref?, path?, token?, token_username? }` (`adminPreviewTemplateSource`): **check without saving**, as C-1124. It reads the repository once (resolve the ref, list the folder, read `aurora-templates.yaml` and each `template.yaml` with `parse_meta`) and answers `{ url (normalized), repo, ref|null, resolved: { kind: "branch"|"tag"|"commit", name, default: bool }, commit_sha, path, manifest: { name, description, packages_url (the manifest's `packages.url` pattern, shown as text, never fetched by the browser) }, templates: [{ template_id, dir, name, summary, version, state, problems: [TemplateProblem] }], truncated }`. Nothing is stored and no audit row is written. A token in the body is used for this read only. Errors: 422 `template_url_invalid` (`details.reason` as C-1122's `library_url_invalid`); 422 `template_unreachable` (the C-1125 reasons in words: needs a token, token refused, not found, branch or tag not found, moved, too large, timeout); 422 `template_manifest_invalid` (no `aurora-templates.yaml` in the folder, or one sanitized message); 409 `template_source_exists` (same `url` + `ref` + `path`). `Cache-Control: no-store`. Rate: 10 per minute per person (429 `rate_limited`).
  - `POST /v1/admin/agent-template-sources` `{ url, ref?, path?, token?, token_username?, update_interval? }` (`adminAddTemplateSource`) → 201. It reads the repository as preview does, then one audited transaction (critical `agent_template_source.add`; metadata url, ref, path, `token: "set"` when given, never the value) writes the source and seals the token. The first check (C-1108) then runs outside that transaction. At most `TEMPLATE_SOURCES_MAX` (20): 422 `template_source_limit`. The token rules are C-1122's (`token` 1..1024 printable ASCII; `token_username` default `x-access-token`).
  - `PATCH …/{id}` `{ update_interval?, ref? ("" clears), path?, token?: string|null, token_username? }` (`adminUpdateTemplateSource`). A change of ref, path or token re-reads the repository first (422 `template_unreachable` keeps the old values). `url` is fixed: a different repository is a new source. Stored versions are never touched. Critical `agent_template_source.update` (`token: "set"|"replaced"|"removed"`).
  - `POST …/{id}/check` (`adminCheckTemplateSource`) and `POST …/check-all` (`adminCheckAllTemplateSources`) → `CheckResult` = `{ source_id, status: "updated"|"up_to_date"|"failed", commit_sha, ingested: [{ template_id, version, status, status_reason }], waiting: [{ template_id, version, since, last_reason }], problems: [{ template_id|null, dir, code, message }], check_failed_reason }` (check-all: `{ items }`). A read failure is a result, not an error. The lease rules and `409 template_check_in_progress` of C-596 apply.
  - `DELETE …/{id}` (`adminDeleteTemplateSource`) → 204, always allowed. Its templates, versions and token are deleted. **Agents made from them do not change**: they are copies, and their provenance keeps the template's id, name and version as text (C-1112). Critical `agent_template_source.remove` (metadata: url, template count).
  - **Poll job**: C-598's shape (claim due sources with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, lease `TEMPLATE_LEASE` 5 min, at most 2 per replica, ±10 % jitter, a failure backs off 1 h). The interval comes from the source's `update_interval`; the instance setting `agents.templates.update_interval` (default `1d`) is the value a new source starts with, and `never` is never due. While any version of the source is `awaiting_package`, the source is due again after at most `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE_RETRY` (15 min).
  - **Kill switch**: the instance setting `agents.templates.enabled` (default `true`). When it is off, the workspace and organization routes (C-1109, C-1110, C-1111) answer 404 `templates_disabled`, the admin routes keep working, and nothing else changes: agents made from templates are ordinary agents.
- C-1108 **Ingest and approval** (P6-TPL-2; D-457(b), (f)).
  - **The check** (per source, under its lease; both triggers). The reader is C-1125's with the template caps (`TEMPLATE_HELPER_FILE_MAX` per blob; the pack and inflate caps of C-1125), the user agent `aurora-templates/<version> git/2.0`, and the source's token on the repository's origin only.
    1. Resolve the ref. If the head commit equals `last_head_sha`, no version is `awaiting_package`, and no template has `head_state = needs_newer_aurora`, the result is `up_to_date` and nothing else is read.
    2. List the folder at the head. Read `aurora-templates.yaml` (`parse_manifest`; a failure is `failed` with `template_manifest_invalid`, and stored rows stay as they are) and every `<dir>/template.yaml` (`parse_meta`).
    3. Per template directory: problems in `template.yaml`, or a directory name that is not its id, are listed. If a stored template exists, it gets `head_state = invalid` and `head_problems`; it **keeps its stored versions** and nothing new is stored. An id held by another source is `id_taken`: it is listed and nothing is stored. Otherwise the `agent_templates` row is created, or its `dir`, `name`, `summary`, `head_version` and `head_state = ok` are updated. A `min_core` above the running core sets `head_state = needs_newer_aurora` and stores nothing. Stored templates whose directory is gone get `removed_upstream = true`, which is cleared if the directory comes back. Such a template **stays as it is**, and the admin decides.
    4. **Candidate**: the head's `version`, when it is semver-newer than every stored version of that template (any status). When nothing is stored yet, it is taken too. A new `awaiting_package` row is written with `commit_sha` = the head and `package_url` rendered. A head version that is not newer is ignored; if it is lower, the problem `version_not_newer` is listed.
    5. **Every `awaiting_package` row** of the source (the new one included): download `package_url`. The download is https, at most 3 redirects, each https and through the destination guard (`http_instance`); the source's token goes as HTTP Basic to the repository's origin only and never after a redirect to another origin; it is streamed under `TEMPLATE_ARCHIVE_MAX`, with a `TEMPLATE_FETCH_TIMEOUT` (30 s) total. A 404 or 410, a 5xx, a timeout or a refused redirect leaves the row `awaiting_package` with `status_reason` (`package_not_found`, `download_failed`) and `last_attempt_at`. After `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE_WAIT` (24 h) from `created_at`, the row becomes `invalid` with that reason, and that version is never tried again. A package over the cap is `invalid` `too_large` at once.
    6. A downloaded package: `read_archive` (problems → `invalid` with the first C-1141 code). Its `template.yaml` `id` and `version` must be the row's (`identity_mismatch`). Then read the template directory **at the row's `commit_sha`** through the reader (every blob under `<dir>/`) and `read_tree` it. The package's `content_sha256` must equal the tree's (`source_mismatch`, `invalid`); a tree that cannot be read keeps the row waiting. On a match, the row becomes `pending_approval` with `content`, `content_sha256`, `archive_sha256` and `size_bytes`. The avatar is re-encoded to a 256×256 PNG first; one that does not decode is `invalid` `template_field` on `avatar.*`.
    7. `last_head_sha` is set to the head. One critical `agent_template.ingest` row is written per version that leaves `awaiting_package` (metadata: template, version, status, reason, commit).
  - **What a newer version changes before it is approved: nothing** (D-456(f)). The offered version, the gallery, plans, creation and existing agents are unchanged by:
    - a version that is `awaiting_package`, `pending_approval`, `rejected` or `invalid`;
    - a head with problems (`head_state = invalid`) or `removed_upstream`;
    - a check that failed, or a repository that cannot be read or was deleted upstream.
    Stored content is never downloaded again and never re-read from the repository.
  - `GET /v1/admin/agent-templates` (`adminListTemplates`) → `{ items: [{ template_id, name, summary, source: { id, name }, offered, removed_upstream, head_version, head_state, head_problems, current_version: string|null (the offered version, below), versions: [{ version, status, status_reason, commit_sha, size_bytes, created_at, last_attempt_at, decided_at, decided_by: { id, display_name }|null }] (newest first), needs_apps: [{ app_id, installed: bool }], needs_libraries: [{ url, ref, path, added_here: bool }] (both from the current version, else the newest pending one) }] }`.
  - `GET /v1/admin/agent-templates/{template_id}/versions/{version}` (`adminGetTemplateVersion`) → `TemplateVersionDetail` = `{ template_id, version, status, status_reason, commit_sha, source: { id, name, repo, ref, path }, content_sha256, archive_sha256, size_bytes, content: TemplateContentView|null }`. `TemplateContentView` = `{ name, summary, readme (the template's `README.md`, in full), effort, min_core, agent: { name, may_message_first }, instructions, inputs: [InputDecl], apps: [AppEntry], skills: [{ name, use_it_when, body, files: [FileView] }], skill_refs: [SkillRef], helpers: [FileView], memories: [{ key, content, locked }] (`locked`: C-1151), automations: [{ kind: "schedule", name, prompt, cron } | { kind: "webhook", name, instructions, verification }], has_avatar }`, with `FileView` = `{ path, size, executable, sha256, text: string|null }` (`text` for valid UTF-8 up to 256 KiB, which includes every executable file, C-1141; else null). It is **everything the agent would be told or given, in full, as data**, never summarized or truncated. The avatar is `GET …/versions/{version}/avatar` (`image/png`).
  - `POST …/versions/{version}/approve` `{ content_sha256 }` (`adminApproveTemplateVersion`) works only from `pending_approval`. A hash that is not the stored one is 409 `template_content_changed`. It sets `approved`, `decided_by` and `decided_at`. `POST …/versions/{version}/reject` (`adminRejectTemplateVersion`) moves `pending_approval` to `rejected`; that version string is never ingested again. Critical `agent_template.approve` / `agent_template.reject`.
  - `PATCH /v1/admin/agent-templates/{template_id}` `{ offered: bool }` (`adminUpdateTemplate`): withdraw or offer again; critical `agent_template.update`.
  - **The offered version** of a template is its newest `approved` version, while `offered` is true. Older approved versions stay stored, because update offers diff against them (C-1112).
  - **Attention** (C-1022): a new item kind `agent_template_version` (`decision: true`) per `pending_approval` version, titled "<name> <version> is waiting for review", with `href` the admin review screen. A version `awaiting_package` for more than 1 h is an attention item without decision: "<name> <version> is waiting for its package".
- C-1109 **Organization switches** (P6-TPL-2; org `owner`/`admin`, **S/K**). `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/agent-templates` (`listOrgTemplates`) → `{ items: [{ template_id, name, summary, version, enabled }] }` (every offered template). `PUT /v1/orgs/{org}/agent-templates/{template_id}` `{ enabled: bool }` (`setOrgTemplate`) writes or deletes the `org_disabled_agent_templates` row; audit `org.template_availability` (ordinary). A template is **available** to a workspace when it is offered and not turned off for the workspace's organization. Every template is on by default.
- C-1110 **Templates for a workspace** (P6-TPL-2; **S/K**, `can_use` on the workspace). Everything here is about the **offered** version.
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agent-templates` (`listWorkspaceTemplates`) → `{ items: [TemplateCard] }`, available templates ordered by name. `TemplateCard` = `{ template_id, name, summary, readme (the offered version's `README.md`, in full, so the gallery can show it without another request), version, source_name (the source's display name, as the admin sources list shows it; display only), has_avatar, apps: [{ app_ids: [string], required, available: bool, skills: [string] (the entry's available apps' app skills, sorted, without repeats; C-1142) }], skill_refs: [{ index, kind, name, required, available: bool, repo: string|null (the reference's library address in C-1142's display form; null for an `organization` reference) }], has_helpers, automations: [{ kind, name }], ready: bool }`. `ready` means every required app entry has at least one available app and every required skill reference is available.
  - `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agent-templates/{template_id}` (`getWorkspaceTemplatePlan`) → `TemplatePlan` = `{ ...TemplateCard, content_sha256, content: TemplateContentView, entries: [{ index, required, why, choices: [{ app_id, app_name, available: bool, unavailable_reason: "not_installed"|"not_available_here"|null, level: "read"|"ask"|"act"|"custom", level_note: "custom_only"|null, custom: { allow_tools, ask_tools, dropped_tools }|null, accounts: [Account] }] }], skill_refs: [SkillRefPlan] }`.
    - `available` is false when the app is not active on the instance (`not_installed`), or not available to the workspace under the organization's app availability (`not_available_here`, `07 §App availability`).
    - `level` is the template's, except `act` on a `custom_only` app, which becomes `ask` with `level_note: "custom_only"`.
    - `custom` lists the template's tools that the app's current catalogue has; `dropped_tools` lists the ones it does not, which are never written.
    - `accounts` are the app's accounts (C-917 shape) **that the caller may give to an agent of this workspace** (the C-504 check), each with its `limit` where it has one.
    - `SkillRefPlan` is C-1142's resolution.
  - `GET …/{template_id}/avatar` (`image/png`). A template that is not available is 404 `not_found`.
- C-1111 **Create an agent from a template** (P6-TPL-3; **S/K**, `can_use` on the workspace).
