  - `POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/agents/from-template` (`createAgentFromTemplate`) `{ template_id, version, content_sha256, name, handle?, inputs: { <id>: string }, accounts: [{ entry: int, app_id, connection_id, level?: "read"|"ask"|"act", custom?: { allow_tools, ask_tools } }], skill_refs: [{ index, library_id, commit_sha }], automations: [{ kind: "schedule", name, enabled: bool, timezone? }] }` → 201 `{ agent: Agent, grants: [ConnectionGrant], skills: [Skill], webhooks: [<the webhook create response, secret included once>], notes: [{ code, entry?, index?, detail }] }`.
  - **Checks, all before any write** (422 with `details.fields` unless stated):
    - The template is available (404 otherwise).
    - `version` and `content_sha256` are the offered version's (409 `template_version_changed`, with the current `TemplatePlan` in `details.plan`).
    - `check_inputs` passes (`/inputs/<id>`, the C-1104 rules).
    - Every required entry has exactly one account (`template_account_missing`, `/accounts`, entry index), and no entry has more than one.
    - `app_id` is one of the entry's apps and is `available` (`template_app_unavailable`). The connection is of that app, `active`, and passes the C-504 check for the caller and this workspace (403 `forbidden` as C-504).
    - The access is the entry's `level` unless the body gives `level` or `custom` (the person changed it). An organization account's limit and `custom_only` apply exactly as C-980 (`422 above_account_limit` with `who_can`, `422 full_grant_forbidden`).
    - Tools not in the current catalogue are dropped (`notes`: `tools_dropped`). A custom choice left with no tool gives no access (`notes`: `access_not_given`).
    - **Skill references** (C-1142): every required `library` reference has a `skill_refs` item (`template_skill_missing`, `/skill_refs`, index). The `library_id` is a library that C-1142's resolution accepts for this reference and workspace (`template_skill_unavailable`). The skill at `commit_sha` is fetched and validated **before** the transaction, exactly as the library add path does (422 `library_skill_invalid`, 422 `library_unreachable`). An optional reference left out is skipped (`notes`: `skill_skipped`). A required `organization` reference must still be present (`template_skill_missing`).
    - `automations` names must be the template's schedules; a schedule left out is created with its template `enabled`. `timezone` is validated as a schedule's.
    - The name, handle and `max_agents` rules of agent creation apply.
    - The rendered instructions are within `BRIEF_MAX` (`template_too_large`).
  - **One transaction**, through the existing writers (no second implementation of any rule):
    - The agent: hosted, the default harness, no model (auto-selection), `effort` from the template (else `medium`), `brief` = the rendered instructions, `proactive_messaging` from `may_message_first`, the avatar.
    - The ordinary new-agent steps (starter grants, D-414).
    - Its agent-tier skills: ordinary editable skills.
    - Each chosen library skill, through **the library add path at the agent tier** with the fetched content: an ordinary library skill, pinned and read-only, with its budgets (422 `library_budget_exceeded` rolls back) and its own refresh and proposals.
    - The helper files (C-1143).
    - Memories (author kind `person`, the caller, provenance "from template <name> <version>"; a template memory with `locked: true` is created locked, `locked_by` the caller, C-1151).
    - Schedules, with creator = the caller, so fire-time authority is the caller's (`10 §Schedules`).
    - Webhooks, **always paused**, each with a new secret.
    - Each account's access, through **the one grant writer** with `via: "template"` (its `grant.create` critical rows, as for any grant).
    - Then the provenance of C-1112.
    **Any failure rolls everything back**; nothing is half-created. Audit `agent.create` gains the metadata `template: "<id>@<version>"`.
  - No rule, resource override, channel, presence, BYO setting, secret, skill library or governance change is ever written by this route (SEC-146).
- C-1112 **Provenance and updates** (P6-TPL-3; one migration, next free number when placed).
  - **Data.** `agents` gains `template_id text null`, `template_name text null`, `template_version text null` and `template_inputs jsonb null` (the checked input values). These are text, not foreign keys, so deleting a source or template changes no agent. A new table `agent_template_parts` = `{ agent_id uuid FK→agents on delete cascade, kind text CHECK in (instructions, skill, helpers, library_skill), key text (empty for instructions and helpers; the skill name; for library_skill the C-1104 `skill_ref_key`), content_sha256 text not null (the C-1104 part hash of what was written; for library_skill the ref key's hash), pk (agent_id, kind, key) }`, written at creation and at each apply.
  - `Agent` gains `template: { template_id, name, version } | null`.
  - **Until the person applies an update, the agent does not change** (D-457(f)). The agent keeps running its own content whatever versions are approved, offered, withdrawn or deleted. Skipping a version, or never answering, changes nothing. Its library skills keep their pinned commits until their own library update is approved.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/template` (`getAgentTemplate`; `can_use`) → `{ template: { template_id, name, version }|null, available: bool, update: null | { version, content_sha256, readme (the new version's `README.md`: what the template is for now; it is shown, never applied, and is not a part), changelog: null (format 1 keeps `CHANGELOG.md` out of the content), inputs_needed: [InputDecl], apps_added: [AppEntry], parts: [{ kind, key, state, current: string|null, proposed: string|null, skill_ref: SkillRefPlan|null }] } }`.
    - `update` is present when an available template with that id has an offered version semver-newer than `template_version`.
    - Parts that differ get a `state`: `unchanged_here` (the agent's part still has the recorded hash; the template changed it), `edited_here` (the agent's part was changed since), `removed_here` (recorded, but the agent no longer has it), `new` (in the new version only), or `removed_upstream` (recorded, not in the new version).
    - `current` and `proposed` are the full texts, rendered with the stored inputs (a needed input renders as `{input.<id>}`). For a skill this is `use-it-when`, the body and a list of its files with sizes and sha256. For `helpers` it is each file's path, size, executable bit and sha256, plus the text of text files. For a `library_skill` it is the reference, with `skill_ref` resolved as C-1142. A library skill's **content** is never a part: the library's refresh proposes it (D-457(g)).
    - Memories and automations are never offered again (they were a starting point): a newer version's memories, schedules and webhooks are used only when an agent is created (C-1111 writes them there); an apply writes none of them, and the offer's parts are only `instructions`, `skill`, `helpers` and `library_skill`. New apps are listed in `apps_added` and given on the Access tab as usual.
    - The proposed texts are rendered with the agent's **current** name and the **creator's** person name — the names the recorded hashes were made with (the person name is re-resolved from the creator's user row, so a creator renaming themselves shows the same way). Renaming the **agent** therefore makes every part whose text contains `{agent.name}` (instructions, skills) show as changed on the next offer, with the name swap as the only difference in the diff, until an apply re-records the hashes with the current name.
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/template/apply` (`applyAgentTemplate`; `can_use`) `{ version, content_sha256, inputs?: { <id>: string }, skill_refs?: [{ index, library_id, commit_sha }], parts: [{ kind, key }] }` → 200, the new `getAgentTemplate` answer. Only the listed parts change:
    - instructions are replaced;
    - a skill is replaced, created (`new`, `removed_here`) or deleted (`removed_upstream`);
    - `helpers` replaces the agent's helper files with the version's (C-1143);
    - a `library_skill` that is `new` is added through the library add path (its `skill_refs` item is required, checked as C-1111);
    - a `library_skill` that is `removed_upstream` deletes the agent's library skill of that name only while it is still sourced from a matching library (C-1142); otherwise it is left alone (`notes`: `skill_kept`).
    An `agent_template_parts` row is written for every part taken. `inputs` supplies `inputs_needed`; stored inputs that still pass are kept.
    Errors: a stale `version` or `content_sha256` → 409 `template_version_changed` ("This template was updated. Check the changes, then apply."); a part not in the offer → 422 `template_part_unknown`; a skill name taken by a library-sourced agent skill → 409 `library_skill_readonly`.
    It is one transaction (library fetches happen before it). The agent's home sync follows as for any brief, skill or helper edit. `template_version` becomes the applied version, also when no part was taken ("skip this version"). Audit `agent.template_apply` (ordinary; metadata: from, to, parts).
  - `DELETE /v1/agents/{agent}/template` (`detachAgentTemplate`; `can_use`) forgets the provenance (columns null, parts deleted), and no further offers are made. Helper files and library skills stay. Audit `agent.template_detach`.
- C-1113 **Limits, settings, audit** (P6-TPL-1/2/3/6).
  - `02 §Limits` gets rows for every `TEMPLATE_*` constant above (`aurora-core/src/limits.rs`): `TEMPLATE_ARCHIVE_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_UNPACKED_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_FILES_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_MEMORIES_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_SKILLS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_SKILL_REFS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_HELPER_FILE_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_TOTAL_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_AUTOMATIONS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_INPUTS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_INPUT_VALUE_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_README_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_APPS_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_MANIFEST_MAX`, `TEMPLATES_PER_SOURCE_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_SOURCES_MAX`, `TEMPLATE_LEASE`, `TEMPLATE_FETCH_TIMEOUT`, `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE_RETRY`, `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE_WAIT`.
  - Instance settings: `agents.templates.enabled` (bool, default true) and `agents.templates.update_interval` (C-590's enum, default `1d`), registered and shown like `plugins.addon_sources.update_interval`.
  - Security-critical audit actions (`04 §Audit`, written with `commit_critical`): `agent_template_source.add`, `.update`, `.remove`; `agent_template.ingest`, `.approve`, `.reject`, `.update`.
  - Ordinary audit actions: `org.template_availability`, `agent.template_apply`, `agent.template_detach`, `agent.helpers_remove`.
- C-1114 **Administration › What agents may do › Agent templates** (P6-TPL-4; `?tab=templates`, after Defaults).
  - **The sources list**: name, repository (`repo`, branch or "default branch", folder), the last head as `sha7`, schedule, last checked with status, template count. **Check now**, **Edit** (schedule, branch, folder, token) and **Remove** (with "Agents made from these templates keep working and do not change").
  - **Add** is the skill library's two-step dialog (C-1129):
    - Step 1: Repository address, Branch or tag, Folder, and the fold "This repository is private" (Access token, User name). Then **Check** (preview).
    - Step 2: what Aurora found. That is the repository name and description, the branch and `sha7`, where packages come from (`packages_url` as text), and each template with its version and state, with problems in words. Then **Add repository**.
  - **The templates list**: name, source, offered version, and chips for "Waiting for review" (with the count, linking to the review page), "Waiting for its package" (with since when) and "Removed from the repository". A head with problems shows "The repository's newest version has problems" with the list. It also shows the apps it needs (with "Not installed here") and the libraries it needs (with "Not added here"). **Withdraw** / **Offer again**.
  - **Review** of a version is a page. It shows everything of `TemplateContentView` in full. First comes **About this template**, the `README.md`, as its source text with a **Preview** toggle that renders it the way people will see it (C-1116). Then: instructions; each skill with its files; helper files (text in full, executable marked); skill references (kind, library, skill, why, and "Comes from that library as it is when an agent is created; its updates are reviewed in the library"); memories; automations; apps with their level or tools; inputs. All of it is marked as text from `<source name>` at `sha7`, with "The package matches the repository at <sha7>". When an approved version exists, a diff of every part against it is shown. **Approve** sends `content_sha256`; **Reject**.
  - While a newer version waits, the page says "People keep using <current version> until you approve this one."
- C-1115 **Organization › Agents › Templates** (P6-TPL-4; `?tab=templates`, org owners and admins): each offered template with its summary and a switch "Offered in <org>". One sentence on top: "Workspace members can create agents from these templates. Turning one off hides it; agents already made from it keep working."
- C-1116 **New agent from a template** (P6-TPL-4).
  - The New agent dialog's **Start from** row keeps the built-in purposes (D-413). It adds **More templates…** when the workspace has at least one available template. That opens the gallery of cards: avatar, name, summary, app icons, what it runs on its own, and "Needs <app>, which isn't available here" or "Needs the skill library <repo>" when not `ready`.
  - **Choosing a card opens its details first** (D-458), so people can decide before deploying. The details show the name, avatar and summary; **About this template**, the `README.md` rendered; what it needs (apps with availability, skill libraries, organization skills); what it runs on its own (schedules, and webhooks created paused); helper files by name; and the version and source name. **Use this template** opens the four-step page. **Back** returns to the gallery.
  - **How a README is rendered** (everywhere people see one): with the shared `Markdown` component (`web/src/components/ui/markdown.tsx`, sanitized, no raw HTML). Images are not loaded and are shown as their alt text. Links are only `https:` or `mailto:`, open in a new tab, and are marked as external. Relative links are plain text. Headings are shifted under the page's own heading. It is marked as text from `<source name>`.
  - **Use this template** opens the page `/o/<org>/w/<ws>/agents/new/<template_id>`. It has a fold **About this template** (the README, closed) above four steps:
    - **About**: name, handle, the inputs.
    - **Accounts**, per entry: the why; a choice among `any_of` apps; an existing account from `accounts`, or **Connect a new account** through the connect journey without the access step (the access is chosen here); and the access control preselected to the template's level (`AccessControl` of C-938, capped as C-980). Optional entries can be skipped.
    - **Automations**: each schedule with its cadence in words, prompt, time zone (the workspace's) and a switch; webhooks are listed as "created paused".
    - **Review**: the summary of what <Agent> can do (C-986); the instructions, skills, helper files and memories in full, as data, folded per part; and **Skills from libraries**, each with its library, the skill's content at the commit (as data) and, for optional ones, a switch "Add <skill>". Unavailable optional references are listed as "Not added: needs the skill library <repo>".
  - **Create** sends C-1111. Afterwards the new agent's chat opens, and each new webhook's secret is shown once (`SecretOnce`). A 409 `template_version_changed` reloads the plan with "This template was updated. Check the changes, then create."
- C-1117 **The agent's Template section** (P6-TPL-4; agent Settings › Manage (D-447), above Export and import; only for agents with `template` or helper files).
  - It says "Made from <template> <version>", and lists the helper files ("<n> helper files in .aurora/helpers", with **View** and **Remove**, C-1143).
  - With an `update`, it says "Version <v> is available. <Agent> keeps working as it is until you apply it." **Review changes** opens a dialog. It starts with the new version's **About this template** (its README, rendered as C-1116, in a fold), then lists each part with its state and a full diff (current vs proposed). Checkboxes are ticked for `unchanged_here` and `new`, and unticked for `edited_here`, `removed_here` and `removed_upstream`. A new library skill comes with its availability (or "Needs the skill library <repo>"). The dialog also shows the inputs needed, the apps added with **Give access** links, **Apply** (C-1112) and **Skip this version** (apply with no parts). A 409 `template_version_changed` closes the dialog, reads the offer again, and the section says "This template was updated. Check the changes, then apply."
  - **Stop following this template** is C-1112 detach, with a confirm.
- C-1118 **Save as template** (P6-TPL-5; web only).
  - Where: the agent Settings › Manage section next to Export (D-447), for the same people as Export, at the `export.agent` altitude.
  - The dialog has these fields:
    - Template name (default the agent's name);
    - id (follows the name until edited; the C-1141 rule);
    - version (default `1.0.0`);
    - summary (required);
    - **What it's for** (required, 1..`TEMPLATE_README_MAX`; a Markdown text area with a Preview toggle, prefilled with an outline to replace: "What <Agent> does", "Who it's for", "What it needs", "What it does on its own", "Adapting it"; Save is refused while the outline's placeholder lines are unchanged). It becomes `README.md`;
    - the export's checklist: instructions, memories, skills, helper files, schedules, webhooks. Memories are **unticked by default**, with "Memories can hold what <Agent> learned about people. Check them before you share this file."; a ticked locked memory is written with `locked: true` (C-1151);
    - one row per app <Agent> has access to (from `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/grants`): the level (or the custom tool lists), **Required**, and **Why** (required, 1..200).
  - **Save** downloads `<id>-<version>.zip`, rooted at `<id>/` so that it can be unzipped into a template repository as it is (C-1103). It holds:
    - the export files of the ticked groups;
    - `template.yaml` (no inputs; the dialog says how to add them, see `25`);
    - `agent.yaml` with `model: default`;
    - schedules without `time_zone`, and no pending one-time schedules (the dialog says how many were left out);
    - the agent's **library-sourced skills as `skills:` references** (`library` = the library's `url`, its `ref` and `path`, the skill's name; `required: true`; `why` = the skill's description, cut to 200), never their content;
    - the agent's helper files under `helpers/` (C-1143);
    - `README.md`: the **What it's for** text, followed by one closing line: "Made with Save as template on <date>. Accounts, secrets and conversations are not included." The dialog itself says how to publish ("Put this folder in a template repository and push; see the publisher guide").
  - Never written: workspace and organization skills (the dialog says they are left out), accounts, connection ids, secrets, webhook addresses, the AI key and library tokens.
  - Text that already contains `{input.`, `{agent.` or `{person.` is refused with one sentence naming the file, because it would be read as a placeholder. An executable file that is not text is refused with one sentence (C-1141).
- C-1119 **Tests** (`18`).
  - Crate: every C-1141 code (`template_readme_missing` included; the README is in `content_sha256`, `CHANGELOG.md` is not); zip bombs and bad names; `read_tree` and `read_archive` equal on the same files (git modes and zip attributes); the executable-text rule; one-pass substitution with hostile values; the canonical hash golden; part hashes (helpers and skill ref keys included); `pack` determinism; manifest and `packages.url` rendering; a web-written golden archive (C-1118) read back.
  - CLI: `check` on a template, a zip and a repository; `list --json`; `pack` byte-identical; the reference workflow's logic run against a local fixture (C-1144).
  - API, sources: lifecycle and poll states; preview without storing; a private fixture repository with a token (sent only to its origin, never after a cross-origin redirect); head unchanged short-circuit.
  - API, ingest: `awaiting_package` retried then expired; `source_mismatch`; `identity_mismatch`; `id_taken`; `min_core`; `head_state = invalid` keeping stored versions; `version_not_newer`; invalid stored once; `removed_upstream`. **The previous version stays offered and usable** through each of those states and through a failed check.
  - API, approval and catalogue: approve with a stale hash; withdraw; organization switch; catalog filtering (not installed, app availability, library not added, organization skill missing, kill switch).
  - API, create from template: every refusal; rollback when the last write fails (the library skill and the helpers included); `via: "template"` grants and their audit rows; C-504 refusal; `above_account_limit`; `custom_only` cap; dropped tools; webhooks paused with the secret once; schedule creator; starter grants; `max_agents`; library skills added pinned and read-only; an optional reference skipped.
  - API, updates: each part state for every kind; edited parts untouched unless chosen; a needed input; stale hash; detach; a library skill of the same name; the agent unchanged while an update waits.
  - The permission matrix rows of every route; audit coverage of the critical actions.
  - Supervisor (C-1143): helper projection modes, pruning and an older supervisor skipping the kind.
  - Web: vitest for each screen and the zip writer; the README rendering rules of C-1116 (a hostile README with raw HTML, a `javascript:` link, a remote image and a relative link renders none of them active); the details view before the four steps; Playwright mocked for the admin tab, the Add dialog and review, the organization tab, the gallery and the four steps, the Template section and Save as template.
- C-1142 **Skill references** (P6-TPL-1 parses them; P6-TPL-2 resolves them; P6-TPL-3 adds them; D-456(d), D-457(c)).
  - **Format** (`template.yaml` `skills:`; at most `TEMPLATE_SKILL_REFS_MAX`, 20). Each item has exactly one of `library` or `organization`, plus `required` (default true) and `why` (1..200):
    - `library: <address>` with `skill: <name>` and optional `ref` and `path`. The address follows the C-1123 rules (normalized the same way, so a page URL is accepted and its ref and folder fill `ref` and `path` when absent). `skill` is a skill name (the `10 §Skills` rules, not `aurora-*`).
    - `organization: <name>`: a skill name expected at the organization or workspace tier.
    A name may appear only once across the template's own `skills/` and its references (`template_skill_ref_invalid`). `SkillRef` (in `TemplateContentView`) = `{ index, kind: "library"|"organization", name, required, why, library: { url, repo, ref, path }|null }`.
  - **Resolution for a workspace** (`SkillRefPlan` = `SkillRef` + `{ available, unavailable_reason, repo, library: { id, label, scope, repo, ref, path, commit_sha }|null, content: { use_it_when, body, files: [FileView] }|null }`; the reference's own `repo` is its library address in the C-1123 display form — `host/path`, no scheme, no trailing `.git` — the same normalization `library.repo` uses, and null for an `organization` reference):
    - **`library`**: the candidate libraries are those the workspace's organization can use: its own, then the instance libraries (C-1128), `active`, while `skills.library.enabled` is on. A candidate matches when its stored `url` equals the normalized address, its `path` equals the reference's (default the root), and, when the reference gives a `ref`, its `ref` equals it (a library without a ref is the default branch and matches a reference without one). The first match wins: the organization's own before instance ones, then by label. The skill must be listed as valid at the library's current head; the browse cache (C-571, 5 min) and `content` come from the library's content read at that `commit_sha`.
      `unavailable_reason` is one of `library_not_added` ("Needs the skill library <repo>. An organization admin can add it."), `skill_not_in_library`, `library_unreadable` (paused or in error), `libraries_disabled`, or `over_budget` (the agent-tier budget could not hold it).
    - **`organization`**: available when an enabled organization-tier skill, or a workspace-tier skill of this workspace, has that name; otherwise `not_in_organization`. Nothing is ever written for it: the effective skill set projects it to the agent as to every agent of the workspace.
  - **Adding** (C-1111, C-1112) uses the same code as the agent-tier `from_library` create (C-572 / `10 §Git skill library`), with the caller's authority (`can_use` for the agent tier). The fetch and validation happen before the transaction; the write happens inside it. The result is an ordinary library skill: pinned to `commit_sha`, read-only, refreshed and proposed by its library, removable and detachable as any library skill.
  - **Trust.** A referenced library skill's content is not part of the template's approval. It is trusted the way every library skill is: an organization or instance admin added that library, and the person sees the content on the Review step before creating. The admin's review shows the reference (C-1114). A template never creates, edits or enables a skill library.
  - **App skills** (P5-APPSKILL) need no reference: they reach every agent that has access to the app, so the template's `apps:` entry is enough. `TemplateCard` and `TemplatePlan` list them per available app (`apps[].skills: [name]`, from the app's active package), so people see "Includes the <App> app's skills".
  - **`core`** is not a kind in format 1 (Aurora ships no built-in skills besides app skills). It is refused as an unknown key.
- C-1143 **Helper files on an agent** (P6-TPL-6; one migration, next free number when placed; D-456(e), D-457(d)).
  - **Data.** `agent_helper_files` = `{ agent_id uuid not null FK→agents on delete cascade, path text not null (the C-1141 helper path below `helpers/`), content bytea not null, executable bool not null, sha256 text not null, size integer not null, created_at timestamptz not null, pk (agent_id, path) }`. Per agent, at most `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_MAX` files and `TEMPLATE_HELPERS_TOTAL_MAX` bytes (checked by the writer). The gateway role gets nothing.
  - **The writer** `agent_helpers::replace_all(tx, agent, files)` (one function, used by C-1111 and C-1112) replaces the agent's set in the caller's transaction and bumps the agent's home-sync generation. Every helper file must be UTF-8 text without a NUL byte (owner decision 2026-11-24, option A: the home sync carries text only): the writer refuses non-text content with 422 `template_helper_invalid` naming the path, so nothing non-text can be stored through any caller. Agent tools cannot write helpers (no tool exists).
  - **Routes** (**S/K**, `can_use` on the agent's workspace):
    - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/helpers` (`listAgentHelpers`) → `{ items: [{ path, size, executable, sha256 }] }`.
    - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/helpers/content?path=` (`getAgentHelper`) → the bytes, with the C-903 headers and `X-Helper-Executable: true|false`.
    - `DELETE /v1/agents/{agent}/helpers?path=` (`deleteAgentHelper`; without `path`, every helper file) → 204.
    Audit: `agent.helpers_remove` (ordinary; metadata: paths).
  - **Projection** (`12 §Home projection`, `16`). A new home-sync part kind `helpers` comes between `apps` and `previews`. Its entries are in the shape of `skills` file entries (content encoding and executable bit as there), at `.aurora/helpers/<path>`. Since the writer refuses non-UTF-8 content, a non-text entry cannot occur; the projection still skips one defensively (a warning, never a write elsewhere). `.aurora/helpers/` is a **managed root**: an entry is accepted only below it with the C-1141 name rules. Files are installed `0555` when executable and `0444` otherwise, in `0555` directories, and the root is pruned to the manifest at commit, so removed helpers disappear. An older supervisor skips the kind, which only means no helper files.
  - **Instructions.** `render_inputs` gains `helpers: { dir: ".aurora/helpers", count }|null`. When it is set, the supervisor's capability fragment says: "Helper files from your template are in `.aurora/helpers/` (read-only). Run or read them from there." A sync that changes only helper files leaves the instruction files byte-identical when the count is unchanged.
- C-1144 **The reference CI** (P6-TPL-1; `25` is normative; the example repository `templates/examples/` carries it; D-456(c)).
  - **GitHub Actions**, `.github/workflows/aurora-templates.yml`, with two jobs.
    - `check` runs on `pull_request` and on `push`, with `permissions: contents: read` and no secret. Steps: checkout; install the `aurora` CLI at a pinned version (the release binary, or `cargo install --locked aurora-cli --version <x>`); then `aurora template check .`.
    - `publish` runs only on `push` to the default branch, `needs: check`, with `permissions: contents: write` and `concurrency: { group: aurora-templates-publish, cancel-in-progress: false }`. For each `{ id, version, dir }` of `aurora template list . --json`:
      - It packs: `aurora template pack <dir> -o dist/<id>-<version>.zip`.
      - If the release `<id>-v<version>` exists, it downloads that release's `<id>-<version>.zip` and compares it with `cmp`. When they differ, it fails with "<id> changed without a new version: raise version in <dir>/template.yaml".
      - Otherwise it runs `gh release create <id>-v<version> dist/<id>-<version>.zip --target "$GITHUB_SHA" --title "<name> <version>"`, with `--notes-file <dir>/CHANGELOG.md` when that file exists.
      - It never uses `--clobber` and never deletes a release.
    - The manifest's `packages.url` for this workflow is `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/download/{id}-v{version}/{id}-{version}.zip`.
  - **GitLab CI** (a sketch in `25` and `templates/examples/.gitlab-ci.yml`): the same two jobs.
    - `publish` uploads to the generic package registry with `CI_JOB_TOKEN` (`PUT …/packages/generic/<id>/<version>/<id>-<version>.zip`), after the same compare against an existing package.
    - The project setting "Allow duplicate generic packages" off is the no-overwrite rule.
    - `packages.url` is the matching download URL.
  - **Any other CI** works if it publishes, at the rendered `packages.url`, the bytes that `aurora template pack` writes, and never replaces them. Aurora checks the package against the repository (C-1108). So a CI that packs something else only makes that version `invalid` (`source_mismatch`); it can never ship content that differs from the repository.
  - A test in P6-TPL-1 runs the workflow's shell logic against a local fixture: a fake `gh` that records calls, a repository folder with two templates, one already released unchanged, one released and changed, and one new.
- C-1145 **The person's shell preference** (FIX-218, D-459; migration `20261201000273`, `03 §terminal_shell_preferences`). The shell (`bash` or `zsh`) a person's web terminal and SSH sessions start on an agent, stored on the server per person and agent.
  - `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/terminal/shell` answers `200 { "shell": "bash" | "zsh", "stored": bool }`. With nothing saved it answers `bash` with `stored: false`.
  - `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/terminal/shell` takes `{ "shell": "bash" | "zsh" }` and answers `200 { shell, stored: true }`. Any other value (a path, another shell, a different case) is 422 `invalid_shell`; nothing is stored.
  - Both need the session cookie (an API key or no cookie is 401), `can_use` of the agent's workspace (404 or 403 as for the terminal) and a hosted agent (BYO is 422 `terminal_not_supported`). The terminal feature switch and the freeze gate do not apply: the choice is also used by SSH, and a frozen agent's setting stays readable and writable. A person reads and writes only their own row.
  - The terminal WebSocket's `?shell=` (C-616, FIX-138) stays as an explicit override; when it is absent the stored preference applies, else `bash`. The dashboard's terminal pane no longer sends it.
  - The SSH grant redemption (`POST /v1/runner/preview-streams/open`, `kind: "ssh"`, C-796) answers `shell`: the stored preference of the session's own person for the grant's agent, `bash` when there is no row. The runner copies it into `exec.open.shell` (`16 §W20`, optional, absent = bash).
  - The value is a closed enum end to end: the table CHECK, `PtyShell` in the API and the protocol, and the supervisor's fixed binary paths. A client never supplies a path.
  - The row is removed with the person or the agent (FK cascade). One writer: the `PUT`.
- C-1146 **Mentions carry the platform's handle and a label** (FIX-219, D-460; `13 §4.1`, `20`; no migration).
  - `message.posted.mentions[]` gains `handle?: string` and `label?: string` (serde default, omitted when absent; schemas regenerated). `handle` is the mention as the platform writes it, sigil included, matching `^[@~#][A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}$` (otherwise the core ignores it); `label` is the display name of the person, bot or space (the core applies `sanitize::label`; empty → ignored).
  - Mattermost: users and bots get `handle: "@<username>"` and `label` = the author block's display-name rule; `~<channel name>` of the integration's team becomes `<#space:ID>` with `kind: "space"`, `handle: "~<name>"`, `label` = the channel's display name, for an open channel, or a private one whose member is the message's author (never `D`/`G`, never deleted; at most 10 names per message; lookups cached one minute; a failure leaves the text as typed).
  - Core: the turn text replaces each `<@user:R>`, `<@bot:R>`, `<#space:R>` whose `R` has a mention with a valid `handle` by that handle, and when any was replaced appends `[Mentioned: @h (label), @b (bot), ~c (channel "label"), …]` (the label omitted when absent or equal to the handle without its sigil; at most 20 entries, then `, …`; `"` stripped from labels) after the message and before the failed-attachment lines. Tokens without a usable mention are left unchanged. Built as: the parser and the handle grammar live in `aurora-channels` (`content::parse_mention_token`, `valid_mention_handle`), shared by the connector's renderer and the core (`inbox/mention_text.rs`); labels also lose `[ ] ( ) < >`; the Mentioned line is clamped separately from the message.
  - `send_message`'s `text` description: "To mention someone or link a channel, write it exactly as messages show it (for example `@jdoe` or `~town-square`)." (No platform name: guard SEC-33 forbids integration names in `services/`.)
- C-1147 **Linking chooses the agent that answers direct messages** (FIX-220, D-461; `13 §5`, `§5.9`, `05`, `14`; no migration; amends C-36 and C-37).
  - `GET /v1/me/dm-default-options?integration_id=` (session only; the caller must be an active member of the integration's organization, the C-36 preview's rule and errors; an unknown integration is 404) → 200 `{ org_id /* the integration's organization; the link page needs it to point a person with no agent at that organization's workspaces */, shared_bot: { handle, display_name } | null, items: [{ agent_id, name, handle, workspace_id, workspace_name, attached }] }`. `items` holds the agents of **that organization** whose workspace the caller is in **in their own right** (`own_workspace_access`, C-1157; amended by D-473: an org admin's implicit `can_use` on every workspace no longer lists other members' agents), sorted by workspace name, then agent name; `attached` is whether the agent has an active shared presence on the integration. `shared_bot` is `null` and `items` empty when the integration is not active, has no shared bot, or `presence_shared` is off.
  - `PUT /v1/me/dm-defaults { integration_id, agent_id }` (C-37) now also attaches: when the agent is the caller's own (C-1157; was `can_use`), the agent belongs to the integration's organization, the integration offers the shared mode and the agent has no shared presence there, the PUT inserts the shared presence and writes the default in **one transaction** (audit `channel_presence.attach` with `metadata.via = "dm_default"`). An agent of another organization, one the caller cannot use and that is not attached, or one reached only through an approved person grant that is not attached, stays 422 `agent_not_reachable` and nothing is written. An agent already attached behaves as before.
  - `POST /v1/channel-links/confirm { token, platform_user_ref, agent_id? }` (C-36): `agent_id` applies the same rule in the **same transaction** as the link (audit `channel_presence.attach` with `via = "link"`). When it is refused (422 `agent_not_reachable`) the whole confirm is refused, no link, presence or default is written, and the token is **not** consumed. Without `agent_id` the confirm is unchanged.
  - Shared helper: `presences::attach_shared_tx` is the one insert of a shared presence (C-34 attach, the PUT and the confirm). No right is widened: the attach goes through `can_use`, the organization match and the integration's capability check, all on the server.
  - Direct-message "not set up" reply (`13 §5` step 3): see D-461(c); no API change.
- C-1148 **Locked memories: data and routes** (P6-MEMLOCK Part A, D-463, D-464; one migration, next free number when placed; `03 §memories`, `05`).
  - `memories` gains `locked boolean not null default false`, `locked_at timestamptz null` and `locked_by uuid null` (FK→users, `on delete set null`). `locked_at` and `locked_by` are set when `locked` becomes true and cleared when it becomes false; `locked_by` is null for an API key without a user, and for a person who was later deleted. No index.
  - `Memory` gains `locked: bool`, `locked_at: string|null` and `locked_by: { id, display_name } | null`.
  - `PUT /v1/agents/{agent}/memories/{key}/locked` (`setMemoryLocked`; **S/K**, the same workspace access as the other memory routes; 404 for an unknown agent or key) `{ locked: bool }` → 200 `Memory`. It is idempotent: the same value changes nothing, writes no audit row and bumps nothing. A change writes the audit row `memory.lock` or `memory.unlock` (ordinary; metadata `{ key }`), bumps the agent's home-sync generation, and writes **no revision** (a lock is not a content change).
  - `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/memories` accepts `locked?: bool` (default false; `true` sets `locked_at`/`locked_by` from the caller). `PUT …/memories/{key}`, `restore` and `redact` keep the lock as it is. A person's `DELETE` of a locked memory deletes it.
  - **The lock binds only the agent** (D-463(a)). Every person route works on a locked memory as on any other.
  - Locked memories count toward `MEMORY_MAX_PER_AGENT` like any other (D-463(c)); a new key at the cap is refused as today even when every existing memory is locked.
- C-1149 **Locked memories: the agent side** (P6-MEMLOCK Part A, D-463, D-464; `10 §Memory`, `12 §Home projection`, `§Memory harvest`, `16`).
  - **One rule, in the memory service.** A write or delete authored by the agent (`author_kind = agent`: the `memory_save` and `memory_delete` tools, and `POST /v1/runner/memory-writes` from the harvest) on a locked memory is refused with 409 `memory_locked`, "This memory is locked: a person set it, and you can read it but not change or delete it. If it looks wrong or out of date, tell the person." The check runs inside the write's transaction, after the agent row lock that `set_locked` also takes, so a lock that commits first always wins. The agent can still create memories under new keys.
  - No agent tool, platform-tool family or `aurora-agent call aurora` verb can lock, unlock, restore or redact. The memory family is exactly `memory_save`, `memory_get`, `memory_list`, `memory_search`, `memory_delete`.
  - **Tools.** `memory_get` returns `locked`; `memory_list` and `memory_search` items carry `locked`. The tool error of a refused `memory_save` or `memory_delete` is the 409's message. Both descriptions gain: "A memory marked locked was set by a person: you can read it, but you cannot change or delete it."
  - **Home sync.** A locked memory's `memory` entry carries `locked: true` (omitted when false, so an unlocked memory's entry is unchanged). Its projected file gets `locked: true` as the last front-matter line. Its index line (in `memory/index.md`, the turn-start recall index and `memory_list`) ends with ` (locked)` after the clamped line, within `MEMORY_INDEX_MAX`. `render_inputs.memory_locked = true` when the agent has at least one locked memory; otherwise absent.
  - **Supervisor.** `FileEntry.locked` (serde default false; unknown fields are still accepted, so an older supervisor ignores it). Locked memory files are installed `0444`, other memory files `0644`. At `final` the projector records the locked keys and the exact bytes it installed for each, in memory only. The harvest **never uploads a locked key**. When such a file's checksum fails, or the file is missing or replaced, the harvest writes the recorded bytes back with `0444` and gives the next turn one notice per changed digest: "memory `<key>` is locked: a person set it, and you can read it but not change or delete it. Your change was undone." An older supervisor that does upload gets the 409, which reaches the turn through the existing `MemoryWriteResult` notice.
  - **Instructions.** When `render_inputs.memory_locked` is set, the memory capability fragment adds: "Memories marked (locked) were set by a person. Use them, but do not try to change or delete them; if one looks wrong or out of date, say so." Without it, the instruction files are byte-identical to before.
- C-1150 **Locked memories: dashboard, export and import** (P6-MEMLOCK Part B, D-463(b), D-464; `14`).
  - **Memory page** (the agent's Memory, at Use; also wherever `MemoryTab` is used):
    - Each locked row shows the shared lock icon and a `Locked` badge after its title.
    - The editor has a switch **Locked** above Save, with the help text "<Agent> can read this memory but can't change or delete it. You can still edit it." The switch saves on its own (`setMemoryLocked`), independently of Save, with the toast "Locked" or "Unlocked". When the memory is locked, a line under it reads "Locked by <display name> on <date>" ("Locked on <date>" without `locked_by`).
    - The new-memory form has a checkbox **Lock it**, unticked by default, with the same help text; ticked, it sends `locked: true` with the create.
    - Revision history is unchanged: a lock is not a revision.
  - **Vocabulary** (`14 §Vocabulary`): **Locked** (a memory), instead of read-only, pinned or protected.
  - **Export** (`14 §Export and import an agent`): `memory/<key>.md` front matter gains `locked: true` for a locked memory; nothing is written for an unlocked one, so existing exports are unchanged. **Import** accepts `locked` as a boolean (any other value refuses the file, naming it), sends it with the create, and the checklist says *3 memories (1 locked)* when any are locked.
- C-1151 **Template memories may arrive locked** (D-463(d); built by P6-TPL-1, -3, -4, -5; amends C-1141, C-1108, C-1111, C-1118).
  - **Format (C-1141).** `memory/<key>.md` front matter gains optional `locked` (a boolean, default false; any other value is `template_field` at `memory/<key>.md#/locked`). It is part of the parsed content and of `content_sha256`, so the admin approves it with the rest.
  - **Review (C-1108, C-1114, C-1116).** `TemplateContentView.memories[]` gains `locked`. The admin review and the person's review mark a locked memory "Locked: <Agent> can read it but not change it".
  - **Create (C-1111).** A locked template memory is created with `locked: true`, `locked_by` = the caller, in the same transaction.
  - **Save as template (C-1118).** A ticked locked memory is written with `locked: true`, as in the export.
  - **Updates (C-1112).** Unchanged: memories are never offered again, so a lock set or removed by a person afterwards stays theirs.

- C-1157 **Chat offers only the person's own agents** (FIX-230, D-473; one migration `20261201000276_own_workspace_access.sql`; `04 §Groups`, `13 §5`, `05`; amends C-36, C-37, C-1147; no new route, no new field except below).
  - View `own_workspace_access (workspace_id, org_id, user_id, role)`: the direct-binding (active, manual or IdP-claimed) and group-binding branches of `effective_workspace_access`, grouped the same way (strongest role), for an active org member not suspended in the workspace. `effective_workspace_access` is redefined as the implicit-owner branch (effective org owners/admins) `UNION ALL` `own_workspace_access`: **its output is unchanged** (pinned by an IT against the old definition). The view joins people tables: API code only, never a shared `aurora-db` function the gateway calls.
  - `authz::own_access(exec, user, ws) -> Option<WorkspaceRole>` reads it. `can_use`, `can_own` and every authorization check still read `effective_workspace_access`.
  - "Own agent" = `own_access` on the agent's workspace, or an approved person reach grant on the integration. It is the test for what chat **offers or picks by itself**: DM single-agent auto-route and the picker, `agents`, `use <handle>`, `GET /v1/me/dm-default-options`, the DM "not set up" test, the binding picker (own workspaces the adder can also own), and the server rule of `PUT /v1/me/dm-defaults` and the confirm's `agent_id` (an agent that is not the caller's own, attached or not, is 422 `agent_not_reachable`; a reach-grant agent works only when already attached). A stored DM default that is no longer an own agent is ignored by routing and kept.
  - Authorization is untouched: thread links, space bindings (`message.rs` `authorized`, `messaging.rs`), `bind <handle>`, Attach (C-34) and the dashboard keep `can_use`/`can_own`.
  - Labels: picker options read `Name (@handle) · Workspace` (the workspace part is shortened first when the 80-character label bound bites); the text fallback lists `` `handle` (Name · Workspace) ``; the `agents` reply shows `` `handle` (Name · Workspace) ``. `use <handle>` with several own matches writes nothing, replies with the matches and `<external_url>/account/channels` (inbox outcome `use_ambiguous`).

- C-1158 **A refused OAuth sign-in says what the provider answered** (FIX-232, D-475; amends C-842 and C-14; no route, migration or OpenAPI change).
  - **When.** The error code stays `oauth_exchange_failed`. A refusal is attached only when the flow is bound to the caller (the landing is known; an unbound or tampered flow still reveals nothing). There are two stages: `authorize`, when the provider sends the person back with `error` (and `error_description`) on the callback; and `token`, when the code exchange answers non-2xx or cannot be reached.
  - **Shape.** `reason: { stage: "authorize" | "token", status?: number /* the token endpoint's HTTP status; absent at authorize and when unreachable */, error?: string /* the OAuth error code */, provider_code?: string }`. `error` is a fixed identifier: at most 64 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, otherwise dropped. `provider_code` is the provider's own error number at the start of `error_description`, before the first colon, as Microsoft Entra writes it (`AADSTS50194: …`): 1–32 ASCII letters and digits with at least one of each, otherwise absent. Nothing else of the provider's answer reaches the page or the dashboard.
  - **Page.** The failure page adds one escaped line: "The provider refused the sign-in: <codes>." (authorize), "The provider refused the token request (HTTP <status>): <codes>." (token), or "The provider's token endpoint could not be reached." `<codes>` is `provider_code, error`, whichever are present. The channel message becomes `{ type: "aurora:connect-failed", app_id?, error, reason? }`.
  - **Dashboard.** The waiting dialog shows "The sign-in did not complete (<error>)." followed by the same sentence. Without a `reason` it adds "Try again." as before.
  - **Log.** The API logs one `warn` ("the provider refused the OAuth sign-in") with `app_id`, `stage`, `status`, `error`, `provider_code` and the `error_description` with control characters removed, cut to 300 characters. The description goes to the server log only.

- C-1159 **A sign-in app's own value in an OAuth URL's path** (FIX-233, D-476; amends C-244 and `09 §3.3`; no route or migration; one OpenAPI field).
  - **Manifest.** A fixed `authorize_url`/`token_url` may contain `{client.<field>}` as a whole path segment (`https://login.microsoftonline.com/{client.tenant}/oauth2/v2.0/token`). The field must be declared in the client's `fields`, be non-secret, and have the new `default` (1–256 characters, a valid segment). A secret field never has a `default`. Only these placeholders are allowed in a fixed URL, and never beside `{instance}`. Violations: `oauth_url_client_field_invalid`, `client_field_default_invalid`.
  - **Value rule.** 1–253 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9.-]`, starting and ending with a letter or digit, no `..` (a GUID, a domain name, `common`). The client's stored public value is used, trimmed; when it is absent or blank, the field's `default` is used.
  - **Sign-in apps (C-244).** `POST /v1/…/oauth-clients` no longer requires a field that has a `default`. On create and `PATCH`, a value for a field that a URL places in its path must pass the value rule, else 422 `invalid_request` on `/fields/<name>` ("must be an ID or a domain name (letters, digits, dots and hyphens)").
  - **App detail.** `AppOauthClient.fields[]` (`OauthClientField`) gains `default: string | null`. The dashboard's sign-in app form shows it as the placeholder, with the hint "Optional. Leave empty to use “<default>”."
  - **Where it is filled.** The connect start (authorize and token URL), the code exchange, the sign-in app Test (C-1070) and the gateway's refresh all fill the URL from the minting client's public fields. A stored value that fails the rule fails closed: the connect start answers 422 as for a missing tenant, the exchange `oauth_exchange_failed`, the Test `not_testable`, and the refresh `unavailable`.
  - **Microsoft 365 1.5.0.** Its client gains `tenant` ("Directory (tenant) ID", default `common`), and both URLs use `{client.tenant}`. Existing sign-in apps keep `common`.

- C-1166 **A sign-in asks for the scopes the request chose** (FIX-237, D-481; amends C-14, C-956 and the `scopes` field of `POST /v1/apps/{app}/connect`; no route, migration or schema change).
  - **Request.** For an `oauth2` method, `scopes` is what the provider's authorize request asks for. The journey sends the chosen level's `scope_presets` (C-956), or the person's edit under More options. Each must be a scope the method declares, else 400 `invalid_request` on `/scopes`. A method that declares scopes needs at least one. Repeats are dropped, and the order is kept.
  - **Without `scopes`.** A reconnect asks for what its connection asked for before (`requested_scopes`, still declared ones only). Otherwise the sign-in uses the method's `default_scopes`, as before. The Reconnect dialog sends none.
  - **Recorded.** The connection's `requested_scopes`, and `missing_scopes` at the callback, are computed from the scopes actually requested. Before this, the server ignored `scopes` for sign-in methods and always sent `default_scopes`, so the dialog's switches and the "Read and change" level had no effect.
  - **Dashboard.** The More options switches of a sign-in method start at the chosen level's preset, which is what is sent, rather than at `default_scopes`. An edit is always sent, even when empty, so the server refuses it rather than falling back to the defaults. With every switch off, the step does not submit: it opens More options and says "Turn on at least one permission."
  - **Built-in presets pinned** where the derived preset would ask for more than a level means: Microsoft 365 1.5.1 (read = the defaults, without `Sites.Read.All`; write = the defaults plus `Mail.Send`, `Calendars.ReadWrite`, `Files.ReadWrite`; superseded by 1.6.0, D-482: mail and calendar only, no `Files.*` or `Sites.*`), Indico 1.6.2 (the extended `read:everything`/`full:everything` stay out; write adds `write:legacy_api`), GitHub 2.5.1, both `github` and `github-ghes` (write = `read:user`, `read:org`, `repo`, without `workflow`). Those scopes are still offered under More options.

## Shared schemas

_Defined alongside the milestone sections above._
